# Project context

This is a digital implementation of a simplified WoW TCG ruleset.
Always check the rules in `References/wow_rules.txt` before implementing any
keyword or card effect — do not rely on general CCG knowledge (Magic,
Hearthstone) since WoW TCG has its own specific rulings.

Card data lives in: `data/cards.csv` (plus `data/tokens.csv` for effect-created
tokens — both load into one `CardDatabase` via `load_all()`)
Active playtest scene: `renderer/tests/playtest.gd` (human vs AI, full UI)
Engine lives in: `game_logic/` — see Architecture section below.

---

## Implementing a card — required steps

1. Find the card in `data/cards.csv` (grep by name). Read the card image to get exact card text. If `image_path` is blank but the image file exists under `assets/cards/`, fill in `image_path` now.
2. **Confirm the card text with the user before writing any code.** Also check `References/wow_rules.txt` for any keywords or mechanics.
3. Implement: engine actions in `game_logic/stack_resolver.gd`, AI heuristics in `game_logic/ai/base_ai.gd` if needed. **A card granting the hero a keyword needs no new flag** — `hero_keyword:KEYWORD` for a while-in-play grant, `hero_grant_keyword:KEYWORD` for a timed one, a `GameState.FORM_GRANTS` entry for something a form confers. All three are read by the one `_has_keyword` funnel.
4. Write headless test scenarios in `game_logic/tests/test_scenarios.gd` covering the new mechanic.
5. Update `data/cards.csv`: set `data_status=verified`, `engine_status=implemented`, fill `power_text` and `effects` recipe. Do this last, after tests pass.
6. **Ask the user whether the newly implemented card should be added to any deck(s)** in `decks/battle_ready/*.json`. Do not add it unprompted.

---

## Architecture (game_logic/)

### Core invariants — violating these is a bug:

- **Rules functions never touch the bus.** They return `Array[GameEvent]`. The caller emits.
- **Rules functions never read Godot nodes.** No `get_tree()`, no autoloads, no scene refs. Pass `db` when def lookups are needed.
- **Zone moves go through `GameLogic.move_card()` only.** Never mutate `zone.card_ids` or `card.zone_id` directly.
- **Derived stats are never cached on CardInstance.** Call `state.get_atk(id, db)` etc. Caching causes stale-read bugs.
- **An in-play card's POWERS are read through `state.effective_def(id, db)`, never `db.get_def(...)`.** Rule 700.3 lets a modifier blank a card's text box (Polymorph), so the printed def is the wrong question for anything in play — keywords, activated powers, triggers and continuous auras alike. It returns the printed def unchanged when nothing is silencing the card, so routing a site costs one dictionary lookup. Reads about a card in a HAND, DECK or GRAVEYARD keep using `db.get_def` (700.2 — powers function in play), and so do reads of printed STATS, cost, name and card type, which a blank text box never touches.
- **Fold inner events:** `events.append_array(inner_call(...))`.

### Key files:

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `game_logic/state/game_state.gd` | Single source of truth. All mutable game data. Also `FORM_GRANTS` — what each named form MEANS (bear → protector, cat → +1 ATK while attacking, both → the Feral break), keyed on the form name so `form_state:NAME` is a Form card's whole recipe. |
| `game_logic/state/player_state.gd` | Per-player flags (resources, pet_capacity, hero_instance_id…) |
| `game_logic/state/card_instance.gd` | Runtime card state (zone, damage, keywords, buffs…) |
| `game_logic/stack_resolver.gd` | ALL rules validation (`can_submit`) and resolution (`_resolve_*`). Priority/chain logic. |
| `game_logic/turn_manager.gd` | Phase advancement, turn start (ready all, clear summoning sickness). |
| `game_logic/actions/primitives.gd` (class `GameLogic`) | Low-level helpers: `move_card`, `deal_damage`, `check_destroyed`, `exhaust_card`, `heal`. `deal_damage` only places damage; callers run `check_destroyed` as a separate step (needed for simultaneous combat damage). |
| `game_logic/events/game_event.gd` | Event constructors. Add typed constructors for every new event. |
| `game_logic/turn_state_flags.md` | Registry of every turn-scoped state: the **turn event log** (`GameState.turn_events` — turn-*history* conditions like Torek's Assault and Thysta read snapshot entries recorded by the primitives; entry types and their fields are documented there and are a rules contract), once-per-turn action gates, and expiring grants. **Check it before adding a turn-history condition** — an existing entry type may already carry what you need; never add a per-card boolean for turn history. |
| `game_logic/priority_policy.md` | Why the engine must NOT auto-pass after each announcement. Holding priority is normally weakly dominated (the opponent gets a window after every resolution, so stacking shields nothing and only leaks information) — **except** that a non-empty chain locks the turn player out of sorcery speed, which is a real reason the non-turn player stacks during the opponent's action phase. **Read before simplifying the priority loop.** |
| `game_logic/ai/base_ai.gd` | AI action selection — `get_reasonable_actions` → scored → best chosen. |
| `renderer/board_renderer.gd` | Visual layer: card nodes, highlights, animations. No rules logic. |
| `renderer/tests/playtest.gd` | The running playtest scene. Wires everything together. AI + human. |
| `input/input_router.gd` | Human input → PendingAction. The only place that calls `submit_action` for humans. |

### Priority / chain flow (rule 410):

```
submit_action(state, action, db) → pushes to pending_actions, proposer keeps priority
pass_priority(state, db)         → consecutive_passes++; at 2: resolve top OR close window
```

**When both players pass with chain empty:**
- If `combat_attack_window` → `_close_attack_window` (protect point → defend window)
- If `combat_defend_window` → `_do_combat_conclusion` (damage)
- If `pending_enter_play_effect` non-empty → stall (scene must handle)
- Otherwise → emit `priority_window_closed` → `TurnManager` advances phase

### Start-of-turn triggered effects (rule 500.2 / 501.1a / 708.1):

501.1a's "None of this uses the chain" covers the ready step's **automatic
actions** (expiries, readying, modifier creation) — NOT the effects they
trigger. Per 500.2 / 708.1 a triggered effect is added to the chain during PPP,
so every start-of-turn effect is respondable.

```
TurnManager._enter_ready
  → automatic actions (no chain)
  → _collect_turn_start_triggers   builds ONE queue, fires nothing
       scans each player's BOARD, plus the turn player's GRAVEYARD (703.3a)
  → _open_window
  → StackResolver.advance_turn_start_triggers   loops until the queue is EMPTY
       adding player has 2+ left? → pending_trigger_order_player
                                     + trigger_order_required
                                     → choose_trigger_order (direct call)
       front trigger needs a target? → pending_trigger_target_player
                                        + trigger_target_required
                                        → choose_trigger_target (direct call)
       → `resolve_turn_start_trigger` link pushed — NO window yet
  → queue empty → ONE priority window opens (turn player first, 410)
  → links resolve top-down, a window between each
```

- **Every waiting trigger goes on the chain in ONE PPP** (708.1a), and **nobody
  gets priority until they all have** (708.1b) — both announcement choice points
  hard-block `can_submit` / `pass_priority`, which is what enforces that.
- **THE ORDER IS LIFO.** The turn player's triggers are added FIRST, so they sit
  at the bottom and **resolve LAST**; the opponent's go on top and **resolve
  FIRST**. That is the rulebook's own 708.1a example (Voss Treebender vs Donna
  Calister). Getting this backwards is the trap of the whole rule.
- **Order WITHIN a player is his own choice** (708.1a), asked only when he has
  two or more waiting — one is not a decision. It is expressed as a repeated
  “which goes on NEXT”, equivalent to naming a full order up front and needing no
  ordering widget; `pending_trigger_front_settled` is what stops the point
  re-asking for the trigger just picked. The prompt says the pick will resolve
  LAST rather than making the player derive it.
- **A trigger's source can be in a GRAVEYARD** (703.3a — Wisp), which is an
  explicit exception to 703.3's face-up-in-play rule. Which zone a key is
  collected from, and re-checked in (`StackResolver._trigger_source_active`), is
  decided by which of `TurnManager`'s three lists it is on — `EACH_TURN_TRIGGERS`,
  `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS`, `GRAVEYARD_TURN_TRIGGERS` — and they must stay disjoint.
  A graveyard entry's “controller” is the card's **owner** (401.2/415.2).

- **`GameState.pending_turn_start_triggers`** holds `{card_id, controller, key,
  args}` entries in 708.1a order (turn player's first). `key`/`args` are the raw
  effects segment, so the queue carries no per-card special cases — the whole
  dispatch is the one `match` in `_resolve_turn_start_trigger`.
- **Only targets are chosen at announcement** (707.1: X, modes, targets — that
  is the complete list). Every other choice belongs to resolution (709.2b).
  Infernal's discard is the worked example: an instant that draws in response
  still saves an empty hand.
- **The source is not re-checked at resolution** (707.3 — an effect exists
  independently of its source). Killing the totem in the response window does
  not stop its ping. Where a clause is genuinely impossible without the source
  (Infernal's control change), 709.2c applies: the link resolves and only as
  much as possible happens.
- **Information a link needs is locked in at announcement where the rules say
  so** (709.2d) — Fireball's attached host rides on the link as `host_id`.
- **Pure heal triggers are skipped when nothing in scope is damaged**
  (`TurnManager._heal_trigger_has_work`, checked as the queue is built). Such a
  link is provably inert — `GameLogic.heal` no-ops and emits no event on an
  undamaged target — so queueing it only costs both players a round of passes.
  Deviation, see `data/rules_deviations.md` "Empty heal triggers". Only the heal
  keys are gated; an inert damage or draw trigger is still queued.
- **Adding a trigger:** add the key to `TurnManager.EACH_TURN_TRIGGERS` (every
  player's turn), `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS` (controller's turn only) or
  `GRAVEYARD_TURN_TRIGGERS` (703.3a — fires from the owner's graveyard), plus a
  `match` arm in `_resolve_turn_start_trigger`; add it to
  `TARGETED_TURN_START_TRIGGERS` too if it announces a target. It is then
  respondable, armor-preventable and 707.3-correct for free. **Never resolve a
  start-of-turn effect inline in `TurnManager`** — that is the bug this
  framework replaced. Headless tests drive it via `_ready_step` /
  `_advance_phase` in `test_scenarios.gd`.

### Combat-step triggered effects (rule 602.1 / 602.3 / 708.1):

The combat twin of the start-of-turn framework above, and it exists for the same
reason. 602.1 and 602.3 each end with the identical sentence: "a priority window
… opens. **Any waiting triggered effects are added to the chain**, and then the
turn player gets priority." So a "when this attacks" / "when this defends" power
triggers as the step reaches that point, but the effect it creates is a
respondable chain link — it does **not** resolve inline as the step passes
through. The rulebook's worked example is Grunt Baranka (602.3).

```
_resolve_propose_combat (602.1)  /  _open_defend_window (602.3)
  → _collect_combat_triggers      builds ONE ordered queue, fires nothing
  → [strike point / ready point / attack-exhaust point may hold the window up]
  → _open_combat_window           the ONE place a combat window opens
       → window flag + *_window_opened
       → advance_combat_triggers  announces the FRONT trigger onto the chain
       → `resolve_combat_trigger` link + normal priority window
       → _resolve_combat_trigger
       → chain empties, both pass → pass_priority calls advance_* for the NEXT
         trigger, and only closes the window (protect point / conclusion) once
         the queue is empty
```

- **`GameState.pending_combat_triggers`** holds `{card_id, controller, key, args}`
  entries in 708.1a order (turn player's first). As with the turn-start queue,
  `key`/`args` are the raw effects segment, so it carries no per-card cases.
- **`StackResolver.COMBAT_TRIGGERS`** is the registry: each key declares its
  `moment` (`"attack"` = 602.1, `"defend"` = 602.3) and its `scope` — `"self"`
  for "when THIS attacks/defends" (only the card in that combat role), `"board"`
  for a watcher on the side ("when your hero attacks", "when an opposing hero or
  ally attacks"). A trigger CONDITION beyond the scope goes in
  `_combat_trigger_watches` and is checked once, as the queue is built.
- **The source is not re-checked at resolution** (707.3). Killing Morik in the
  response window does not stop the draws. Where a clause is impossible without
  the source, 709.2c applies — Baranka's is the worked case.
- **"If you do" is 709.2f**: the rider is gated on the preceding action having
  actually been PERFORMED. Baranka checks for a real `card_destroyed` event, not
  merely that she is gone, so a replaced destruction leaves the attacker alive.
- **`_open_combat_window` is the only place a combat window opens.** The strike
  point, ready-on-strike point, ready-on-attack point and attack-exhaust point
  each hold a window up and then hand it there, so a trigger can never be skipped
  by whichever of them happened to fire.
- **Adding a combat trigger:** add the key to `COMBAT_TRIGGERS` plus a `match`
  arm in `_resolve_combat_trigger`. It is then respondable and 707.3-correct for
  free. **Never resolve a combat trigger inline in `_resolve_propose_combat` /
  `_open_defend_window`** — that is the bug this framework replaced.
- **Deliberately NOT part of this:** the strike points (602.1/602.3) and the
  protect point (602.2) — the rules say of each "none of this uses the chain",
  they are not triggered effects. The "you may pay COST" attack points (Windseer
  Tarus, Windfury Totem) and the optional targeted attack-exhaust points (Chops /
  Voss / Gartok) ARE triggered effects and belong on the chain too, but each
  carries a direct-call choice with its own UI and AI flow; they remain listed in
  `data/rules_deviations.md`.

### Play-triggered effects (rule 412.1a / 708.1):

The third member of the trigger-queue family, after start-of-turn and combat.
For an ongoing power that watches its own controller PLAYING a card — Spiritual
Healing, "When you play a Holy ability, your hero heals 2 damage from target
hero or ally."

**A card is PLAYED when it is announced onto the chain (412.1a)**, and per 708.1
the effect the trigger creates is added to the chain during that same PPP. So it
goes on **top of the card that triggered it** and therefore resolves FIRST:

```
submit_action(play_ability)          card moves to chain, cost paid, link pushed
  → _check_form_break_ability        Forms break HERE (see below)
  → collect_play_triggers            queue = every matching watcher in play
  → advance_play_triggers            front trigger needs a target?
                                       yes → pending_trigger_target_player
                                             + pending_trigger_kind = "play"
                                             → choose_trigger_target (direct call)
       → `resolve_play_trigger` link ON TOP of the play + priority window
       → _resolve_play_trigger
       → link resolves → pass_priority announces the NEXT queued trigger
```

- **The chain is NOT empty while this queue drains** (the triggering card is
  still under it), which is exactly why it cannot reuse the start-of-turn
  queue's chain-empty drain point. The next trigger is announced from
  `pass_priority`'s post-resolution path instead.
- **Interrupting the triggering card does not take the trigger with it** (711 +
  707.3): by then it is a separate link sitting above a dead one.
- **`GameState.pending_trigger_kind`** (`"turn_start"` / `"play"`) is what tells
  `choose_trigger_target` which queue it is answering — the two share the choice
  point, and with it the entire UI and AI flow.
- **Adding a play trigger:** add the key to `StackResolver.PLAY_TRIGGERS` (with
  its card-type gate, plus `"match": "class"` when field 1 is a CLASS ICON
  rather than a type-line tag — Lok'delar), to `TARGETED_PLAY_TRIGGERS` if it
  announces a target, and a `match` arm in `_resolve_play_trigger`. It is then respondable and
  707.3-correct for free.
- **Form break is deliberately NOT part of this.** "Destroy this card when you
  play a non-Feral ability" fires from `submit_action` at the same moment, but
  resolves **inline**: it is mandatory, free, targetless and choiceless, so a
  window would offer nobody anything (the Thysta / Venomstrike deviation). It
  moved here from the played card's resolution — see `data/rules_deviations.md`
  "Form break timing", which that change RESOLVED. One consequence: a play that
  actually broke a form is **no longer retractable** (a destroyed card can't be
  un-destroyed), carrying Sever the Cord's `_cost_paid_irreversibly` mark.

### Mandatory immediate-resolution choices (bypass pending_actions):

Some choices must resolve without going through the stack:
- `StackResolver.choose_discard(state, card_id, db)` — pending discard
- `StackResolver.choose_pet_sacrifice(state, card_id, db)` — pet uniqueness violation
- `StackResolver.choose_control_discard(state, card_id, db)` / `decline_control_discard(state, db)` — Infernal's "discard a card, or target opponent gains control". Opened **from the resolution** of its start-of-turn chain link (709.2b — see the Start-of-turn triggered effects section), not when the trigger fires. Unlike a mandatory discard the player MAY decline: control changes (rule 401.3 — card moves to the new controller's ally_row, `just_summoned` set, pet uniqueness re-checked for the new controller). `pass_priority` is also hard-blocked while pending; in the UI the pass button becomes the decline option ("Give up control [Ctrl+Space]") and hand cards highlight red for the discard. Declining is gated behind Ctrl+Space (or the pass button / a card click) — plain Space is absorbed with a hint, so an accidental tap can't hand the card to the opponent.

These are called directly (not via `submit_action`). The blocking guard in `can_submit` prevents anything else while they're pending.

### Combat step flow (rule 602):

```
propose_combat resolves →
  attacker exhausts + combat_started event
  "when this attacks" powers trigger (queued, not fired)
  combat_attack_window = true → attack_window_opened event
    [queued attack triggers go on the chain, one at a time]
    [both players get priority — can use instants, ally powers, etc.]
  both pass → _close_attack_window →
    protect point (if protectors exist) OR
    "when this defends" powers trigger (queued, not fired — Grunt Baranka)
    combat_defend_window = true → defend_window_opened event
      [queued defend triggers go on the chain, one at a time]
      [both players get priority again]
    both pass → _do_combat_conclusion → damage → combat_concluded
```

**Ally activated powers** (e.g. Grimdron) are legal during attack/defend windows even for the non-turn player — `_can_use_ally_power` only requires `priority_player == source_player` (no `turn_player` restriction), because "use only on your turn" is an explicit card-text restriction, not the default.

---

## Card data format (data/cards.csv columns)

```
set_id, card_num, name, cost, card_type, faction, class_restriction, card_subtype,
subtype_detail, printed_atk, dmg_type, printed_health, rarity, keywords,
power_text, data_status, engine_status, effects, image_path, mute_when
```

**`effects` string** — pipe-separated segments, each colon-separated:

| Segment | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `activated_power:COST:EFFECT:AMOUNT:DMG_TYPE:TARGETS:EXTRACOST` | Ally/equipment activated power. `EFFECT` includes `draw`. `EXTRACOST` (optional 7th field) is a card-specific extra cost paid alongside resources+activate, e.g. `exhaust_hero` (Mooncloth Robe). It is a **`+`-joined list of independent tokens**, each optionally carrying its own `:`-argument — WHAT the power costs beyond resources is one axis, whether the printed cost carries an `[Activate]` tap symbol is another (Besh'iah is `sacrifice_ally+no_activate`, Gertha's cost with the tap symbol dropped). **Always ask through `StackResolver.power_has_extra_cost` / `power_extra_cost_arg`, never compare the raw field** — a two-token card matches no equality test. `no_activate` marks a plain payment power (701.2) whose printed cost has no [Activate] tap symbol — no self-exhaust, no summoning sickness, repeatable each turn (Hierophant Caydiem: "3 →..."). `put_damage_self:N` is the same idea but the extra cost is self-damage (Acolyte Demia). `activate_put_damage_self:N` is self-damage that KEEPS the [Activate] tap symbol — the source also exhausts and has summoning sickness (Kena Shadowbrand). `rfg_allies:N` removes N ally cards from your graveyard from the game as a cost (Augustus Corpsemonger — the specific N are auto-chosen in graveyard order, see `data/rules_deviations.md`). `sacrifice_ally` destroys a chosen ally in your party as a cost; pair with `TARGETS=friendly_ally` so the target picker only offers your own allies, the sacrifice may be the source itself (Bizzik Sparkcog). With any OTHER `TARGETS` kind the power is a **two-pick**: the sacrifice is its own `sacrifice_id` param, picked before the effect's `target_id` in the router's two-phase flow, and the phase-1 prompt says "select an ally to sacrifice" (`StackResolver.power_sacrifice_is_separate` is the single gate — engine validation, UI flow and AI all read it). Gertha, The Old Crone (`TARGETS=ally`) and Besh'iah (`TARGETS=ability`) share every part of it but the effect. Like `sacrifice_self` the destroy is paid at **ANNOUNCEMENT** (707.1e), so it cannot be answered at all. `sacrifice_self` destroys the SOURCE itself as a cost and implies no [Activate] tap symbol — no exhaust, no summoning sickness (Kavai the Wanderer: "1, Destroy Kavai →..."; Mana Agate `azeroth_57`, an ongoing Ability in the hero_row — the source may be an ally OR any in-play card); the destroy is paid at **ANNOUNCEMENT** (707.1e — costs are paid inside the announcement sequence, before 707.1f adds the link), so the source is in its owner's graveyard before anyone gets priority and killing it in response is impossible; per 707.3 the link then resolves in full without it. `EFFECT` value `destroy_ally` destroys the (any-party) target ally — pair with `TARGETS=ally` (Augustus). `EFFECT` value `destroy_ability_or_equipment` destroys a target in-play ability OR equipment card, either player's — pair with `TARGETS=ability_or_equipment` (candidates = Burn Away's + Shattering Blow's pools via `get_destroy_kind_candidates`; even the highlight probe requires one to exist) |
| `activated_power:X:...` | **X-cost activated power** ("Chipper" Ironbane `dark_portal_160`, 2-cost 3/1 Alliance Dwarf Warrior: "(X), Destroy "Chipper" Ironbane → Destroy target ability or equipment with cost X" = `activated_power:X:destroy_ability_or_equipment:0::ability_or_equipment:sacrifice_self`). `COST` literal `X` = the `x_value` announced with the action (Aimed Shot's convention on the power path). `_ally_activated_power` sets `cost_x` and leaves `resource_cost` 0 — **read the price through `StackResolver.power_resource_cost(ap, x_value)`**, the one place the fixed and X cases are reconciled, so pay (`submit_action`), refund (`retract_last`) and affordability (`_can_use_ally_power`) can't disagree. **X is not a free choice**: `power_cost_matches_target` requires `X == the target's PRINTED cost` (`printed_cost` off its `CardDef` — never a modified one; an in-play card was paid for long ago, exactly as with Trophy Kill's cost band), so the card is really "pick a target you can afford, then pay its cost" and **the UI derives X from the click** (`InputRouter._ally_power_x_for`) instead of opening the Boris-style X dialog. The no-target highlight probe additionally requires an **affordable** candidate to exist, so he goes dark when the only ability on the board is unpayable. Everything else is Kavai's: same `ability_or_equipment` pool, same 706 resolution re-check, same `sacrifice_self` timing (paid at ANNOUNCEMENT, which also makes the announcement **non-retractable**). AI: **target-first**, which is what the X demands — see the Chipper entry under implemented cards |
| `equipment:SLOT:DEF[:CAPACITY]` | Marks an Equipment card: `SLOT` (chest/back/neck/trinket/…) drives slot uniqueness (rule 414.3); `DEF` is defense (0 = no damage prevention). Equipment enters the hero row. Optional 4th field `CAPACITY` is the number in parentheses on the type line (414.3b) — how many cards with that slot tag a player may control. Defaults to **1**, which every armor/weapon slot printed so far is; Ramstein's Lightning Bolts is the first **Trinket (2)**. `_check_equipment_uniqueness` and `choose_equipment_sacrifice`'s re-check both compare against `_equipment_slot_capacity`, the STRICTEST capacity in the conflicting set — so the Two-Handed↔Off-Hand cross-conflict (414.3c, always 1) still violates on the second card |
| `ongoing` | Marks an Ability as ongoing (rule 305.2a — stays in play instead of resolve-and-graveyard). Non-totem ongoing abilities enter the controller's hero_row (`_resolve_play_ongoing_ability`). `is_ongoing_def()` also routes an **Instant** Ability with this flag to `play_ability` (enters play) instead of `play_instant` in both `_action_type_for`s |
| `totem[:ELEMENT]` | Marks a Totem (rule 305.3 — Searing Totem): an ability **ally** that enters the controller's **ally_row** (not hero_row), can't be proposed as an attacker (`is_totem_def` gate in `get_legal_attackers` / `_can_propose_combat`), but can be attacked/targeted like any ally. ATK/health come from the CSV `atk`/`health` cols. Pairs with an `ongoing` segment. Play is instant-speed (type line "Instant Ability") |
| `activated_power:0:destroy_all_named:0:::sacrifice_self` + `destroy_named:NAME:KIND` | **Destroy every in-play card of a KIND with a given NAME** (The Immovable Object `dark_portal_254`, 4, Equipment — Armor — Shield, Off-Hand (1), DEF 5: "Destroy The Immovable Object → Destroy all weapons named The Unstoppable Force"; The Unstoppable Force `dark_portal_285`, 5, Two-Handed Weapon — Mace, Melee (1), 5 ATK / strike 2: "Destroy The Unstoppable Force → Destroy all armor named The Immovable Object"). A matched pair — each is the other's only answer, and each pays with itself. **Non-targeted and mandatory**: the text says "all … named X", not "target", so nothing is announced, **706 Untargetable is irrelevant**, and there is no highlight probe to dark — the power stays legal (just pointless) with nothing on the board to break. **Symmetric**: no "opposing" clause, so BOTH players' matching cards go, the controller's own included — which is priced in, since the pair is mutually exclusive by design. The only limit is how much health the party can pay, which is the card: hero damage is moved onto your allies one point at a time. **UI — the repeat-count flow:** the power is free and repeatable, and the damage heading for your hero is rarely exactly 1, so the interface asks for a COUNT instead of making the player activate it N times. Left-clicking the card (a deliberate narrow exception — every other in-play card keeps left-click = attack) or the context menu's "Activate Power" opens targeting; clicking one of your allies then opens the count dialog with **Cancel / OK / OK and Pass**, its ceiling being that ally's remaining health and a red warning once the count would destroy it (405.3 permits an exactly-fatal cost, so it warns rather than blocks). **Nothing is submitted until OK**, so Cancel and Esc are free. The count then becomes **N separate announcements**, each paying its own cost — `StackResolver.power_repeats_by_count` is a UI convenience with no rules meaning, and the engine never sees a "repeat". "OK and Pass" submits and passes in one click, which is the common case (you are answering damage already on its way). The ally that pays is the **sturdiest** in the party and **never one at 1 health** — a deliberately dumb, cruel heuristic that grinds the party down without ever trading a whole card for 1 point of hero damage |

Soul Link's counted `prevent_damage_amount` grant with two additions, and **read the target carefully**: it is NOT the character being shielded — that is always the caster's own hero, named by the card, so that half announces nothing and can never fizzle — but the character being **warded AGAINST**. (1) **The source SCOPE.** The grant rides the hero as `prevent_damage_amount_from:<id>:<type>`, the id carried IN the stat name because `Buff.amount` is an int and what this must carry is an instance id (Mocking Blow's `can_attack_only:<id>` is the same trick). `GameLogic.granted_shield` / `consume_granted_shield` take an optional `source_id` and count a scoped grant only for a matching packet; **`_prevention_offer` passes it too**, so a shield warding against somebody else never suppresses an armor point for a packet it could not cover. Scoped grants are spent **before** unscoped ones — both prevent identically, so spending the reflecting (and perishable) one first is never worse for its controller, the same auto-choice made for Augustus' `rfg_allies`. (2) **The REFLECT.** `GameLogic.prevent` **cannot deal it** — it runs inside the prevention pipeline of a packet that has not landed yet, and dealing damage there is re-entrant — so it queues entries on **`GameState.pending_shield_reflects`** and `StackResolver._drain_shield_reflects` fires them at the two points damage has finished landing (`_apply_packet_group` and `_do_combat_conclusion`), **the same two Skorn and Cold Blood sweep at**. The reflect is dealt by the shielded player's **HERO** as printed, tagged `from_ability` (Chromatic Cloak's “deal damage with an ability”) and carrying the shield's own `dmg_type`, and goes through `defer_packets`, so armor prevention opens normally on the character being reflected at; a reflect itself prevented by a second shield simply queues again, which terminates because shields deplete. Consequences worth holding onto: it shields the **HERO only**, so it can never save an ally — which is why it belongs in a deck whose hero **protects** (602.2) and eats the attacks on purpose; **unpreventable damage (Lionheart Helm, Chastise) bypasses it, consumes none of it and therefore reflects nothing** (717 short-circuits before every shield); a **destroy effect ignores it** entirely; and because 5 prevented is 5 holy back, the card is removal as well as prevention — it kills the attacker outright on a big swing. Duration `turns`:1, so the end-of-turn sweep gives “this turn” for free, correct when cast on the OPPONENT's turn, which is the only turn it usually matters. Target is `hero_or_ally`, **either party's** (no “opposing” clause), re-checked at resolution (706 / 4217 — a warded-against character that left play or became Untargetable fizzles the whole thing, card still spent). **Instant speed is the card** — it is flashed into the opponent's attack window; the CSV type cell said `Ability`, which would have made it nearly dead, and was corrected. “Protection Hero Required” is the Talent-spec restriction the deck authorizer already enforces via rule 100.2c (`Protection Talent` in the tags column), not a new in-game gate. | `prevent_next_damage_target:N` (hero power) | **Counted damage shield on a TARGET character** (Graccus `azeroth_4`, Alliance Human Paladin hero, 29 health, Uncommon: “(3), Flip Graccus ➜ Prevent the next 3 damage that would be dealt to target hero or ally this turn.”). Soul Link's `prevent_next_hero_damage` grant reached from a **hero flip** and pointed at a real **target**, which is the whole difference: Soul Link names your own hero, so it announces nothing and can never fizzle, while this one announces a character and is **re-checked at resolution** (706 / 4217 — a target that left play or became Untargetable fizzles the shield, and the flip is still spent). Everything else is inherited whole: the same `prevent_damage_amount` `Buff` (duration `turns`:1, so the end-of-turn sweep gives “this turn” for free — correct even when flipped on the OPPONENT's turn, which is the only turn it usually matters — and leaving play clears it), spent point by point in **`GameLogic.prevent`** branch (a4), which was **already target-agnostic** (it reads any `CardInstance`), so an ALLY shield needed no primitives code at all. It sits **before the armor pool** and `StackResolver._prevention_offer` nets it out, so a packet the shield fully covers never opens an armor point; **unpreventable damage (Lionheart Helm, Chastise) still lands and consumes NONE of it** (717 short-circuits before every shield), and a **destroy effect ignores it** — it prevents damage only. Pool is `hero_or_ally`, **either party's** (“target hero or ally”, no “friendly” clause), so a human may shield the opponent's board; an in-play ability, an attachment and equipment are never legal (`_is_hero_or_ally` gates submission and the resolution re-check alike). A hero is always in play, so the no-target highlight probe never darks the power. Needed **no UI code** — the router's generic hero-power target branch enumerates every in-play hero and ally and filters through `can_submit`. **Instant speed** (701.3 — no `require_turn_player`), which is the card: it is flipped inside the opponent's attack window, or in response to a damage link still on the chain (priority is LIFO, so the shield is standing when the damage lands). The one economic difference from Soul Link is decisive: that power is free and repeatable, while **the hero flip is once per GAME** (`PlayerState.has_used_hero_power` is never reset), so this is one 3-point shield, ever. **The same key is also read as an ALLY activated power** (`activated_power:0:prevent_next_damage_target:1::hero_or_ally` — Korthas Greybeard `dark_portal_174`): everything above is inherited whole, and only the ECONOMY differs — a plain `[Activate]` tap symbol and no resource cost, so the source exhausts at announcement (412.2) and is summoning-sickness gated, making it **1 point every time she readies** instead of 3 points once ever, the grants simply **stacking**. `hero_or_ally` is the existing generic power target kind, so submission, the router's targeting flow and the resolution re-check needed no new code. Either party's characters are legal ("target hero or ally"), and a HERO target matters wherever a hero-protector grant is out (Draconian Deflector, Bear Form). The UI shows a ⛓ badge on every ready-blocked card (`TurnManager.is_ready_blocked` probe → `CardNode.set_ready_locked`, refreshed from playtest `_refresh_ready_lock_badges`; Entangling Roots hosts get it too) |

(2) **ALIGNMENT** (`""` = any) is matched against the recipient's printed alignment, so a **neutral** ally in a Horde party gets nothing. (3) **"Allies in your party"** is the controller's ally_row read live, so **TOTEMS qualify** (305.3a) while the **HERO** never does, and the opponent's board is untouched. | `on_enter:reveal_pick:CARD_TYPE:N` | **Enter-play reveal-and-pick** (Overseer Oilfist `dark_portal_245`, 2-cost 1/2 Dwarf Warrior, **Unique**, neutral): "When Overseer Oilfist enters play, reveal the top four cards of your deck. Put a revealed equipment card into your hand and the rest on the bottom of your deck." Big Game Hunter's `reveal_pick:Equipment:4` quest reward reached from an **enter-play trigger** — the shared `_reveal_pick` helper is called with the same grammar and the same optional trailing flags, so the graveyard-browser UI, the `can_submit` / `pass_priority` hard block while `pending_reveal_pick_player` is set, and `BaseAI._pick_ai_reveal`'s pick quality all come along with no new UI or AI code. Non-targeted and mandatory (nothing is announced, 706 is irrelevant), so it fires **inline** in `_bring_ally_into_play` beside `create_token` rather than going on the chain — and unlike the optional enter-play triggers there is nothing to decline: the choice **always opens** even when nothing matches, so the player sees the revealed cards and acknowledges with an empty pick (`choose_reveal_pick(state, "")`), sending all four to the bottom. "Your deck" is the entering ally's **controller's**. **Revealing is not a draw** (410.6b): nothing calls `GameLogic.draw_one`, so a short deck reveals only what it has and an empty one is a harmless no-op, never a decking loss. The rest go to the **bottom in revealed order**. `CARD_TYPE` matches the parsed `CardDef.card_type`, so **Equipment covers armor, weapons and Items alike** (rule 304) — every Equipment card is one of the three. No AI modeling beyond the shared pick heuristic |
| `create_token:TOKEN_DEF_ID[:N]` (quest reward) | Same effect as a quest reward segment (Tooga's Quest `azeroth_359`): "Reward: Put a unique Turtle ally token named Tooga with 1 ATK and 1 health into play." Handled in `_apply_quest_reward`, same `_put_token_into_play` path |
| `rfg_self_next_turn:draw:N` | Delayed self-removal carried by a token (Tooga): "At the start of your next turn, remove Tooga from the game. If you do, draw N cards." Queued as a `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS` entry, gated on `CardInstance.created_on_turn` so it can't fire on the turn the token was created. The removal is a plain `move_card` to RFG — **not** a destroy, so no `card_destroyed` and no destruction triggers; the draw is the "if you do" rider and only happens because the removal did. Putting the trigger on the TOKEN rather than on the quest is what makes the fizzle free: a token that is already gone is never collected. Killing Tooga **in response to the trigger** denies both the removal and the draw (709.2c). Goes on the chain as a `resolve_turn_start_trigger` link during the ready step and is fully respondable — see the **Start-of-turn triggered effects** section below. |
| `destroy_target:ally` | Destroy a target ally (Vanquish-style) |
| `choose_destroy:armor:weapon` | **"Choose one or both: destroy target armor; or destroy target weapon"** (Crushing Blow `dark_portal_120`, 4, Ability — Fury, Warrior, Common). The engine's first "choose one or both" that is neither Totemic Call's condition-gated multi-modal (each mode there shares ONE `target_id`, since only one mode of that card is ever targeted) nor Ravenous Bite's two MANDATORY targets — this is Ravenous Bite's two-independent-target shape (`target_id` / `target_id_2`, each re-checked independently at resolution, 706/709.2a — killing one in response fizzles only that half) with each half made individually **OPTIONAL**, and "at least one" enforced instead of "both". `choose_destroy_kinds(def)` parses the `+`-free, `:`-joined KIND list off the segment (`armor`, `weapon`, in printed order); `_can_play_choose_destroy` requires every NON-EMPTY slot to be a legal target of its own kind (`_is_destroyable_kind` — armor via `_is_destroyable_armor`, weapon via the new `get_destroy_kind_candidates(..., "weapon")` case backed by `_is_weapon_equipment`) and requires at least one slot non-empty; an empty slot is a legal skip, not an illegal announce. **Either player's armor/weapon is legal** (no "opposing" clause). Resolution (`_apply_choose_destroy`) destroys whichever announced half(s) are STILL legal at that point, independently — `_destroy_card_trigger` per half, so `on_destroyed` triggers and the `ally_destroyed` turn-log entry fire normally for each. The highlight probe (`_any_choose_destroy_candidate`) is lit while EITHER pool has a candidate anywhere, dark only when both are empty. AI (`_targeted_instant_actions`): unlike the Sunder-Armor-style loop above (one action per candidate, left for scoring to rank) this is announced as ONE action, so the AI builds it directly — the best OPPOSING candidate of each kind clearing Burn Away's value bar (printed cost ≥ the spell's own) fills that slot, both slots fill when both clear the bar, one alone when only that kind does, and the card is held when neither opposing pool clears it. UI (`input_router.gd`): its own small state machine (`_cd_source`/`_cd_phase`/`_cd_picks`) rather than a rider on `_targeting_first_target`, precisely because every other two-pick flow has no notion of "skip this pick" — **BOTH kinds are offered at once** and the player picks in whatever order they like (click a weapon first and armor stays open, and vice versa), a kind with an empty pool never being offered at all; clicking the **CARD ITSELF** finishes the cast with whatever has been picked so far (Chain Lightning's early-submit gesture, and the reason no pick can ever be "skipped" into a zero-pick cast); Esc/right-click cancels the whole cast at any point. **The branch lives in `_handle_targeting_click` ahead of the per-action-type match, NOT in `_handle_instant_targeting_click`** — Crushing Blow is a plain (sorcery-speed) Ability, so its clicks route to `_handle_ability_targeting_click`, and a branch sitting in the instant handler is simply unreachable for it. Picked targets highlight **blue** — see the Highlight color conventions section |
| `destroy_target:armor` | **Destroy target ARMOR** (Sunder Armor `azeroth_149`, 1, Instant Ability — Protection, Warrior). Shattering Blow's `destroy_target:equipment` pool narrowed by Gartok's `_is_armor_equipment` read: an in-play Equipment whose `card_subtype` is one of `ARMOR_SUBTYPES` (cloth/leather/mail/plate/shield — rule 304, the printed type line reads "Armor — <subtype>"). A **weapon** (Sword, Axe…) and an **Item** (Ramstein's Lightning Bolts) are Equipment but are **never** legal targets. `_is_destroyable_armor` is the ONE predicate behind submission (both `_can_play_instant` and `_can_play_ability`), the candidate pool (`get_destroy_kind_candidates(..., "armor")`, which Gartok's attack-exhaust pool now reads too, so the two can't disagree) and the resolution re-check (706/4217 — armor destroyed or made Untargetable in response fizzles the destroy, the card is still spent). **Either player's armor is legal** (no "opposing" clause), so a human may break their own; the highlight probe goes dark with no armor in play (706.2). Instant speed, so it can strip a defender's DEF **in the combat window before the prevention point opens** (717.2c), which is the card. AI: inherits the Burn Away / Shattering Blow branch of `_targeted_instant_actions` (opposing targets with printed cost ≥ spell cost — at cost 1 that is every armor printed), never its own |
| `destroy_target:exhausted_ally` | Destroy a target **exhausted** ally, either party (Coup de Grâce `azeroth_93`, 2, Ability — Assassination). A subset of the ally-only destroy restriction (`_instant_targets_ally_only` includes `exhausted_ally`): the target must be an ally AND exhausted, checked at submission (`_destroy_requires_exhausted`) and re-checked at resolution (`_is_exhausted_ally`, 706 — fizzles if it left play OR readied in response). The highlight probe (`_targeted_play_has_legal_target`) also filters to exhausted allies, so the card goes dark when none is exhausted. AI (`_targeted_instant_actions`): destroys the single most valuable exhausted OPPOSING ally by `card_value_score` (always worth it — a spent attacker) |
| `except_friendly_attached` | Rider on a `destroy_target:ability` segment (Purge `azeroth_114`, 1, Instant Ability — Elemental, Shaman): "Destroy target ability **unless it's attached to a friendly hero or ally**." Narrows Burn Away's pool — an in-play ability is illegal when it is an attachment whose HOST is a hero or ally controlled by the CASTER. "Friendly" is judged on the host's controller, not the attachment's, so an opponent's Entangling Roots sitting on your own ally is protected from your own Purge (the card's deliberate blind spot); an attachment on a *weapon* (Windfury Weapon) isn't on a hero or ally and stays targetable. `_is_destroyable_ability` gates submission (`_can_play_instant`/`_can_play_ability`), the highlight probe (`_targeted_play_has_legal_target` — the card goes dark when every in-play ability is friendly-attached) and the resolution re-check (706/4217 — a host that changes control mid-chain fizzles the destroy). AI: inherits Burn Away's `_targeted_instant_actions` branch (opposing targets with cost ≥ spell cost; at cost 1 that's effectively always) — not tagged as a combat instant |
| `gouge_cant_ready` | Pairs with an `exhaust_target:hero_or_ally` segment (Gouge `azeroth_99`, 1, Instant Ability — Combat Combo): "It can't ready during its controller's next ready step." After the exhaust resolves (applied even if the target was already exhausted), sets a one-shot `gouge_skip_ready` counter on the target + emits `card_ready_locked`. `TurnManager._ready_blocked` returns true while the counter is present (drives the ⛓ badge probe); `_enter_ready` consumes it exactly at the target controller's next ready step — the card skips that one ready step whether or not something else also blocked it, then readies normally after. Survives the end-of-turn buff sweep (it's a counter, not a turns-buff), so it times correctly when cast on the opponent's turn. AI: Gouge is tagged `combat_instant_exhaust` (held; answers an opposing attacker — hero or ally — via `exhaust_attacker_action`, fizzling the proposal per 601.3); the ready-lock rider is not modeled |
| `hero_damage_destroys_ally_this_turn` | Pairs with a `draw:N` segment (Cold Blood `azeroth_92`, 1, Instant Ability — Assassination Talent, Rogue): "Draw a card. When your hero deals damage to an ally this turn, destroy that ally." Devilsaur Leggings' conclusion trigger widened to **every** damage source and narrowed to one turn — so instead of a hook per source it is evaluated off the **turn event log** (`GameState.turn_events`, see `game_logic/turn_state_flags.md`): every damage packet in the game records a `damage_dealt` entry in `GameLogic.deal_damage`, so `StackResolver._fire_cold_blood` covers combat, abilities, hero/ally/equipment powers, totems and end-of-turn burns by construction. Swept at exactly two points, right after damage has landed: `_apply_packet_group` (the prevention pipeline) and `_do_combat_conclusion` (beside Devilsaur's trigger, so combat's simultaneous packets have BOTH landed before anything is destroyed — a defender still retaliates). The grant is player-wide and this-turn like Rapid Fire's, but what it stores is an **index** into the log (`PlayerState.cold_blood_from_index`, -1 = inactive, cleared at every turn start): only entries from the index on count, which makes the trigger **forward-looking** as printed — an ally the hero damaged EARLIER this turn is not retroactively doomed. Re-scanning already-handled entries is harmless (the ally is out of play). Only damage actually DEALT counts (the log is written after prevention, 717.2b) — and per 405.2 that includes overkill in full. The destroy is mandatory and free — no cost, no choice, nothing on the chain — goes through `_destroy_card_trigger`, so `on_destroyed` triggers (Boneshanks) fire normally, and applies to **any** ally the hero damages, the controller's own included ("an ally", not "an opposing ally"). "Assassination Hero Required" is the Talent-spec restriction the deck authorizer already enforces via rule 100.2c (`Assassination Talent` in the tags column), not a new in-game gate. No AI modeling — untagged, so it is played in the develop step as a 1-cost cantrip |
| `target_cost_min:N` / `target_cost_max:N` | Printed-**cost band** rider on a `destroy_target:ally` segment (Trophy Kill `dark_portal_40`, 3, Instant Ability — Marksmanship, Hunter: "Destroy target ally with cost 4 or more" = `destroy_target:ally|target_cost_min:4`; Prey on the Weak `dark_portal_85`, 2, Instant Ability — Assassination, Rogue: "…cost 4 or less" = `destroy_target:ally|target_cost_max:4`). A **rider, not a target kind** — the target stays an ordinary `ally`, so every ally-only path (`_instant_targets_ally_only`, the friendly-only variant, the router's `can_submit`-filtered target list) applies unchanged and the two mirror cards need no UI code. Both bands are **inclusive**, so a cost-4 ally is a legal target for either card. The cost measured is the target's **printed** cost (`printed_cost` off its `CardDef`, `cost_base` for a hypothetical X-cost ally) — never `get_play_cost`: an in-play ally was paid for long ago and cost modifiers apply to cards being PLAYED. `_destroy_cost_band_ok` gates submission (both `_can_play_instant` and `_can_play_ability`), the highlight probe (`_targeted_play_has_legal_target` — the card goes dark when no ally in play falls in the band) and the resolution re-check (706/4217). AI: no new code — the generic `destroy_target:ally` branch of `_targeted_instant_actions` enumerates opposing allies and is filtered by `can_submit` (the band) then `_destroy_is_worth_it` (target cost ≥ spell cost, which the `min` band satisfies by construction) |
| `play_cost_sacrifice_ally` | **Sacrifice-an-ally additional play cost on a HAND card** (Sever the Cord `azeroth_131`, 2, Instant Ability — Demonology, Warlock): "As an additional cost to play Sever the Cord, destroy an ally in your party. Destroy target ally." A **rider, not a target kind** — the effect stays a plain `destroy_target:ally`, so the announce, the 706/4217 resolution re-check, `_instant_targets_ally_only`, the highlight probe, the router's target list and the AI's threat detection are all inherited from Vanquish. What is new is the COST. Gertha/Besh'iah's `sacrifice_ally` EXTRACOST is an *activated-power* cost ALSO paid at **ANNOUNCEMENT** (707.1e); this is the hand-card form of the same timing (rule 412.2, like The Love Potion's `exhaust_allies`) — which is the card: bounce or kill the target in response and the sacrifice has still been spent, for nothing. The sacrifice rides the play as its own **`sacrifice_id`** param (never `target_id` — the effect destroys a different ally), validated against `get_play_sacrifice_candidates` (any card in the caster's own ally_row, ready or exhausted, **totems included** per 305.3a) by `_play_cost_sacrifice_ok`, which gates both `_can_play_instant` and `_can_play_ability`. An unpayable additional cost makes the card unplayable, so `_targeted_play_has_legal_target` **goes dark with no ally in the caster's party**. Naming the sacrifice as the destroy target too is a **legal announcement** (targets are chosen before costs are paid) and simply fizzles at resolution. Because a destroyed ally can't be refunded, `can_retract` **blocks the announcement** — the generic `_cost_paid_irreversibly` params mark set in `submit_action`, which any future irreversibly-paid cost can reuse. UI: two sequential picks via the `_targeting_first_target` two-phase flow (`InputRouter._is_play_cost_sacrifice`), the sacrifice first with Gertha's "select an ally to sacrifice" prompt; clicking the spell during phase 2 steps back to phase 1, and nothing is paid until submission, so Esc is free. AI (`_targeted_instant_actions`): eats our **least valuable** ally via the shared `_cheapest_sacrifice_ally` (no source to exclude — the spell is in hand) and only when the target scores **strictly higher** than the sacrifice, so it never pays a better ally for a worse one; no ally to eat → no actions at all |
| `play_cost_put_damage_hero:N` | **Put-damage-on-your-own-hero additional play cost on a HAND card** (Life Tap `azeroth_128`, 2, Instant Ability — Affliction, Warlock, Uncommon): "As an additional cost to play Life Tap, put 2 damage on your hero. Draw two cards." Sever the Cord's `play_cost_sacrifice_ally` rider with the currency changed from a body to the caster's own HEALTH — and that removes the whole announcement half: the card names **your hero**, a hero is always in play, so there is nothing to pick, no `sacrifice_id`, **no submission gate and no candidate pool**, and the highlight probe can never dark it. Like every `play_cost_*` rider it is paid at **ANNOUNCEMENT** (rule 412.2) rather than at resolution, so interrupting the link (711) or otherwise answering it leaves the damage paid for nothing; `can_retract` blocks the announcement via the shared `_cost_paid_irreversibly` params mark, since healing is not un-paying. Rule **405.3** is the cost: *put*, not dealt, so `GameLogic.put_damage` is the primitive — it is **unpreventable** (armor, Devotion Aura's reduction, Brother Rhone, Bestial Wrath and the counted shields all do nothing about it), it feeds **no damage watcher** (Skorn, Cold Blood, Berserking's counters, `discard_per_damage`, and Thysta's "if no damage was dealt this turn", which then FIRES), and it **can be exactly fatal** — a hero's death is state-based rather than a destruction (118.4/704) and `check_destroyed` refuses to move a hero, so the payment site runs `_hero_state_based_death` itself rather than waiting for the priority-gate sweep. The `draw:N` half needed no code at all (Blink's segment), and being a real **draw** it is locked by Brain Freeze (415.9f) — under the lock the cost is still paid and you get nothing. **Instant speed is the card**: it is flashed into the opponent's combat window to dig for an answer. AI (`get_reasonable_actions`, its own branch ahead of the pure-draw gate): `_pure_draw_amount` returns 0 for a card with a second segment, so Life Tap would otherwise fall through **ungated** and eventually tap itself to death — the health gate is the important half (hero must stay above `FREE_BLOCK_HERO_FLOOR` after paying), with the ordinary hand-room gate (503.2a, minus one because the spell itself frees a slot) read through the new `_draw_segment_amount`, which unlike `_pure_draw_amount` doesn't bail on a second segment because that segment is a COST, not a second payoff |
| `rfg_instead` | Rider on a `destroy_target:ally` segment (Shred Soul `dark_portal_114`, 3, Ability — Affliction, Warlock): "Remove target ally from the game." A **rider, not a target kind** — the target stays an ordinary `ally`, so the announce, the 706/4217 resolution re-check, `_instant_targets_ally_only`, the highlight probe, the router's target list and the AI's threat detection (`_is_ally_destroy_spell`, Withdraw's save, Kavai's doomed-sacrifice) are all inherited from Vanquish with no new code. What changes is only the DESTINATION: `_destroy_removes_from_game` swaps `_destroy_card_trigger` for a `move_card` to the target's **owner's** RFG zone (415.7a) + `card_removed_from_game`. The ally is therefore **not destroyed** — no `card_destroyed`, so `on_destroyed` triggers (Boneshanks) never fire and "when an ally is destroyed" watchers see nothing, and nothing reaches a graveyard for recursion (Ophelia, Augustus, Cannibalize) to find. A token was going to RFG anyway (`move_card`'s token branch), so it behaves identically. AI: none needed — `_targeted_instant_actions`' generic `destroy_target:ally` branch plus `_destroy_is_worth_it` cover it |
| `on_destroyed:EFFECT[:ARGS]` | Death-triggered effect fired when the card is destroyed (rule 703, `_fire_on_destroyed`, after the card reaches the graveyard). `deal_damage_aoe:AMOUNT:DMG_TYPE:opposing` auto-fires (no choice). `destroy_target:ally` (Boneshanks `dark_portal_201`: "When [this] is destroyed, destroy target ally.") is a **mandatory targeted** death trigger: it queues a `{card_id, controller}` entry in `pending_death_triggers` and opens a direct-call choice (`pending_death_target_player`, `death_target_required` event) resolved by the destroyed card's controller via `StackResolver.choose_death_target()` — NOT the chain; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending. Drained one at a time; chained deaths (destroying another Boneshanks) queue behind. Fizzles only when no ally is in play (`get_death_target_targets`). Targets any ally either party (may be forced onto the controller's own board). Human controller is PROMPTED even in hotseat (the choice is board-public; scene points the router at the controller). AI: `BaseAI.choose_death_target` picks the highest-value enemy ally, else the least valuable own ally when forced. See `data/rules_deviations.md` "Boneshanks" |
| `on_destroyed:deal_damage_hero_per_hand:AMOUNT:DMG_TYPE` | **Targeted death trigger with a HERO pool** (Vexra Darkfall `azeroth_265`, 5-cost 5/2 Horde Undead Mage: "When Vexra Darkfall is destroyed, she deals 1 arcane damage to target hero for each card in its controller's hand"). Boneshanks' mandatory targeted death trigger — same `pending_death_triggers` queue, same direct-call `choose_death_target` (NOT the chain), same hard block on `can_submit`/`pass_priority` — with a different pool and a different effect, which is what made the queue carry its effect: entries now hold `key`/`args` (the raw effects segment) like the turn-start and combat queues, and `get_death_trigger_targets(state, db, key)` is the one dispatcher behind the open/fizzle check, the resolution re-check, the router's target list and the AI. `get_active_death_target_targets` asks it about whichever trigger is currently awaiting a pick, so a **mixed queue resolves correctly** and a trigger that has run out of targets now fizzles **on its own** instead of clearing the queue. Pool = both players' in-play, 706-legal heroes ("target hero", so a controller may be forced to point it at their own). The amount is `AMOUNT ×` the cards in the **TARGET hero's controller's** hand, counted at **RESOLUTION** (709.2b) — a card drawn or played between the death and the pick changes it, an empty hand resolves for 0 (it does not fizzle: a hero is always a legal target), and aiming it at your own hero burns you for **your** hand. The source is Vexra, an **ally**, so the packet is not tagged `from_ability` (no Chromatic Cloak) and carries the segment's `dmg_type`; it goes through `defer_packets`, so armor prevention opens normally. Secondary kills don't recurse (`recursive_destroy = false`, as for the AoE flavour). AI (`BaseAI.choose_death_target`): always the **opposing** hero — the damage scales with the target's own hand, so self-targeting is pure self-harm |
| `return_to_hand:ally` | "Put target ally into its owner's hand" (Withdraw `azeroth_172`, 3, Instant Ability). Ally-only (`_instant_targets_ally_only`), instant speed. Resolution re-checks 706 + `_is_ally` (fizzles if the ally left play), then `GameLogic.move_card` to the owner's hand — leaving play resets damage/exhaust/buffs (400.6a, generalized in `move_card` to ANY play→non-play move, not just graveyard) and destroys attachments (400.5). Bounced in response to an opposing removal spell aimed at it, that spell fizzles at the 709.2a recheck; a bounced attacker/defender fizzles a pending combat proposal (601.3). Emits `card_returned_to_hand`. AI: tagged `combat_instant_save_bounce` (held, never blind-played); `BaseAI.save_bounce_action` plays it ONLY to interrupt opposing targeted removal on the chain aimed at our ally (destroy_target/destroy_ally, or lethal targeted damage incl. modal damage modes and ally-power damage; ally cost ≥ Withdraw's cost) — deliberately NEVER to dodge a combat attack (an attack costs the opponent no card; the bounce burns our card AND re-pays the ally's cost) |
| `return_to_hand:opposing_ally` | **Opposing-only bounce** (Fear `azeroth_123`, 1, Ability — Affliction, Warlock, Common): "Put target opposing ally into its owner's hand." Withdraw's `return_to_hand` narrowed to the OPPONENT's side — the exact mirror of Fall Back's `friendly_ally`, and a **subset of the ally-only restriction** rather than a target kind of its own, so `_instant_needs_target`, `_instant_targets_ally_only`, the highlight probe and the router's `can_submit`-filtered target list all cover it with **no UI code**. Like `friendly_ally` and unlike the other narrowings it cannot be judged from the def alone — it needs the caster — so `_instant_targets_opposing_ally_only` is checked at each call site (which knows `action.source_player`) and re-checked at RESOLUTION against the **spell card's controller read live**: an effect that changes control of the target while the link waits (Infernal, Helwen, Staff of Dominance) fizzles the bounce in **either** direction — an ally that became ours is no longer "opposing", one that became theirs is no longer "in your party". The highlight probe scans every player EXCEPT the caster, so the card goes **dark on an empty opposing board** even with allies of our own in play. Everything else is Withdraw's: 706/4217 re-check (a target that left play or became Untargetable fizzles it, the card is still spent), 400.6a reset of damage/exhaust/buffs, 400.5 destruction of attachments, and a **token ceases to exist** instead of reaching a hand. The other half of the difference from Withdraw is SPEED: Fear is a plain `Ability`, so it can never be flashed in to fizzle a combat proposal at the 601.3 recheck. `StackResolver.return_to_hand_kind(def)` is the public accessor. AI (`_targeted_instant_actions`): a bounce is tempo, not removal, so **tokens first** — a bounced token is permanent removal for 1 (it goes to RFG), which outranks any real ally whatever they score — else the most valuable opposing ally with **printed cost ≥ the spell's** (`_destroy_is_worth_it`'s first bar; trading a card for a 0-cost body is how this gets wasted), and it holds the card entirely when nothing clears that bar. Deliberately untagged, unlike Withdraw's held `combat_instant_save_bounce` — Fear is offensive and sorcery-speed, so it plays in the develop step. Not modelled: an ally with an enter-play trigger hands its controller that trigger again when replayed |
| `aura:N` | **Aura (N) type-line tag** (rule 414.3b tag-count uniqueness — Devotion Aura `azeroth_66`). The third slot tag after Form and Aspect, and it needed **one line**: `SLOT_TAGS` drives the whole check, `slot_tag_spec` reads the tag off the segment, and `_check_form_uniqueness` counts only in-play cards sharing the ENTERING card's tag — so the sacrifice choice point (`pending_form_sacrifice_*` → `choose_form_sacrifice`), its UI and the AI all came along unchanged, since the candidate id list fully describes the choice and the flow is tag-agnostic. **An independent slot**, so a player may control one Form AND one Aspect AND one Aura at once. Always paired with `ongoing`. Auras carry no `form_break`/`form_break_on` and no `on_destroyed:pay_return_hand`, so a Feral form-break never touches one — though playing one (a non-Feral ability) does break a Feral Form, per that Form's own printed condition. Note `choose_form_sacrifice`'s re-check assumes capacity 1, true of all three shipped tags |
| `party_damage_reduction:N` | **Blanket party-wide damage reduction** (Devotion Aura `azeroth_66`, 5, Ability — Protection, **Aura (1)**, Paladin, Rare: “Ongoing: If a hero or ally in your party would be dealt damage, prevent N of that damage”). The engine's first reduction that covers a whole party — armor is hero-only (717.2c) and Brother Rhone's shield is one card and combat-only. Applied in **`GameLogic.prevent`** as branch (a3b), off `party_damage_reduction`, a live read of the TARGET's own controller's hero_row: “in your party” is that controller's **hero plus its ally_row** (the `heal_party` convention, totems included per 305.3a), so an in-play ability, an attachment and equipment are never trimmed and the **opponent's party is untouched**. Being live is what makes it cover characters that arrive after the aura and lift the instant it leaves play. **A reduction, not a shield**: it trims N off EVERY packet from EVERY source, combat or not, and never depletes. Applied **before** the counted shields (Soul Link / Holy Shield) and before the armor pool — it is free and unlimited, so spending it first means the depleting resources absorb only the remainder, which is never worse for its controller (Holy Shield's scoped-before-unscoped reasoning); `_prevention_offer` nets it out too, so a packet the aura fully covers never opens an armor point. **The consequence worth holding onto: a packet reduced to 0 CEASES TO EXIST** (717.2b) — `deal_damage` returns before recording anything — so there is no `damage_dealt` event and no turn-log entry, and every damage watcher in the game goes blind to chip damage: Skorn, Cold Blood, Berserking's counters, whelp bounce, `discard_per_damage`, and Thysta's “if no damage was dealt this turn”, which then FIRES. A Searing Totem ping becomes nothing at all. Unpreventable damage (Lionheart Helm, Chastise, Annihilator) bypasses it entirely (717 short-circuits first). Copies are summed rather than maxed — Aura (1) makes that impossible for one player today, but summing is what stays correct if a capacity is ever raised. **AI: modelled**, in `BaseAI.combat_kills` — the one shared “does this combat remove that card?” predicate behind `find_safe_lethals` and `combat_trade_value`, the same spot Brother Rhone and Plagueborn Meatwall were taught — so every trade and protector decision counts the reduction on offence and defence for free, Plagueborn Meatwall's reflect included |
| `stance:N` | **Stance (N) type-line tag** (rule 414.3b tag-count uniqueness — Berserker Stance `azeroth_136`; Stance is the Warrior's flavor of the Druid's Form). Shares ALL of Form's machinery (`_check_form_uniqueness` → `pending_form_sacrifice_*` → `choose_form_sacrifice`) via the tag-agnostic `SLOT_TAGS` list, so it needed one line. **Independent slot**, so a player may control one Form AND one Stance at once. Always paired with `ongoing`. No `form_break`/`form_break_on`/`on_destroyed:pay_return_hand` — playing a second Stance triggers the ordinary sacrifice choice, nothing more |
| `aspect:N` | **Aspect (N) type-line tag** (rule 414.3b tag-count uniqueness — Aspect of the Hawk `azeroth_34`; Aspect is the Hunter's flavor of the Druid's Form). Shares ALL of Form's machinery (`_check_form_uniqueness` → `pending_form_sacrifice_*` → `choose_form_sacrifice`, and the scene/AI handling — the candidate id list fully describes the choice, so the flow is tag-agnostic), but it is an **independent slot**: `StackResolver.SLOT_TAGS` lists every slot tag and the check counts only in-play cards sharing the ENTERING card's tag, so a player may control one Form and one Aspect at once. Always paired with `ongoing`. Aspects carry no `form_break`/`form_break_on` and no `on_destroyed:pay_return_hand`, so a Feral form-break never touches one and destroying one opens no choice point — while playing an Aspect (a non-Feral ability) does break a Feral Form, per that Form's own printed condition. `form_slot_count`/`is_form_def` are now thin wrappers over `slot_count_for(def, "form")`, so Form-specific code (BaseAI's bear-form check) is unaffected. Note: `choose_form_sacrifice`'s re-check assumes capacity 1 — true of both shipped tags; a capacity-2 tag would need the capacity carried on the pending state |
| `form:N` | **Form (N) type-line tag** (rule 414.3b tag-count uniqueness — Bear Form `azeroth_18`, Bash `azeroth_17`, Cat Form `dark_portal_19`, Claw `dark_portal_20`; all Form (1)). Always paired with `ongoing` — Forms are ongoing Abilities living in the hero_row. A player may control at most N in-play Forms: a second one triggers an immediate sacrifice choice mirroring the Pet/Unique flow (`_check_form_uniqueness` → `pending_form_sacrifice_*` → `choose_form_sacrifice`, direct call; `can_submit` hard-blocks). Playing the new Form IS the shapeshift — the AI (and normally a human) destroys the OLD one (scene keeps `new_card_id` from the event) |
| `form_break:TAG` | Form self-destroy condition (glossary Bear/Cat Form): "Destroy this card when you strike with a weapon or play a non-TAG ability." **For a NAMED form this is derived, not written** — `StackResolver.form_break_tag` asks `GameState.FORM_GRANTS` by form name first, so `form_state:bear` alone carries the Feral break and no shipped Form card contains this segment (Claw's "Feral Combo" tag matches by substring). The explicit segment survives only as the fallback for an **unnamed** form (one carrying the Form (N) slot tag but no `form_state`), which the table cannot describe. Checked at ability RESOLUTION (`_check_form_break_ability` in `_resolve_play_instant` + `_resolve_play_ongoing_ability` — covers plain, ongoing, totem and attachment ability plays) and at the strike point (`_check_form_break_strike` in `choose_strike`). Weapon *activated powers* and armor prevention never break a form; ally/equipment/quest plays don't either. Travel Form would omit the segment; Shadowform's inverted condition is `form_break_on:TAG` (next row). See `data/rules_deviations.md` "Form break timing" |
| `form_break_on:TAG` | **Inverted** Form self-destroy condition (Shadowform `azeroth_88`): "When you play a Holy ability, destroy Shadowform" = `form_break_on:Holy`. The mirror of `form_break:TAG` — the Form breaks when the played ability's tags DO contain TAG, instead of when they don't. Handled in the same `_check_form_break_ability` loop (`form_break_on_tag` accessor; a def carries at most one of the two, and `form_break_on` wins where both appear). **Ability plays only** — unlike the Feral forms, whose printed text names weapon strikes explicitly, Shadowform's clause doesn't, so `_check_form_break_strike` deliberately ignores it (it keys on `form_break_tag`, which is "" for Shadowform). Shadowform also has no `on_destroyed:pay_return_hand` clause, so breaking it opens no choice point |

An on-play effect segment of a Form card — Bash's slot, resolved in `_resolve_play_ongoing_ability` before the ongoing half settles into the hero_row — and the **untargeted** twin of Ravenous Bite's pump: a `Buff` (stat `atk`, duration `turns`:1) placed on the **caster's own hero**, so the end-of-turn sweep gives "this turn" for free (correct even when cast on the opponent's turn, which instant speed makes possible) and `get_atk` section (1) reads it live. Nothing is announced — no target, no choice — so 706 is irrelevant and the pump **can never fizzle**; a hero is always in play. Distinct from Cat Form's `hero_atk_while_attacking:N` on the axis that matters: this one is **unconditional**, so it counts while defending and while protecting (with bear form's protector grant on the same card, the retaliation is the usual payoff), and it is a one-shot on-play buff rather than an ongoing grant — the buff sits on the HERO, so **destroying the Form does not take it back**. It also makes an otherwise 0-ATK hero a legal attacker, since that gate probes `get_atk` live. AI: no new hook — Maul is tagged `combat_instant_bear_form` alongside Bear Form, so `bear_form_action` flashes it in during an attack window for the protector grant and the pump rides along; that hook now spends the **cheapest** affordable bear-form card in hand (Form (1) means the second copy would have to eat the first anyway, and Bear Form's pay-2 return clause is worth more than +1 ATK). The offensive use — playing it on your own turn to swing for one more — is deliberately not modelled, the same tempo call left out of Thangal |
| `hero_atk_while_attacking_in_form:FORMS:N` | **"While your hero is in FORM, it has +N ATK while attacking"** (Predatory Strikes `azeroth_29`, 1, Ability - Feral **Talent**, Druid, Rare: "...in bear form or cat form..." = `hero_atk_while_attacking_in_form:bear+cat:2`). Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. Cat Form's `hero_atk_while_attacking:N` with a form gate; `FORMS` is a **`+`-joined list** of qualifying form names, so one segment covers "bear form or cat form". Read live in `GameState._aura_atk_mods` beside the ungated version - never cached, so shapeshifting switches the bonus on and breaking the form drops it, mid-combat included. **The aura and the Form need not be the same card**: any in-play source declaring `form_state:bear` or `:cat` satisfies it (Bear Form, Bash, Maul, Cat Form, Claw), which is exactly why a card keying on a form must read `form_state` and never a grant. Like the ungated version it is **defender-independent**, so it is safe inside `assume_attacking` forecasts and inside the `get_legal_attackers` hero gate - a 0-ATK hero in form is a legal attacker - unlike Bala's `atk_vs_exhausted_defender`. Hero only (never an ally) and controller-scoped. "While attacking" is literal: a **defending** hero gets nothing, which is the card's real limit next to bear form's defensive grant. Stacks with the Form's own bonus and per copy. **The form check is `GameState.hero_form_states`** - the ONE implementation of that read, which `StackResolver.hero_is_in_form` now delegates to: a continuous modifier has to ask it from inside `get_atk`, and GameState must not depend on the resolver. "Feral Hero Required" is the Talent-spec restriction the deck authorizer already enforces via rule 100.2c (`Feral Talent` in the tags column), not a new in-game gate. No AI modeling beyond the shared `get_atk` reads |
| `hero_atk_while_attacking:N` | Cat form ongoing grant: the controller's HERO has +N ATK **while attacking**. Live in `GameState._aura_atk_mods` (hero_row source scan), gated on `is_attacking` — defender-independent, so it's included in `assume_attacking` forecasts AND the `get_legal_attackers` hero gate (which now probes `get_atk(hero, db, true)`; a 0-ATK hero with a while-attacking bonus — Cat Form, a Ryn Dreamstrider buff — is a legal attacker, matching the printed rules). Never cached. **Cat Form and Claw no longer carry this segment** — the grant is derived from `form_state:cat` via `GameState.FORM_GRANTS`, read in the same `_aura_atk_mods` but keyed on the form NAME. The segment stays live for a future NON-form card printing the same grant; no card carries both, so nothing double-counts |
| `enters_with_counters:NAME:AMOUNT` | **Counters placed on a card as it enters play** (Blood Fury `dark_portal_135`, 4+X, Ability — Horde, **Orc Hero Required**, Uncommon: "Blood Fury enters play with X fury counters on it" = `enters_with_counters:fury:X`). AMOUNT is a literal number or the token **`X`**, in which case it is the `x_value` announced with the play (Aimed Shot's convention on the hand-card path — `cost_base` 4, `cost_x` true, total paid at submission and refunded on retraction). Counters live in `CardInstance.counters`, the dictionary Berserking already uses, so the orange ATK-corner badge comes along for free (`_refresh_atk_badges` now shows the TOTAL across counter kinds, so a new counter card needs no UI change). Applied in `_resolve_play_ongoing_ability` **right after the `move_card` into the hero row**, not as an on-play effect segment: the counters are part of the card entering play, nothing can respond between the two, and a reader that finds the card in play always finds its counters with it. X is **not re-derived** at resolution — an X-cost card can't be announced with X < 1 (`_can_afford_play`) and the resources were paid at announcement, so what entered play matches what was bought. Emits `counter_changed` |
| `hero_atk_while_attacking_per_counter:NAME:N` | **"Your hero has +N ATK while attacking for each NAME counter on this"** (Blood Fury). Cat Form's `hero_atk_while_attacking` grant scaled by a **counter count read live** off the source card, in the same `GameState._aura_atk_mods` hero_row scan. Hero only (never an ally) and controller-scoped ("**your** hero"); like the ungated version it is **defender-independent**, so it is safe inside `assume_attacking` forecasts and inside the `get_legal_attackers` hero gate — a 0-ATK hero holding counters is a legal attacker — unlike Bala's `atk_vs_exhausted_defender`. "While attacking" is literal: a **defending** hero gets nothing. The distinction from Berserking is that **nothing ever adds or removes a fury counter after the card enters play** — Berserking accumulates them on hero damage and CASHES THEM IN when the hero attacks (a combat trigger emptying the card), whereas these are fixed at announcement and paid for once, so the bonus is permanent for as long as the card is in play. Stacks per copy and lifts the instant a source leaves play. Never cached, so every downstream reader (the attack cursor, `forecast_atk`, `combat_trade_value`, the ATK badge) gets it with no new code |
| `on_destroyed:pay_return_hand:COST` | Bear/Cat Form death trigger: "When [this] is destroyed, you may pay COST. If you do, put it into your hand." Opens a direct-call choice (`pending_form_return_*`, `form_return_opened`, `choose_form_return(state, pay, db)` — like the whelp bounce; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block) only when affordable; paying returns the card to hand IMMEDIATELY (deviation — see `data/rules_deviations.md` "Form return timing"). Bash and Claw do NOT have this clause. AI always pays (`BaseAI.choose_form_return`) |
| `no_draws_this_turn` | **Board-wide draw lock for the turn** (Brain Freeze `azeroth_49`, 3, Instant Ability — Frost, Mage, Rare): "Players can't draw cards this turn." The engine's first prohibition on DRAWING, and the first modifier of any kind whose scope is **both players at once** — the printed text says "players", plural, with no controller clause, so it locks its **own caster** too. Nothing is announced (no target, no choice), so 706 Untargetable is irrelevant and it can never fizzle; the highlight probe never darks it. The lock is a bare bool on **`GameState`** rather than on `PlayerState` for that same reason, and it is enforced in **`GameLogic.draw_one`** — the ONE "a player is required to draw" primitive — so the draw step (501.2a), every draw effect shipped and every future one respect it by construction, with no per-effect hook. Per rule **415.9f** it is a lock on drawing ALONE: an event that puts a card into a hand from a deck is a draw only if it says the card is *drawn*, so a graveyard fetch (Call the Spirit, Medoc), a reveal-and-pick (Eagle Eye, Overseer Oilfist) and a "look at" (Track Humanoids) all still work — which is also the real limit on the card. **A blocked draw does NOT deck its player** (a prohibition means the draw event never happens, so nobody was "required to draw from an empty deck" — 410.6b): Brain Freeze can never win the game on its own, see `data/rules_deviations.md` "Brain Freeze". Emits `draws_locked` on resolution and `draw_blocked` at each swallowed draw, both logged by playtest since an effect resolving into nothing is otherwise invisible. "This turn" is free — the flag is cleared at every turn start beside the turn event log (`game_logic/turn_state_flags.md`, Category C). **Instant speed is the whole card**: see the AI entry under implemented cards |
