Everything else is inherited whole: **711.3** (only a card PLAYED onto the chain is a candidate — a *placed* resource is a `place_resource` link and never qualifies), **711.2** (a link can't interrupt itself, and costs the interrupted link already paid are NOT refunded), "ability card" meaning `card_type == "Ability"` so both Ability and Instant Ability qualify but an **Instant Ally never does**, either player's link being legal (so a human may counter their own), and the 706/4217 re-check at resolution — a link already interrupted by something else fizzles this one, with the card still spent. The one behavioural difference from Escape Artist: Counterspell has **no targetless mode**, so with an empty chain it is unplayable and the highlight probe goes **dark** (706.2), where Escape Artist stays lit on its remove-attackers half. UI needed no new flow — `_is_interrupt_mode` gained a top-level branch, and the router asks the pool WITHOUT the rider and filters through `can_submit`, so each card's own rider applies. AI (`BaseAI.counterspell_action`, tagged `combat_instant_counterspell` — held, and never blind-played since an empty chain isn't even legal): two bars in order — an opposing ability aimed at our **HERO** is countered at any printed cost (Escape Artist's reasoning: a discard, lasting debuff or burn never trades on its own), otherwise the most expensive opposing ability whose printed cost is at least Counterspell's own (`_destroy_is_worth_it`'s convention — countering a 1-cost cantrip with a 2-cost card is how it gets wasted). Never our own link |
| `mode:interrupt_ability:targets_your_hero` | **Interrupt a link on the chain** (rule 711 — Escape Artist `dark_portal_129`, 1, Instant Ability, Alliance, **Gnome Hero Required**): "Interrupt target ability card that's targeting your hero." The engine's first counterspell, and the first target that is a LINK rather than a card in play — so it has its own pool (`get_interrupt_candidates`) instead of going through `_is_legal_target`, which asks about in-play characters. A candidate must be (a) backed by a `play_instant`/`play_ability` pending action whose card sits in the shared `chain` zone — which is how **711.3** falls out by construction: a card *placed* on the chain (412.1, a resource) is a `place_resource` link and can never be targeted — (b) an **Ability card** (`card_type == "Ability"`, so both Ability and Instant Ability, never an Instant Ally), and (c) announcing the caster's hero in any target slot (`_link_targets_hero` scans `target_id`/`_2`/`_3`/`heal_target_id` **and** `target_ids`, so single-target, multi-slot and id-list spells are all covered). `_is_interrupt_target` is the ONE predicate behind submission, the router's target list and the resolution re-check (706/4217 — a link already interrupted, or retargeted off our hero, fizzles), and it excludes the interrupting card itself (**711.2**: a link can't interrupt itself). Resolution (`_interrupt_link`) removes the link from `pending_actions` and moves the card to its **owner's** graveyard (711.1) + emits `link_interrupted`; the whole text is interrupted and **costs already paid are NOT refunded** (711.2), which is why nothing there touches resources or undoes an announcement cost. Priority afterwards needs no special handling — `pass_priority`'s post-resolution path already hands it to the turn player, as 711.1 requires. Either player's ability is legal (the printed text says simply "target ability card"), so a human may interrupt their own. UI: the mode button enters the normal targeting flow and the chain card is clicked like any other target (`InputRouter._is_interrupt_mode` is the single switch — the mode index on `_targeting_mode` is what distinguishes a chain pick from a hero-or-ally pick). The `targets_your_hero` rider carries the "…targeting your hero" clause; it lives on the MODE rather than in the pool so Counterspell (`azeroth_51`), which is the same effect without the clause, can share `get_interrupt_candidates` — see the `interrupt_ability` row above. AI: see `BaseAI.escape_artist_action` |
| `remove_attackers:CONDITION` | "Remove all attackers from combat" gated on a condition (Blink `azeroth_48`: `hero_defending` — the caster's hero is the CURRENT defender, i.e. the defend window is open with `combat_defender` = their hero; during the attack window the hero is only a *proposed* defender, so the clause no-ops — the strictness is deliberate, "defending" exists only after the protect point, 602.3). Resolution clears `state.combat_attacker` and emits `attacker_removed_from_combat`; per 602.4 the combat step does NOT end immediately — the window plays out and the conclusion's 603.1b check then deals no damage. Each half re-checks its own target at resolution, so killing/bouncing one target fizzles only that half. Buffs are `Buff` stat `atk`, duration `turns`:1 — the existing end-of-turn sweep gives "this turn" expiry for free, correct even when cast on the opponent's turn. `GameState.get_atk` now floors at 0 (a character can't have negative ATK) while KEEPING the raw negative buff, so a later +ATK counts from the true value. Aimed at a proposed attacker while its combat proposal is on the chain, the 601.3 recheck (`attacker.is_exhausted`) fizzles the proposal — same interrupt path as Litori's `target_cant_attack`; too late once the attack window opens. | `friendly_pets_keyword:KEYWORD` | **Controller-scoped Pet keyword aura** — "Ongoing: Your Pets have KEYWORD" (Protect the Master `dark_portal_38`, 1, Instant Ability — Beast Mastery, Hunter: protector). Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. The same live read as `all_allies_keyword` and in the same place (`StackResolver._has_keyword`, using the optional trailing `state` param), narrowed on **both** axes that aura is broad on: the scan is **controller-relative** (`_pet_keyword_aura` reads only the affected card's own controller's rows — "**your** Pets", so an opposing Pet is never granted anything, unlike Lust for Battle) and the recipient must be a **Pet** (`_is_pet` — an ally_row card whose def's `card_subtype` is "Pet", the same read the 414.3b pet-uniqueness check and Bestial Wrath's target kind use, so a non-Pet ally of the controller's is untouched). Being read live is what makes it cover Pets that enter play AFTER the aura resolved and makes the grant lift the instant the aura leaves play, with no bookkeeping. Protector is a defensive grant, so with pet capacity at 1 the card normally shields exactly one body — it scales only where an effect has raised `PlayerState.pet_capacity`. No AI modeling: the aura is not played for proactively, but `get_legal_protectors` reads `_has_keyword` live, so the AI's protector decision tree offers the Pet as a blocker for free |
| `hero_keyword:KEYWORD` | **Controller-scoped HERO keyword aura** — "Your hero has KEYWORD" (Sentry Gwynn `azeroth_215`, 5-cost 3/4 Alliance Night Elf Warrior: elusive). The third live read in `StackResolver._has_keyword`, after `all_allies_keyword` and `friendly_pets_keyword`, and the narrowest: the scan is **controller-relative** (`_hero_keyword_aura` reads only the affected card's own controller's hero_row + ally_row — "**your** hero", so the opponent's hero is never granted anything) and the recipient must be that player's **hero instance** (`_is_hero`), so the controller's own allies are untouched too. Unlike the two auras above the source is an **ALLY**, not an ongoing Ability, so it sits in the ally_row and can be attacked off the board — and it needs no `ongoing` segment. Being read live is what makes the grant lift the instant the source leaves play. With `elusive` the effect is defensive and total: elusive is "can't be **proposed as** a defender" (601.2b), so every attack is funnelled at the controller's allies, and both gate sites (`_can_propose_combat`'s 601.2b check and `get_legal_defenders`) already ask `_has_keyword(hero, "elusive", db, state)`, so no combat code was needed. **Protecting is unaffected** — 602.2 is a different gate, so a hero with a protector grant (Draconian Deflector, Bear Form) can still step in. Note the aura on an ally body is self-limiting: once the rest of the party is gone the source herself is the only legal defender, so killing her is how the opponent reaches the hero again. Second card on this key: **Treesong** (`azeroth_220`, 5-cost 4/4 Alliance Night Elf Druid, Uncommon) is `hero_keyword:protector` — pure CSV, no engine code, since `_hero_keyword_aura` is keyword-generic and `get_legal_protectors` already asks `_has_keyword` of every hero-row card, the hero instance included. **This is now the ONLY aura recipe that grants a hero a keyword.** The old bare `hero_has_protector` flag and its separate branch in `get_legal_protectors` are gone: Draconian Deflector carries `hero_keyword:protector`, and bear form's protector is derived from `GameState.FORM_GRANTS` (see `form_state:NAME`). So every route — a printed keyword, a timed `grant_<keyword>` Buff (Warrax), this aura, the Pet/race/all-ally auras, and a form's grant — is answered by the ONE `_has_keyword` funnel, which is exactly what `get_legal_protectors` asks, so a gate and the rule cannot disagree. **The scan covers ATTACHMENTS too** (Rockbiter Weapon): an attachment lives in the shared `attached` zone rather than in a row, so `_hero_keyword_aura` also sweeps `cards_in_zone("attached")` filtered on the **attachment's own controller** — “your hero” is the caster's, which is what `_resolve_attach` sets and what follows the attachment if control of it changes, the HOST having no say in it (an attachment of yours on an opposing weapon still grants to YOUR hero). Same scan shape as `GameState._marked_target_atk_aura`, and 400.5 does the cleanup for free. **Use `hero_keyword:KEYWORD` for any new card granting the hero a keyword while it is in play**; `hero_grant_keyword` for a timed one; `FORM_GRANTS` for something a form confers. 602.2's other gates still apply to both (an **exhausted** hero can't protect; 602.2b stops it protecting itself). No AI modeling — both are played as vanilla bodies, and every combat query reads `_has_keyword` live, so both AIs respect the grant for free |

The party is the **controller's ally_row** read live: it counts the **source itself** (she is an ally in your party), counts **totems** (305.3a), and never counts the hero or the opponent's board. "Four or more" is a threshold, not a per-ally scale — a fifth ally adds nothing. Never cached, so the grant switches on and off as allies arrive and leave, mid-combat included, and every downstream reader (`get_legal_attackers`, the attack cursor, `forecast_atk`, `combat_trade_value`, the ATK/HP badges) gets it for free. **The health half can kill her**: a damaged Kailis whose party drops below four may land at 0 or fewer effective health, which is a state-based death (118.4/704) — swept by `StackResolver.drain_state_based_deaths` at the priority gate, since the ally that took the party under four may have LEFT play by a route that isn't a destruction (bounce, exile, a control change) and so never ran `_check_aura_loss_deaths`. No AI modeling beyond the shared `get_atk`/`get_max_hp` reads |

X is read **LIVE off the sacrifice the instant before it dies** (`state.get_atk`), inside the cost block that already pays it, so an ATK buff or aura on the sacrifice counts and one answered in the response window does not. Like every `sacrifice_ally` cost it is paid at **ANNOUNCEMENT** (707.1e), and X is captured as the ally dies (riding the action as `_sac_atk`), so the damage can no longer be shrunk to nothing by answering the sacrifice. The damage is dealt by **Mezzik**, an ally, so it is NOT tagged `from_ability` (no Chromatic Cloak) and carries the power's own `dmg_type`; it goes through `defer_packets`, so armor prevention applies normally. Chip damage for a whole ally is how the card is wasted, so with nothing killable it holds |

| `activated_power:0:put_hand_ally_into_play:0` | **An ally out of your hand, straight into play, free** (Seraph the Exalted `dark_portal_190`, 5-cost 1/5 Alliance Human Priest, Rare): "[Activate] → Put an ally card from your hand into play if its cost is less than or equal to the number of resources you have." AI (`BaseAI.choose_hand_play`): the ally arrives free, so it simply takes the highest `card_value_score`, ties broken on the dearer printed cost; `_get_ally_power_actions` gates the tap on the pool being non-empty, since the untargeted else-branch would otherwise fire her every turn for nothing |

The heal lands inline — no packet pipeline, nothing to prevent — and is capped by the damage actually present, so 10 is a ceiling rather than a payout. Sorcery speed (a plain `Ability`), so it can't be flashed in as a combat save. Careful with the word: an activated power (`activated_power:1:heal_target:2:...`, The Hammer of Grace), an enter-play trigger (`on_enter:heal_target:2`, Ra'chee) and a modal mode (`mode:heal_target:3`) all carry it further along their own segment and are NOT this — `StackResolver._heal_target_amount` matches `parts[0]` only. AI (`_targeted_instant_actions`): the one targeted hand spell whose target must be **FRIENDLY**, so the branch is a deliberate narrowing of the printed pool — our own most-damaged character, hero winning a tie (it is the one character whose loss ends the game), and the card is **held entirely** while nothing on our side is damaged |
| `heal_party:N` (top-level) | **"Your hero heals N damage from each hero and ally in your party"** (Prayer of Healing `azeroth_84`, 3, Ability — Holy, Priest, Uncommon). Lady Courtney Noel's `heal_party` sweep reached from a **HAND card** — the same live read of the caster's own hero plus their **ally_row** (totems included, 305.3a), with the opponent's party untouched. **Non-targeted and friendly-only**, so nothing is announced, **706 Untargetable is irrelevant**, and it can **never fizzle** — there is no target to lose, which is the structural difference from `heal_target` beside it. The party is read at **RESOLUTION**, so an ally that arrived in the response window is healed and one that left is not. The **HERO** is the healer as printed, but a heal lands **inline** (no packet pipeline, nothing to prevent and no `from_ability` tag for Chromatic Cloak to boost), so the source only matters to a future "when your hero heals" watcher. Capped by the damage actually present, so N is a ceiling rather than a payout; an undamaged party is a legal no-op (`GameLogic.heal` emits nothing) rather than an illegal announce, and a `cannot_be_healed` lock (Mortal Strike) empties that one character's share alone. `StackResolver._top_level_heal_party_amount` matches `parts[0]` only — an activated power (`activated_power:0:heal_party:1`, Lady Courtney Noel) and an enter-play trigger (`on_enter:heal_party:2`, Stylean Silversteel) carry the same word further along their own segment and are **NOT** this. AI (`get_reasonable_actions`): a non-targeted ability falls through to the generic play branch, so an ungated AI would cast it on an undamaged board every time it drew one — it is gated exactly like the activated power, on somebody in our own party actually carrying damage |
| `require_hero_undamaged` | **"Use only if your hero has no damage on it"** (Clarity of Thought `dark_portal_68`, 4, Ability — Holy, Priest, Uncommon). A **USE restriction** in the `require_turn_player` family (rule 701.1), not an effect condition — and the two consequences of that are the whole of it. (1) It is checked at **ANNOUNCEMENT only** (`_can_use_ally_power`) and deliberately **never re-checked at resolution**: damage landing on the hero in the response window does NOT fizzle the draw, exactly as the turn is not re-checked for a `require_turn_player` power. Contrast Thangal's `require_form_state`, which IS re-checked (709.2a) because the form is what the effect READS — a draw reads nothing about the hero. Contrast also Point Blank's `require_hero_defending`, an effect condition that makes the card legal everywhere and fizzling instead. (2) It restricts **nothing else** — the power stays instant-speed, so an undamaged hero may draw in a combat window or in response to a link. **"No damage ON IT" is damage counters, not missing health**, so ANY single point turns it off (it is not a threshold) and healing back to full turns it on again — which is what pairs it with the Priest heals. The gate reads the ACTION controller's own hero, so the opponent's damaged hero is irrelevant, and because the highlight probe goes through the same `can_submit` the card darks by itself while the hero is hurt. AI: no new code — the shared `draw` power branch's hand-room gate (503.2a) still applies and the condition arrives free through `can_submit` |
| `heal_x_from_target:DMG_TYPE` | Hero power: pay X resources → heal X from target hero or ally (Boris) |
| `shuffle_graveyard_into_deck` | Quest reward (Blueleaf Tubers `azeroth_349`, Quest, pay 2): "Reward: Shuffle your graveyard into your deck." First **argument-less** reward segment, so it is dispatched ahead of `_apply_quest_reward`'s `parts.size() < 2` guard — that guard exists to skip the flag segments riding alongside a reward (`require_turn_player`, `complete_cost_destroy_self`), and a bare reward key would otherwise be silently skipped too. `GameLogic.shuffle_graveyard_into_deck` moves the whole zone through `move_card` and shuffles: every card in a player's graveyard is OWNED by that player (415.9d), so no per-card owner check is needed, and the opponent's graveyard is untouched ("**your** graveyard"). It is **not a draw and not a search** — nobody looks at a card, so 410.6b can't fire (completing it on an empty deck is safe) and nothing is revealed. Emits `deck_shuffled` even on an empty graveyard, which is what drives the SFX. Tokens are never in a graveyard to begin with (they go to RFG), so none can be recycled. No AI modeling — completed generically like any cost-only quest |
| `complete_cost_destroy_self` | Quest completion **cost** paid by destroying the quest ITSELF (Into the Maw of Madness `azeroth_353`, Quest, no resource cost): "Destroy Into the Maw of Madness to complete this quest. Reward: Draw a card." The Love Potion's `exhaust_allies` timing on an irreversible cost: `quest_cost_destroys_self` gates it and `submit_action` destroys the quest **at announcement** (rule 412.2), so only the REWARD rides the chain — an opponent who interrupts that link cancels the draw and the cost stays paid (711.2). Not to be confused with the `sacrifice_self` EXTRACOST on an activated power, which is deliberately also paid at announcement; both are in the graveyard before anyone gets priority. The destroy goes through `_destroy_card_trigger`, so `on_destroyed` triggers and the `ally_destroyed` turn-log sweep behave as for any destruction, and the card reaches its **owner's** graveyard (415.9d). Because a destroyed card can't be un-destroyed the announcement is non-retractable — the same `_cost_paid_irreversibly` params mark Sever the Cord uses. `_resolve_use_quest` skips both the "already used" guard and the face-down flip for such a quest (there is no resource-row card left to flip), and the renderer's `quest_completed` handler skips the flip too unless the node is still in a resource zone. Re-completion needs no gate: the card is no longer a face-up resource, so `_can_use_quest` rejects it. **The quest is a resource while it sits face-up**, so the real price is a card AND a permanent -1 ramp. AI (`BaseAI`, quest branch): held below `DESTROY_SELF_QUEST_MIN_RESOURCES` (6 total resources) — early ramp beats a card — and held on a full hand, where the draw would be discarded at wrap-up (503.2a) |
| `complete_cost_exhaust_self` + `enters_play_exhausted` | Quest completion **cost paid by exhausting the quest itself** (Lazy Peons `dark_portal_303`, Quest, Horde, no resource cost: "Orc Hero Required. Lazy Peons enters play exhausted. Exhaust Lazy Peons to complete this quest. Reward: Draw a card."). Into the Maw of Madness' `complete_cost_destroy_self` with the destroy softened to an exhaust: `quest_cost_exhausts_self` gates it and `submit_action` exhausts the quest **at announcement** (rule 412.2), before the resource cost so the quest can never pay for its own completion, and only the REWARD rides the chain — an opponent who interrupts that link cancels the draw and the cost stays paid (711.2). Unlike the destroy it **is refundable**, so `retract_last` readies it back (before the resource refund loop, which walks the pay order in reverse and would otherwise spend its one ready-back on the quest) and the announcement stays **retractable**. `_can_use_quest` requires the quest to be **READY**, which is the whole reason `enters_play_exhausted` matters: per **710.1b** it is processed DURING entry (`_resolve_place_resource`), so the card adds nothing to the resource row on the turn it is placed and can't be completed until it readies. Re-completion needs no gate — resolution flips it face down like any other quest. **The real consequence is on the automatic resource pay order** (`_resource_pay_order`): the player never picks which resource pays, so an ordinary cost would silently spend Lazy Peons and destroy its text — such a quest is therefore spent **dead last**, after face-down cards and ordinary face-up quests, and a `complete_cost_destroy_self` quest is spent **FIRST** (its own completion exhausts it anyway, so paying with it is free). `requires_hero_race:Orc` is deck legality only (100.2b). No AI modeling — the generic quest branch completes it whenever `can_submit` allows |
| `reveal_pick:CARD_TYPE:N` | Quest reward **or hand-ability effect** (Eagle Eye — the segment grammar, the flags and the `_reveal_pick` helper are shared, so the browser UI and the AI's pick quality come along for free; the dispatch lives in both `_apply_quest_reward` and `_resolve_play_instant`). Reveal the top N cards; put a revealed card of `CARD_TYPE` (`Equipment`/`Ally`/`Ability`; matches the parsed `CardDef.card_type`, so `Instant Ally`→`Ally`) into hand, rest to the bottom of the deck in revealed order. If at least one matches, sets `pending_reveal_pick_*` and emits `reveal_pick_opened`; the scene resolves it via `StackResolver.choose_reveal_pick()` (direct call, not the stack — like pet sacrifice; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending). The choice ALWAYS opens (even with no match) so the player sees the revealed cards; the browser shows every revealed card graveyard-style, non-matching ones tinted red and non-selectable, a matching card MUST be picked before OK, and if none match the player clicks OK (empty pick — `choose_reveal_pick(state, "")`) to send all revealed cards to the bottom. Big Game Hunter (Equipment:4), Kibler's Exotic Pets (Ally:3), Zapped Giants (Ability:3). `CARD_TYPE` `Any` makes every revealed card selectable. Optional trailing flags (order-independent, from field 4 on): `opponent` hands the PICK to the other player (The Princess Trapped), `to_top` puts the picked card back on TOP of the deck instead of into hand, `private` makes it a "look at" instead of a public reveal (both: It's a Secret to Everybody) — see `reveal_pick:Any:N:opponent` below |
| `reveal_pick:Any:N:opponent` | **Opponent-decided** reveal-pick (The Princess Trapped `azeroth_357`, Quest, pay 2): "Reveal the top two cards of your deck. Target opponent chooses one. Put that card into your hand and the other one on the bottom of your deck." First card whose DECIDER differs from its owner, so the pending choice now carries both: `GameState.pending_reveal_pick_player` is the OWNER (whose deck was revealed, whose hand the pick goes to) and `pending_reveal_pick_chooser` is the DECIDER. Every blocker (`can_submit` / `pass_priority`) still keys off the owner field — only input routing and AI pick-quality key off the chooser. `reveal_pick_opened` carries `chooser`; `choose_reveal_pick` is unchanged (the picked card still goes to the OWNER's hand, the rest to the bottom of the OWNER's deck). "Target opponent" is auto-chosen — see `data/rules_deviations.md` "The Princess Trapped". UI: the reveal browser opens for the chooser via `_route_choice(chooser, "public")` — in hotseat the revealed cards are shown to both players and only the input is re-pointed (no hand hiding, no handoff). AI: `_pick_ai_reveal(candidates, hostile)` inverts its ranking when choosing FOR the opponent — it hands them the LEAST valuable revealed card |
| `reveal_pick:Any:N:to_top:private` | **Look-at-and-stack** reveal-pick (It's a Secret to Everybody `azeroth_354`, Quest, pay 1): "Look at the top three cards of your deck. Put one on the top of your deck and the rest on the bottom." Two flags on the shared reveal-pick machinery. `to_top` (`GameState.pending_reveal_pick_to_top`): the picked card is **not moved at all** — the revealed cards never left the top of the deck, so once the others are pushed to the bottom in revealed order it is back on top by itself; nothing reaches the hand. `private` (`pending_reveal_pick_private`): a "look at" is the chooser's alone, so the scene routes the browser through `_route_choice(chooser, "private")` instead of `"public"`, and the shared game log never names the kept card (`reveal_pick_resolved` carries `to_top`). In hotseat both players share a screen so privacy is nominal; it is what a future two-device build keys off to send the opponent nothing. With `Any` a pick is always available, so the empty-acknowledgement branch only comes up on an empty deck. AI: unchanged `_pick_ai_reveal` (keeps the most valuable card) |
| `qmode:EFFECT[:ARGS]` + `qchoice_both_race:RACE` | **Quest reward choice** ("Choose one: …; or draw a card. If your hero is a RACE, you may choose both" — Hidden Enemies `dark_portal_302`, A New Plague `dark_portal_304`, Thwarting Kolkar Aggression `dark_portal_309`, Crown of the Earth `dark_portal_289`, Poison Water `dark_portal_305`). Each `qmode:` segment wraps one reward mode; `qchoice_both_race:RACE` names the hero race (substring of the hero def's tags, like `requires_hero_race`) that unlocks picking BOTH modes, in an order of the completer's choosing. The quest resolves into a mandatory direct-call choice (`_open_quest_choice` → `pending_quest_choice_*` → `StackResolver.choose_quest_modes(state, [mode] or [first, second], db)`; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block). Unavailable modes can't be picked and knock out "both" (see `data/rules_deviations.md` "Quest reward choices"); chosen modes queue in `quest_mode_queue` and run front-first, each re-checked at run time. Mode effects: `draw:N`; `ally_ferocity_this_turn` and `ally_cant_attack_this_turn` (Hidden Enemies' gift and The Perfect Stout's lock — **one choice point serving two grants**: the completer picks any in-play ally, either party, via `choose_quest_ally_grant_target`, 706 respected, and **`GameState.pending_quest_ally_grant_kind`** (`"ferocity"` / `"cannot_attack"`) is what tells them apart, so the router's targeting flow and the pool need know nothing about which reward opened the point — Gartok's `pending_attack_exhaust_kind` pattern. Both grants are Buffs with duration `turns`:1, so the end-of-turn sweep gives “this turn” for free and leaving play clears them: `grant_ferocity` read by `_has_keyword`, and Litori Frostburn's `cannot_attack` restriction read by `get_legal_attackers`. The lock is **attacking only** — protecting is a different gate (602.2) and the printed text says nothing about it, so unlike Frost Shock no `cannot_protect` rides along); `each_player_destroys_ally` (gated on "an ally in your party"; every player WITH an ally picks their own sacrifice via `choose_plague_destroy`, completer first); `opponent_quest_face_down` ("target player" auto-opponent — deviation; the TARGET player picks which face-up quest flips via `choose_quest_facedown`; same spent face-down state as completion, no reward); `hand_to_deck_draw` (whole hand to the bottom of the deck in hand order, draw that many); `shuffle_graveyard_pick` (Poison Water — the completer CHOOSES any number of cards in their OWN graveyard, zero included, to shuffle back into their deck; a choice, not a target, so 706 is irrelevant, and the pool is read at RESOLUTION via `get_quest_shuffle_candidates` → `choose_quest_graveyard_shuffle`, which drops ids that left the graveyard and de-dupes. Blueleaf Tubers' `shuffle_graveyard_into_deck` is the whole-zone, choiceless flavour; this is `GameLogic.shuffle_cards_into_deck`. Always **available** — "any number" includes zero, so it never knocks out the "choose both" — but an empty graveyard opens no choice point at all. Emits `deck_shuffled` even on an empty pick). UI: one centered popup listing every choice as its own button — each available mode alone, plus, when "both" is allowed and exactly two modes are available, **both ORDERINGS** (`"Shuffle, then draw"` / `"Draw, then shuffle"`), since 707.1c leaves the order to the chooser and it matters: drawing first puts the card in hand before the other mode's picker opens, while shuffling first can put that very card back into the deck. Any other count of available modes falls back to a single "Both" in printed order, and so do the quests listed in `playtest.QUEST_BOTH_DRAW_FIRST` (**Hidden Enemies**), whose "Both" is forced to draw first — a UI simplification, legal only because neither of that card's modes can see what the other did (the ferocity picker reads the BOARD, and nothing can be played between the two modes). Sub-picks via targeting mode (the ferocity / can't-attack grant), red mandatory modes (plague/facedown) or the graveyard browser (Poison Water's multi-select, min 0 / max the whole graveyard — Cancel/Esc is the EMPTY pick, not a decline). AI: `choose_quest_modes` ALWAYS takes both when allowed (special mode first, draw last); single pick prefers the special mode only when useful (`_quest_mode_useful`); the plague AI holds the quest until the opponent has an ally; as Kolkar's target it flips its LEAST valuable quest (rarity, then cost, random tie); Poison Water's shuffle is taken over the draw only once the deck is down to `GRAVEYARD_RECYCLE_DECK_FLOOR` (10) cards, and then takes the WHOLE graveyard back (`choose_quest_graveyard_shuffle`) |
| `combat_damage_in_form_draw:FORM:COST` | **Pay-to-draw when your hero is dealt combat damage in a named form** (Feral Rage `azeroth_21`, 5, Ability — Feral, Druid, Rare): "Ongoing: When your hero is dealt combat damage while in bear form, you may pay (1). If you do, draw a card." Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. Opened at **combat conclusion** (`_maybe_open_feral_rage`, beside the whelp bounce) rather than off the turn event log, because "combat damage" is the whole condition and the log records source/target/amount but **not whether a packet was combat damage** — widening that contract for one card was the alternative. **No role clause**: the card says "is DEALT combat damage", so BOTH packets are scanned and it fires for a hero defending, a hero that stepped in as protector, and an **attacking hero taking the defender's retaliation**. The amount is irrelevant — one hit is one offer, for 1 damage or 10 — but there is **one offer per in-play copy** (the power is on each card, not on the hero), queued in `GameState.pending_feral_rage_queue` and drained one at a time. Damage **prevented in full was never dealt** (717.2b), so a hit fully absorbed by armor triggers nothing. FORM is read through `hero_is_in_form` at the moment of the hit, so **Bash counts as bear form** and Cat Form/Claw do not (see `form_state`); a form broken later does not retract an offer already open. The cost is checked twice — when queueing and again when opening, since an earlier offer in the same queue may have spent the resources — and an unaffordable offer **never opens**. "If you do" is 709.2f: the draw happens only because the payment did. Optional direct-call point (`StackResolver.choose_feral_rage(state, pay, db)`; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending — NOT the chain, see `data/rules_deviations.md`). UI: the whelp bounce's inline pay/decline popup, board-public so any human decides inline. AI (`BaseAI.choose_feral_rage`): **always pays** — the offer only opens on damage already taken, usually on the opponent's turn when the resources would go unspent anyway. Deliberately not gated on hand size (drawing into a full hand wastes the resource at wrap-up, but that needs a full hand AND a bear-form hero under attack) |
| `recursion_on_ally_death_same_name` | **"When an ally is destroyed, its controller may search his deck for an ally card with the same name and put it into play exhausted"** (Circle of Life `azeroth_19`, 8, Ability — Restoration, Druid, Rare). Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. Reads the same **`ally_destroyed`** turn-log entries as `recursion_on_opposing_ally_death` above and sweeps at the same three destruction points (`_fire_circle_of_life`, called beside `_fire_recombobulation`), so combat, damage, destroy effects, sacrifice costs and state-based deaths all feed it by construction. Two things differ from that card. (1) The cursor is **board-global** (`GameState.ally_destroy_watch_index`, Skorn's shape) rather than a per-player grant index: this is a static power on whatever is in play, so one cursor serves every copy — **every in-play Circle fires for an entry** before the cursor advances past it, and a Circle played LATER still advances the cursor when no watcher is out, so it can never fetch retroactively for allies that died before it existed. (2) The trigger is **symmetric and the beneficiary is not the card's controller** — "**ITS** controller" is whoever controlled the dying ally, so a Circle refills the OPPONENT's board off the opponent's deck too. Pool is `get_circle_candidates`: **ally CARDS of that name in that player's own DECK**, asked through the shared `is_ally_card_def`, so a **Totem card counts** (305.3a); the name is snapshotted onto the queue (the corpse may move again before the choice is answered). No "non-token" clause is needed — a token isn't deckable, so its name matches nothing. The replacement **enters play exhausted per 710.1b** — it does not enter ready and then exhaust, so the flag is set before `_bring_ally_into_play` rather than with `exhaust_card` after (the rulebook uses this very card as its 710.1b example); otherwise it is a normal entry, with summoning sickness, on_enter triggers, uniqueness checks and the opposing-ally-enters watchers. **Optional** direct-call choice (`pending_circle_queue`/`pending_circle_player` → `StackResolver.choose_circle_of_life`, `""` declines; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending — NOT the chain, see `data/rules_deviations.md`); declining is legal twice over (the "may", plus **413.3**: a search of a non-public zone for a specified description can fail to find), and the deck is **shuffled either way** (413.2, emits `deck_shuffled`). A death with no matching card in that deck opens no choice at all. UI: the graveyard browser (`_gy_circle_mode`, min 0 / max 1 — Cancel/Esc declines), routed `_route_choice(player, "private")` — unlike the Recombobulation fetch a **deck is hidden information**, so an off-seat hotseat player peeks rather than showing the room. AI (`BaseAI.choose_circle_of_life`): always takes it — the body goes straight into PLAY, so there is no hand-size reason to decline and every candidate is a copy of the same card. Deck thinning is deliberately not modelled |
| `recursion_on_opposing_ally_death:N` | **Delayed quest reward grant** (Operation Recombobulation `dark_portal_292`, Quest, Alliance, **Gnome Hero Required**): "Reward: When an opposing non-token ally is destroyed this turn, you may put an ally card from your graveyard into your hand." Cold Blood's grant shape on a quest: completing it stores an **index** into the turn event log (`PlayerState.recomb_from_index`, -1 = inactive, cleared at every turn start), so the trigger is **forward-looking** as printed — an opposing ally that died earlier this turn is not retroactively fetched — and re-completing a second copy can't rewind past deaths the first already handled. What it reads is the new **`ally_destroyed`** turn-log entry (see `game_logic/turn_state_flags.md`), recorded wherever `GameEvent.card_destroyed` is built (`GameLogic.check_destroyed`, `GameLogic.destroy_card`, the 400.5 attachment sweep), so combat, damage, destroy effects, sacrifice costs and state-based deaths all feed it by construction. `is_ally`, `controller` and `is_token` are **snapshots** — by read time the card is in a graveyard (its zone can no longer say "ally") and a token has gone to RFG. `_fire_recombobulation` sweeps at the three points a destruction can have just happened (the two resolver destroy wrappers plus `_apply_packet_group`, whose non-recursive branch destroys without a wrapper) and advances the index past everything scanned, so **each qualifying death fires exactly once** and several deaths in one event queue one choice each. The fetch is an **optional** direct-call choice (`pending_recomb_queue`/`pending_recomb_player` → `StackResolver.choose_recombobulation`, `""` declines; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending — NOT the chain, see `data/rules_deviations.md`), and a death with no ally card in the completer's graveyard opens no choice at all. Pool is `get_recomb_candidates`: **ally CARDS in the completer's OWN graveyard** — card type, not zone (an ally token never reached a graveyard to begin with), asked through the shared `StackResolver.is_ally_card_def`, so a **Totem card counts** per 305.3a (see the Totems-are-ally-cards note under Card data format). `requires_hero_race:Gnome` is deck legality only (100.2b). UI: the graveyard browser (`_gy_recomb_mode`, min 0 / max 1 — Cancel/Esc declines), routed `_route_choice(player, "public")` since a graveyard is open information. AI (`BaseAI.choose_recombobulation`): the fetch is free, so it takes the most valuable ally card back and declines only on a full hand, where the card would be discarded at wrap-up (503.2a) for nothing |
| `exhaust_allies:N` | Quest completion **cost** paid in exhausted allies (The Love Potion `azeroth_356`, Quest, cost 1): "Exhaust two allies in your party and pay (1) to complete this quest. Reward: Draw a card." The allies are announced WITH the completion (`ally_ids` param, like the graveyard-target rewards) and validated in `_can_use_quest` — N distinct READY cards in the completer's own ally_row (totems included; `get_quest_exhaust_candidates`). They are exhausted in `submit_action` on chain entry alongside the resource cost (rule 412.2, so the same ally can't pay for two announcements), and `retract_last` readies them again — that branch also refunds the quest's RESOURCE cost, which no quest did before. The `_skip_target_check` probe requires N ready allies to exist, so the quest goes dark otherwise. UI: clicking the quest opens a **pre-submission** picker (`InputRouter.start_ally_exhaust_selection` → `ally_exhaust_select_requested` → playtest `_on_ally_exhaust_select_requested`), a centered popup shaped like the armor-prevention one; the board is modal except the candidate allies (`_set_board_block` allowlist), clicking one toggles GREEN (selectable) ⇄ BLUE (selected), Confirm appears only at exactly N picks, and Cancel/Esc leaves the game state byte-identical because nothing was submitted. AI: `BaseAI._choose_quest_exhaust_allies` (overridable hook) spends the allies it would miss least — non-attackers first, then lowest `card_value_score` |
| `turn_start_discard_or_give_control` | At the start of your turn: discard a card, or the opponent gains control of this (Infernal). Queued as a `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS` entry. **The discard choice is made at RESOLUTION, not at announcement** — 707.1 locks in only X, modes and targets, and a discard is none of those, so per 709.2b it is a resolution choice: an instant that DRAWS in response can still save an empty hand. `_resolve_turn_start_trigger` opens the choice (`pending_control_discard_*`), resolved via `choose_control_discard` / `decline_control_discard`. Sacrificing the Infernal in response escapes it entirely — a card in a graveyard has no controller to give, so 709.2c performs only as much as possible, which is nothing. Goes on the chain as a `resolve_turn_start_trigger` link during the ready step and is fully respondable — see the **Start-of-turn triggered effects** section below. |
| `end_of_turn_damage_opposing:AMOUNT:DMG_TYPE` | At the end of your turn, this deals AMOUNT damage to each opposing hero and ally (Infernal). Fires in `TurnManager._apply_end_of_turn_effects` |
| `turn_start_pay_or_destroy:COST` | **UPKEEP** — "At the start of your turn, pay (COST) or destroy this" (Rain of Fire `azeroth_129`, 4, Ability — Destruction, Warlock, Rare). The engine's first recurring cost a card charges its own controller to stay in play. Queued as a `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS` entry, so it goes on the chain as a `resolve_turn_start_trigger` link during the ready step and is fully respondable — see the **Start-of-turn triggered effects** section. **The payment is made at RESOLUTION** (709.2b — 707.1 locks in only X, modes and targets, and a payment is none of those), so resources freed in the response window can still pay it; Infernal's discard is the identical call. From the resolution it opens a direct-call pay/decline point (`pending_upkeep_player`/`_card_id`/`_cost` → `StackResolver.choose_upkeep(state, pay, db)`, NOT the chain; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending) — so no queue is needed even with several copies out, since the trigger queue announces one link at a time and this block stops the next. **Unaffordable is not a choice**: with fewer resources than COST the card is simply destroyed and no point opens at all (Feral Rage's "the offer never opens when it can't be afforded", except that here the failure has a consequence). **709.2c**: a source that left play in the response window has nothing to pay for and nothing to destroy, so sacrificing it in response escapes the upkeep entirely — the same clean escape Infernal has from its control clause. Declining goes through `_destroy_card_trigger`, so `on_destroyed` triggers and the `ally_destroyed` turn-log entry behave as for any destruction and the card reaches its **owner's** graveyard (415.9d). Note the two halves interact: an unpaid upkeep destroys the card at the START of the turn, so it is not in play at the END of it and burns nothing (703.3). UI: the Feral Rage inline pay/decline popup, board-public (spending resources and losing a card in play are both open information). AI (`BaseAI.choose_upkeep`): the decision is about what the card will DO, read live off the OPPONENT's board rather than off its cost — lethal on their hero pays at any price, otherwise the end-of-turn burn must total `UPKEEP_MIN_DAMAGE` (3, i.e. hero + 2 allies at 1 damage) AND leave at least one resource spare, since the upkeep is charged before the action phase |
| `hero_health_bonus:N` | **“Ongoing: Your hero has +N health”** (Last Stand `azeroth_143`, 1, Instant Ability — Protection Talent, Warrior, Rare). Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. The **only health aura that reaches a HERO**: `_aura_health_mods` used to return early for a Hero and every other health aura is explicitly “allies in your party”, so the hero branch answers this key and skips the ally-scoped scans entirely. Controller-scoped (“**your** hero” — the scan reads only that hero's own controller's hero_row, so the opponent's is never touched); stacks per copy; read **live**, never cached. **The consequence is the whole card**: losing the aura shrinks the hero's max health, which can leave an already-damaged hero at 0 or fewer effective health with nothing having been destroyed and no damage dealt — a state-based death (118.4/704) that **ENDS THE GAME**. Heroes are not in the ally_row, so neither death sweep saw them before this card; both now call `_hero_state_based_death`, which emits the `game_over` directly because `GameLogic.check_destroyed` deliberately refuses to move a hero. `_check_aura_loss_deaths` covers the source being destroyed (the upkeep decline), and `drain_state_based_deaths` at the priority gate covers every other way it could leave play (bounce, exile, a control change). Damage is untouched by the bonus — only the ceiling moves |
| `turn_start_discard_or_destroy:N` | **UPKEEP PAID IN CARDS** — “At the start of your turn, discard N cards or destroy this” (Last Stand). Rain of Fire's `turn_start_pay_or_destroy` with the currency changed, so the two share the entire choice point: which one it is rides on **`GameState.pending_upkeep_kind`** (`"resources"` / `"discard"`, where `pending_upkeep_cost` is then a CARD count) and `StackResolver.choose_upkeep(state, pay, db)` is still the one entry point, with the same hard block on `can_submit`/`pass_priority`, the same 709.2b timing (the payment is neither X, a mode nor a target, so it belongs to RESOLUTION — cards drawn in the response window can still pay it) and the same **709.2c** escape (a source sacrificed in the response window has nothing to pay for and nothing to destroy). **Paying opens the ordinary pending discard** (the Mias the Putrid machinery), so WHICH cards go is a second, separate choice made after the decision to pay — and the source survives once the cost is committed. **Unaffordable is not a choice**: with fewer cards in hand than N the card is simply destroyed and no point opens at all; the hand cannot change in between, since the point hard-blocks priority. Declining goes through `_destroy_card_trigger`, so `on_destroyed` triggers and the `ally_destroyed` turn-log entry behave as for any destruction. UI: the same inline pay/decline popup, worded from the event's `kind`. AI (`BaseAI.choose_upkeep`): a `hero_health_bonus` branch checked BEFORE the burn branch, because it is existential — pay at any price when dropping the aura would be an immediate loss, keep paying while the hero would sit inside `FREE_BLOCK_HERO_FLOOR` without it, and stop once it is comfortably clear, since N cards a turn is far too steep a rent for health you are not using |
| `graveyard_turn_start_pay_to_hand:COST` | **A power that works from your GRAVEYARD** (Wisp `dark_portal_197`, 1-cost 0/1 Alliance Ally — Wisp, Uncommon): "At the start of your turn, if Wisp is in your graveyard, you may pay (COST). If you do, put Wisp into your hand." The engine's first power that functions anywhere other than in play, and it is legal because **703.3a** is an explicit exception to 703.3: "a card's power that can trigger only while that card is in a non-play zone is active only while that card is revealed in that zone." So the trigger is collected from the turn player's **graveyard** rather than from the board — which is the ONLY structural difference; everything downstream is shared with the in-play start-of-turn triggers (a `resolve_turn_start_trigger` chain link, added in the same 708.1a PPP, fully respondable, 707.3-correct). Which zone a key is scanned in is decided by which of `TurnManager`'s three trigger lists it sits on (`GRAVEYARD_TURN_TRIGGERS` here), and that same membership drives the still-active re-check at announcement (`StackResolver._trigger_source_active`), so an in-play copy can never fire a graveyard power and vice versa. A graveyard card has no controller (401.2) and is only ever in its **OWNER's** graveyard (415.2), so "your turn" and "your graveyard" both read `card.owner`. **The payment is a 709.2b RESOLUTION choice** (707.1 locks in only X, modes and targets, and a payment is none of those), so it opens from the LINK resolving rather than when the trigger fires — resources freed inside the response window can still pay it, Infernal's discard being the identical call. **Unaffordable opens no point at all** (Feral Rage's rule), and **709.2c**: a Wisp exiled out of the graveyard in the response window (Cannibalize, Ophelia) has nothing to return, so the link resolves and only as much as possible happens — nothing, with the resources unspent. Nothing is announced (no target, no choice), so **706 Untargetable is irrelevant** and it can never fizzle for want of a target. **Declining is FREE and non-destructive**, which is the whole difference from Rain of Fire's `turn_start_pay_or_destroy` upkeep: the card stays put and the offer comes back **every single turn** for as long as it sits in the graveyard, so a 1-cost 0/1 chump blocker / sacrifice fodder / Love Potion payment never runs out. Direct-call point (`pending_gy_return_*` → `StackResolver.choose_gy_return(state, pay, db)`, NOT the chain; `can_submit` / `pass_priority` hard-block while pending), UI = the Feral Rage inline pay/decline popup, board-public since a graveyard is open information. AI (`BaseAI.choose_gy_return`): the body is cheap and cannot be wasted the way a draw can, so it declines only on the two provable wastes — a full hand (the card would be discarded at wrap-up, 503.2a) and resources it still needs, the offer resolving in the READY step before the whole action phase, hence a spare-resource floor rather than mere affordability |
| `turn_start_destroy_self` | **A card that destroys itself at the start of your turn** (Shadowmeld `dark_portal_131`, 3, Ability, Alliance, **Night Elf Hero Required**, Uncommon): "Ongoing: Your hero is elusive and untargetable. At the start of your turn, destroy Shadowmeld." Fire Nova Totem's `turn_start_destroy_self_damage_opposing` with the burn rider removed, so what is left is a pure **EXPIRY**: the card pays for its own ongoing grant by leaving play, and the key carries no arguments at all. A `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS` entry, so it goes on the chain as a `resolve_turn_start_trigger` link during the ready step and is **fully respondable** (see the Start-of-turn triggered effects section) — the destroy belongs to **RESOLUTION**, so the grant is still up while the link waits and the opponent gets one window inside it. Mandatory, free, targetless and choiceless, so nothing is announced and **706 Untargetable is irrelevant**. Per **709.2c** a source already gone when the link resolves has nothing to destroy and the link resolves into nothing — but the grant lifted anyway, since every aura read is live off the board, so bouncing or exiling it in the window buys the opponent nothing that killing it wouldn't. The destroy goes through `_destroy_card_trigger`, so `on_destroyed` triggers and the `ally_destroyed` turn-log entry behave as for any destruction, and the card reaches its **owner's** graveyard (415.9d). **The timing is what makes the card work**: on a sorcery-speed Ability it is played on your own turn and dies at your NEXT ready step, before your action phase — so what it actually buys is the OPPONENT's whole turn under the grant, and never a moment of your own. No AI modeling |

If you do, it deals 3 fire damage to each opposing hero and ally." Infernal's `end_of_turn_damage_opposing` sweep moved onto the **start-of-turn trigger queue** and paid for with the source itself — a one-shot **FUSE**, not a repeating ping like every other totem power. A `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS` entry, so it goes on the chain as a `resolve_turn_start_trigger` link during the ready step and is **fully respondable** (see the Start-of-turn triggered effects section) — which is the card's whole shape: the destroy belongs to **RESOLUTION**, so the opponent gets exactly one window to answer it. **709.2f gates the burn on the destroy having actually been PERFORMED** (Grunt Baranka's rule, checked against a real `card_destroyed` event for the source, not against it merely being gone), and that does real work in both directions — a source killed in the response window destroys nothing and therefore deals nothing (709.2c), and so does one whose destruction was **replaced** by a removal from the game, so bouncing or exiling it answers the card as well as killing it. The damage is dealt by the **TOTEM** ("**it** deals"), not by the controller's hero, so packets are NOT tagged `from_ability` (no Chromatic Cloak) and **World in Flames' fire doubling doesn't see them either** — that keys on the hero being the source; Rain of Fire's key is the hero-sourced flavour. They carry the printed `dmg_type` and go through `defer_packets`, so armor prevention opens normally in a fixed order (opposing allies first, then their hero). The board is read at **RESOLUTION**, so an ally that arrived in the response window is hit and one that left is not; the controller's own side is never touched ("each **opposing** hero and ally"). Non-targeted, so nothing is announced and **706 Untargetable is irrelevant**. The destroy goes through `_destroy_card_trigger`, so `on_destroyed` triggers and the `ally_destroyed` turn-log entry behave as for any destruction. No AI modeling |
| `end_of_turn_hero_damage_opposing:AMOUNT:DMG_TYPE` | "At the end of your turn, **your hero** deals AMOUNT DMG_TYPE damage to each opposing hero and ally" (Rain of Fire). Infernal's `end_of_turn_damage_opposing` with the SOURCE changed, and the source is the entire difference between the two keys: the packets come from the controller's **HERO** and are tagged **`from_ability`** (an in-play Ability is dealing the damage — Fireball's ongoing turn-start burn is the precedent), so **Chromatic Cloak's +1 applies**, and they carry the printed `dmg_type`, so a fire version also **doubles under World in Flames**. Infernal's packets are dealt by the ALLY and get neither. Same turn-player-scoped sweep (`TurnManager._apply_end_of_turn_effects` — "at the end of YOUR turn", so **not** Thysta's each-player sweep), same `defer_packets` route, so armor prevention opens normally in a fixed order (opposing allies first, then their hero). The board is read at the end phase, so an ally that arrived this turn is hit and one that left is not; **703.3** needs no code, since the sweep only visits `cards_in_play` |
| `end_of_turn_damage_own_victims:AMOUNT:DMG_TYPE` | "At the end of **each turn**, this deals AMOUNT DMG_TYPE damage to **each hero and ally it dealt damage to this turn**" (Venomstrike `dark_portal_41`, 4-cost 1/5 Scorpid **Pet**, Hunter). Thysta's each-player sweep (`_apply_each_turn_end_effects` — **new each-player end triggers go there**) with the condition turned into a **victim list**: instead of asking whether the turn log holds any `damage_dealt` entry, it reads the entries whose `source_id` is this card and burns each distinct target. So it covers combat damage, ability and power damage, and any future way the card deals damage, by construction — and "this turn" is free, the log being cleared at every turn start. Victims are **de-duplicated** (damaging one ally three times still burns it once) and hit in first-damaged order as ONE deferred group, so several armor prevention offers open in a fixed order. **Rule 703.3 is the whole card**: a triggered power is active only while its card is face up in play, and the sweep only visits `cards_in_play`, so a Venomstrike destroyed earlier in the turn burns nothing — killing it before the end phase is the answer. Once the burn HAS gone out, 707.3 applies as usual and killing it changes nothing. **408.2b**: a victim no longer in play gets no packet (an ally it killed in combat isn't burned again), and one dead victim doesn't cancel the rest. Not restricted to opposing characters ("each hero and ally") — damage your own side and you burn your own side; the **amount is flat**, not the damage dealt, so a 1-point poke sets up a 4-point hit. Copies never cross-feed: the filter is `source_id`, not the card name. NOT tagged `from_ability` (an ally's power is not an ability), and it resolves **inline**, not on the chain — mandatory, free, no target, no choice (same deviation as Thysta, see `data/rules_deviations.md`). No AI modeling |

So combat damage, ability and power damage, and any future way the card deals damage all save it by construction, and "this turn" is free (the log is cleared at every turn start). **"YOUR turn", so the arm lives in `_apply_end_of_turn_effects`** (Infernal's turn-player sweep), NOT Thysta's each-player one — damage dealt on the opponent's turn (retaliating as a defender) cannot save it, since the log is cleared before its own end phase reads it. Damage **prevented in full was never dealt** (717.2b), so a swing entirely absorbed by armor doesn't save it; 405.2 overkill counts in full. Not restricted to opposing characters or to combat — damaging your own side counts. **Rule 703.3** needs no code: the sweep only visits `cards_in_play`, so a card already destroyed, bounced or exiled this turn is never asked. The destroy goes through `_destroy_card_trigger`, so `on_destroyed` triggers and the `ally_destroyed` turn-log entry behave as for any destruction, and it resolves **inline**, not on the chain — mandatory, free, no target, no choice (same deviation as Thysta / Venomstrike, see `data/rules_deviations.md`). AI: `BaseAI.use_it_or_lose_it_attack_action`, called last in every `decide_action` (immediately before `forced_attack_action`, so every voluntary line has had its say) — on our own turn only, and only while it has dealt no damage yet, it proposes `_least_bad_attack` (the ranking shared with `forced_attack_action`: a kill we survive, else a trade, else their hero, else anything legal). Losing the card for nothing is the alternative, so even a bad attack beats no attack |

"That player" is the **turn player**, so on her controller's own idle turn she burns their own hero; the clock is symmetric. The condition reads the **turn event log** (`GameState.turn_events` / `has_turn_event("damage_dealt")` — see `game_logic/turn_state_flags.md`), recorded in `GameLogic.deal_damage` (its only write site, so every damage source in the game feeds it by construction) and cleared at each turn start: ANY damage, any source, any character, either side. Recorded AFTER prevention, so damage absorbed in full doesn't count (717.2b, matching whelp bounce / `discard_per_damage`); `put_damage` self-costs (405.3) never record. The log is kept **unconditionally, in every game** — the condition is retroactive, and a Thysta entering play mid-turn must still see damage dealt before she arrived, which nothing on the board records. The condition is **sampled once** before the sweep, so two copies both fire rather than the first silencing the second. Damage goes through `defer_packets` (armor prevention point preserved); it resolves **inline**, not on the chain — mandatory, free, no target, no choice (deviation, see `data/rules_deviations.md` "Thysta Spiritlasher"). No AI modeling |

Instant speed. Key timing (601.3 vs 602.1/602.4): played in RESPONSE to a combat proposal still on the chain, the proposal's legality recheck interrupts it — combat never starts and the attacker never exhausts. Played during an attack/defend window it's too late — "can't attack" is not a remove-from-combat effect; the current combat proceeds. AI ignores the rider (still tagged `combat_instant_dmg`) — see `data/rules_deviations.md` |
| `cannot_attack_source` (rider) | **"Can't attack YOUR HERO this turn" — a PARTIAL attack lock** (Wing Clip `dark_portal_42`, 1, Instant Ability — Survival, Hunter, Common: "Target hero or ally can't attack your hero this turn. Your hero deals 1 melee damage to that character" = `deal_damage_to_target:1:melee:cannot_attack_source`). A restriction rider on Frostbolt's existing 4th field, and the **fifth** member of the compulsion/restriction family — see the **Compulsion / taunt families** section. Every other restriction either removes attacking outright (`cannot_attack`) or NARROWS the legal defenders to a set (`can_attack_only:<id>`, `sarmoth_taunt`); this one **SUBTRACTS one character** from the pool, so the target may still attack the caster's ALLIES. It rides as a `cannot_attack:<id>` restriction Buff with the id IN the stat name (Mocking Blow's trick — `Buff.amount` is an int and what this must carry is an instance id), duration `turns`:1, so the end-of-turn sweep gives "this turn" for free and leaving play clears it; on every path that reaches the rider the packet's `source` IS the caster's hero ("**your** hero deals…"), so nothing extra is announced. **`StackResolver._attacker_barred_from` is the ONE read**, and it has two call sites, which is the whole card: `get_legal_defenders` (subtracted **before** the tax filter and the 601.2c narrowing, so a **"can't" beats a "can only"** — a narrowing onto a barred character comes out empty and 601.2c's own fallback hands back the rest of the list) and the **601.3 re-check** in `_resolve_propose_combat`, which makes it an **INTERRUPT**: cast in response to a proposal naming the caster's hero, that defender is no longer legal when the proposal resolves, so **combat never starts and the attacker never exhausts** — Litori Frostburn's timing, narrowed to one defender instead of all of them. A proposal aimed at one of the caster's ALLIES is therefore NOT interrupted. **With no allies of your own in play the lock is total**, since the hero was the only legal defender. Once the attack window is open it is too late (602.1 — the attacker is already attacking). The damage half is ordinary: hero-sourced and `from_ability` (Chromatic Cloak applies), **melee** (Aspect of the Hawk does not), through `defer_packets`. Riders land only on a **surviving** target, which is unobservable here — a dead character can't attack either way. AI: tagged `combat_instant_wing_clip` (held, never blind-played) with `BaseAI.wing_clip_action` — fired only on an opposing `propose_combat` naming **our own hero**, when the attacker has 4+ ATK or would drop the hero, and held when a damage instant could just kill the attacker instead |
| `damage_unpreventable` | **"That damage can't be prevented" printed on the SPELL** (Chastise `azeroth_76`, 2, Ability — Holy, Priest, Uncommon: "Your hero deals 2 holy damage to target hero or ally. That damage can't be prevented" = `deal_damage_to_target:2:holy|damage_unpreventable`). A bare **rider segment** on a `deal_damage_to_target` play, not a target kind or a new effect key — the targeting, the 706/4217 resolution re-check, the packet pipeline and the AI's damage math are all inherited unchanged. What is new is WHERE unpreventability lives: every earlier case is **derived from the SOURCE** (`GameLogic.is_damage_unpreventable` — Lionheart Helm's aura, Annihilator's weapon clause), while this one is a property of the damage itself, so it rides the **packet** as an `unpreventable` field. Honoured in exactly two places, which is what keeps them from disagreeing: `_prevention_offer` (the armor point never opens — an offer that can't help is never offered, so the defender's armor stays READY) and `_apply_packet_group`, which passes it into `GameLogic.deal_damage`'s opts. Set **only when true** — `prevent` derives the flag from the source when the opt is absent, so an explicit false would silently override Lionheart Helm. Per **717** it short-circuits before every shield and **consumes none of them**: an armor pool, Brother Rhone's shield, Bestial Wrath's `prevent_all_damage`, Katsin's combat shield and Soul Link's counted pool are all bypassed intact. **Replacement effects are not prevention and still apply** — Chromatic Cloak's `from_ability` +1, a typed hero bonus and World in Flames' doubling are all computed in `defer_packets` before any of this. AI: no new modeling (the damage plays like any burn), but `BaseAI._forecast_hero_damage` had to be taught it — that read asked `is_damage_unpreventable` of the SOURCE, so without the flag check Soul Link would bank a shield against a packet it can never absorb |
| `target_attacker` + `require_hero_defending` | **Damage aimed at whoever is attacking, but only while your hero defends** (Point Blank `dark_portal_37`, 2, Instant Ability — Marksmanship, Hunter, Common: "If your hero is defending, it deals 3 ranged damage to target attacker" = `deal_damage_to_target:3:ranged|target_attacker|require_hero_defending`). Two **riders** on Quick Strike's damage effect rather than a new key, so the packet pipeline, the 706/4217 re-check, the replacement effects and the AI's damage math are all inherited. (1) **`target_attacker`** narrows the pool to the character currently attacking — per **602.1** a proposed attacker *becomes* an attacker as the combat step starts, so the pool is non-empty from the **attack** window onward, and in this engine's 1-on-1 model it is the single `combat_attacker`. It is a real **target**, so it is announced, 706 Untargetable applies, and it is re-checked at resolution: something that walked out of combat in the response window (Avanthera, Blink) is no longer an attacker and the spell fizzles. The highlight probe darks the card whenever nobody is attacking (706.2). (2) **`require_hero_defending`** is Blink's condition, and **the important thing about it is that it is an EFFECT condition, not a use restriction**: per **602.3** defending exists only after the protect point, so it is true only while the **defend window** is open with the caster's own hero as the settled defender — a hero that stepped in as **protector** qualifies (602.2 → 602.3), one merely *proposed* as defender during the attack window does not. It is checked at **RESOLUTION only** (709.2c — "if some of the link is impossible to perform, only as much as possible is performed", here nothing), so the card is **perfectly legal to play anywhere** and simply fizzles, card spent. That is deliberate and is the rule 707.1 reading: the announcement sequence checks modes, X, targets and costs and never asks whether an effect will accomplish anything — and a deliberately fizzling play can still matter to a future "when you play a <class> ability" payoff. Gating the announcement instead would be an engine-only use restriction the card doesn't print, the exact mistake `data/rules_deviations.md` records under the RESOLVED For the Horde! / Rayder / Ryn Dreamstrider entry; the convenience half lives in the **`mute_when:hero_not_defending`** token instead. `StackResolver.hero_defending_condition_ok` is the ONE read, shared by the resolution, the mute token's twin and the AI gate, so they cannot disagree. The **HERO** is the source as printed, so the packet is tagged `from_ability` (**Chromatic Cloak's +1**) and carries `ranged` (**Aspect of the Hawk's +1**); it goes through `defer_packets`, so armor prevention opens normally. AI: tagged `combat_instant_dmg` (held, never blind-played), with `combat_instant_action` gated on `hero_defending_condition_ok` — so it is never spent in the attack window, where it would resolve into nothing — and then played on the shared damage-instant math (the 3 must convert the trade into a kill, and the attacker must cost at least as much as the spell) |
| `on_play_ability_heal:TAG:N` | **"When you play a TAG ability, your hero heals N damage from target hero or ally"** (Spiritual Healing `azeroth_90`, 2, Ability — Holy Talent, Priest, Rare). Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. The engine's first **PLAY trigger**, and the third member of the trigger-queue family after start-of-turn and combat — see the **Play-triggered effects** section. **The timing is the card**: per 412.1a a card is PLAYED as it is ANNOUNCED onto the chain, and per 708.1 the effect goes on the chain in that same PPP, i.e. **on top of the card that triggered it** — so the heal **resolves FIRST** (play Smite, heal before the damage) and it survives that card being **interrupted** (711) or fizzling, the effect existing independently of what created it. `TAG` is matched as a **substring** of the played card's subtype/tags, the convention `form_break` / `gy_tag` / `ability_cost_mod_by_tag` already use, so "Holy" matches a **Holy Talent** card and a second Spiritual Healing therefore triggers the first — correct, the card says "a Holy ability" with no exclusion. The registry `PLAY_TRIGGERS` carries the **card-TYPE gate** (`Ability`), so a Holy-tagged ALLY or equipment never triggers it. Controller-scoped ("when **YOU** play"), read live off the board, and **one trigger per copy** in play — they queue and are drained one at a time, each announcing its own target. The target is the ordinary hero-or-ally pool (`get_turn_start_trigger_targets`), **either party's**, so a human may repair the opponent's board; it is announced at 707.1d through the **shared** choice point (`pending_trigger_target_player` + the new `pending_trigger_kind`, the `pending_attack_exhaust_kind` pattern), which is what let it reuse the totem trigger's whole UI flow with no new router code. Re-checked at resolution (706/709.2a — a target that left play or became Untargetable fizzles the link), while per **707.3** the SOURCE is not: destroying the watcher in the response window does not stop the heal. An **undamaged target is a legal no-op** (`GameLogic.heal` emits nothing), so the link can never fizzle for want of something to do. AI: the scene's generic trigger-target branch is **opponent-only**, which is exactly backwards for a heal, so `_handle_trigger_target` gained a friendly arm — our own most-damaged character, hero winning a tie |
| `on_play_ability_ranged_atk:CLASS:N` | **Play trigger watching a CLASS ICON** (Lok'delar, Stave of the Ancient Keepers `dark_portal_279`, 2, Two-Handed Weapon — Staff, Melee (1), 1 ATK / 2 Strike, Hunter, Rare: "When you play a <Hunter> ability, your Ranged weapons have +1 ATK this turn"). The second member of the **Play-triggered effects** family after Spiritual Healing, so per 412.1a a card is PLAYED as it is ANNOUNCED and per 708.1 the effect goes on the chain in that same PPP — **on top of the ability that triggered it**, and therefore resolving FIRST and surviving that ability being interrupted (711) or fizzling. What is new is the **matched axis**: field 1 is a **CLASS**, not a type-line tag, which is why `PLAY_TRIGGERS` entries now carry a `match` key (`"tag"` by default, `"class"` here) read by `_play_trigger_watches`. That is a **parsed** read, never a substring one — a single-class card stores the full name (`Hunter`) while a multi-class card stores concatenated abbreviations (`DrHuRo`), and both must count — so the format moved onto **`CardDef.parse_class_restriction` / `CardDef.has_class`**, with `DeckManager` delegating to it: the card DATA owns its own format, and the rules engine no longer needs the deck loader. A card with **no** class restriction belongs to no class, so a neutral ability never triggers it. The registry's `card_type` gate is `Ability`, so an **Instant Ability counts** (it parses to `Ability`) while a Hunter **ally** or **equipment** never does. The effect is **Elendril's flip grant reused verbatim** — `PlayerState.ranged_weapon_atk_bonus`, applied to that player's Ranged weapons in `GameState.get_atk` (2b) and cleared at every turn start, so "this turn" is free and correct even when the ability is played on the opponent's turn. Nothing is announced (no target, no choice), so **706 is irrelevant and it can never fizzle**; per **707.3** the source is not re-checked, so destroying the stave in the response window does **not** take the bonus back. Grants **stack**: each qualifying ability adds another +1, and a second copy queues its own trigger. **The card's own joke is worth knowing**: it is a MELEE weapon that boosts RANGED ones, and that combination is legal only because Ranged weapons cost **0 hands** in the wielding model — a 2-handed staff and a bow coexist, while Two-Handed still locks out Off-Hand (414.3c). It never boosts itself. No AI modeling |

(1) The race is matched as a **substring of the recipient's `tags` OR `card_subtype`**, because the two CSV conventions differ: a real ally carries "Dwarf Warrior" in the **tags** column while a TOKEN carries it in **subtype**. Reading both is what makes Magni's own tokens qualify, and it is the same double read `_play_trigger_watches` does. (2) **"In your party" is the hero AND the ally row** (the `heal_party` / `buff_while_party_size` convention), so a **DWARF HERO is granted it too** — with Magni out, Boris Brightbeard himself becomes a legal protector. The **source is self-inclusive** when it shares the race, which Magni does. Controller-scoped, so an opposing Dwarf is never granted anything; read live, so it covers allies arriving after the aura and lifts the instant the source leaves play. Protector needed no combat code — `get_legal_protectors` already asks `_has_keyword` of every card in both rows, the hero instance included — and 602.2's other gates still apply (an **exhausted** card can't protect, and 602.2b stops the proposed defender protecting itself) |
| `end_of_turn_create_token:TOKEN_DEF_ID[:N]` | **A token at the end of EACH turn** (King Magni Bronzebeard). The token enters the SOURCE's controller's party whoever's turn it was ("into play", with no other player named), through the shared `StackResolver.put_token_into_play` → `_bring_ally_into_play`, so summoning sickness, the uniqueness queue and the opposing-ally-enters watchers (Stone Guard Rashun) all apply; arriving in the END phase it is summoning-sick either way and can attack on its controller's next turn. **Rule 703.3** needs no code — the sweep only visits `cards_in_play`, so a source destroyed earlier in the turn makes nothing, and killing it is the answer to the engine. Highlight probes check affordability at X=1. UI: clicking the card opens the Boris-style X dialog (`x_select_requested`), then the normal targeting flow with X riding on `_instant_params`. Quick Strike's `deal_damage_to_target` with the amount turned into a **live board read** — Skewer's trick, but off a WEAPON rather than a chosen ally: `BASE` is the flat part and the bonus is **`StackResolver.best_melee_weapon_atk`**, the one read the resolution, the AI's lethal math and the UI's targeting cursor all ask, so the three can never disagree about what the spell deals. Pool is the caster's own hero_row filtered by `_is_melee_weapon` (Equipment + a `strike_cost:` segment + `dmg_type` Melee), so a **Ranged** weapon and an **Item** add nothing. **Ready state is deliberately not checked** — the card says "one of your Melee weapons", not a ready or struck one, so an exhausted weapon still supplies its ATK (this is not a strike; 303.2b never enters into it). WHICH weapon is a choice the printed card leaves open, and the **best is taken automatically** rather than opening a choice point — picking a weaker one is never advantageous (same reasoning as Augustus' auto-chosen `rfg_allies`, see `data/rules_deviations.md`). Enforced in **`GameLogic.heal`**, the ONE choke point every heal in the game goes through (`GameLogic.is_heal_blocked`) — abilities, activated powers, enter-play triggers, party sweeps, attachment and turn-start triggers alike — so a new healing effect respects the lock by construction. The heal is **not reduced or redirected: it simply does not happen**, emitting no event at all, so "if you do" style riders hanging off a heal never fire either. A **separate grammar from `mode:`**, deliberately: a single-choice modal announces ONE mode as an int and every reader of `selected_mode` assumes that (the AI's chain scanners, Escape Artist's interrupt plumbing), and no `mode:` segment carries a per-mode **availability CONDITION**, which is what putting the element IN the segment buys. Paired with `requires_hero_race:Troll` (deck legality only, 100.2b). His 0 printed ATK is no gate — the ATK>0 attacker check applies to **heroes** only, so he may be proposed as an attacker on an undamaged board, it is just usually pointless. No AI modeling beyond the shared `get_atk` reads |

A **combat trigger** (602.1) — the power triggers as the combat step starts and the effect goes on the chain as the attack window opens, so it is fully respondable; see the **Combat-step triggered effects** section. Registered `{"moment": "attack", "scope": "self"}`, i.e. read off the **attacker's own def**, so an attacking hero or a different ally never fires it. "Each player" resolves in turn order (attacker's controller first, then the opponent) and the draws go through `GameLogic.draw_one`, so the decked rule (410.6b) applies — **attacking with Morik on an empty deck loses you the game**. Per 707.3 the source is not re-checked, so killing Morik in the response window does NOT stop the draws. Both players hold the drawn card during the attack/defend windows and may play it there. No AI modeling — played as a vanilla ferocity 2/2, the symmetric draw being roughly neutral |

Non-targeted, so nothing is announced and **706 Untargetable is irrelevant**. It is a respondable chain link like every other combat trigger (see the **Combat-step triggered effects** section), and the board is read **LIVE at resolution** — an ally that arrived in the response window is hit, one that left is not — controller's side first so sequential armor prevention offers open in a fixed order. Per **707.3** killing her in that window does NOT stop the burn (and by then she is simply not in play to be excluded). The source is the **ALLY**, so packets are not tagged `from_ability` (no Chromatic Cloak) and World in Flames' fire doubling doesn't see them either (that keys on the hero being the source); they carry the segment's `dmg_type` and go through `defer_packets`, so armor prevention opens normally. **The symmetry is the cost**: swinging with her chips your own hero and party, and at 2 health she dies to a second copy or to any 2-damage sweep. No AI modeling — played as a vanilla 2/2 |
| `on_defend_vs_ally_reflect_damage:DMG_TYPE` | **Defend trigger that dumps its own damage back onto the attacker** (Plagueborn Meatwall `dark_portal_228`, 5-cost 0/6 Horde Undead Abomination, Protector): "When Plagueborn Meatwall defends against an ally, remove all damage from Plagueborn Meatwall, and he deals that much melee damage to each attacking ally." Grunt Baranka's defend-side combat trigger (`{"moment": "defend", "scope": "self"}`, with the same "against an **ALLY**" clause in `_combat_trigger_watches` — an attacking HERO never triggers it) with the self-trade swapped for a reflect, so it inherits that card's whole framework: it fires at **602.3** as the proposed defender BECOMES the defender — covering both being attacked directly and stepping in with his own **Protector** — and the effect it creates is a respondable **chain link**, not an inline resolution (see the **Combat-step triggered effects** section). **The timing is the entire card.** The link resolves INSIDE the defend window, so the reflect lands **before the combat conclusion**: an attacking ally killed by it is out of play by the time `_do_combat_conclusion` runs, which then cancels under **603.1b** and deals no combat damage in either direction — the wall takes no retaliation at all. (His printed ATK is 0, so the reflect is his only offense either way.) **X is the damage actually REMOVED**, read at RESOLUTION (709.2b): damage dealt to him in the response window makes the reflect BIGGER, an undamaged wall reflects nothing (408.2a — a packet of 0 or less is never created), and per **709.2c** a wall killed in the window removes nothing and so deals nothing.
