"Each attacking ally" is read LIVE off `_attacking_ally_ids` (one card in this engine's single-attacker model, a list because the text says "each"). The source is the **ALLY**, so packets are not tagged `from_ability` (no Chromatic Cloak) and carry the segment's `dmg_type`; they go through `defer_packets` like any other damage. AI: no new hook, but `BaseAI.combat_kills` — the shared "does this combat remove that card?" predicate behind `find_safe_lethals` and `combat_trade_value` — was taught the reflect (defending only, ally attackers only, `damage_taken >= the attacker's current HP`), so **every** protector and trade decision counts a damaged wall as lethal. Per **707.3** the source is not re-checked (destroying the card in the response window does not stop the heal), which is why the hero is re-read from `PlayerState` rather than off the source card; an **undamaged hero is a legal no-op** (`GameLogic.heal` emits nothing) and the link is queued anyway, and a `cannot_be_healed` lock (Mortal Strike) empties it |

Per **707.3** the source is not re-checked — destroying the breastplate in the response window does not stop the heal — and the hero is re-read from `PlayerState` rather than off the source card for that reason. The engine's first **defend-side** combat trigger, and the rulebook's own worked example for combat triggers on the chain (602.3) — see the **Combat-step triggered effects** section. Direct-call choice (`pending_attack_exhaust_*`, `StackResolver.choose_attack_exhaust(state, target_id, db)`, `""` = decline; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending) — not on the chain, see `data/rules_deviations.md` "Attack-exhaust triggers". Pool = `_is_armor_equipment` over `get_destroy_kind_candidates(..., "equipment")`: an in-play Equipment whose `card_subtype` is one of `ARMOR_SUBTYPES` (cloth/leather/mail/plate/shield — rule 304, the printed type line reads "Armor — <subtype>"), 706-legal. A **weapon** (Sword, Axe…) and an **Item** (Ramstein's Lightning Bolts) are Equipment but are NOT armor. Either player's armor is legal ("target armor" with no restriction), so a human may exhaust their own. The point is what makes the card: it fires BEFORE the attack window, so exhausting the defender's armor spends its DEF and the **prevention point never opens** at the conclusion (717.2c — only READY DEF>0 equipment is offered, `get_ready_def_armor`), and the hit lands in full. Optional `exhaust_it` rider (Entangling Roots: "…and exhaust it") exhausts the host as part of the resolution. **Host leaves play → attachment destroyed by the game** (400.5/410.6c) — handled generically in `GameLogic.move_card` (any move of a host to a non-play zone destroys its attachments; moves within play, e.g. control change, keep them attached). They live on the **ATTACHMENT**, never on the host (the host's own counters are its business), which is what the countdown trigger reads and what the badge shows. Unlike `enters_with_counters` the amount is always a literal — there is no X-cost attachment — so no `x_value` is threaded through. The HOST is locked in at announcement (709.2d), so bouncing the attachment in the response window still burns what it was attached to — the rulebook's own Kryton Barleybeard example. Covers every source by construction: `GameLogic.prevent` derives it from the source whenever a caller doesn't pass an explicit flag. | `prevent_combat_damage_from_attacking_allies` | Self-referential prevention shield (Brother Rhone `dark_portal_157`, 0/1 Alliance Dwarf Paladin, Protector): "Prevent all combat damage that would be dealt to Brother Rhone by attacking allies." Unlike armor this is automatic and free — no decision point, no cost, and it protects an ALLY rather than a hero. Applied in `GameLogic.prevent` off the TARGET's own def, gated on the packet's `combat_attack` opt (set only for the attacker→defender packet of `_do_combat_conclusion`), and on the source being an ally_row card: an attacking HERO's combat damage lands, a defender's retaliation is unaffected, and non-combat ally damage (abilities, activated powers) lands. Unpreventable damage bypasses the shield without consuming it. AI, three parts, all driven by the pure probe `GameLogic.blocks_all_combat_damage` (so a future shield card inherits every one of them): (1) **defence** — `BaseAI.blocks_for_free` makes a free block a protector candidate in EVERY branch of `GenericAI.choose_protector` (and short-circuits `BaseAI.choose_protector`), ranked ahead of spending fodder but behind a `safe_lethal` block, which removes a card rather than stopping one attack. (2) **bait resistance** — protecting EXHAUSTS the protector (602.2), so the free block is once per turn and a cheap attacker can bait it away. `BaseAI.free_block_worth_spending` holds it back only when the opponent has another ready **ally** attacker (a hero attacker couldn't be blocked for free either) with strictly greater forecast ATK, AND the current attack is recoverable — kills nothing of ours and leaves the hero above `FREE_BLOCK_HERO_FLOOR`. A dead ally is permanent; an unblocked hit is not. (3) **offence** — `get_reasonable_actions` never proposes an ALLY attack against a defender that prevents all of its damage: no damage, no kill, and the defender doesn't even exhaust, so there is nothing to weigh. Source-dependent, so the HERO attacking Rhone (0/1, shield is ally-only) stays offered, as does an ally attacking *around* him — forcing the protect decision is exactly how the block gets stripped |

Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. The engine's **first modifier to armor DEF**, which is what introduced `StackResolver.get_armor_def(state, card_id, db)` — the ONE place an in-play equipment's DEF is read (printed value + auras). **Route every new DEF reader through it**: the prevention pool gate (`get_ready_def_armor`), the amount actually prevented (`choose_prevention`), `BaseAI.choose_prevention`, GenericAI's opposing-block forecast, Gartok's `_best_attack_exhaust_armor` and the playtest button label all ask it, so they can never disagree about what a piece of armor is worth. Controller-scoped ("**your** armor" — the scan reads only the armor's own controller's hero_row, so the opponent's is never touched) and restricted to **ARMOR** via `_is_armor_equipment` (Gartok's `ARMOR_SUBTYPES` read): a **weapon** and an **Item** are Equipment but not armor, so neither is lifted off DEF 0 into the shielder pool. The form gate is `hero_is_in_form` read **live**, so it is the form's NAME (`form_state`) that matters, not the grant that form happens to carry — breaking the form drops the bonus with no bookkeeping, and shapeshifting into it switches the bonus on mid-combat. Stacks per copy; floored at 0. **The consequence worth knowing: it lifts a DEF 0 armor (Mooncloth Robe, Chromatic Cloak) to DEF 1, making it an exhaustable shielder for the first time** — `get_ready_def_armor`'s "> 0" gate reads `get_armor_def`, so that falls out rather than being special-cased. Stacks per copy. The type line carries no "(N)", so rule 414.3b tag uniqueness does **not** apply: any number of Shouts may be in play at once. No AI modeling needed — every forecast path (`forecast_atk`, `combat_trade_value`, `find_safe_lethals`) reads `get_atk` live |
| `opposing_allies_atk_mod:N` | **Opponent-scoped ally ATK aura** — "Ongoing: Opposing allies have -N ATK" (Demoralizing Shout `azeroth_140`, 3, Instant Ability — Fury Shout, Warrior). Battle Shout's mirror, and **Hootie's `opposing_characters_atk_mod` narrowed to allies**: read in the same `_opposing_atk_aura` scan (every player EXCEPT the affected card's controller), but gated on the affected card being an **ally**, so an opposing **HERO** is untouched — that is the whole difference between the two keys. Totems get it (305.3a); a weapon never does. Stacks per copy, and `get_atk`'s `max(atk, 0)` floor keeps ATK non-negative while leaving the raw negative total intact, so a later +ATK counts from the true value. Lifts the instant the source leaves play. No AI modeling needed |

Controller-relative like `opposing_allies_cant_attack` (the scan skips the affected card's own controller), but unlike Lust for Battle's `all_allies_keyword` the source is an **Ally**, so the aura lives in the ally_row and can simply be attacked and killed to shut it off. Read live in `GameState._aura_atk_mods` → `_opposing_atk_aura` — never cached, so it covers characters that arrive after the source and lifts the instant the source leaves play. **"Heroes and allies" is characters only**: the gate admits an ally_row card (totems included) or that player's hero instance, which keeps the modifier off **weapons** — they sit in the hero row with printed ATK of their own (303), and without the gate an opposing Krol Blade would be silently weakened too. Stacks per copy. `get_atk`'s existing `max(atk, 0)` floor keeps ATK non-negative while leaving the raw negative total intact, so a later +ATK counts from the true value (Ravenous Bite's rule). AI: no proactive modeling, but every forecast path (`forecast_atk`, `combat_trade_value`, `find_safe_lethals`) reads `get_atk` live, so combat math is correct on both offence and defence for free |
| `opposing_allies_attack_tax:N` | **Rule 600.3 attack tax** — "Ongoing: Opposing allies can't attack heroes or allies in your party unless their controller pays (N) for each attacker" (Winter's Grasp `azeroth_60`, 3, Ability — Frost, Mage). Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. The engine's first **"can't attack unless its controller pays a cost"** modifier — the fourth compulsion family (see the **Compulsion / taunt families** section), and structurally unlike the other two: it neither forbids an attack nor forces one, it PRICES one. The CR is explicit that the payment "is an additional cost to adding an effect to the chain proposing that character as an attacker", so it is paid at **ANNOUNCEMENT** (412.2) in `submit_action`'s new `propose_combat` arm and — being resources rather than a destroyed permanent — **refunded by `retract_last`**, unlike Sever the Cord's sacrifice; a proposal that fizzles at the 601.3 re-check keeps it paid. **`StackResolver.attack_tax(state, attacker, defender, db)` is the one read**, live off the board, with two independent scopes: the ATTACKER must be an ally ("opposing **allies**" — an attacking hero is never taxed, which is the card's real limit) and the DEFENDER must belong to the aura's controller ("heroes or allies in **your** party"). In a duel the second is every legal defender such an ally has, so the tax is total; the check reads the defender's controller anyway, so a partial-board version would need no new code. Stacks per copy (each aura is its own continuous effect and all must be satisfied) and lifts the instant the source leaves play. **Affordability is a LEGALITY gate, not a post-hoc choice**: `get_legal_defenders` drops a defender whose tax we can't pay — filtered **before** the 601.2c narrowing, so an unaffordable specified defender falls back to the rest of the list instead of forbidding combat — and `get_legal_attackers` drops an ally with no affordable defender (`_owes_unpayable_attack_tax`, which short-circuits on a board with no aura). `_resolve_propose_combat`'s 601.3 re-check deliberately does NOT ask again: by then the tax is paid and the resources are gone. **It also finally triggers 600.3's interaction with 600.2** — a character under both a "must attack if able" modifier and a tax "is unable to attack (and consequently need not attack) unless its controller CHOOSES to pay", so `get_must_attack_ids` counts an attacker as forced only while some legal defender is FREE; a player is never compelled to spend resources, which makes this a clean answer to Lynda Steele / Mocking Blow. AI (`get_reasonable_actions`' combat enumeration): `can_submit` filters what it can't afford, but affording is not the same as being worth it — a **taxed** proposal is offered only when the combat removes the defender (`combat_trade_value` `safe_lethal`/`both`) or is face damage the attacker survives, so it will not pay to chuck an ally into a bigger body. UI: the payment is otherwise invisible, so playtest logs an `attack_tax_paid` line |
| `opposing_allies_cant_attack` | Static aura (Lady Jaina Proudmoore): while a card with this flag is in play, that player's OPPONENT can't propose any of its **allies** as attackers (hero unaffected). Evaluated live via `_allies_attack_locked` in `get_legal_attackers` + `_can_propose_combat` — never cached. AI does not model the lock |
| `opposing_cant_protect` | Static aura (Hannah the Unstoppable): while a card with this flag is in play, that player's OPPONENT has NO legal protectors — neither Protector allies nor hero grants. Evaluated live via `_protect_locked` at the top of `get_legal_protectors` (returns `[]`) — never cached; the protect point simply never opens. AI does not model the lock |
| `whelp_bounce` | Triggered equipment power (Green Whelp Armor): when an **attacking ally** deals combat damage that actually LANDS on the wielder's hero, the wielder MAY pay 2 to return that ally to its owner's hand. First *triggered* (not `[Activate]`/passive) equipment power. Detected at the end of `_do_combat_conclusion` (`_maybe_open_whelp_bounce`): attacker was an ally, defender was the wielder's hero, ≥1 damage landed (armor DEF/block absorbing all → no trigger), both still in play, wielder can afford 2. Opens a direct-call choice point (`pending_whelp_bounce_*`, `whelp_bounce_opened`) resolved via `StackResolver.choose_whelp_bounce(state, pay, db)` — like the strike point; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending. Fires once per combat (single-attacker combat model). AI: `BaseAI.choose_whelp_bounce` bounces allies with cost ≥ 2. Scene: `_handle_whelp_bounce` (AI auto-resolves; any human — including the off-screen hotseat player, the choice is board-public — gets inline pay/decline buttons like the ready-on-attack point) |

Ancestral Spirit's announce-a-graveyard-card shape with the destination changed — the single chosen card rides the play as `target_id`, so the graveyard browser, the submission gate and the resolution re-check are all inherited; `_ability_fetches_from_graveyard` keys on `dest == "hand"` while excluding defs with an `activated_power` segment (the same segment appears on Sen'zir's hero power and on quest rewards, neither of which routes through the ability play path). Differences from the reanimate: **no cost cap** (nothing enters play, so there is nothing to afford — the fetch reaches an ally of any cost) and nothing enters play at all, so there is no controller change and no enter-play trigger. The pool is `get_graveyard_search_candidates` with `owner:own`, i.e. ally CARDS in the CASTER's own graveyard — asked through `is_ally_card_def`, so a **Totem card counts** (305.3a) and an Ability card never does. The target is **mandatory and single**, so unlike Cannibalize's "any number" an empty pool makes the card unplayable and the highlight probe goes dark (706.2); it is re-checked at resolution (706-style — a target exiled or otherwise moved out of the graveyard in response fizzles the fetch, and the spell is still spent). Hand size is a non-issue: the spell leaves the hand as the fetched card enters it. AI: reuses `_reanimate_action` (the highest-cost ally card in our own graveyard — cost is the board-value proxy), so no new AI code |

Remove Cold Snap from the game. Put up to X Frost ability cards with different names from your graveyard into your hand." Call the Spirit's fetch with four independent changes, each its own rider so a future card can take any one of them. (1) **The count is the announced X**: the MAX field is the literal token `X`, resolved through **`StackResolver.graveyard_max_count(req, x_value)`** — the one place the fixed and X cases are reconciled, so the submission gate, the router's browser ceiling and the AI can't disagree. X is also the price (`cost_base` 2, `_can_afford_play` still demands `x_value >= 1`), so the card is literally "pay per card recovered". (2) **`gy_tag:TAG`** narrows the candidate pool by a type-line tag, substring-matched on the def's `tags` the way `form_break` and `ability_cost_mod_by_tag` do — so "Frost Talent" matches `Frost`, and the pool is Abilities (Instant Ability parses to `Ability`, so both speeds qualify) in the **caster's own** graveyard only. (3) **`gy_distinct_names`** is a constraint on the chosen SET, not on the pool: duplicates stay candidates and `graveyard_picks_names_distinct` rejects an announcement naming two of one name. Names can't change, so resolution needs no re-check; the **router dedupes the browser by name** (copies in a graveyard are indistinguishable), which makes every selection the UI can produce a legal one. (4) **`rfg_self`** replaces the usual go-to-graveyard at the end of `_resolve_play_instant` with a move to the **owner's** RFG zone (415.7a) — so no later copy can ever fetch a Cold Snap back. **"Up to X" includes zero**, so unlike Call the Spirit's mandatory single pick an empty graveyard does NOT make the card unplayable and the highlight probe stays lit; the picks ride `target_ids` (`graveyard_pick_is_multi`) and each is re-checked independently at resolution, so a card exiled in response drops out without fizzling the rest. This card is also what factored the three graveyard-search play gates (reanimate / fetch / exile) into the one **`_graveyard_play_ok`**, shared by `_can_play_instant` and `_can_play_ability` — previously only the sorcery-speed path had them, and Cold Snap is an **Instant** Ability. UI: the X dialog first, then the graveyard browser with X as its ceiling (`InputRouter.confirm_x_value` routes to `start_ability_graveyard_selection` instead of board targeting when the card searches a graveyard). "Frost Hero Required" is the Talent-spec restriction the deck authorizer already enforces via rule 100.2c, not a new in-game gate. AI (`BaseAI._graveyard_multi_fetch_action`): the decision is **how many to buy**, not which — X is the smallest of what it can pay for, how many DISTINCT names the graveyard holds (a second copy of a name is unfetchable, so paying for it is waste) and the hand room left once the spell itself leaves the hand (503.2a); with nothing to fetch it holds the card entirely |

Each announced card is re-checked at resolution (706-style — still in a graveyard) and cards go to their OWNER's RFG zone (415.7a), so the heal counts what was ACTUALLY removed, not what was announced: recovering a target in response shrinks the heal. `requires_hero_race:Undead` is deck legality only (100.2b). Mandatory, free, no target, no choice, so nothing goes on the chain; readying an already-ready weapon or hero is a harmless no-op. **The payoff is a second combat in the same turn**: 303.2c allows one weapon per combat, so a readied hero + weapon can propose another attack, and since the trigger requires only that combat damage LANDED (not a kill), it fires off nearly any connected swing. | `weapon_combat_dmg_break_weapon:COST` | **Triggered weapon power that breaks one of the VICTIM's weapons unless he pays** (The Shatterer `azeroth_334`, 4, Weapon — Mace, Melee (1), 3 ATK / strike 2, PaRoShWa, Uncommon: "When your hero deals combat damage with The Shatterer to a hero, destroy one of that hero's controller's weapons unless he pays (2)"). A combat **conclusion** trigger (rule 703) in the Thrash Blade / Wraith Scythe family, taking two of that family's restrictions and leaving the third off: it names a **specific weapon** (the `state.combat_struck_weapons` read Annihilator's `combat_damage_unpreventable` and Thrash Blade's ready use, **not** the generic `_hero_wields_flag` scan — striking a different weapon while a Shatterer sits in the row triggers nothing), the victim must be a **HERO** (Iceblade Hacker's ally clause inverted), and there is **no role clause**, so a DEFENDING hero retaliating with a struck Shatterer fires it exactly as an attacking one. Damage must actually be **DEALT** — a swing prevented in full was never dealt (717.2b) and breaks nothing. **The novelty is who is asked.** Every earlier pay/decline point (Rain of Fire's upkeep, the whelp bounce, Feral Rage, Wisp) is offered to the card's own controller; this one is offered to the **OPPONENT**, and per the CR glossary **"Unless"** it resolves as *that player may pay (2); if he does not, destroy the weapon* — so the payment comes FIRST and only a decline reaches the destruction. **Being unable to pay is not a choice**, so an unaffordable cost opens no payment point at all and goes straight to the destruction (Feral Rage's "the offer never opens when it can't be afforded", except that here the failure has a consequence). **Which weapon is a CHOICE, not a target** — nothing is announced, so **706 Untargetable is irrelevant** and it can never fizzle — and the card does not say "its controller chooses", so the pick belongs to the effect's controller: the **STRIKER**. It is skipped entirely when the victim controls exactly one weapon (a pick between one thing is not a decision), and with **no weapon on the victim's side nothing happens at all** — nothing to destroy, hence nothing worth paying for and no payment point either. Pool is `get_weapon_break_candidates` (`_is_weapon_equipment` over that player's hero_row, so **armor and Items are never candidates**), read **LIVE** each time a point opens rather than trusted from the ids the point carried — so a weapon that left play meanwhile is simply gone from it. Both points are direct calls, NOT the chain (`StackResolver.choose_weapon_break_pay` / `choose_weapon_break`; `can_submit` and `pass_priority` hard-block while either is pending), and the pick is **mandatory** — the payment window was the only way out, so an id outside the pool is refused rather than treated as a decline. Both heroes can qualify from one combat (each striking a Shatterer at the other), so offers **queue** and drain one at a time, Feral Rage's shape. The destroy goes through `_destroy_card_trigger`, so `on_destroyed` triggers and the `ally_destroyed` turn-log entry behave as for any destruction and the card reaches its **owner's** graveyard (415.9d). Per **707.3** the source is not re-checked — destroying the Shatterer in between changes nothing — and it is itself a legal pick, so **a Shatterer can break a Shatterer**. UI: the whelp bounce's inline pay/decline popup for the payment (board-public — spending resources and losing a card in play are open information, so the off-screen hotseat player decides inline), and a button-per-weapon popup for the pick rather than the graveyard browser, the pool being at most two cards today. AI: `BaseAI.choose_weapon_break_pay` pays whenever the player holds a weapon whose printed cost is at least the cost (the striker picks and will take the best, so a player holding only a Scarlet Kris lets it go); `choose_weapon_break` takes the most expensive, ATK breaking a tie |
| `berserk_counter_on_hero_damage` + `berserk_atk_on_hero_attack:N` | Berserking (`dark_portal_134`, 3, Ability — Horde, Troll Hero Required): "Ongoing: When your hero is dealt damage, put a berserk counter on Berserking. When your hero attacks, remove all berserk counters from Berserking. Your hero has +1 ATK this combat for each counter you removed." Ongoing Ability living in the hero_row (paired with `ongoing` + `requires_hero_race:Troll`). **One counter per damage EVENT**, not per point (the card says "is dealt damage") — hooked in `GameLogic.deal_damage` (`_add_berserk_counters`) after the packet lands, so armor prevention that reduces a hit to 0 adds no counter and `put_damage` (405.3, a self-damage cost) never triggers it. The attack half is a **combat trigger** (602.1, `scope: board` in `COMBAT_TRIGGERS` — it fires off the controller's own HERO attacking, so it is read off every card the player controls rather than off the attacker): the power triggers as the combat step starts and the effect goes on the chain as the attack window opens, so it is respondable. On resolution it erases the counters and adds `per × removed` to `GameState.combat_atk_bonus[hero]`, a live "+N ATK this combat" grant read in `get_atk` (3c) and cleared with `combat_struck_weapons` at conclusion (both the normal and cancelled branches). The counters are read at RESOLUTION (709.2b), so damage dealt to the hero in the response window adds a counter that the link then cashes in. Pending counters also feed `get_atk(..., assume_attacking)` via `_pending_berserk_atk` (3d), so a 0-ATK hero holding counters is a legal attacker and the attack cursor forecasts the boost — no double-count, since the trigger erases the counters as it fills the bonus. Stacks across copies. UI: the count shows on the card as an orange badge in the ATK-badge corner (`CardNode.update_counter`, refreshed from playtest `_refresh_atk_badges`). No AI modeling |
| `party_ready_on_attack:COST` | Party-wide version of `ready_on_attack:COST` (Windfury Totem `azeroth_118`, an Air Totem): "When each hero or ally in your party attacks for the first time each turn, you may pay COST. If you do, ready that character." `_open_ready_on_attack_point` falls back to `_party_ready_on_attack_cost` (lowest such flag among the controller's in-play cards) when the attacker has no OWN `ready_on_attack`. Both sources share the ONE `attacked_this_turn` gate — a character covered by both (Windseer Tarus under a Windfury Totem) still readies at most once per turn (attack + ready once, never three attacks over). Read live; grant lifts when the totem leaves play |
| `ready_on_strike:COST` | Ongoing attachment trigger (Windfury Weapon `azeroth_119`, paired with `attach:melee_weapon`): "When you strike with attached weapon for the first time each turn, you may pay COST. If you do, ready that weapon and your hero." Opened inside `choose_strike` after a successful first strike this turn with that weapon (`_open_strike_ready_point` → `pending_strike_ready_*` → `choose_ready_on_strike`, direct call; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block; the held combat window opens after). Gate: `windfury_struck_this_turn` weapon counter (cleared at turn ready step). AI (`BaseAI.choose_ready_on_strike`) pays only while ATTACKING. See `data/rules_deviations.md` "Windfury Weapon" |
| `rapid_fire_ready_on_strike:COST` | The **player-wide, this-turn** flavour of `ready_on_strike` (Rapid Fire `azeroth_43`, 2, Ability — Marksmanship, Hunter): "Whenever you strike with a Ranged weapon this turn, you may pay COST. If you do, ready that weapon and your hero." A plain Ability (no `ongoing`, no attachment) that resolves to the graveyard after setting `PlayerState.rapid_fire_ready_cost` (-1 = inactive; cleared at every turn start beside `melee_strike_discount`), so it is forward-looking — strikes already made this turn are past. `_open_strike_ready_point` gained it as a **second, independent source** alongside Windfury's attachment: it covers every Ranged weapon (`CardDef.dmg_type` == "Ranged") the player strikes with, and is deliberately **NOT once per turn** — the `windfury_struck_this_turn` gate is neither read nor consumed on this path, because repeat strikes are the card's entire purpose. Both sources on one strike offers the cheaper cost. Everything downstream is shared with Windfury Weapon (`pending_strike_ready_*` → `choose_ready_on_strike`, the inline UI, `BaseAI.choose_ready_on_strike` paying while attacking), so no new UI or AI code — except the play gate. **AI (`BaseAI._rapid_fire_worth_playing`):** the card does nothing on its own, so it is only played with a READY Ranged weapon in play and enough resources this turn to strike **at least twice** — `play cost + 2 x strike cost + ready payment` (the cheapest ready Ranged weapon sets the bar; strike cost read through `get_strike_cost`, so discounts and Margaret Fowl's tax count). Below that the card would be wasted, so it is held. Emits `rapid_fire_gained` |
| `attach:melee_weapon` | Attach-target kind restricting the attachment to a Melee weapon (Equipment with a `weapon` segment + `dmg_type` Melee) the caster controls (Windfury Weapon). `_attach_targets_weapon_only` / `_is_melee_weapon` gate submission (`_can_play_instant`/`_can_play_ability`), `_resolve_attach`, and the highlight probe (`_targeted_play_has_legal_target`). Targets picked from the caster's hero_row in `input_router._get_ability_targets`. AI: `_attach_actions` puts it on its own highest-ATK Melee weapon |
| `attach:opposing_hero_or_ally` + `party_atk_vs_attached:N` | **Opposing-only attachment carrying an ATK aura against its own host** (Marked for Death `azeroth_39`, 2, Instant Ability — Marksmanship, Hunter): "Attach to target opposing hero or ally. Ongoing: Allies in your party have +1 ATK while attacking attached character." Two firsts. **`attach:opposing_hero_or_ally`** is the first attach kind that excludes the caster's own characters — every other kind (`ally`, `hero`, `hero_or_ally`, `melee_weapon`) allows a friendly target — so `_attach_targets_opposing_only` is checked at each call site (which knows the caster) rather than off the def alone: submission (`_can_play_instant` / `_can_play_ability`) and the 400.2 resolution re-check, where a host that **changed control to the caster** (Infernal, Nyn'jah) no longer matches the attach description and sends the attachment to its owner's graveyard. The router's generic ability target list is `can_submit`-filtered, so no UI code was needed, and the opposing hero is always in play, so the card never goes dark. **`party_atk_vs_attached:N`** is the first ATK aura whose source is an **attachment** rather than a card in a row: read live in `GameState._marked_target_atk_aura` (off `_aura_atk_mods`), gated on the affected card being the actual `combat_attacker` AND `combat_defender` being the attachment's host — defender-dependent exactly like Bala Silentblade's `atk_vs_exhausted_defender`, which is why it deliberately does **not** key on `is_attacking` (an `assume_attacking` forecast has no defender yet and must not claim the bonus; same known limit — the attack cursor can't preview it, the damage still lands at the conclusion). "Allies in your party" is literal: controller-scoped, and a **HERO** attacking the host gets nothing. Stacks per copy; the grant lifts the instant the attachment leaves play, which killing the host does for free (400.5). AI (`_attach_actions`): aims at the opposing **HERO** — where the AI attacks most, and the one host that can't be killed to shed the mark — and holds the card entirely with an empty party, where nothing would benefit |
| `turn_start_look_top_card` | **Look at the top card of your deck and choose where it goes** (Track Humanoids `azeroth_46`, 2, Ability — Survival, Hunter): "Ongoing: At the start of your turn, look at the top card of your deck. You may put it on the bottom of your deck." Paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in the controller's hero_row; queued as a `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS` entry, so it goes on the chain as a `resolve_turn_start_trigger` link and is fully respondable (see the **Start-of-turn triggered effects** section). **Looking is not a draw** (410.6b): nothing calls `GameLogic.draw_one`, so an empty deck opens no choice at all and can never deck the controller. The card also **never leaves the deck** — "keep it on top" is literally not moving it, so only the bottom branch does any work (`move_card` back into the same zone appends = bottom, the Blueleaf Tubers convention) and the deck size is invariant either way. WHICH card is looked at is read at **RESOLUTION** (709.2b), so a mill or a draw in the response window changes what the controller sees. The decision is a direct-call binary choice (`pending_track_look_player` / `_card_id` → `StackResolver.choose_track_placement(state, to_bottom, db)`, like the whelp bounce; `can_submit` / `pass_priority` hard-block while pending) — NOT the chain. **Private information**: `track_look_opened` carries the card so the deciding player's own screen can name it, `track_look_resolved` deliberately does not, and the shared game log may only say where it went (It's a Secret to Everybody's rule). UI: the inline two-button popup, labelled **Top (keep it)** / **Bottom**. AI (`BaseAI.choose_track_placement`): a flat **80% keep / 20% bury** — judging it properly needs a curve/board model the AI doesn't have, so the bias just keeps the deck moving; overridable, and a future AI should replace it outright rather than tune the number |
| `strike_cost:STRIKE_COST` | Marks an Equipment card as a weapon (rule 303) — always paired with an `equipment:SLOT:DEF` segment (slot e.g. `melee_weapon` for uniqueness, DEF 0). `STRIKE_COST` is the resource cost to strike (the number in the card's bottom-right circle — NOT the `Melee (1)` slot-uniqueness number on the type line). ATK comes from the CSV `atk` column, damage type from `dmg_type` ("Melee" gates the strike discount). See "Weapons & striking" section |
| `weapon_atk_this_turn:N` | **"Your weapons have +N ATK this turn"** (Heroic Strike `azeroth_142`, 1, Ability — Arms, Warrior, Uncommon). Elendril's flip (`ranged_weapon_atk_bonus`) with the type restriction dropped — the card says "weapons", not "Ranged weapons" — so it is a new sibling field, `PlayerState.weapon_atk_bonus_this_turn`, applied in `GameState.get_atk` beside that one and cleared at the start of every turn. Untargeted and unconditional: nothing is announced, so it can never fizzle. A plain, non-ongoing `Ability`, so it is sorcery speed — played in the main phase before combat, to pump whichever weapon the hero strikes with that turn (**303.2b sums every struck weapon**, so under Dual Wield the bonus applies once per weapon struck, same as `weapon_atk_bonus`). No AI modeling |
| `weapon_atk_bonus:N` | **Static ATK aura on your WEAPONS** (Eye of Rend `azeroth_288`, 1, Armor — Leather, **Head (1)**, DEF 0, DrHuRo, Uncommon: "Your weapons have +1 ATK"). Elendril's flip (`PlayerState.ranged_weapon_atk_bonus`, "your Ranged weapons have +3 ATK this turn") as a **static aura** instead of a timed grant, and **untyped** — the card says "weapons", so Melee and Ranged alike. Read live in `GameState._weapon_atk_aura` off the WEAPON's own controller's hero_row, from `get_atk` section (2c), i.e. **applied to the weapon itself rather than to the wielder** — which is the whole reason it needed no combat, AI or UI code: every downstream reader asks `get_atk` of the weapon and gets it for free (the **303.2b struck-weapon sum**, `best_melee_weapon_atk` for Mortal Strike, `BaseAI._forecast_strike_atk` and `choose_strike_weapon`, the `get_legal_attackers` 0-ATK-hero gate, the attack cursor and the ATK badge). A **weapon** is an in-play Equipment carrying a `strike_cost:` segment (the `is_weapon` flag the same loop already computes), so armor and an **Item** gain nothing, and neither does the hero's own ATK. Controller-scoped ("**your** weapons"), so an opposing weapon is never boosted; read through `effective_def`, so a blanked source (Polymorph) grants nothing; and live, so it covers weapons equipped after it and lifts the instant the source leaves play. Stacks per copy — Head (1) caps that at one per player today, but a future non-armor source would add. **The consequence worth holding onto: it is paid once per weapon STRUCK, not once per combat.** 303.2b sums every struck weapon into the one conclusion packet, so under **Dual Wield** two 1-ATK weapons swing for 2 printed **+ 2 aura**; contrast a "+N ATK while attacking" grant on the HERO (Berserking, Cat Form, Blood Fury), which is counted once however many weapons struck. That falls out of 303.2b rather than being special-cased — `_test_eye_of_rend_stacking_and_kris_free_strike` pins it. No AI modeling needed |

Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. Read live in `GameState._ability_cost_aura`, called from **`get_play_cost`** — the one choke point every cost path already goes through (affordability gating in `_can_afford_play`, payment/refund in `submit_action`/`retract_last`, the AI's cost math, the router's resource preview), so the discount covers all of them with no new code. `TAG` matches the def's type-line `tags` by **substring**, the way `form_break` does ("Elemental Talent" matches "Elemental") — which means Elemental Focus discounts a second copy of itself. `card_type == "Ability"` only: an Elemental **ally** is untouched, an Instant Ability is not (its type line parses to `Ability`). Controller-scoped ("**you** pay") — the scan reads only the costed card's own controller's hero_row, so the opponent's Elemental abilities are unaffected. **The FLOOR is on the reduction, not on the cost**: a printed-0 or printed-1 ability is returned untouched (never raised), and the discount simply can't take a more expensive one below `FLOOR`. Stacks per copy. Never cached — covers cards drawn after the aura resolved and lifts the instant it leaves play. No AI modeling needed (`BaseAI` reads `get_play_cost`) |
| `ally_cost_mod:DELTA:FLOOR` | Static **play-cost aura on ally cards** (Diplomacy `dark_portal_128`, 3, Ability, Alliance: "Human Hero Required. Ongoing: You pay (1) less to play allies, to a minimum of (1)" = `ongoing|requires_hero_race:Human|ally_cost_mod:-1:1`). Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. Elemental Focus' `ability_cost_mod_by_tag` with the type-line TAG filter swapped for a **card-TYPE** one, read live in `GameState._ally_cost_aura` off **`get_play_cost`** — the one choke point every cost path already goes through (affordability gating in `_can_afford_play`, payment/refund in `submit_action`/`retract_last`, the AI's cost math, the router's resource preview), so the discount covers all of them with no new code. **"Allies" is `CardDef.is_ally_card`**, so a **Totem card counts** (305.3a) and an **Instant Ally** does too, while an Ability (Diplomacy itself included), Equipment or Quest never does — which is what moved that predicate onto `CardDef`, with `StackResolver.is_ally_card_def` / `is_totem_def` now thin wrappers over it (GameState must not depend on the resolver). Controller-scoped ("**you** pay") — the scan reads only the costed card's own controller's hero_row, so the opponent's allies are unaffected. **The FLOOR is on the reduction, not on the cost**: a printed-0 or printed-1 ally is returned untouched (never raised), and the discount simply can't take a dearer one below `FLOOR`. Stacks per copy. Applied after Elemental Focus' aura and before Nature's Swiftness' one-shot discount (`_type_cost_auras` is the one place the play-cost auras are composed); the two type filters are disjoint, so they can never both bite on one card. Never cached — covers cards drawn after the aura resolved and lifts the instant it leaves play. `requires_hero_race:Human` is deck legality only (100.2b). No AI modeling needed (`BaseAI` reads `get_play_cost`) |
| `next_card_cost_mod:DELTA` | **One-shot, this-turn play-cost discount** (Nature's Swiftness `azeroth_28`, 3, Ability — Restoration Talent, Druid: "You pay (5) less to play your next card this turn" = `next_card_cost_mod:-5`). A plain Ability (no `ongoing`) that resolves to the graveyard after setting `PlayerState.next_card_cost_mod` (0 = inactive; cleared at every turn start beside `melee_strike_discount` / `rapid_fire_ready_cost` / `cold_blood_from_index`), so it is forward-looking — cards already played this turn are past — and an unused discount expires with the turn. Read live in `GameState._next_card_discount`, called from **`get_play_cost`**, the choke point every cost path goes through (affordability gating, payment, refund, the AI's cost math, the router's preview), so it covers all of them with no new code; while the grant is live it therefore shows on **every** card in hand, which is correct — any one of them could be the next card. Applied AFTER Elemental Focus' aura and floored at 0: EF's "to a minimum of (1)" restricts EF's own reduction, not a later one. Applies to **any** card type (ally/ability/instant/equipment), not to placing a resource (not a "play") and not to quest completion. **Consumed at chain entry** by the next card played (412.2, in `submit_action` right after `_pay_cost`), and the spent amount is stashed on the action's params so `retract_last` restores the grant and refunds exactly the discounted cost; a card that RESOLVES — or fizzles — keeps it spent, it was played. The best (largest) discount wins if granted twice; grants don't stack, since each is about "your next card". Emits `next_card_discount_gained` / `next_card_discount_spent`. "Restoration Hero Required" is the Talent-spec restriction the deck authorizer already enforces via rule 100.2c. AI (`BaseAI._next_card_discount_worth_playing`): the card does nothing on its own, so it is played only when the hand holds another card that costs **at least the discount** (anything cheaper wastes part of it) AND is affordable once discounted out of what is left after paying for Nature's Swiftness itself — a 6-cost needs 1 spare resource, and so on. X-cost cards are skipped (their printed cost is a floor, not the price) |
| `strike_cost_mod:SELF:OPPOSING` | Static **strike-cost aura** (Margaret Fowl `dark_portal_179`, 4-cost 5/3 Alliance Human Rogue: "You pay 1 less to strike with weapons. Opponents pay 1 more to strike with weapons" = `strike_cost_mod:-1:1`). Read live in `StackResolver.get_strike_cost` (`_strike_cost_aura` scans BOTH players' hero_row + ally_row) — the single choke point every strike path already goes through (`get_strikeable_weapons`, `choose_strike`, the AI's `_cheapest_ranged_strike_cost`, the playtest strike-button label), so the tax covers affordability gating, the `get_legal_attackers` 0-ATK-hero-with-a-weapon gate and the UI for free. Unlike Gorebelly's `melee_strike_discount` this applies to **any** weapon (the card says "weapons", not "melee") and is not consumed by striking. Never cached — covers weapons equipped after the aura and lifts the instant the source leaves play. Stacks per copy; the total floors at 0. `get_strike_cost` gained a trailing optional `db` param for it (the aura is skipped, not crashed, where a call site has no database). **Either half may be 0** — Edgemaster's Handguards (`azeroth_286`) is `strike_cost_mod:-1:0`, the discount with no tax, so the opponent's strikes are untouched. No AI modeling beyond the shared cost math |
| `ready_hero` | Hero flip power (Thangal `azeroth_16`, 3): "Ready Thangal." Non-targeted and choiceless — the SOURCE hero readies itself, a no-op if it is already ready — so nothing is announced and the router's targetless `use_hero_power_direct` path covers it with no UI code (`_hero_power_needs_target` deliberately doesn't list it). Always paired with `require_form_state:bear`, which is what makes it worth 3: Bear Form grants the hero **protector**, so a hero readied during an opposing attack is a blocker again (readying happens before the protect point, 602.2), and on your own turn it buys a second hero attack. AI (`BaseAI.thangal_ready_action`, wired into all three `decide_action`s): Dragonkin Menace's heuristic minus the choice — fired only on the OPPONENT's turn, while an opposing attack is underway (`_opposing_attack_underway`) and our hero is actually exhausted. The offensive use (a second attack on our own turn) is **deliberately not modeled** — that is a tempo call the AI has no model for — and the untargeted else-branch of `_get_ally_power_actions`' hero-power enumeration is gated on the hero being exhausted so a ready hero is never flipped for a no-op |
| `hero_grant_keyword:KEYWORD` | **Hero flip power granting the hero a keyword this turn** (Warrax `azeroth_8`, Alliance Dwarf Warrior hero, 30 health, Uncommon: “(1), Flip Warrax ➔ Warrax has protector this turn.”). Non-targeted and choiceless — the SOURCE hero grants itself the keyword — so nothing is announced, 706 Untargetable is irrelevant, the router's targetless `use_hero_power_direct` path covers it with no UI code (`_hero_power_needs_target` deliberately doesn't list it), and it can never fizzle: a hero is always in play. The grant is the **same Buff shape as `grant_keyword_target`** (stat `grant_<KEYWORD>`, duration `turns`:1), which is the whole implementation — `StackResolver._has_keyword`'s buff read is deliberately NOT ally-gated (unlike the `all_allies_keyword` aura read beneath it), and `get_legal_protectors` already asks `_has_keyword` of every card in the hero row, **the hero instance included**, so no combat code was needed. The end-of-turn sweep gives “this turn” for free — correct even when flipped on the OPPONENT's turn, which is the point of the card. Distinct from `hero_keyword:protector`, which is a static aura read off an in-play CARD (Draconian Deflector, Treesong) rather than a timed buff on the hero; the two stack harmlessly, and 602.2's other gates still apply (an **exhausted** hero can't protect, and 602.2b stops it protecting itself when it is the proposed defender). **Instant speed** (701.3, no `require_turn_player`), which is what makes it work: flipped inside the attack window it makes the hero a legal protector before the protect point opens. **The flip is once per GAME, not once per turn** — `PlayerState.has_used_hero_power` is deliberately never reset (see `TurnManager._enter_ready`), so the grant only ever lasts the single turn it is spent on. AI (`BaseAI.warrax_protector_action`, wired into all three `decide_action`s, with a `return result` gate in the on-turn hero-power enumeration so it is never blind-flipped) is strict for exactly that reason — an “any attack will do” heuristic burns the whole game's flip on the first 2/2 that swings. It requires: off-turn, an opposing attack actually underway, a READY hero, the hero not already a protector by ANY route (one `_has_keyword` call — the same funnel `get_legal_protectors` asks, so it cannot disagree with the rule; Draconian Deflector is in Warrax's own deck, so this is the likely case, not an exotic one), protecting not locked out entirely (Hannah's aura, a Stealth attacker), the current defender an ALLY of ours that DIES to the attack (`combat_kills`; 602.2b means an attack already aimed at our hero is never a case), and the hero left above `FREE_BLOCK_HERO_FLOOR` after absorbing it |
| `form_state:NAME` / `require_form_state:NAME` | **What form a hero is IN**, and a gate on it (Bear Form `azeroth_18` "Your hero is in bear form" → `form_state:bear`; Cat Form `dark_portal_19` → `form_state:cat`; Thangal "Use only while he's in bear form" → `require_form_state:bear`). **Every card printing the clause needs the flag, including the ones that pair a form with an on-play effect** — Bash (`azeroth_17`, bear) and Claw (`dark_portal_20`, cat) are easy to miss because the form is the second half of their text; `_test_form_state_flags` in `test_deck_manager.gd` pins the whole set against the real database, so add new form cards there. **What the form GRANTS hangs off this flag — it is not on the card at all.** `GameState.FORM_GRANTS` maps the form NAME to its keywords, its "+N ATK while attacking" and its `break_tag`, so declaring `form_state:bear` IS the whole recipe: protector and the Feral break come with it, read live. That is what makes the name the single source of truth, and it retired the hand-copied `hero_has_protector` / `form_break:Feral` / `hero_atk_while_attacking:1` segments — along with the latent bug of testing for a grant to mean "bear form", since a grant is no longer on the card to test. `form:N` stays a separate axis: it is the 414.3b slot tag, and **Shadowform proves slot ≠ named form** — it occupies Form (1), declares no `form_state`, and keeps its own `form_break_on:Holy`. Unnamed forms keep explicit segments; named ones must not restate them (`_test_form_state_flags` fails them if they do). `StackResolver.hero_is_in_form(state, player, name, db)` scans the player's **hero_row live**, so the gate lifts the instant the Form leaves play by any route (a `form_break`, a destroy, a second Form sacrificed to the slot check). It restricts neither the turn nor the speed — a form-gated power stays instant-speed (701.3), which is the whole point of Thangal. Checked at announcement (`_can_activate_power`) **and re-checked at resolution** (709.2a): a form broken in the response window fizzles the power (`action_fizzled`, reason `form_state_lost`) with the cost and the flip already spent |
| `melee_strike_discount:N` | Hero flip power (Gorebelly): pay N less the next time you strike with a Melee weapon this turn (`PlayerState.melee_strike_discount`; consumed by the next melee strike, cleared at every turn start) |
| `two_handed` | Marks a weapon as Two-Handed. **It is not a rule of its own** — it simply makes the card cost **2 hands** instead of 1 (`StackResolver.equipment_hand_cost`), and rule 414.3c ("a player can't control both a Two-Handed and an Off-Hand card") then falls out of the hands cap: 2 + 1 = 3 against a capacity of 2. See the **Wielding** section under Card uniqueness. Rod of the Ogre Magi, The Unstoppable Force |
| `wielding:KEYWORD` | **A rule 406 wielding keyword granted to your hero** (Dual Wield `dark_portal_127`, 1, Instant Ability, HuRoWa, Uncommon: "Ongoing: Your hero has dual wield" = `ongoing|wielding:dual_wield`). Always paired with `ongoing`, so the card lives in its controller's hero_row. `KEYWORD` indexes `StackResolver.WIELDING_GRANTS`, whose entries are the **dials each keyword raises** — nothing else about a wielding keyword is written anywhere, so adding one is a table entry. Dual Wield raises `melee_capacity` (the Melee slot's 414.3b cap) to 2 and `melee_strikes` (the 303.2c one-weapon-per-combat cap, rule 406.6) to 2, and deliberately leaves `hands` at 2 — which is exactly the printed card: 1H + 1H is two hands and legal, while 2H + 1H (3 hands) and 2H + 2H (4) are not, and belong to Two-Handed Dual Wield in the CR's table. Read **live** by `get_wielding_limits`, scanning the player's hero_row + ally_row for the segment plus the hero def's own printed keywords, best value winning (grants don't stack). Controller-scoped ("**your** hero"), so the opponent is never affected. Being live is what implements **406.2b** — a hero LOSING the keyword while holding an allowed combination violates on the spot — with no bookkeeping; see the Wielding section for why that check is per-PLAYER rather than per-card. No AI modeling: the AI plays it as a vanilla 1-cost ongoing, and both the uniqueness sweep and `choose_strike_weapon` read the dials live, so it gets the second strike for free |
| `power_weapon` | AI-only flag for "power weapons" — weapons whose value is their activated power, not the strike (Rod of the Ogre Magi, later Hypnotic Blade). The engine still offers the strike normally (a human may strike); the AI never strikes with one (`choose_strike_weapon` filters them out) and excludes it from `forecast_atk` (so it never proposes a hero attack on its account). Pair with an `activated_power` segment the AI already plays |
| `elusive` | (keyword in keywords col, not effects) — can't be chosen as defender |
| `protector` | (keyword) — can intercept attacks |
| `ferocity` | (keyword) — no summoning sickness |
| `stealth` | (keyword) — 602.2a: while this character is attacking, characters can't protect (`get_legal_protectors` gate) |
| `ranged` | (keyword) — long-range, defender can't deal combat damage back |
| `untargetable` | (keyword) — can't be chosen as a target of links (`_is_legal_target` in stack_resolver.gd, checked at submission AND resolution); combat/AoE unaffected. Chain Lightning's 2nd/3rd targets — and ALL three of Multi-Shot's targets — are a card-specific exception (allow flag) |

**`data_status`**: `verified` = card text confirmed from image  
**`engine_status`**: `implemented` = effects string is live in engine

### Totems are ally cards in EVERY zone (rule 305.3a)

"Totems are ability allies and count as both **in all zones**" — the rulebook's
own worked example is Resurrection, i.e. Finkle Einhorn / Ancestral Spirit's
effect verbatim. A Totem's printed `card_type` is `Ability` / `Instant Ability`,
so a bare `card_type == "Ally"` test silently misses it.
**`StackResolver.is_ally_card_def(def)` is the one predicate** for "is this an
ally CARD" — `card_type == "Ally"` OR `is_totem_def`. It backs the `Ally` type
filter in `get_graveyard_search_candidates` (Finkle Einhorn, Ancestral Spirit,
Battle of Darrowshire, Cannibalize, Ophelia's `graveyard_ally` pool, The Missing
Diplomat's deck search), `get_recomb_candidates`, and Augustus' `rfg_allies`
cost count/payment. Reanimation needs no special routing: `_bring_ally_into_play`
already puts the card in the ally_row, sets `just_summoned` and fires the
opposing-ally-enter watchers, and the "totems can't attack" gate reads
`is_totem_def` live in `get_legal_attackers` / `_can_propose_combat`.
**Do NOT use it for in-play scans** — there, "an ally in your party" is the
controller's **ally_row read live**, which includes totems by construction
(`atk_per_damage_party`, `_party_size_buff`, `party_health_aura`). Test a
RECIPIENT by zone (`inst.zone_id == inst.controller + "_ally_row"`), never by
`card_type`, or the check silently drops totems — the bug `party_health_aura`
had. Tests: `_test_totem_is_an_ally_card_in_the_graveyard`, `sc41-a2`.

### Tokens (`data/tokens.csv`)

Cards created by an effect rather than dealt from a deck (Mya's Mechanical
Dragonling, Tooga's Quest's Tooga). They live in their **own CSV** with the
same columns as `cards.csv`, and are loaded into the **same `CardDatabase`** —
every lookup in the engine, renderer and AI resolves a card by id, so tokens
must share the database. `CardDatabase.load_all()` loads both files and is the
only thing call sites should use (`load_csv(path, as_tokens)` is its plumbing);
defs from `tokens.csv` get `CardDef.is_token = true`.

- **Ids** are `token_<slug>` (no collector number). Instances are minted at
  runtime as `<controller>_token_<n>` off `GameState.token_counter`.
- **Not deckable** — `DeckManager.authorize_deck_def` rejects any `is_token`
  def alongside the rule 100.1 hero check. Without that gate a token would look
  legal in a deck list purely because it resolves in the database.
- **A token ceases to exist when it leaves play.** The redirect lives in
  `GameLogic.move_card`: ANY play → non-play move of a card with
  `CardInstance.is_token` goes to the owner's RFG zone instead. One branch
  covers destroy, bounce (Withdraw), discard and every future removal by
  construction, and no token can ever reach a graveyard for recursion
  (Ophelia, Augustus) to find. It changes only the DESTINATION — `destroy_card`
  still emits `card_destroyed` first, so `on_destroyed` triggers and "when an
  ally is destroyed" watchers see a token exactly as a real card.
  See `data/rules_deviations.md` "Tokens".
- **Renderer:** a token has never been in a zone, so no CardNode exists for it —
  `playtest.gd`'s `card_moved` handler spawns one as it enters the ally_row
  (same pattern as the drawn-card branch). Art of any resolution renders at card
  size for free: `CardNode`'s TextureRect is a fixed 75×105 with
  `STRETCH_SCALE` + `EXPAND_IGNORE_SIZE`, so the source pixel size is ignored.
  Aspect ratio is NOT preserved — a source far off 5:7 would be stretched.
- Adding a token: drop the art in `assets/cards/tokens/`, add a row to
  `data/tokens.csv` (`engine_status=implemented`, `image_path` set), and run the
  Godot importer once so the image has a `.import` file — `load()` returns null
  without it.

**CSV field quoting:** always quote `power_text` and `card_subtype` values to safely handle commas and quotes. Use standard CSV quoting: wrap the field in double quotes and escape internal quotes as `""`. For example:
```
azeroth_56, Frostbolt, "Instant Ability — Frost", "Deal 3 frost damage to target hero or ally. That hero or ally can't attack this turn."
```

**One row per line — a quoted field must NOT contain a line break.** `CardDatabase.load_csv` splits the raw text on `\n` and treats one line as one record before `_split_csv_line` ever sees the quotes, so an embedded newline turns one row into two malformed ones. Both then fail the column-count check and are **skipped with a `push_warning`, not an error** — the card silently doesn't exist in the database, and the first symptom is usually a deck failing authorization with "Card 'x' is unknown or not implemented". Join multi-clause card text into a single line with ". " (see Shadowform, `azeroth_88`).

**Engine behavior that deviates from the printed rules/card text** (e.g. a
timing restriction not in the printed text but true by construction of the
duel format) must be tracked in `data/rules_deviations.md`, with a code
comment at the enforcement site pointing back to it. Don't hardcode a
card-specific check in `stack_resolver.gd` for this — add a data-driven
effects flag instead (see `require_turn_player` for the pattern).

**`require_turn_player` is the ONE turn-player gate** — quests, hero powers,
ally powers and equipment powers all read it through
`StackResolver.requires_turn_player`. It implements the printed "Use only on
your turn" (rule 701.1) and **restricts the turn and nothing else**: such a
power stays instant-speed, so it is legal during its controller's own combat
windows and in response to a link already on the chain. The stricter
"non-combat action phase + priority + empty chain" reading is the separate
**Basic** restriction (701.1a) — a printed keyword no card in the pool carries,
so there is deliberately no flag for it. Don't re-add a phase or empty-chain
check to this gate. **It is no longer used as an engine-only off-turn
lockout.** For the Horde!, Rayder and Ryn Dreamstrider used to carry it for
that reason; they now carry `mute_when:opponent_turn` instead, so they are
legal off-turn as printed and merely stop holding priority windows open (see
`data/rules_deviations.md` "RESOLVED — For the Horde! / Rayder / Ryn
Dreamstrider off-turn lockout"). For a card that is POINTLESS rather than
ILLEGAL at some times, reach for a `mute_when` token — see the Auto-mute
section — not this flag.

---

## Card uniqueness

**All four uniqueness rules are STATE-based, not on-play triggers** — they ask
what a player controls in play, never how it got there. So none of them is
checked by the effect that put the card there: `GameLogic.move_card` queues
every card arriving in an `_ally_row` or `_hero_row` on
`GameState.pending_uniqueness_ids`, and `StackResolver.drain_uniqueness_checks`
drains that queue at the priority gate (it brackets `pass_priority`), running
`_check_equipment_uniqueness` / `_check_pet_uniqueness` /
`_check_unique_uniqueness` / `_check_form_uniqueness` on each. Each check is
self-guarding, so the queue carries no per-card knowledge. **A new way to put a
card into play — or to change control of one — therefore needs no uniqueness
code at all**; that is what makes Nyn'jah's steal *and* the reversion when she
leaves play both get checked, which a per-resolution check structurally cannot
do. A choice already pending stops the drain, so several violations queue and
are answered one at a time. `decline_control_discard` (Infernal) drains
explicitly, being a direct call with no resolution behind it.

WoW TCG allies are **NOT unique** by default. Do not assume uniqueness unless the card text says so or the card is a **Pet** (Pet uniqueness is enforced separately).

**Pet uniqueness (rule 414.3b):** A player may control at most `PlayerState.pet_capacity` pets (default 1) in their ally_row simultaneously. When a second Pet arrives in that row, the drain above runs `_check_pet_uniqueness` (non-interruptible), setting `pending_pet_sacrifice_player` and emitting `pet_sacrifice_required`. The player must call `StackResolver.choose_pet_sacrifice()` directly (not via `submit_action`).

Pet capacity is a player attribute (`PlayerState.pet_capacity`, default 1) that card effects can increase.

**Wielding — the hands model (rules 406, 414.3b, 414.3c):** equipment legality is **two independent caps**, and every variety of wielding uniqueness falls out of them:

1. **Slot capacity** (414.3b) — the "(N)" on the type line, read off `equipment:SLOT:DEF:CAPACITY`. Head (1), Off-Hand (1), Melee (1), Trinket (2).
2. **Hands** — a hero has `BASE_HAND_CAPACITY` (2). `StackResolver.equipment_hand_cost`: a One-Handed Melee weapon costs 1, a **Two-Handed** weapon 2, an **Off-Hand** equipment 1 (406.3a), everything else 0.

**414.3c needs no rule of its own** — a Two-Handed weapon plus an Off-Hand equipment is 3 hands against a capacity of 2. That is what replaced the old hard-coded `two_handed`↔`off_hand` cross-conflict. **Ranged weapons deliberately cost ZERO hands**: they have their own slot and their own keyword (406.4), and giving them a hand cost would silently forbid the legal Ranged + Off-Hand board.

The rule 406 keywords are then just numbers on those two dials (`WIELDING_GRANTS`, see the `wielding:KEYWORD` effects row). Only **Dual Wield** is shipped; the comment on that constant carries the dials for Two-Handed Dual Wield, Sextuple Wield, Dual Shield, Ranged Dual Wield and Infinite Wield, and the last three need a new dial each rather than just a number.

`get_wielding_violation(state, player_id, db)` is the ONE answer — it returns the conflicting card ids over the player's **whole hero row**, considering both caps, and `check_wielding` opens the ordinary equipment-sacrifice choice with them. `choose_equipment_sacrifice` re-asks that same question rather than shrinking a remembered id list, because a destroy can repair the board in ways a list can't express: killing the Two-Handed weapon of a 2H+Off-Hand pair frees the hands without the Off-Hand going anywhere.

**Why this one is per-PLAYER, not per-card.** Every other uniqueness rule is triggered by a card ARRIVING, which is what the `pending_uniqueness_ids` queue records. **406.2b** is triggered by a card LEAVING — the hero loses the keyword while holding an allowed combination — and nothing entered play, so there is no queue entry to hang it on. `drain_uniqueness_checks` therefore sweeps **both players** unconditionally after draining the queue. That is the state-based moment, and the scan is cheap and self-guarding.

**Weapons stack; hero ATK bonuses do not.** Combat conclusion deals ONE packet per side off a single `get_atk`, and 303.2b sums every struck weapon into it — so a second strike is worth exactly the second weapon's ATK, while a "+N ATK while attacking" grant (Blood Fury's fury counters, Berserking's cash-in, Ryn Dreamstrider's buff, Cat Form, Predatory Strikes) is counted **once**, not once per strike. The intuitive opposite reading is wrong, so `_test_dual_wield_atk_stacking` pins it. Those grants are also all `is_attacking`-gated, so a **defending** hero that strikes gets its weapons and nothing else. `BaseAI.forecast_atk` had to be taught the same thing — see `_forecast_strike_atk`, which sums up to `melee_strikes` weapons greedily by ATK against a **running resource budget** (strikes are paid one at a time, so a second weapon is only real if the budget survives the first) and respects 406.6's Melee/Ranged mix ban. Without that the AI under-reads every Dual Wield lethal, its own and the opponent's.

**Striking (406.6).** The same dials raise 303.2c's one-weapon-per-combat cap: `get_strikeable_weapons` reads `melee_strikes`, refuses to offer a weapon already struck this combat ("two Melee **weapons**", so a weapon readied mid-combat by Windfury doesn't come round again), and enforces 406.6's "never mix a Melee and a Ranged strike in one combat". A second strike is a **second strike point**, not two weapons offered at once — `_after_strike_resolved` re-opens it after a successful strike (and after any ready-on-strike point), the same shape as the prevention point re-opening per armor, so each strike is its own decision and is paid for separately. Declining never re-opens, or a decline would loop.

**Name-based uniqueness (rule 414.3a — the "Unique" tag):** cards whose `keywords` column contains `Unique` (parsed to `"unique"`). A player may not control two or more in-play cards that share a name and both carry Unique (Lady Jaina Proudmoore). `_check_unique_uniqueness` runs from the drain above; on violation it sets `pending_unique_sacrifice_player`/`_ids` and emits `unique_sacrifice_required`. Resolve via `StackResolver.choose_unique_sacrifice()` (direct call, not the chain — same pattern as pet/equipment sacrifice; `can_submit` hard-blocks while pending). UI mirrors the equipment-sacrifice mode (red highlight, pass button shows "Destroy a duplicate"); AI keeps the first copy.

---

## Highlight color conventions (input_router.gd → board_renderer.gd)

- **Green** (`Color(0.2, 1.0, 0.3)`) — default, normal playable cards / targeting
- **Red** (`Color(1.0, 0.25, 0.25)`) — mandatory choice: discard (`start_discard_mode`) or pet sacrifice (`start_pet_sacrifice_mode`)

- **Blue** (`BoardRenderer.SELECTED_TARGET_COLOR`) — a target ALREADY PICKED in a multi-pick announcement (Crushing Blow, Ravenous Bite, Chain Lightning, Multi-Shot, Cleave, Lightning Storm, Skewer, Sever the Cord, Shock and Soothe). `InputRouter.get_selected_target_ids` is the one place every flow's picks are collected for display, and `selected_highlights_updated` is emitted LAST by `refresh_highlights()` so it paints over the other two passes — a picked target normally drops out of the remaining-candidates list and would otherwise go dark, looking exactly like a card that was never clicked.

`refresh_highlights()` emits `highlights_updated(ids, color)`. The color is stored as `_highlight_color` in `InputRouter` and reset to green when the mandatory mode ends.

---

## AI conventions (game_logic/ai/base_ai.gd)

- `get_reasonable_actions` only returns actions for the **turn player** (non-turn player returns empty — AI passes during opponent's priority windows, including combat windows).
- **Targeting rules:** AI always targets **opponents only**. Never self-target for damage/destroy effects. This applies to:
  - `_targeted_instant_actions` (Vanquish, etc.) — only `opp + "_ally_row"` and opp hero
  - `_handle_enter_play_target` in `playtest.gd` — same: `opp` only
  - `_get_ally_power_actions` — already enemy-only
- `_destroy_is_worth_it(state, db, player_id, target_id, spell_cost)` — AI only plays destroy spells when target cost ≥ spell cost AND no friendly ally can solo-kill the target in combat.
- **Combat instants (held cards / ambush):** `BaseAI.COMBAT_INSTANT_TAGS` tags cards (by `card_def_id`) the AI must HOLD — `get_reasonable_actions` never blind-plays them; `combat_instant_action()` plays them only when the AI is **being attacked**, during attack/defend windows (see `ai_functions.md` for the exact math). Quick Strike (`azeroth_165`) is tagged `combat_instant_dmg`. The AI announces `target_id = state.combat_attacker` at submission. All AI classes inherit the behavior (BaseAI.decide_action; FullRandomAI checks it before random play).
- **Targeted instants announce their target at play time** (`_instant_needs_target` covers `destroy_target` AND `deal_damage_to_target`) — humans get the standard cancellable (Esc) targeting flow via `start_targeting`, highlighted with the looser `can_play_instant_no_target_check` probe. There is no deferred target pick for instants.

---

## Deck system (spec §9 — implemented)

Deck lists live as JSON files on disk, NOT in playtest.gd. Playtest only picks deck ids.

- `decks/battle_ready/`, `decks/ideas/` — one JSON per deck (`deck_id` = filename stem).
  - `battle_ready/` (6 decks): the curated, playtested lists — Grennan, Sen'zir, Omedus, Elendril, Timmo, Moonshadow.
  - `ideas/` (9 decks): everything else — Ta'zo, Boris, Litori, Gorebelly, Dizdemona, Radak, Thangal, Warrax, Graccus. Work-in-progress lists, still fully playable and findable under "All decks"; each still sets `recommended_ai_id` (`ai_generic`), the correct AI if one is slotted onto the deck.
- `ai_profiles/*.json` — `AIProfile` (`ai_id`, `ai_class`: "base"|"fullrandom"|"generic", `strategy_data`). All 8 demo decks specify `ai_generic` (GenericAI — see `game_logic/ai/ai_roster.md` and `ai_functions.md`).
- Classes: `DeckDefinition`/`DeckCardEntry` (game_logic/deck_definition.gd, deck_card_entry.gd), `DeckLibrary`/`DeckLibraryIndex` (scan/categorize, ids only), `DeckManager` (single entry point: `get_available_decks()`, `load_deck()`, `validate_deck()` [structural: hero + ≥60 cards], `authorize_deck()` [see below], `get_runtime_deck()` → `Deck`, `load_ai_profile()`, `make_ai_for_deck()`).
- **Deck authorizer (rule 100 legality):** `DeckManager.authorize_deck(deck_id, db)` / `authorize_deck_def(deck, db)` → error strings ([] = legal). Checks every card id resolves in the CardDatabase (catches unknown AND not-yet-implemented ids — the db only loads `engine_status=implemented`), no Hero cards among the 60 (100.1), max 4 copies per name (100.4), faction icons match the hero, and class icons share a class with the hero (100.2a). Class restriction is enforced ONLY on Abilities/Equipment — an ally's class column is its flavor subtype, never a deckbuilding restriction. **Talents (rule 100.2c):** a card whose tags column ends in `" Talent"` (`DeckManager.talent_spec`, e.g. Aimed Shot "Marksmanship Talent", Spirit Bond "Beast Mastery Talent") — since our heroes list no talent spec (206.1), the authorizer rejects a deck mixing Talents of two different specs; a Talent plus non-Talent cards of any subtype is fine. If heroes ever gain a spec trait, the hero-match branch of 100.2c goes in `authorize_deck_def`. Multi-class cards use concatenated two-letter abbrevs in the class column (`MaPrLo`; Lo=Warlock — `DeckManager.CLASS_ABBREVS`). Called by playtest `_launch_game` before the menu is torn down, so a hand-edited custom deck fails with readable menu errors instead of breaking mid-game; `test_deck_manager.gd` authorizes every shipped deck (this replaces composition-pinning asserts).
- **Nothing except DeckManager reads deck files or calls DeckLibrary.** Playtest menu has a category dropdown per player ("All decks" vs "Battle Ready") that filters the deck dropdown via `get_available_decks()`; "Recommended AI" player type uses `make_ai_for_deck()`. An "Avoid mirror matches" checkbox (default on) excludes the other player's fixed deck from a random pick, or refuses to start on a fixed mirror.
- **`card_entries` ordering is a convention — keep it whenever you touch a deck file.** Three blocks in this order: **Quests**, then **Equipment**, then **everything else** (allies, abilities, instants). Within each block the sort key is **rarity, then cost**: rarity **Epic → Rare → Uncommon → Common** (rarest first), and inside a rarity tier **cheapest cost first**. Ties on both keep their existing relative order (a stable sort — an edit should never reshuffle untouched cards). Type, rarity and cost all come from `data/cards.csv`; an **X-cost card sorts on the printed number** (`1+X` → 1, a bare `X` → 0), and a **blank `rarity` cell sorts after Common**, so if a card lands at the bottom of its block, fill the CSV in rather than hand-placing it. The engine ignores the order entirely (`DeckManager` builds the runtime deck and shuffles), so this is purely for reading and diffing a list — which is also why an edit must not gratuitously reorder: the diff should show only the cards that changed.
- **Deck files are hand-edited JSON — preserve each file's byte-order mark.** Five of the shipped decks (`alliance_warlock_dizdemona`, `horde_warlock_radak_doombringer`, `horde_shaman_grennan_stormspeaker`, `horde_priest_omedus_the_punisher`, `horde_mage_tazo`) are UTF-8 **with** BOM and the rest are without; a script that rewrites them all uniformly silently flips half of them. Read with `utf-8-sig`, write back in whichever encoding the file already had. `_card_name` / `_hero_name` are comment fields for humans (underscore-prefixed, ignored by the loader) — keep them in sync with the id when swapping a card, or the next reader is misled.
- Tests: `game_logic/tests/test_deck_manager.gd`. **Run it after any deck edit** — it authorizes every shipped deck (rule 100 legality: 60 cards, faction/class icons, 4-copy limit, Talent specs), so a bad swap fails there rather than at the playtest menu.

Demo decks (all 60 cards): Grennan (azeroth_10, battle_ready), Omedus (azeroth_12, battle_ready), Timmo (azeroth_7, battle_ready), Moonshadow (azeroth_6, battle_ready), Sen'zir (azeroth_14, battle_ready), Elendril (battle_ready), Ta'zo (azeroth_15, ideas), Boris (azeroth_1, ideas), Dizdemona (azeroth_2, ideas), Radak (azeroth_13, ideas).

---

## Currently implemented cards (engine_status = implemented)

Notable implemented mechanics:
- **Elusive** (keyword): can't be chosen as defender
- **Protector** (keyword): can intercept attacks
- **Ferocity** (keyword): no summoning sickness
- **Stealth** (keyword): while this character is attacking, characters can't protect (rule 602.2a)
- **Ranged** (keyword): defender can't deal combat damage back (Long-Range)
- **Untargetable** (keyword): can't be chosen as a target of links (rule 706); combat and non-targeted effects (AoE) unaffected. A target that becomes Untargetable after the announce fizzles at resolution (glossary 4217)
- **on_enter:deal_damage_to_target** — Taz'dingo-style enter-play targeted effects
- **on_enter:destroy_exhausted_damaged_ally** — Ghank (`dark_portal_210`, 3/3 Stealth Orc Rogue): "When Ghank enters play, you may destroy target exhausted ally with damage on it." First **optional** targeted enter-play trigger — the choice only opens when a legal target exists (`get_enter_play_destroy_targets`: an ally, either party, both exhausted AND `damage_taken > 0`, 706-legal); no target → the trigger silently doesn't fire, no pop-up. Declinable via `StackResolver.decline_enter_play_effect` (`optional` flag on `pending_enter_play_effect`; Esc for human, AI declines unless an OPPOSING ally is a target — never self-harm). Resolution re-checks exhausted+damaged+legal (706)
- **activated_power** — Grimdron (1 fire dmg to hero or ally, usable during combat windows)
- **sarmoth_taunt** — Sarmoth (opposing characters that can attack Sarmoth must only target Sarmoth). See the **Compulsion / taunt families** section — this is a 601.2c "can attack only" modifier with no compulsion attached
- **Lynda Steele** (`dark_portal_178`, 5-cost 3/5 Alliance Human Warrior, Protector) — "(1) → Target ally must attack this turn if able" (`activated_power:1:must_attack_target:0::ally:no_activate`). The engine's first **mandatory** combat modifier: rule 600.2 is a pass-priority lock, not an ordering rule, so it is the first restriction that constrains what a player may *stop* doing rather than what they may do. Repeatable (`no_activate`) and, because the lock bites in the target's controller's action phase while the grant is "this turn", an instant-speed play for the **opponent's** turn only. See the effects-table row, the **Compulsion / taunt families** section and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Lynda Steele". Tests: `_test_lynda_must_attack_locks_pass`, `_test_lynda_if_able_releases`, `_test_ai_must_attack`.
- **Mocking Blow** (`azeroth_144`, 1, Instant Ability — Arms, Warrior, Rare) — "Your hero deals 1 melee damage to target hero or ally. This turn, that character must attack if able and can attack only your hero" (`deal_damage_to_target:1:melee:must_attack+can_attack_only_source`). Lynda's compulsion plus the 601.2c redirect she lacks, on Frostbolt's existing damage-rider field — the card the CR itself uses to define the modifier class, so it is also what unified `sarmoth_taunt` and the new buffs into one specified-defender set in `get_legal_defenders`. See both effects-table rows and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Mocking Blow". Tests: `_test_mocking_blow_damage_and_redirect`, `_test_mocking_blow_stacks_with_taunt`.
- **Slam** (`dark_portal_124`, 3, Ability — Fury, Warrior) — "Your hero deals X melee damage to target hero or ally, where X is 1 plus the ATK of one of your Melee weapons" (`deal_damage_weapon_atk:1:melee`). **Pure CSV** — Mortal Strike's weapon-derived damage minus the anti-heal rider, at sorcery speed instead of instant: `_instant_targets_hero_or_ally_only` and `_can_play_ability` already cover `deal_damage_weapon_atk`, so no engine code was needed. Being a plain `Ability` (not Instant), it can't be flashed into a combat window — it's a main-phase swing, priced above Mortal Strike since it lacks that card's lock. Plain `Fury`, **NOT** `Fury Talent`, so no rule 100.2c spec restriction. No AI modeling. Test: `_test_slam`.
- **Mortal Strike** (`azeroth_145`, 2, Instant Ability — Arms Talent, Warrior, Rare) — "Arms Hero Required. Your hero deals X melee damage to target hero or ally, where X is 1 plus the ATK of one of your Melee weapons. That character can't be healed this turn" (`deal_damage_weapon_atk:1:melee|cant_be_healed_this_turn`). Two firsts: a damage amount read off a **weapon** in play, and the engine's first **anti-heal** restriction — which is why the check lives in `GameLogic.heal` itself rather than at any call site. "Arms Hero Required" is the Talent-spec restriction the deck authorizer already enforces via rule 100.2c (`Arms Talent` in the tags column), not a new in-game gate. See both effects-table rows. **AI (`BaseAI.mortal_strike_action`, tagged `combat_instant_mortal_strike` — held, never blind-played, and wired into all three `decide_action`s):** aimed at the opposing **HERO** and nothing else, for exactly two reasons. (1) **Lethal** — and honestly so: `_hero_armor_soak` subtracts every ready DEF the defender could still exhaust at the prevention point (717.2c), so a "lethal" their armor covers is not one. (2) **A hard counter to a heal** — `_chain_heals_hero` scans the chain (`state.pending_actions`) for an opposing link that heals their own hero: it resolves the link's source card (`card_id` for a hand card or ally/equipment power, `hero_id` for a hero flip), reads its effect segments for a heal (`heal_target`, `deal_damage_and_heal`'s second slot, `heal_x_from_target`, an `activated_power` whose effect is a heal, plus the untargeted `heal_party` sweeps, which name nobody but include their hero by definition), and for a **modal** link asks `selected_mode` so only the ANNOUNCED mode counts (707.1c). Our own links are never candidates. Deliberately **never pointed at an ally**, even to kill one — the two conditions above are what the card is held for. Tests: `_test_mortal_strike`, `_test_mortal_strike_heal_lock`, `_test_ai_mortal_strike`.
- **Cleave** (`azeroth_138`, 4, Ability — Arms, Warrior, Rare) — "Your hero deals X melee damage to each of up to two target allies, where X is 1 plus the ATK of one of your Melee weapons" (`cleave_weapon_atk:1:melee`). Mortal Strike's weapon-derived amount applied to Multi-Shot's multi-target shape, narrowed to 2 slots and allies-only — see the effects-table row for the full mechanism, the shared UI machinery generalization (`_multi_target_max`), and the AI hook. Sorcery speed (a plain `Ability`, unlike Mortal Strike), so it's a main-phase two-for-one rather than a combat trick. Tests: `_test_cleave_weapon_atk_two_targets`, `_test_cleave_scope_fizzle_and_highlight`, `_test_ai_cleave`.
- **Rend** (`azeroth_146`, 1, Instant Ability — Arms, Warrior, Common) — "Attach to target hero or ally.
