Ongoing: At the start of your turn, your hero deals 1 melee damage to attached character" (`ongoing|attach:hero_or_ally|attached_damage_turn_start:1:melee`). **Pure CSV** — Fireball's attachment with the on-attach burst removed, so every part is inherited: the targeting flow, the 400.2 resolution re-check (a host that left play or became Untargetable sends the attachment to its owner's graveyard), 400.5 (the host leaving play destroys it), and the `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS` queue entry that puts the burn on the chain during the ready step, fully respondable. Two things follow from the missing burst rather than from new code: the card **can never kill its own host on the way in**, so unlike Fireball it can't fizzle its own ongoing half; and with nothing happening at announcement it is a pure investment — at 1 resource it pays for itself on the second tick. The **HOST is locked in at announcement** (709.2d), and per 707.3 the source is not re-checked, so the burn is the attachment's, not Rend's. Instant speed (an Instant Ability carrying `ongoing` routes to `play_ability`), which is what lets it go down in a combat window. `Arms` is a plain type-line subtype, NOT `Arms Talent`, so rule 100.2c does not apply and it mixes freely with any other spec — unlike Mortal Strike. AI: no new code — `_attach_actions` already aims an `attached_damage_turn_start` attachment at the opposing **HERO** (guaranteed value, no fizzle risk, and a hero host can't be killed to shed it). Test: `_test_rend`.
- **destroy_target:ally** — Vanquish
- **Pet uniqueness** — pet_capacity enforced with immediate sacrifice choice
- **Infernal** (azeroth_127) — start-of-turn "discard or give control" choice (declinable, unlike a forced discard) + end-of-turn 1 fire to each opposing hero and ally
- **Equipment** — Mooncloth Robe (first equipment). Plays via `play_equipment` into the hero row; `activated_power` with `draw` effect + `exhaust_hero` extra cost. Slot uniqueness (rule 414.3) enforced with an immediate destroy choice mirroring Pet uniqueness (`pending_equip_sacrifice_*`, `choose_equipment_sacrifice`, `equipment_sacrifice_required`). See "Equipment" section below.
- **Dual Wield** (`dark_portal_127`, 1, Instant Ability, HuRoWa, Uncommon) — "Ongoing: Your hero has dual wield. (Can have a second one-handed Melee weapon instead of an Off-Hand equipment. Can strike with a second Melee weapon during the same combat.)" (`ongoing|wielding:dual_wield`). The engine's first **rule 406 wielding keyword**, and the card that replaced the hard-coded 414.3c cross-conflict with the general **hands model** — see the Wielding section under Card uniqueness and the `wielding:KEYWORD` effects-table row. Both halves of the card are one mechanism: the same dials that raise the Melee slot cap raise 303.2c's one-strike-per-combat cap, so nothing about "a second Melee weapon" is written twice. It is also the first grant whose LOSS is a rules event (406.2b), which is why the wielding check is swept per-player at the priority gate rather than queued per arriving card. Tests: `_test_hands_model`, `_test_dual_wield_uniqueness`, `_test_dual_wield_second_strike`.
- **The Immovable Object** (`dark_portal_254`, 4, Equipment — Armor — Shield, Off-Hand (1), DEF 5, PaShWa, Uncommon) / **The Unstoppable Force** (`dark_portal_285`, 5, Two-Handed Weapon — Mace, Melee (1), 5 ATK / strike 2, PaWa, Uncommon) — "Destroy <self> → Destroy all weapons named The Unstoppable Force" / "… all armor named The Immovable Object" (`equipment:off_hand:5|activated_power:0:destroy_all_named:0:::sacrifice_self|destroy_named:The Unstoppable Force:weapon` / `equipment:melee_weapon:0|strike_cost:2|two_handed|activated_power:0:destroy_all_named:0:::sacrifice_self|destroy_named:The Immovable Object:armor`). The first destroy effect selected **by card NAME** rather than by target, kind or cost band, and the first mass destroy of any sort — Ramstein's symmetric, non-targeted, `sacrifice_self` shape with the AoE swapped for a name-and-kind sweep. A joke pair that answers only each other: whoever fires first breaks both copies on the board, theirs and their own. Statwise they are worth playing on their own — DEF 5 is the highest armor in the pool, 5 ATK / strike 2 the biggest weapon. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_immovable_unstoppable`, `_test_immovable_unstoppable_scope`, `_test_ai_immovable_unstoppable`.
- **Ramstein's Lightning Bolts** (`dark_portal_267`, 3, **Equipment — Item, Trinket (2)**, Uncommon, no class restriction) — "(2), Destroy Ramstein's Lightning Bolts → Your hero deals 1 nature damage to each hero and ally" (`equipment:trinket:0:2|activated_power:2:deal_damage_aoe_all:1:nature::sacrifice_self`). First **Item** (rule 304: armor, items and weapons are all Equipment, so it needs no new card type — it plays via `play_equipment` into the hero row) and first slot with a **capacity above 1**, which is what added the optional CAPACITY field to the `equipment:` segment. First symmetric board-wide AoE. See both effects-table rows. Tests: `_test_ramstein_lightning_bolts`, `_test_trinket_slot_capacity_two`, `_test_ai_ramstein_gate`.
- **Truesilver Breastplate** (`azeroth_301`, 6, Armor — Plate, **Chest (1)**, DEF 2, PaWa, Uncommon) — “When your hero defends, it heals 1 damage from itself” (`equipment:chest:2|on_hero_defend_heal:1`). The first **board-scope defend trigger**: every combat trigger before it was `scope: "self"` on the card in the combat role, while this is an equipment watching its controller's hero — Berserking's attack-side watcher, on the defend side. See the effects-table row. It rewards a hero that defends *repeatedly*, so it is built for the same shell as Draconian Deflector and Warrax's protector flip; because 602.3 settles the defender after the protect point, stepping in as protector counts. Tests: `_test_truesilver_breastplate`, `_test_truesilver_breastplate_protect_and_scope`.
- **Maxum Ironbrew** (`azeroth_204`, 4-cost 2/4 Alliance Dwarf Paladin, Common) — “When Maxum Ironbrew defends, he heals 2 damage from himself” (`on_defend_heal_self:2`). Truesilver Breastplate's defend-side heal aimed at the **source itself**, so it is the first `scope: "self"` defend trigger that is neither a destroy (Grunt Baranka) nor a reflect (Plagueborn Meatwall) — see the effects-table row. **The timing question the card raises is worth knowing:** per 602.3 the trigger fires as he *becomes* a defender, the effect is a chain link resolving **inside the defend window**, and the combat concludes only “as a defend window closes” (603.1) — so the heal is **always before combat damage**, is capped by damage he was already carrying, and never soaks the incoming hit. That makes him a wall that repairs **between** blocks, not during one: a fresh Maxum is a plain 2/4, while one that survived a hit is back to nearly full by the next attack. Protecting counts (602.2 → 602.3), which is how he is meant to be used. Tests: `_test_maxum_ironbrew`, `_test_maxum_ironbrew_scope`.
- **Green Whelp Armor** (azeroth_291) — first *triggered* equipment power: when an attacking ally deals combat damage to your hero, you may pay 2 to bounce it to its owner's hand (`whelp_bounce`; `_maybe_open_whelp_bounce` at combat conclusion → direct-call `choose_whelp_bounce`)
- **Litori Frostburn** (azeroth_5) — `target_cant_attack` flip: cancels an enemy combat proposal on the chain via the 601.3 recheck (attacker doesn't exhaust); too late once the attack window is open. First "instant save that doesn't kill the attacker" AI behavior (`BaseAI.hero_disable_action`)
- **Hero powers**: Ta'zo flip (deal_damage_to_target:3:fire), Dizdemona (deal_x_damage_to_ally), Omedus (deal_damage_aoe), Grennan (heal), Boris Brightbeard (heal_x_from_target — pay X resources, heal X from any hero or ally), Radak Doombringer (radak_pet_sacrifice — flip: sacrifice Pet with cost X, deal X shadow dmg to target)
- **Thangal** (`azeroth_16`, Horde Tauren Druid hero, 28 health, Feral) — "(3), Flip Thangal → Ready Thangal. Use only while he's in bear form" (`ready_hero|require_form_state:bear`). First hero power that **readies** anything, and the first card gated on **which form** the hero is in rather than on what the form grants — see both effects-table rows. The gate is neither a turn nor a speed restriction, so the power is instant-speed: flipped during an opposing attack it puts the hero's bear-form protector grant back on its feet before the protect point (602.2), and on your own turn it buys a second hero attack. Tests: `_test_thangal_readies_hero_in_bear_form`, `_test_thangal_form_lost_in_response_fizzles`, `_test_ai_thangal_readies_to_protect`.
- **Warrax** (`azeroth_8`, Alliance Dwarf Warrior hero, 30 health, Uncommon) — “(1), Flip Warrax ➔ Warrax has protector this turn” (`hero_grant_keyword:protector`). The first hero power that grants its own hero a KEYWORD, and it needed no combat code at all — see the effects-table row. Cheap (1) and instant-speed, so the flip is held for the opponent's attack window, where it turns a 30-health hero into a blocker. **It is a ONE-SHOT**: the hero flip is once per game (`has_used_hero_power` is never reset), so the protector grant lasts only that one turn and never comes back — which is what the AI gate is built around. The hero of `decks/ideas/alliance_warrior_warrax.json`. Tests: `_test_warrax_protector_flip`, `_test_ai_warrax_flips_to_block`.
- **Quest cards** — basic cost-based quest completion
- **Reveal-and-pick quests** — Big Game Hunter (`azeroth_348`, Equipment:4), Kibler's Exotic Pets (`azeroth_355`, Ally:3), Zapped Giants (`azeroth_361`, Ability:3): reveal top N, keep one revealed card of a type, rest to bottom (`reveal_pick` recipe; human picks via the reveal browser, AI keeps the most valuable via `_pick_ai_reveal`)
- **The Princess Trapped** (`azeroth_357`, Quest, pay 2) — "Reveal the top two cards of your deck. Target opponent chooses one. Put that card into your hand and the other one on the bottom of your deck" (`reveal_pick:Any:2:opponent`). First card whose **decider differs from its owner**, which is what introduced `pending_reveal_pick_chooser` and the scene's `_route_choice` funnel (see the effects-table row and the Hotseat section).
- **It's a Secret to Everybody** (`azeroth_354`, Quest, pay 1) — "Look at the top three cards of your deck. Put one on the top of your deck and the rest on the bottom" (`reveal_pick:Any:3:to_top:private`). First reveal-pick whose destination is the **top of the deck** rather than the hand, and the first private "look at" (routed `_route_choice(chooser, "private")`; the shared log doesn't name the kept card) — see the `reveal_pick:Any:N:to_top:private` effects-table row.
- **Eagle Eye** (`azeroth_37`, 2, Instant Ability — Beast Mastery, Hunter) — "Look at the top four cards of your deck. Put one into your hand and the rest on the bottom of your deck" (`reveal_pick:Any:4:private`). It's a Secret to Everybody's private look-at **minus `to_top`** (the pick goes to hand) — and the first reveal-pick reached from a **hand card** rather than a quest reward, which is the only new code: a `reveal_pick` case in `_resolve_play_instant` calling the same `_reveal_pick` helper with the same flag parsing. Everything downstream is shared — the pending choice hard-blocks `can_submit`/`pass_priority` until answered, the graveyard-browser modal opens via `_route_choice(chooser, "private")`, and the AI picks with `_pick_ai_reveal` — so no UI or AI code was needed. Looking at the deck is **not a draw** (410.6b): a short deck reveals only what it has and an empty one is a harmless no-op, never a decking loss. With `Any`, every revealed card is selectable, so the empty-acknowledgement branch only comes up on an empty deck. Tests: `_test_eagle_eye_look_at_four`, `_test_eagle_eye_blocks_and_short_deck`.
- **Overseer Oilfist** (`dark_portal_245`, 2-cost 1/2 Dwarf Warrior, **Unique**, neutral, Common) — "When Overseer Oilfist enters play, reveal the top four cards of your deck. Put a revealed equipment card into your hand and the rest on the bottom of your deck" (`on_enter:reveal_pick:Equipment:4`). Big Game Hunter's quest reward and Eagle Eye's hand-card look-at reached from a **body** — the third caller of the shared `_reveal_pick` helper, and the first non-targeted, mandatory enter-play trigger that opens a post-resolution choice, so it needed only a `match` arm beside `create_token` in `_bring_ally_into_play`. See the effects-table row. A 2-cost body that digs four deep for a weapon or armor; it exercises name-based Unique uniqueness (414.3a). Tests: `_test_overseer_oilfist`.
- **Choose-one-or-both quests** (`qmode:` framework) — **Hidden Enemies** (`dark_portal_302`, Horde, pay 3: target ally has ferocity this turn / draw; Orc → both), **A New Plague** (`dark_portal_304`, Horde, pay 4 during your turn: each player destroys an ally in his party / draw; Undead → both), **Thwarting Kolkar Aggression** (`dark_portal_309`, Horde, pay 3 during your turn: opponent turns a quest face down / draw; Troll → both), **Crown of the Earth** (`dark_portal_289`, Alliance, pay 3: hand to bottom of deck + redraw / draw; Night Elf → both). **Poison Water** (`dark_portal_305`, Horde, pay 3: shuffle any number of cards from your graveyard into your deck / draw; Tauren → both). First modal quest rewards, first buff-granted keyword (ferocity), first quest-flip effect, first *chosen-subset* graveyard recycle. See the `qmode:` effects-table row. Poison Water tests: `_test_poison_water_shuffle_pick`, `_test_poison_water_empty_pick_and_gates`.
- **The Perfect Stout** (`dark_portal_293`, Quest, Alliance, pay 3, Common) — “Reward: Choose one: Target ally can't attack this turn; or draw a card. If your hero is a Dwarf, you may choose both” (`qmode:ally_cant_attack_this_turn|qmode:draw:1|qchoice_both_race:Dwarf`). **Hidden Enemies' exact twin with the grant inverted** — a lock aimed at the OPPONENT rather than a gift for your own board — which is what turned that reward's target pick into one kind-aware choice point (`pending_quest_ally_grant_kind`) instead of a ferocity-specific one. See the `qmode:` effects-table row. **The timing is the card**: the grant is “this turn” and the lock bites during the TARGET's controller's action phase, so completing it on your OWN turn locks nothing they were going to use — it is an instant-speed play for the opponent's turn, the same shape as Lynda Steele. Either party's allies are legal (“target ally”), so a human may lock their own; with no ally in play the mode is unavailable and knocks out “choose both”. AI: `_quest_mode_useful` takes the lock over the draw only on THEIR turn and only while an ally of theirs could actually attack, and `_best_attack_lock_target` picks their highest-forecast-ATK legal attacker, never one of ours. Tests: `_test_perfect_stout_choice`, `_test_perfect_stout_ai`.
- **Feral Rage** (`azeroth_21`, 5, Ability — Feral, Druid, Rare) — "Ongoing: When your hero is dealt combat damage while in bear form, you may pay (1). If you do, draw a card" (`ongoing|combat_damage_in_form_draw:bear:1`). The fifth trigger hosted at the combat conclusion (after Green Whelp Armor, Randipan/Samuel Grey, Devilsaur Leggings and Iceblade Hacker) and the first **conditioned on a form**, so it is the first real consumer of `form_state` beyond Thangal's gate. The card pairs with what bear form already does — the form grants the hero **protector**, so a bear druid soaks attacks constantly and this turns that into a draw engine. See the effects-table row and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Feral Rage". Tests: `_test_feral_rage_draws_on_combat_damage`, `_test_feral_rage_form_and_affordability_gates`, `_test_feral_rage_scope_copies_and_prevention`.
- **Circle of Life** (`azeroth_19`, 8, Ability — Restoration, Druid, Rare) — "Ongoing: When an ally is destroyed, its controller may search his deck for an ally card with the same name and put it into play exhausted" (`ongoing|recursion_on_ally_death_same_name`). The seventh effect conditioned on turn history and the **second reader of the `ally_destroyed` log** — which is what proved the entry type carries more than one independent cursor (see `game_logic/turn_state_flags.md`). Notable as the rulebook's own worked example for **710.1b** ("enters play exhausted" is processed DURING entry, not as a ready entry followed by an exhaust), and as the first **symmetric recursion** effect: "its controller" means the card refills whichever player lost the ally, the opponent included. See the effects-table row and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Circle of Life". Tests: `_test_circle_of_life_replaces_destroyed_ally`, `_test_circle_of_life_is_symmetric_and_optional`, `_test_circle_of_life_scope_and_copies`.
- **Operation Recombobulation** (`dark_portal_292`, Quest, Alliance, cost 4, **Gnome Hero Required**) — "Pay (4) to complete this quest. Reward: When an opposing non-token ally is destroyed this turn, you may put an ally card from your graveyard into your hand" (`requires_hero_race:Gnome|recursion_on_opposing_ally_death:1`). First quest reward that is a **lasting this-turn trigger** rather than a one-shot effect, and the fourth effect conditioned on turn history (after Torek's Assault, Thysta Spiritlasher and Cold Blood) — the card that added the **`ally_destroyed`** entry type to the rule contract log (`game_logic/turn_state_flags.md`). See the `recursion_on_opposing_ally_death` effects-table row and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Operation Recombobulation". Tests: `_test_recombobulation_fetch_on_opposing_ally_death`, `_test_recombobulation_scope_and_decline`.
- **Are We There, Yeti?** (`azeroth_346`, Quest, pay 6, Uncommon) — "Reward: Put three Mechanical Yeti ally tokens with 1 ATK and 1 health into play" (`create_token:token_mechanical_yeti:3`). Pure data — Tooga's Quest already reaches `_put_token_into_play`, whose optional count field had no card using it until now; the three tokens are distinct instances that each run the full entry path (summoning sickness, uniqueness checks, opposing-ally-enters watchers). Unlike Tooga the token is NOT Unique, so three coexist, and it carries no delayed-removal trigger — it is a plain 1/1 that ceases to exist when it leaves play. No AI modeling. Tests: `_test_are_we_there_yeti`, plus `_test_tokens_csv_loads` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which asserts every mintable token resolves 1/1 in the REAL database (a malformed tokens.csv row is skipped with a `push_warning`, so the token would otherwise silently not exist and the reward would no-op).
- **Dragonkin Menace** (`azeroth_341`, Quest, Alliance, cost 3, Uncommon) — "During an opponent's turn, pay (3) to complete this quest. Reward: Ready a hero or ally in your party" (`require_opponent_turn|ready_party_character`). Two firsts. **`require_opponent_turn`** is the printed mirror of `require_turn_player` and restricts the TURN and nothing else (701.1): the completion stays instant-speed, so it is legal in the opponent's combat windows and in response to a link on the chain — which is the entire card. **`ready_party_character`** is a **CHOICE, not a target** ("a hero or ally", no "target"), so 706 Untargetable is irrelevant and an Untargetable ally is perfectly choosable; the pool (`get_quest_ready_candidates`) is the completer's own hero plus their `ally_row` (totems included, 305.3a), narrowed to **exhausted** characters — readying a ready one is a no-op, and an all-ready party opens no choice point at all. The pick is made at RESOLUTION (709.2b) as a direct call (`StackResolver.choose_quest_ready_target`, joined to `_quest_choice_pending` so `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending), not on the chain, like every other quest sub-choice. UI: the ferocity-pick targeting flow (`start_quest_ready_targeting`, mandatory — Esc restarts it), routed `_route_choice(player, "public")` since everything involved is in play. **AI (`BaseAI.ready_protector_quest_action`, wired into all three `decide_action`s):** completion is legal at any priority in the opponent's turn, so the AI needs telling when 3 resources are worth it — it fires only while an opposing attack is underway (their `propose_combat` on the chain, or an open attack/defend window with their attacker) AND we hold an **exhausted Protector**; in the attack window that readies it before the protect point (602.2) so it blocks this very attack, later it buys a block against their next one. `choose_quest_ready_target` picks the most valuable exhausted protector, else the most valuable exhausted ally, hero last. **Deliberately not modeled:** refunding a spent `[Activate]` power for a second use in one round — whether that is worth 3 resources is card-specific, while a re-usable protector almost always is. Tests: `_test_dragonkin_menace_opponent_turn_and_ready`, `_test_dragonkin_menace_ai_readies_protector`.
- **Counterattack!** (`azeroth_343`, Quest, Horde, cost 3, Uncommon) — "While there are more allies in an opponent's party than in yours, pay (3) to complete this quest. Reward: Draw two cards" (`require_opponent_more_allies|draw:2`). The mirror of The Defias Brotherhood's `require_ally_count` gate, and it shares that card's timing exactly: the "While …" clause restricts **using** the completion power, so it is checked at ANNOUNCEMENT only (`_can_use_quest`, which also drives the highlight probe — the quest goes dark on an equal or smaller opposing board) and is **not** re-checked at resolution. It is not a target, so an opposing ally killed in the response window does not fizzle the reward, and the cost stays paid. "Allies in a party" is the `ally_row`, **totems included** (305.3a); "more … than" is strict, so a tie doesn't qualify, and the condition is one-way — our own bigger party never enables it. In a duel "an opponent's" is the single opponent, so nothing is chosen. No AI code: the generic quest branch completes it whenever `can_submit` allows, with no full-hand gate (the same as every other draw-reward quest — only the `complete_cost_destroy_self` branch has one). Test: `_test_counterattack_requires_bigger_opposing_party`.
- **Blueleaf Tubers** (`azeroth_349`, Quest, pay 2) — "Reward: Shuffle your graveyard into your deck" (`shuffle_graveyard_into_deck`). First graveyard-recycling effect and first argument-less reward segment. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_blueleaf_tubers_shuffles_graveyard`, `_test_blueleaf_tubers_empty_graveyard`.
- **Into the Maw of Madness** (`azeroth_353`, Quest, no cost) — "Destroy Into the Maw of Madness to complete this quest. Reward: Draw a card" (`complete_cost_destroy_self|draw:1`). First quest whose completion cost is **destroying itself**, and the first cost paid in a destroyed permanent that is the quest — paid at announcement per 412.2, so the reward alone sits on the chain and is interruptible while the cost stays paid. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_maw_of_madness_destroy_cost`, `_test_maw_of_madness_reward_cancelled`, `_test_ai_maw_of_madness_gate`.
- **Lazy Peons** (`dark_portal_303`, Quest, Horde, no cost, **Orc Hero Required**) — "Lazy Peons enters play exhausted. Exhaust Lazy Peons to complete this quest. Reward: Draw a card" (`requires_hero_race:Orc|enters_play_exhausted|complete_cost_exhaust_self|draw:1`). First quest whose completion cost is **exhausting itself**, first card that enters play exhausted (710.1b), and the card that turned the automatic resource pay order from a cosmetic convention into a rules-relevant one — see the effects-table row and the comment on `StackResolver._resource_pay_order`. Tests: `_test_lazy_peons_exhaust_cost`, `_test_resource_pay_order_exceptions`.
- **The Love Potion** (`azeroth_356`, Quest, cost 1) — "Exhaust two allies in your party and pay (1) to complete this quest. Reward: Draw a card" (`exhaust_allies:2|draw:1`). First quest with a **non-resource completion cost**, and the first pre-submission (cancellable, zero-state-change) cost picker in the UI — see the `exhaust_allies` effects-table row.
- **Totem** (rule 305.3) — Searing Totem (`azeroth_116`, Instant Ability—Elemental, Fire Totem, 0/1). Ability ally that enters the ally_row, can't attack, can be attacked/targeted like an ally. Ongoing power deals 1 fire damage to a chosen hero/ally at the start of each turn (`totem:fire` + `ongoing_damage_each_turn:1:fire`; direct-call `choose_trigger_target` picks the target, then the trigger goes on the chain with a priority window before the damage — rule 500.2/410). First card of the general Totem framework.
- **Shadowmeld** (`dark_portal_131`, 3, Ability, Alliance, **Night Elf Hero Required**, Uncommon) — "Ongoing: Your hero is elusive and untargetable. At the start of your turn, destroy Shadowmeld" (`requires_hero_race:Night Elf|ongoing|hero_keyword:elusive|hero_keyword:untargetable|turn_start_destroy_self`). The first card to carry **two `hero_keyword` segments**, and the first ever grant of **untargetable to a hero** — and both halves are **pure CSV**: `_hero_keyword_aura` scans every segment on a card, and both gate sites already ask `_has_keyword(..., db, state)`, so no combat or targeting code was needed. The one new key is `turn_start_destroy_self` (see the effects-table row). The two keywords cover the two different ways a hero is reached and together they close both: **elusive** is 601.2b, so the hero can't be proposed as a defender and every attack is funnelled at your allies, while **untargetable** is 706, so no link may name it either — a hero that cannot be attacked and cannot be targeted, for exactly one of the opponent's turns. What it does NOT stop is everything that chooses nobody: an AoE (Flamestrike, Ramstein's Lightning Bolts, Rain of Fire's end-of-turn sweep) lands as normal, which is the real answer to it. Protecting is unaffected either way (602.2 is a different gate), so a hero with a protector grant can still step in — and doing so makes it the defender without ever having been proposed as one. `requires_hero_race:Night Elf` is deck legality only (100.2b). No AI modeling — it is played as a vanilla 3-cost ongoing, and every combat and targeting query reads `_has_keyword` live, so both AIs respect the grant for free. Tests: `_test_shadowmeld_hero_elusive_and_untargetable`, `_test_shadowmeld_expiry_is_respondable`, plus `_test_shadowmeld_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins all four segments against the REAL database — the pinning matters precisely BECAUSE the aura is pure data: a typo in either keyword is completely silent, leaving a card that quietly grants one thing, or nothing.
- **Wisp** (`dark_portal_197`, 1-cost 0/1 Alliance Ally — Wisp, Uncommon) — "At the start of your turn, if Wisp is in your graveyard, you may pay (1). If you do, put Wisp into your hand" (`graveyard_turn_start_pay_to_hand:1`). The engine's **first power that functions from a zone other than play** (rule 703.3a), and the card that made a trigger's source zone part of the trigger contract — see the effects-table row. It is also a **recurring** offer rather than a one-shot: declining costs nothing, so the same 0/1 body comes back for 1 resource every turn it sits in the yard, which is what makes an otherwise blank vanilla ally an engine (chump blocker, sacrifice fodder, The Love Potion's exhaust cost). Tests: `_test_wisp_returns_itself_from_the_graveyard`, `_test_wisp_decline_is_free_and_recurs`, `_test_wisp_scope_and_gates`, plus `_test_wisp_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the effects key AND the disjointness of the three trigger-zone lists against the REAL database — the pinning matters precisely BECAUSE the card is pure CSV: a typo would leave a silent vanilla 0/1 that never comes back, with nothing to fail on.
- **Fire Nova Totem** (`dark_portal_93`, 1, Ability — Elemental, **Fire Totem (1)**, 0/1, Common) — "Ongoing: At the start of your turn, destroy Fire Nova Totem. If you do, it deals 3 fire damage to each opposing hero and ally" (`ongoing|totem:fire|turn_start_destroy_self_damage_opposing:3:fire`). The first totem that is a **one-shot fuse** rather than an engine — it pays for its own effect with itself — and the first start-of-turn trigger gated on **709.2f**'s "if you do". See the effects-table row. Two things separate it from every other shipped totem. (1) It is a plain **`Ability`**, NOT an `Instant Ability`, so unlike Searing / Earthbind / Healing Stream / Windfury it is **sorcery speed** and can't be dropped into a combat window. (2) Because the destroy happens when the chain link RESOLVES, the opponent gets one clean window every turn to answer it, and **any** way of removing it — destroy, bounce, exile — denies the burn entirely. Left alone it is 3 damage to their whole board for 1 resource. Tests: `_test_fire_nova_totem_blows_up`, `_test_fire_nova_totem_answered_in_window`.
- **Earthbind Totem** (`azeroth_107`, 2, Instant Ability—Elemental, Earth Totem (1), 0/1) — "Ongoing: Opposing allies can't ready during their controllers' ready step" (`ongoing|totem:earth|opposing_allies_cant_ready`). First ready-lock aura; ready-blocked cards show the ⛓ badge.
- **Healing Stream Totem** (`azeroth_111`, 1, Instant Ability—Restoration, Water Totem (1), 0/1) — "Ongoing: At the start of each turn, [this] heals 1 damage from each hero and ally in your party" (`ongoing|totem:water|heal_party_each_turn:1`).
- **Windfury Totem** (`azeroth_118`, 4, Instant Ability—Enhancement, Air Totem (1), 0/1) — "Ongoing: When each hero or ally in your party attacks for the first time each turn, you may pay (1). If you do, ready that character" (`ongoing|totem:air|party_ready_on_attack:1`). Party-wide `ready_on_attack`; shares the once-per-turn `attacked_this_turn` gate — see the `party_ready_on_attack` effects-table row.
- **Windfury Weapon** (`azeroth_119`, 2, Instant Ability—Enhancement) — "Attach to one of your Melee weapons. Ongoing: When you strike with attached weapon for the first time each turn, you may pay (1). If you do, ready that weapon and your hero" (`ongoing|attach:melee_weapon|ready_on_strike:1`). First attach-to-weapon and first ready-on-strike point — see the `attach:melee_weapon` / `ready_on_strike` effects-table rows and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Windfury Weapon".
- **Rapid Fire** (`azeroth_43`, 2, Ability — Marksmanship, Hunter) — "Whenever you strike with a Ranged weapon this turn, you may pay (1). If you do, ready that weapon and your hero" (`rapid_fire_ready_on_strike:1`). Windfury Weapon's ready-on-strike point granted **to the player instead of to one weapon**, and the first such trigger with **no once-per-turn gate** — chaining ranged strikes is the point of the card. See the effects-table row. Test: `_test_rapid_fire_repeat_ranged_strikes`.
- **Long-Range** (keyword) — Tanwa the Marksman (dark_portal_235, 4/3). While a Long-Range character is attacking, the defender deals no combat damage back (`_do_combat_conclusion` zeroes `def_dmg` when the attacker has `long_range`; has no effect when the character is defending instead).
- **Weapons & striking** — Krol Blade (azeroth_331, first weapon) + Gorebelly (azeroth_9, melee strike discount flip). See "Weapons & striking" section
- **Edgemaster's Handguards** (`azeroth_286`, 3, Armor — Mail, **Hands (1)**, DEF 1, HuPaShWa, Uncommon) — "You pay (1) less to strike with weapons" (`equipment:hands:1|strike_cost_mod:-1:0`). **Pure CSV** — Margaret Fowl's strike-cost aura with the **OPPOSING half at 0**, so it is a pure discount and never taxes the opponent; read live off `get_strike_cost`, the single choke point every strike path already goes through (`get_strikeable_weapons`, `choose_strike`, the AI's cost math, the `get_legal_attackers` 0-ATK-hero-with-a-weapon gate, the playtest strike-button label), so affordability, legality and UI all inherit it with no new code. Applies to **any** weapon (the card says "weapons", not "melee"), unlike Gorebelly's Melee-only flip — and unlike that flip it is **not consumed** by striking, so it is every strike, every turn. Stacks per copy and the total floors at 0; Hands (1) caps that at one per player today, but a future non-armor source would add. Lifts the instant it leaves play. **The slot is the one thing worth reading twice**: `hands` is a brand-new slot string with the default capacity 1, and despite its NAME it is ARMOR — `equipment_hand_cost` charges only Two-Handed, Off-Hand and Melee weapons — so it costs **0 hands** in the rule 406 wielding model and coexists with a Two-Handed weapon (which fills both). At DEF 1 it is also a real, if small, shielder at the prevention point (717.2c). No AI modeling beyond the shared cost math. Tests: `_test_edgemasters_handguards`, plus `_test_edgemasters_handguards_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the slot, the DEF, the capacity, the 0-hands cost and BOTH halves of the aura against the REAL database — a wrong sign or a stray opposing value would silently tax the wielder instead of discounting.
- **Eye of Rend** (`azeroth_288`, 1, Armor — Leather, **Head (1)**, DEF 0, DrHuRo, Uncommon) — "Your weapons have +1 ATK" (`equipment:head:0|weapon_atk_bonus:1`). The engine's first **static** weapon ATK aura (Elendril's flip is the timed, Ranged-only version), and the reason it cost one function: the bonus is applied to the WEAPON inside `get_atk` rather than to the wielder, so combat, Mortal Strike, the AI's strike forecast, the attacker gate and the ATK badge all inherit it — see the effects-table row for why that also makes it pay **once per weapon struck** under Dual Wield. At **DEF 0** it blocks nothing on its own, which is what makes it a 1-cost slot investment rather than armor; Stoneform or Natural Defenses lifting it to DEF 1 turns it into an exhaustable shielder as well, since `get_ready_def_armor`'s "> 0" gate reads `get_armor_def`. Tests: `_test_eye_of_rend_weapon_atk_aura`, `_test_eye_of_rend_stacking_and_kris_free_strike`, plus `_test_eye_of_rend_and_scarlet_kris_recipes` in `test_deck_manager.gd`.
- **Deathdealer Breastplate** (`azeroth_283`, 5, Armor — Mail, **Chest (1)**, DEF 1, HuPaShWa, Rare) — "Your weapons have +2 ATK" (`equipment:chest:1|weapon_atk_bonus:2`). **Pure CSV** — Eye of Rend's static weapon ATK aura at +2, in the CHEST slot (Truesilver Breastplate's) instead of HEAD, so it needed no engine code: the bonus is applied to the **WEAPON** inside `get_atk` rather than to the wielder, and every downstream reader inherits it — the **303.2b struck-weapon sum**, `best_melee_weapon_atk` (Mortal Strike), `BaseAI._forecast_strike_atk` and `choose_strike_weapon`, the `get_legal_attackers` 0-ATK-hero gate, the attack cursor and the ATK badge. Untyped ("weapons"), so Melee and **Ranged** alike; controller-scoped, read through `effective_def` (a blanked source grants nothing), and live, so it covers weapons equipped after it and lifts the instant it leaves play. **Two consequences worth holding onto.** (1) Like Eye of Rend it is paid **once per weapon STRUCK, not once per combat** (303.2b), so under **Dual Wield** two weapons each gain +2 — contrast a "+N ATK while attacking" grant on the HERO, counted once however many weapons struck. (2) Head and Chest are **different slots**, so it **stacks with Eye of Rend** for +3 on every weapon, while a second Chest card (Truesilver) violates 414.3b. At DEF 1 it is also a small shielder at the prevention point (717.2c). No AI modeling needed. Tests: `_test_deathdealer_breastplate`, plus `_test_deathdealer_breastplate_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the slot, the DEF, the capacity, the amount and the Head/Chest disjointness against the REAL database.
- **Scarlet Kris** (`azeroth_333`, 2, Weapon — Dagger, **Melee (1)**, 1 ATK / **strike 0**, HuRoShWa, Uncommon) — no text box; **pure CSV** (`equipment:melee_weapon:0|strike_cost:0`). Notable only for being the engine's first **strike cost 0** weapon, and that is a real difference rather than a small number: `get_strike_cost` returning 0 means a hero can strike it at **zero resources**, so the `get_legal_attackers` gate (which admits a 0-ATK hero only while a strikeable weapon is *affordable*) can never turn it down, and it never competes with a card the player wanted to play that turn. Margaret Fowl's opposing +1 tax and Gorebelly's discount both still apply through the same `get_strike_cost` funnel — the total floors at 0 — so a Kris under an opposing Fowl costs 1. It pairs naturally with Eye of Rend, which lifts the weakest weapon in the pool to a free 2 ATK a swing.
- **Frostbolt / Frost Shock** (`azeroth_56`, 3 frost; `azeroth_109`, 2 frost) — Instant Abilities that deal frost damage to a target hero/ally, then apply a "can't attack" (Frostbolt) or "can't attack or protect" (Frost Shock) restriction to a surviving target (`deal_damage_to_target` 4th field → `_apply_damage_riders`). Tagged `combat_instant_dmg` like Quick Strike; AI plays them for the damage only.
- **Shadow Bolt** (`azeroth_132`, 3, Ability — Destruction, Warlock, Uncommon) — "Your hero deals 3 shadow damage to target hero or ally. When that character is destroyed this turn, its controller discards a card" (`deal_damage_to_target:3:shadow|mark_destroy_discard:1`). The engine's first **delayed trigger attached to a CHARACTER** rather than to a player or a card in play — and the third reader of the `ally_destroyed` turn log (after Operation Recombobulation and Circle of Life), the one that showed the entry type supports a **consumable** watcher with no cursor at all. The mark outlives the spell: the bolt's own kill fires it, and so does a combat death, another player's removal, or a sacrifice, any time later in the same turn. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_shadow_bolt_kill_discards`, `_test_shadow_bolt_delayed_trigger`, `_test_shadow_bolt_scope`.
- **Chastise** (`azeroth_76`, 2, Ability — Holy, Priest, Uncommon) — "Your hero deals 2 holy damage to target hero or ally. That damage can't be prevented" (`deal_damage_to_target:2:holy|damage_unpreventable`). The first card to print unpreventability on the **damage** rather than have it derived from the source, which is what moved the flag onto the packet — see the effects-table row. Sorcery speed and either party's characters are legal, so a human may burn their own; the real point is that armor, Brother Rhone, Bestial Wrath and Soul Link all do nothing about it, and none of them is spent trying. Tests: `_test_chastise_unpreventable`, `_test_chastise_ignores_shields`.
- **Smite** (`azeroth_89`, 5, Ability — Holy, Priest, Rare) — "Your hero deals 4 holy damage to target hero or ally. That damage can't be prevented" (`deal_damage_to_target:4:holy|damage_unpreventable`). Chastise at twice the size for more than twice the price — **pure CSV**, since the `damage_unpreventable` rider is a general one; see that card's entry and the effects-table row. The pair is pinned against the REAL database by `_test_unpreventable_holy_recipes` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, because a typo in the rider would silently leave the damage preventable with nothing to fail on.
- **Spiritual Healing** (`azeroth_90`, 2, Ability — Holy **Talent**, Priest, Rare) — "Holy Hero Required. Ongoing: When you play a Holy ability, your hero heals 2 damage from target hero or ally" (`ongoing|on_play_ability_heal:Holy:2`). The engine's first **play-triggered** effect, which is what added the third trigger queue — see the **Play-triggered effects** section and the effects-table row. The heal goes on the chain ON TOP of the ability that triggered it, so it resolves first and survives that ability being countered. "Holy Hero Required" is the Talent-spec restriction the deck authorizer already enforces via rule 100.2c (`Holy Talent` in the tags column), not a new in-game gate. It pairs with the unpreventable Holy burn (Chastise, Smite): each of those repairs 2 as it goes out, and a second copy doubles it. - **Lok'delar, Stave of the Ancient Keepers** (`dark_portal_279`, 2, Two-Handed Weapon — Staff, Melee (1), 1 ATK / 2 Strike, Hunter, Rare) — "When you play a Hunter ability, your Ranged weapons have +1 ATK this turn" (`equipment:melee_weapon:0|strike_cost:2|two_handed|on_play_ability_ranged_atk:Hunter:1`). The second **play trigger** after Spiritual Healing, and the first to watch a **class icon** — see the effects-table row. It is also what moved the class-column format off `DeckManager` and onto **`CardDef`** (`parse_class_restriction` / `has_class`), so deck legality (100.2a) and this trigger now read one implementation. The card is a poor weapon on purpose — 1 ATK for a 2-cost strike, occupying the Melee slot — and pays for itself only in a deck packed with Hunter abilities AND Ranged weapons to receive the bonus. Tests: `_test_lokdelar_ranged_bonus_on_hunter_ability`, `_test_lokdelar_scope_and_wielding`, plus `_test_lokdelar_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the class argument, the amount and the two_handed flag against the REAL database — the class in particular fails silently if mistyped, leaving a trigger that watches a class no card has.
- **Resurrection** (`azeroth_86`, 4, Ability — Holy, Priest, Rare) — "Put target ally card from your graveyard into play if its cost is less than or equal to the number of resources you have" (`graveyard_to_play:Ally:1:1:own:resources`). **Pure CSV** — Ancestral Spirit's reanimate with the trailing `health_minus_1` damage mode dropped, which is the entire difference and the whole point: this one returns the ally at **full health**. Everything else is inherited: the dynamic cost cap read against `get_total_resources` (**total** resources, exhausted or not), own-graveyard-only, the graveyard browser, the 706-style resolution re-check (a card exiled out of the graveyard in response fizzles it) and `BaseAI._reanimate_action`'s highest-cost pick. The ally enters through `_bring_ally_into_play` like any other entry, so summoning sickness, its `on_enter` triggers, the uniqueness queue and the opposing-ally-enters watchers all apply. This is also the rulebook's own worked example for **305.3a** — a **Totem card counts as an ally card** in a graveyard, so it is a legal target. Tests: `_test_resurrection_reanimate`, plus `_test_resurrection_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the ABSENT damage mode against the real database.
- **King Magni Bronzebeard** (`azeroth_191`, 9-cost 6/8 Alliance Dwarf Warrior, **Unique**, Epic) — "At the end of each turn, put an Alliance Dwarf Warrior ally token with 1 ATK and 1 health into play. Dwarves in your party have protector" (`end_of_turn_create_token:token_dwarf_warrior|friendly_race_keyword:Dwarf:protector`). Two firsts, and they are one card: the engine's first **recurring** token generator (every end phase, both players' — see the effects-table row) and its first **race-scoped** aura, which is what taught the keyword read to match a race across BOTH the `tags` and `subtype` columns. The halves feed each other — every token he makes is itself a Dwarf, so it arrives already carrying protector, and the wall thickens by one body per turn while he lives. The aura reaching a **Dwarf hero** (Boris, Gorebelly, Warrax) is deliberate: "your party" is the hero and the ally row throughout the engine. Answer him by killing him — the grant is live, and 703.3 stops the tokens the moment he is gone. Ships with the `token_dwarf_warrior` row in `data/tokens.csv`. Tests: `_test_magni_spawns_a_token_each_turn`, `_test_magni_dwarf_protector_aura`.

First drain effect — see the `drain_heal_per_damage` effects-table row. Tagged `combat_instant_dmg`; AI plays it for the damage, drain not modeled.
- **Mind Spike / Mind Blast** (`azeroth_82`, 2, 1 shadow; `azeroth_80`, 5, 2 shadow — Ability — Shadow, Priest) — "Your hero deals N shadow damage to target hero or ally. Sorcery-speed (plain Ability) targeted damage + the damaged character's controller discards one card per damage dealt. First `discard_per_damage` cards — see the effects-table row.

The `discard_per_damage` rider on an ally activated power; reuses the ally-power damage path.
- **Boneshanks** (`dark_portal_201`, 3-cost 3/2 Horde Undead Warrior Ally) — "When Boneshanks is destroyed, destroy target ally" (`on_destroyed:destroy_target:ally`). First **targeted death trigger**: a mandatory direct-call choice (`pending_death_triggers` queue → `choose_death_target`) opened for the destroyed card's controller, prompted even in hotseat. See the `on_destroyed` effects-table row.
- **Purge** (`azeroth_114`, 1, Instant Ability — Elemental, Shaman) — "Destroy target ability unless it's attached to a friendly hero or ally" (`destroy_target:ability|except_friendly_attached`). - **Waz'luk** (`dark_portal_242`, 1-cost 2/1 Horde Troll Mage, Common) — "When Waz'luk enters play, he deals 1 fire damage to target hero" (`on_enter:deal_damage_to_hero:1:fire`). First enter-play effect with a **hero-only** target pool — see the effects-table row. A 1-cost 2-power body that pays for itself the moment it lands, and the burn is on the chain, so it can be answered before it resolves (but not by killing Waz'luk — 707.3). Tests: `_test_wazluk_burns_target_hero`, `_test_wazluk_hero_only_pool_and_707_3`.
- **Ra'chee** (`dark_portal_230`, 1-cost 1/2 Horde Troll Priest, Common) — "When Ra'chee enters play, he heals 2 damage from target hero or ally" (`on_enter:heal_target:2`). First enter-play trigger that HEALS, and the first mandatory targeted enter-play effect whose AI targeting is friendly rather than opposing — see the effects-table row. A 1-cost body that repairs 2 damage the turn it lands; the heal is on the chain, so it can be answered before it resolves (but not by killing Ra'chee — 707.3). Tests: `_test_rachee_heals_target_on_enter`, `_test_rachee_pool_fizzle_and_707_3`.
- **Rak Skyfury** (`azeroth_257`, 1-cost 1/1 Horde Orc Shaman, Uncommon) — "When Rak Skyfury enters play, ready your hero and one of your weapons." Galway Steamwhistle's power on a 1-cost body and as a one-shot — see the effects-table row. The point of both cards is the same: 303.2c allows one weapon per combat, so readying hero + weapon buys a **second combat in one turn**. No AI modeling beyond the shared weapon-ready heuristic. Tests: `_test_rak_skyfury_readies_on_enter`.
- **Warchief Thrall** (`azeroth_267`, 9-cost 7/8 Horde Orc Shaman, **Unique**, Epic) — "Other Horde allies in your party have +3 ATK and +3 health." The engine's first **two-stat** party aura and its first **alignment-filtered** one — see the effects-table row for the three filters and for why the health half can kill on the way out. At 9 resources he is the most expensive card in the pool, and the aura is why: a board of three Horde allies gains +9/+9 the moment he lands. The counterplay is to kill him, which un-buffs the party and can take a damaged ally with it. No AI modeling — every forecast path reads `get_atk` / `get_max_hp` live, so both AIs weigh the buffed bodies correctly on offence and defence for free. Tests: `_test_warchief_thrall_aura`.
- **Masten Everspirit** (`azeroth_250`, 5-cost 4/2 Horde Tauren Shaman, Rare) — "When Masten Everspirit is destroyed, you may put him from your graveyard into your hand." The first **optional** death trigger with no target and no cost, and it needed no new choice point at all: it reuses Wisp's graveyard-return point at cost 0 — see the effects-table row. **The body is the point**: 4 ATK on 2 health trades with almost anything and then comes straight back, so the opponent has to answer him with **exile** (Shred Soul, Cannibalize, Ophelia) or by making the recursion moot rather than by killing him. Note the return only happens on a DESTRUCTION — a bounce, an exile or a discard never fires it. Tests: `_test_masten_everspirit_returns`.
- **Karkas Deathhowl** (`azeroth_247`, 4-cost 2/3 Horde Orc Warlock) — "When Karkas Deathhowl enters play, you may put target ally into its owner's hand" (`on_enter:return_to_hand_ally`). Sister Rot's optional enter-play shape with Withdraw's bounce as the effect; pool is `get_death_target_targets` (any in-play ally, either party, 706-legal) — **the source herself included**, since she is in play by the time her own trigger fires and the printed text says simply "target ally". A consequence worth knowing: the pool is therefore never empty, so unlike Sister Rot the choice ALWAYS opens and Esc (decline) is the only way out when nothing is worth bouncing. Resolution re-checks 706 then `move_card` to the OWNER's hand — damage/exhaust/buffs reset (400.6a), attachments destroyed (400.5), a combat proposal it was part of fizzles (601.3), and a token ceases to exist instead of reaching a hand. AI: bounces the opponent's most expensive ally, declines rather than bouncing its own. Tests: `_test_karkas_bounces_ally`, `_test_karkas_self_bounce_decline_and_fizzle`.
- **Bhenn Checks-the-Sky** (`dark_portal_199`, 2-cost 2/1 Horde Tauren Druid, **Instant Ally**) — "When Bhenn Checks-the-Sky enters play, you may exhaust target ally" (`on_enter:exhaust_ally`). Karkas Deathhowl's optional enter-play trigger with an exhaust as the effect — see the effects-table row. **AI: held as a combat instant** (`combat_instant_exhaust_on_enter`), flashed in during an opposing ally's combat proposal so her exhaust fizzles it at the 601.3 recheck — Exhaustion's role on a body, minus the ability to answer an attacking hero. In the Grennan deck (2 copies, replacing Ancestral Spirit). Tests: `_test_bhenn_exhausts_ally`, `_test_ai_bhenn_freezes_proposal`.
- **Dark Cleric Jocasta** (`azeroth_233`, 6-cost 3/5 Horde Undead Priest, Uncommon) — "When Dark Cleric Jocasta enters play, you may put target ally card from your graveyard into your hand" (`on_enter:graveyard_to_hand_ally`). Call the Spirit's fetch on a body, and the **first enter-play target that lives in a graveyard rather than in play** — which is why it is picked in the graveyard browser instead of by clicking the board, and why it is the one enter-play effect whose submission gate skips the in-play `_is_legal_target` check. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_jocasta_fetches_ally_from_graveyard`, `_test_jocasta_optional_fizzle_and_707_3`, `_test_jocasta_ai_and_totem`.
- **Staff of Dominance** (`dark_portal_282`, 4, Two-Handed Weapon — Staff, Melee (1), 1 ATK / 5 Strike, DrMaPrShLo, Uncommon) — "Staff of Dominance enters play exhausted. (X), [Activate], Destroy Staff of Dominance ➜ Gain control of target ally with cost X. Use only on your turn" (`equipment:melee_weapon:0|strike_cost:5|two_handed|enters_play_exhausted|activated_power:X:gain_control_ally:0::ally:activate_sacrifice_self|require_turn_player`). The engine's first **permanent** control change (Helwen's and Nyn'jah's are conditional links), the first `enters_play_exhausted` **equipment**, and the card that split the self-destroy cost into a tap-symbol and a no-tap flavour (`activate_sacrifice_self`). Those last two are what price it: a four-cost weapon that does nothing on the turn it lands, then eats itself and X resources to take a body — but the body is yours for good. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_staff_of_dominance_steals`, `_test_staff_of_dominance_gates`, `_test_staff_of_dominance_fizzle_and_ai`.
- **Helwen** (`azeroth_126`, 4-cost 2/2 Warlock Succubus Demon, **Pet (1)**, Rare) — "You may choose not to ready Helwen during your ready step. [Activate] ➜ While Helwen remains exhausted, you control target ally" (`may_stay_exhausted|activated_power:0:control_ally_while_exhausted:0::ally`). The engine's first **ally** theft and its first borrowed-control link whose condition isn't Nyn'jah's — which is what moved the condition onto the stolen card (`stolen_condition`) and put a re-check inside `GameLogic.ready_card`, so the link now ends however the thief readies. Also the first card that makes the ready step a **decision**: the ready loop leaves her exhausted and opens a direct-call choice (501.1a — the step's automatic actions never use the chain). The price is exact and recurring — keeping the ally costs her untap every single turn, so she is a 4-cost body that never attacks while she is working. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_helwen_steals_ally`, `_test_helwen_optional_ready`, `_test_helwen_scope_and_ai`.
- **Nyn'jah** (`dark_portal_226`, 4-cost 3/3 Horde Troll Rogue) — "When Nyn'jah enters play, you may ready target opposing equipment. You control that equipment while Nyn'jah remains in your party" (`on_enter:steal_equipment`). First **control-change effect that isn't permanent** and the first one aimed at equipment, which is what introduced the borrowed-control link (`CardInstance.stolen_by` / `stolen_ids`, re-checked in `GameLogic.move_card`) — and, because control can change without anything being *played*, what turned uniqueness from a per-resolution check into the **state-based queue** described under Card uniqueness. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_nynjah_steals_equipment`, `_test_nynjah_control_reverts`, `_test_nynjah_targeting_and_slot_conflict`, `_test_uniqueness_is_state_based`.
- **Sister Rot** (`dark_portal_233`, 5-cost 5/2 Horde Undead Priest) — "When Sister Rot enters play, you may destroy target ability" (`on_enter:destroy_ability`). Hur Shieldsmasher / Zygore Bladebreaker's **optional** enter-play destroy on Burn Away's target pool: `get_enter_play_ability_targets` (= `get_destroy_kind_candidates(..., "ability")` filtered by 706), so ongoing abilities, totems and attachments of EITHER party are legal — the caster's own included (no Purge-style friendly clause) — and equipment is not. No legal target → the trigger silently doesn't fire (no pop-up); with one, Esc declines (`decline_enter_play_effect`, the shared `optional` flag). AI (playtest's optional-destroy branch, shared with Hur/Zygore): destroys the opponent's most expensive ability, declines rather than hitting its own. Tests: `_test_sister_rot_destroys_ability`, `_test_sister_rot_decline_and_fizzle`.
- **Ghost Wolf** (`azeroth_110`, 2, Ability — Enhancement, Shaman, Uncommon) — "Ongoing: Exhaust your hero ➜ If your hero is defending, remove all attacking allies from combat" (`ongoing|activated_power:0:remove_attacking_allies:0:::exhaust_hero+no_activate`). Blink's remove-from-combat effect as a **repeatable ongoing power** rather than a one-shot card, narrowed to attacking **allies** — see the effects-table row. Two things are worth holding onto. (1) **What it buys is asymmetric**: 602.4 + 603.1b cancel the damage in both directions, so the opponent loses the attacker's exhaust (602.1), any weapon strike and anything else they committed, while you pay one hero exhaust — on their turn, when your hero was not attacking anyway. The only real cost is that the hero can no longer **protect** afterwards. (2) It is the third card in the family whose "if" clause is an **effect condition rather than a use gate** (after Point Blank and against Avanthera's genuine gate), and the contrast is the clearest in the pool: Avanthera's `remove_self_from_combat` is refused outside combat because "if [she] is in combat" is 602.3b legality, while this one is legal anywhere and fizzles. AI (`BaseAI.ghost_wolf_action`, held out of `_get_ally_power_actions` entirely — the generic untargeted branch would exhaust our hero at every window for a clause that is almost never true — and wired into all three `decide_action`s): fired exactly when the card answers something, i.e. an open defend window with our hero as the defender and an **ally** attacking, with no value bar beyond that, since the removal is close to free. Tests: `_test_ghost_wolf_removes_attacking_ally`, `_test_ghost_wolf_scope_and_fizzle`, `_test_ai_ghost_wolf`, plus `_test_ghost_wolf_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the effect key, the `exhaust_hero` cost, the `no_activate` flag and the mute token against the REAL database — each fails silently if mistyped.
- **Avanthera** (`dark_portal_154`, 2-cost 3/2 Alliance Night Elf Hunter, Ranged, Common) — "(1) ➜ If Avanthera is in combat, remove her from combat" (`activated_power:1:remove_self_from_combat:0:::no_activate`). The engine's first **self**-removal from combat, and the first card whose legality gate is the rules term "in combat" — which is narrower than it looks: **602.1** says a proposed attacker "becomes an attacker … **but is not yet in combat (602.3)**", so the power is legal in the **defend window only**, from either combat role. See the effects-table row for why 602.4 + 603.1b make it cancel the combat **in both directions**. The card is a resource-cheap, repeatable tax on committing to a fight: the opponent exhausts an attacker, maybe strikes a weapon, maybe spends a pump, and then there is no combat at all. **AI (`BaseAI.avanthera_escape_action`, held out of `_get_ally_power_actions` entirely — the untargeted else-branch would fire it in every defend window, including fights she WINS — and wired into all three `decide_action`s):** fired only when the combat would actually kill her, asked through the shared `combat_kills` predicate, so a Devotion Aura reduction, a Brigg-style finisher and a Plagueborn Meatwall reflect are all counted for free. Unlike Katsin's shield there is deliberately **no `card_value_score` comparison on a trade**: escaping cancels the conclusion both ways, so the kill is lost either way and the only question left is whether we keep a 2-cost 3/2 for 1 resource. The engine's own gate is the authority on timing (the hook asks `can_submit`), so the AI cannot drift from the rule. Tests: `_test_avanthera_removes_herself_from_combat`, `_test_avanthera_attacking_and_repeatable`, `_test_ai_avanthera`, plus `_test_avanthera_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the effect key, the (1) cost, the ABSENT `TARGETS` and the `no_activate` flag against the REAL database — the pinning matters precisely BECAUSE the card is pure CSV on the data side: a typo would leave a silent vanilla 3/2 with nothing to fail on
- **Warmaster Hork** (`dark_portal_241`, 7-cost 6/5 Horde Orc Warrior, Ferocity, Rare, **Orc Hero Required**) — "(2) → Warmaster Hork has +1 ATK this turn" (`requires_hero_race:Orc|activated_power:2:buff_atk_self:1:::no_activate`). First activated power that buffs its OWN source rather than a target — see the effects-table row. Ferocity plus a repeatable, resource-only pump makes him a mana sink the turn he lands: 6 ATK is the floor, not the ceiling, and the buffs stack per use. `requires_hero_race:Orc` is deck legality only (100.2b). Test: `_test_warmaster_hork_pump`.

In the Gorebelly deck (1 copy, replacing Fa'tafi). Test: `_test_sunder_armor_destroys_armor`.
- **Crushing Blow** (`dark_portal_120`, 4, Ability — Fury, Warrior, Common) — "Choose one or both: Destroy target armor; or destroy target weapon" (`choose_destroy:armor:weapon`). The engine's first "choose one or both" over two INDEPENDENTLY optional targets — see the effects-table row for how it differs from both Totemic Call's condition-gated multi-modal and Ravenous Bite's two-mandatory-target shape, and for the UI's own small skip/step-back state machine. Sorcery speed, so it's a main-phase answer rather than a combat trick, and it can two-for-one a board carrying both a weapon and armor. - **Dismantle** (`azeroth_96`, 2, Ability — Combat, Rogue, Uncommon) — “Destroy target equipment” (`destroy_target:equipment`). **Pure CSV** — Shattering Blow's (`azeroth_168`) printed effect verbatim at half the cost, with a class restriction in place of the neutral card's open legality, so it needed no engine, UI or AI code. **“Equipment” is the whole pool** (rule 304 — every Equipment card is armor, a weapon or an Item), so unlike Sunder Armor's narrowed `destroy_target:armor` it takes a Krol Blade, a Lionheart Helm and Ramstein's Lightning Bolts alike; **either player's is legal** (no “opposing” clause), so a human may break their own, and the highlight probe goes dark with no equipment in play (706.2). Re-checked at resolution (706 / 4217 — equipment destroyed or made Untargetable in response fizzles the destroy, the card is still spent). **Sorcery speed is the real distinction from Sunder Armor**, not the pool: its CSV type cell was already correct as a plain `Ability`, so it can never be flashed into a combat window to strip a defender's DEF before the prevention point opens (717.2c) — that is the 1-cost instant's job, while this is a cheap main-phase answer to anything equipped. Type-line tag is plain `Combat`, **NOT** `Combat Talent`, so no rule 100.2c spec restriction — it mixes with any Rogue spec. AI: no new code — it inherits the Burn Away / Shattering Blow branch of `_targeted_instant_actions` (opposing targets only, printed cost ≥ the spell's own). That value bar does real work here where it was nearly free at Shattering Blow's cost 4: at 2 the card is **held** rather than spent on a 1-cost weapon. Test: `_test_dismantle_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the segment, the cost and the sorcery speed against the REAL database, and asserts the pool still matches Shattering Blow's — the pinning matters precisely BECAUSE there is no engine code: a typo would leave a silent 2-cost do-nothing with nothing to fail on.

AI: the proactive `destroy_ability` branch of `_get_ally_power_actions` explicitly skips `sacrifice_self` powers (Lafiel only spends a tap; Mildred spends herself) — she is cashed in solely by `doomed_sacrifice_action`. Pairs with Elemental Focus, whose discount buys an extra point of damage. See the `divided_damage` effects-table row and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Lightning Storm". - **Healing Touch** (`azeroth_22`, 3, Ability — Restoration, Druid) — "Your hero heals 10 damage from target hero or ally" (`heal_target:10`). Natural Selection's heal mode as a standalone card, and the first hand card on that key — see the effects-table row. The AI's targeting is the one place the printed pool and the heuristic deliberately part company: the card can legally heal the opponent, and the AI never will. Test: `_test_healing_touch`.
- **Totemic Call** (`azeroth_117`, 4, Instant Ability — Elemental, Shaman, Rare) — "Choose one or more: If you have an Air Totem, ready your hero and one of your Melee weapons; or if you have an Earth Totem, allies in your party have +1 ATK this turn; or if you have a Fire Totem, your hero deals 2 fire damage to target hero or ally; or if you have a Water Totem, draw two cards" (`mode_if_totem:air:ready_hero_and_melee_weapon|mode_if_totem:earth:party_allies_atk_this_turn:1|mode_if_totem:fire:deal_damage_to_target:2:fire|mode_if_totem:water:draw:2`). The engine's first **multi-modal** link — "choose one or MORE", where every earlier modal card (Natural Selection, Escape Artist) chooses exactly one — and its first per-mode **availability condition**; see the `mode_if_totem` effects-table row for both, and for the errata's whole-card interrupt. **The card is a payoff for the totem framework**: it does nothing on an empty board and is a four-for-one with all four elements down, so its real cost is the four totems that had to survive. Note the shipped totems are exactly one per element — Searing (fire), Earthbind (earth), Healing Stream (water), Windfury (air) — and each carries **Totem (1)** uniqueness on its own element, so "all four modes" is reachable but takes the whole set. **Instant speed is worth having**: flashed into a combat window the Air mode readies a spent weapon for a second strike (303.2c) and the Fire mode burns an attacker. Two things are easy to get backwards: the Earth grant is **not** "while attacking" (it counts on defence, unlike Rayder's), and the Air mode readies **Melee** weapons only, unlike Galway Steamwhistle's "one of your weapons". **UI:** one yes/no prompt per AVAILABLE mode in printed order — an unavailable mode is never offered, because the prompts are built from the engine's own `available_totem_modes()` rather than from the def, so the interface and the legality gate cannot disagree. The fire mode gets **no** yes/no: it opens the ordinary targeting flow, where Esc / right-click declines **that mode alone** and the remaining prompts continue. Nothing reaches the engine until the last answer (The Love Potion's pre-submission rule), so cancelling is free, and answering "no" to everything cancels the cast — "one or more" cannot be satisfied by zero. The Air mode's **weapon pick is deliberately not part of that flow**: it is a resolution choice (709.2b) and opens later through Galway's existing point, only when there are two or more. **AI** (`BaseAI._multi_modal_action`): the modes don't compete — they are all free once the card is paid for — so the policy is simply "take every available mode that has work to do": Air only when something would actually ready, Earth only with an ally to receive it, Water only with hand room (503.2a) and a deck to draw from, Fire only with a target worth burning (a killable opposing ally, else their hero), which is also how it avoids announcing a target it doesn't want — the errata makes a bad one fatal to the whole card. With nothing worth taking it holds the card, since zero modes is not a legal announcement. Tests: `_test_totemic_call_modes_and_availability`, `_test_totemic_call_all_four_modes`, `_test_totemic_call_resolution_rechecks`, `_test_totemic_call_weapon_choice_and_scope`, `_test_ai_totemic_call`, plus `_test_totemic_call_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins all four elements, all four inner effects, the instant speed and the "exactly one targeted mode" property against the REAL database — a mistyped element is completely silent, leaving a mode no totem can ever unlock.
- **Natural Selection** (`azeroth_27`, 3, Instant Ability — Restoration, Druid) — "Choose one: Your hero deals 3 nature damage to target hero or ally; or your hero heals 3 damage from target hero or ally" (`mode:deal_damage_to_target:3:nature|mode:heal_target:3`). First **modal** link (rule 707.1c) — see the `mode:` effects-table row.

See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_cold_blood_hero_damage_destroys_ally`, `_test_cold_blood_scope_and_expiry`.
- **Gouge** (`azeroth_99`, 1, Instant Ability — Combat Combo, Rogue) — "Exhaust target hero or ally. It can't ready during its controller's next ready step" (`exhaust_target:hero_or_ally|gouge_cant_ready`). Exhaustion/Bash's freeze plus a one-shot ready-lock (a `gouge_skip_ready` counter consumed at the target controller's next ready step). Tagged `combat_instant_exhaust` — the AI answers an opposing attacker (hero or ally). See the `gouge_cant_ready` effects-table row.
- **Keyword grants** (`grant_keyword_target:KIND:KEYWORD`) — general framework for "target ally has KEYWORD this turn", reusing the existing target kinds and the Hidden Enemies `grant_<keyword>` Buff (see the effects-table row). First cards: **Sneak** (`azeroth_152`, 1, Instant Ability, Alliance): "Target ally has elusive this turn" (`grant_keyword_target:ally:elusive`) — a combat save, cast on our own proposed defender so the 601.3 recheck fizzles the proposal without the attacker exhausting (`BaseAI.elusive_save_action`, tagged `combat_instant_save_elusive`). **Into the Fray** (`azeroth_153`, 1, Ability, Horde): "Target ally in your party has ferocity this turn" (`grant_keyword_target:friendly_ally:ferocity`) — sorcery-speed, friendly-only; the AI aims it only at summoning-sick allies with ATK > 0.

"With damage on it" is read as pre-existing damage — see the effects-table row and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Brigg".
- **Board-wide keyword auras** (`all_allies_keyword:KEYWORD`) — general framework for "Ongoing: All allies have KEYWORD", read live inside `_has_keyword` (see the effects-table row; this is what added the optional `state` param there). First cards: **Lust for Battle** (`azeroth_154`, 4, Ability, Horde): "Ongoing: All allies have ferocity" (`ongoing|all_allies_keyword:ferocity`). **From the Shadows** (`azeroth_151`, 5, Ability, Alliance): "Ongoing: All allies have elusive" (`ongoing|all_allies_keyword:elusive`) — with it out, no ally on either board can be proposed as a defender, so every attack goes at a hero (Protectors still protect). Both are symmetric: they grant to the OPPONENT's allies as well, and to allies that arrive after the aura.
