- **Sentry Gwynn** (`azeroth_215`, 5-cost 3/4 Alliance Night Elf Warrior, Rare) — "Your hero has elusive" (`hero_keyword:elusive`). The keyword aura above narrowed to the controller's own **hero**, and the first one printed on an ally body rather than an ongoing Ability — see the `hero_keyword` effects-table row. While she is in play the opponent can't propose the hero as a defender at all (601.2b), so every attack must go at an ally; the hero can still protect (602.2). Test: `_test_sentry_gwynn_hero_elusive`.
- **Treesong** (`azeroth_220`, 5-cost 4/4 Alliance Night Elf Druid, Uncommon) — “Your hero has protector” (`hero_keyword:protector`). Sentry Gwynn's controller-scoped hero keyword aura with the keyword swapped, and **pure CSV** — see the `hero_keyword` effects-table row for why it needed no engine code. Draconian Deflector now carries the same recipe. Read live off her being in play, so the grant lifts the instant she is killed: unlike an equipment granting the same thing, the answer to Treesong is to attack her. Test: `_test_treesong_hero_protector`.
- **Stalwart Protector** (`azeroth_148`, 1, Instant Ability — Protection, Warrior, Uncommon) — "Ongoing: Your hero has protector" (`ongoing|hero_keyword:protector`). Treesong's grant on an ongoing Ability instead of a body, and **pure CSV** — `_hero_keyword_aura` already scans the hero_row, and `get_legal_protectors` already asks `_has_keyword` of every card there including the hero instance. **Speed is the whole card**: an Instant Ability carrying `ongoing` routes to `play_ability` (`is_ongoing_def`), so at 1 resource it can be flashed into the opponent's ATTACK window — before the protect point opens (602.2) — turning a hero into a blocker mid-combat, which neither Treesong (a 5-cost body) nor Draconian Deflector (4-cost armor) can do. It composes harmlessly with Warrax's timed flip and stacks with any other grant; 602.2's other gates still apply (an **exhausted** hero can't protect, and 602.2b stops it protecting itself). No AI modeling — it is played as a vanilla 1-cost ongoing, and every combat query reads `_has_keyword` live, so both AIs respect the grant for free. Tests: `_test_stalwart_protector`.
- **Kailis Truearc** (`azeroth_189`, 1-cost 1/1 Alliance Night Elf Hunter, Rare) — "Kailis Truearc has +2 ATK and +2 health while there are four or more allies in your party" (`buff_while_party_size:4:2:2`). First conditional self-modifier to move **max health** as well as ATK, which is what added the priority-gate state-based death sweep (`StackResolver.drain_state_based_deaths`): a grant can now lapse while a damaged card just sits there, with nothing having been destroyed to trigger `_check_aura_loss_deaths`. See the effects-table row. Test: `_test_kailis_truearc_party_size_buff`.
- **Ritual Sacrifice** (`dark_portal_112`, 2, Ability — Demonology, Warlock, Common) — "Ongoing: Destroy an ally in your party ➜ Your hero deals 1 shadow damage to target hero or ally" (`ongoing|activated_power:0:deal_damage_to_target:1:shadow:hero_or_ally:sacrifice_ally+no_activate|hero_deals_damage`). Mezzik Darkspark's two-pick sacrifice power hosted on an **ongoing Ability** body (Mana Agate's shape) rather than on an ally, and the card that introduced the `hero_deals_damage` rider — see the effects-table row. **Free and repeatable**: no `[Activate]` tap symbol and no resource cost (`no_activate`, 701.2), so it is a sac outlet limited only by how many allies the party can feed it, and it never exhausts. It lives in the hero_row, so it can never eat ITSELF and the pool is strictly the controller's own ally_row (totems included, 305.3a). The fixed amount is what separates it from Mezzik in the one case that matters: like every `sacrifice_ally` cost the sacrifice is paid at **ANNOUNCEMENT** (707.1e), so it cannot be answered at all. Target is re-checked at resolution (706/4217 — a target that left play fizzles the damage while the cost stays paid), and per 707.3 killing the SOURCE in response does not stop it. AI (`_get_ally_power_actions`, its own branch beside Mezzik's — the two split on `amount_raw == "sac_atk"`, because a FIXED amount makes the sacrifice's ATK irrelevant and the two halves independent): **which ally to eat** is the only real question, and the answer is *only one that is about to die anyway* — `_is_doomed`, the same "we are losing it regardless" test Kavai / Moira / Mildred cash themselves in on (a lethal opposing link on the chain, or death at the open combat window's conclusion). That gate is the whole heuristic, and it matters far more here than for Mezzik: with no tap and no cost, an ungated branch would eat the party for 1 damage a body every turn. **Where to point it** is then nothing special — the body is already free, so the damage is pure upside and it reuses the ordinary damage targeting (`find_lethal` → `rank_lethal_targets`, then most-damaged) rather than inventing a second heuristic. The forecast goes through `StackResolver.preview_hero_damage_amount`, so **Chromatic Cloak's +1, Shadowform's typed bonus and World in Flames' doubling all count** when asking what is lethal — the packet is hero-sourced, which is exactly what those auras read, and a Cloak can genuinely change which target it picks. Tests: `_test_ritual_sacrifice_eats_ally_for_damage`, `_test_ritual_sacrifice_repeatable_and_pool`, `_test_ritual_sacrifice_response_and_fizzle`, plus `_test_ritual_sacrifice_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins `no_activate`, the two-pick TARGETS and the rider against the REAL database (each would fail silently if mistyped).
- **Mezzik Darkspark** (`azeroth_207`, 1-cost 1/1 Alliance Gnome Warlock, Uncommon) — "[Activate], Destroy an ally in your party → Mezzik Darkspark deals X shadow damage to target hero or ally, where X is the ATK of the destroyed ally" (`activated_power:0:deal_damage_to_target:sac_atk:shadow:hero_or_ally:sacrifice_ally`). First power whose damage is read off the COST it just paid — see the effects-table row. Needed no UI work: the two-phase sacrifice flow keys on `power_sacrifice_is_separate` and delegates phase 2 by target kind, which already covers `hero_or_ally`. Tests: `_test_mezzik_darkspark_sacrifice_damage`, `_test_mezzik_sacrifice_killed_in_response_and_ai`.
- **Vexra Darkfall** (`azeroth_265`, 5-cost 5/2 Horde Undead Mage, Uncommon) — "When Vexra Darkfall is destroyed, she deals 1 arcane damage to target hero for each card in its controller's hand" (`on_destroyed:deal_damage_hero_per_hand:1:arcane`). Second targeted death trigger after Boneshanks, and the one that made that queue **effect-aware** (`key`/`args` entries + `get_death_trigger_targets`, the shape the turn-start and combat trigger queues already use) — so a death trigger now brings its own target pool and fizzles independently of the others. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_vexra_darkfall_death_burn`, `_test_vexra_hand_read_at_resolution_and_ai`.
- **Nightbloom** (`azeroth_211`, 2-cost 1/1 Alliance Night Elf Druid, Rare) — "(1), [Activate] -> You may put a card from your hand into your resource row face down and exhausted" (`activated_power:1:put_hand_card_as_resource:0`). First **ramp** effect in the pool, and the first card to exercise **rule 412.1c** (an effect-placed resource is additional and doesn't consume the one-per-turn placement). See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_nightbloom_extra_resource`, `_test_nightbloom_decline_gates_and_ai`.
- **Seraph the Exalted** (`dark_portal_190`, 5-cost 1/5 Alliance Human Priest, **Human Hero Required**, Rare) — "[Activate] → Put an ally card from your hand into play if its cost is less than or equal to the number of resources you have" (`requires_hero_race:Human|activated_power:0:put_hand_ally_into_play:0`). The first effect that puts a card into play **from a hand**, and the first repeatable one from any zone — Ancestral Spirit's "cost <= the number of resources you have" cap (`get_total_resources`) reached every turn instead of once, off a body that is itself a 5-health wall. Nothing is paid, so the ally dodges its own cost entirely while still entering play normally (summoning sickness, `on_enter` triggers, uniqueness, the opposing-ally-enters watchers). Mandatory and choice-not-target, so the pick is made at resolution and the opponent can neither answer nor 706 it. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_seraph_puts_ally_into_play`, `_test_seraph_cap_gates_and_ai`.
- **Coup de Grâce** (`azeroth_93`, 2, Ability — Assassination, Rogue) — "Destroy target exhausted ally" (`destroy_target:exhausted_ally`). Sorcery-speed removal restricted to exhausted allies of either party; fizzles if the target readies before resolution. AI destroys the most valuable exhausted opposing ally. See the `destroy_target:exhausted_ally` effects-table row.
- **Trophy Kill** (`dark_portal_40`, 3, Instant Ability — Marksmanship, Hunter) / **Prey on the Weak** (`dark_portal_85`, 2, Instant Ability — Assassination, Rogue) — "Destroy target ally with cost 4 or more" / "…cost 4 or less" (`destroy_target:ally|target_cost_min:4` / `|target_cost_max:4`). Mirror cards banded on the target's **printed** cost; both bands are inclusive, so a cost-4 ally is legal for either. First cost-restricted removal — see the `target_cost_min` / `target_cost_max` effects-table row. Test: `_test_cost_banded_destroy`.
- **Life Tap** (`azeroth_128`, 2, Instant Ability — Affliction, Warlock, Uncommon) — "As an additional cost to play Life Tap, put 2 damage on your hero. Draw two cards" (`draw:2|play_cost_put_damage_hero:2`). The engine's first additional play cost paid in **health** rather than in a card, and the first `play_cost_*` rider that announces **nothing** — the card names your own hero, so there is no pick, no gate and nothing that can make it unplayable. See the effects-table row for why 405.3's *put* makes the payment unpreventable, watcher-silent and potentially lethal. The CSV type cell said `Ability`, which would have cost the card its whole point, and was corrected: instant speed is what lets you dig for an answer inside the opponent's combat window. Tests: `_test_life_tap_pays_health_for_cards`, `_test_life_tap_cost_scope_and_lethality`, `_test_ai_life_tap`.
- **Sever the Cord** (`azeroth_131`, 2, Instant Ability — Demonology, Warlock) — "As an additional cost to play Sever the Cord, destroy an ally in your party. Destroy target ally" (`destroy_target:ally|play_cost_sacrifice_ally`). First **hand card with a sacrifice additional cost**, and the first cost paid in destroyed permanents at announcement — which is also the first non-retractable announcement (`_cost_paid_irreversibly`). See the `play_cost_sacrifice_ally` effects-table row. In the Dizdemona deck (2 copies, replacing Shred Soul). Tests: `_test_sever_the_cord_sacrifice_cost`, `_test_sever_the_cord_paid_at_announcement`, `_test_ai_sever_the_cord`.
- **Shred Soul** (`dark_portal_114`, 3, Ability — Affliction, Warlock) — "Remove target ally from the game" (`destroy_target:ally|rfg_instead`). Vanquish's removal at the same speed, but exile rather than destruction — no death triggers, no graveyard. See the `rfg_instead` effects-table row. In the Radak deck (2 copies, replacing Vanquish). Test: `_test_shred_soul_removes_ally_from_game`.
- **Escape Artist** (`dark_portal_129`, 1, Instant Ability, Alliance, **Gnome Hero Required**) — "Choose one: Interrupt target ability card that's targeting your hero; or if your hero is defending, remove all attackers from combat" (`requires_hero_race:Gnome|mode:interrupt_ability:targets_your_hero|mode:remove_attackers:hero_defending`). Two firsts: the engine's first **interrupt** (rule 711 — see the `mode:interrupt_ability` effects-table row) and the first modal card mixing a **targeted** mode with a **targetless** one, which is what made modal targeting mode-aware (`mode_target_kind` / `_modal_target_ok`) and made `InputRouter.choose_modal_mode` submit straight from the mode button when the chosen mode has nothing to point at. Mode 2 is Blink's `remove_attackers:hero_defending` verbatim, so it inherits every ruling there (defend window only, 602.4, attacker stays exhausted). `requires_hero_race:Gnome` is deck legality only (100.2b). AI (`BaseAI.escape_artist_action`, tagged `combat_instant_escape` — held, never blind-played): the mode priority IS the policy. It **interrupts whenever an opposing ability on the chain targets our hero, with no value bar at all** — an ability aimed at a hero is a discard, a lasting debuff or burn that never trades, reliably worth a 1-cost card, and unlike a damage answer there is nothing to compute (`_best_interrupt_target` takes the most expensive such link by printed cost and never our own). Failing that it removes attackers only in the DEFEND window with our hero defending and the hit worth a card: the attacker is an **ally costing 3+**, or forecast incoming damage is **4+** (`_escape_dodge_worth_it` — the ATK bar is what catches a cheap ally pumped into a real threat). Tests: `_test_escape_artist_interrupts_ability`, `_test_escape_artist_remove_attackers_mode`, `_test_ai_escape_artist`.
- **Brain Freeze** (`azeroth_49`, 3, Instant Ability — Frost, Mage, Rare) — "Players can't draw cards this turn" (`no_draws_this_turn`). The engine's first prohibition on drawing, and its first **symmetric, board-wide** modifier — see the effects-table row for the scope (415.9f: drawing only, so fetches and reveal-picks are untouched) and for why a blocked draw does not deck its player. **AI (`BaseAI.brain_freeze_action`, tagged `combat_instant_brain_freeze` — held, never blind-played, and wired into all three `decide_action`s):** the obvious line — flash it in before the opponent's draw step — is a bad trade, one card and 3 resources for one card. It is played as a **counter** instead: priority is LIFO, so a Brain Freeze announced on top of a draw link resolves FIRST and the link beneath it resolves into nothing with its controller having already paid for it (412.2), which is worth several cards when the link is a completed quest's reward, a cracked Mana Agate or an Ilandre refill. `_chain_draws_cards` is the read — `_chain_heals_hero`'s shape, resolving each link's source card (`card_id` for a hand card or an ally/equipment power, `hero_id` for a hero flip) and scanning its effect segments for a draw in the positional convention (field 0 for a plain segment, field 1 for a `mode:`/`qmode:` one, field 2 for an `activated_power`), with a **modal** link contributing only its ANNOUNCED mode (707.1c). A **quest** is counted on its printed reward alone, since its `qmode:` choice is not made until resolution (709.2b) — and that is exactly the link the card wants to answer. Per 415.9f a graveyard fetch is deliberately **not** a draw link: the lock does nothing about it, so firing at one would waste the card. The AI also **holds it entirely while a draw of OURS is on the chain** — the lock is symmetric, so that would deny us, not them. Tests: `_test_brain_freeze_locks_draws`, `_test_brain_freeze_scope_and_decking`, `_test_ai_brain_freeze`.
- **Counterspell** (`azeroth_51`, 2, Instant Ability — Arcane, Mage, Rare) — "Interrupt target ability card" (`interrupt_ability`). The engine's second interrupt and its first **unconditional** one: Escape Artist's counterspell half promoted from a mode to a top-level segment, with the "targeting your hero" clause lifted out of the shared pool and onto that card's mode as the `targets_your_hero` rider. See the effects-table row. Unlike Escape Artist it goes **dark on an empty chain** (706.2 — no targetless mode to fall back on). Tests: `_test_counterspell_interrupts_any_ability`, `_test_counterspell_pool_and_fizzle`, `_test_escape_artist_keeps_hero_rider`, `_test_ai_counterspell`.
- **Blink** (`azeroth_48`, 2, Instant Ability — Arcane, Mage) — "Draw a card. If your hero is defending, remove all attackers from combat" (`draw:1|remove_attackers:hero_defending`). First remove-from-combat effect — see the `remove_attackers` effects-table row.
- **Point Blank** (`dark_portal_37`, 2, Instant Ability — Marksmanship, Hunter, Common) — "If your hero is defending, it deals 3 ranged damage to target attacker" (`deal_damage_to_target:3:ranged|target_attacker|require_hero_defending`). Two riders on the generic damage effect — see the effects-table row. It is the clearest case in the pool of the distinction between an **effect condition** and a **use restriction**: the card can be played at any priority and will simply fizzle unless the defend window is open with your own hero defending, which is what 707.1 and 709.2c together require, and which keeps it available to a future "when you play a Hunter ability" payoff. The convenience of not being nagged lives in `mute_when:hero_not_defending`, and the AI is gated on the same condition rather than on a second copy of the reasoning. In a Hunter shell it is a hard answer to a big attacker: 3 ranged, **4 under Aspect of the Hawk**, and 4 under a Chromatic Cloak, on top of whatever your hero deals back. Tests: `_test_point_blank_burns_the_attacker`, `_test_point_blank_pool_and_ai`, plus `_test_point_blank_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins both riders, the amount and the mute token against the REAL database — each fails silently if mistyped, and a missing `require_hero_defending` would leave an unconditional 3-damage instant.
- **Wing Clip** (`dark_portal_42`, 1, Instant Ability — Survival, Hunter, Common) — "Target hero or ally can't attack your hero this turn. Your hero deals 1 melee damage to that character" (`deal_damage_to_target:1:melee:cannot_attack_source`). The first **partial** attack lock — see the effects-table row and the Compulsion / taunt families section. At 1 resource it is Litori Frostburn's interrupt on a hand card: flashed into a proposal aimed at your hero, the 601.3 re-check fizzles it and the attacker doesn't even exhaust. What it deliberately can't do is save an ALLY — the lock names your hero only. Tests: `_test_wing_clip_bars_the_hero_only`, `_test_wing_clip_interrupts_the_proposal`, `_test_ai_wing_clip`, plus `_test_wing_clip_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`.
- **Fear** (`azeroth_123`, 1, Ability — Affliction, Warlock, Common) — "Put target opposing ally into its owner's hand" (`return_to_hand:opposing_ally`). Withdraw's bounce narrowed on **both** axes at once: the target kind gains the first **opposing-only** ally restriction (the mirror of Fall Back's `friendly_ally`, and the reason that narrowing is now a matched pair), and the speed drops from Instant Ability to plain Ability — so unlike Withdraw it can never interrupt a combat proposal, and is purely a proactive tempo play. Pure narrowing otherwise: no new resolution code, no UI code. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_fear_bounces_opposing_ally`, `_test_fear_scope_speed_and_tokens`, `_test_fear_fizzle_and_ai`.
- **Withdraw** (`azeroth_172`, 3, Instant Ability) — "Put target ally into its owner's hand" (`return_to_hand:ally`). First bounce effect; the counter-spell save play (bounce your own targeted ally, the opposing removal fizzles per 709.2a). See the `return_to_hand` effects-table row.
- **War Stomp** (`dark_portal_137`, 3, Instant Ability, Horde, **Tauren Hero Required**) — "Exhaust all opposing heroes and allies" (`requires_hero_race:Tauren|exhaust_all_opposing`). - **Berserking** (`dark_portal_134`, 3, Ability — Horde, **Troll Hero Required**) — "Ongoing: When your hero is dealt damage, put a berserk counter on Berserking. When your hero attacks, remove all berserk counters from Berserking. Your hero has +1 ATK this combat for each counter you removed" (`ongoing|requires_hero_race:Troll|berserk_counter_on_hero_damage|berserk_atk_on_hero_attack:1`). First card with **visible counters** (orange badge, ATK-badge corner) and the first "+N ATK this combat" grant (`GameState.combat_atk_bonus`) — see the effects-table row.
- **Blood Fury** (`dark_portal_135`, 4+X, Ability — Horde, **Orc Hero Required**, Uncommon) — "Blood Fury enters play with X fury counters on it. Ongoing: Your hero has +1 ATK while attacking for each fury counter on Blood Fury" (`ongoing|requires_hero_race:Orc|enters_with_counters:fury:X|hero_atk_while_attacking_per_counter:fury:1`). The second counter card after Berserking, and its mirror image: Berserking's counters arrive for free during the game and are **spent** on one combat, while these are **bought once with X** and never leave — a permanent hero ATK aura whose size you pay for up front. It is also the first **X-cost hand card that announces nothing else**, which is what let the X dialog submit straight from itself (`InputRouter.confirm_x_value`, `_play_needs_target`) instead of handing off to a targeting flow. See both effects-table rows. `requires_hero_race:Orc` is deck legality only (100.2b). AI (`BaseAI._counter_x_action`): X is both the price and the whole payload, so it buys the **largest affordable X** and **holds the card below X = 2** — at X = 1 it is a 5-resource ability for +1 ATK on hero attacks, which is not worth a card. Tests: `_test_blood_fury`, `_test_blood_fury_gates_and_ai`.
- **Tokens** (`data/tokens.csv`) — general framework: a separate CSV loaded into the same `CardDatabase`, non-deckable, and voided on any play → non-play move (see the Tokens section under Card data format). First cards: **Mya, Dragonling Wrangler** (`dark_portal_184`, 3-cost 2/2 Alliance Gnome Mage): "When Mya enters play, put a Mechanical Dragonling ally token with 1 ATK and 1 health into play" (`on_enter:create_token:token_mechanical_dragonling`) — first token creator. **Tooga's Quest** (`azeroth_359`, Quest, pay 3 during your turn): "Reward: Put a unique Turtle ally token named Tooga with 1 ATK and 1 health into play. At the start of your next turn, remove Tooga from the game. If you do, draw two cards" (`create_token:token_tooga|require_turn_player`, with `rfg_self_next_turn:draw:2` on the token) — first token from a quest reward and first delayed self-removal trigger. Tooga is Unique (414.3a). No AI modeling needed: Mya is played as a plain ally and the quest completion is generic.
- **Predatory Strikes** (`azeroth_29`, 1, Ability - Feral **Talent**, Druid, Rare) - "Feral Hero Required. Ongoing: While your hero is in bear form or cat form, it has +2 ATK while attacking" (`ongoing|hero_atk_while_attacking_in_form:bear+cat:2`). The first form-gated ATK aura, and the third consumer of `form_state` after Thangal's gate and Natural Defenses' DEF aura - the one that made the form read a single implementation on `GameState` (`hero_form_states`) rather than a resolver-only scan. See the effects-table row. The offensive counterpart to Natural Defenses: both are 1-cost Feral ongoings that pay off only while a Form is up. Tests: `_test_predatory_strikes_form_gated_atk`.
- **Natural Defenses** (`azeroth_26`, 1, Instant Ability — Feral, Druid, Rare) — "Ongoing: Each of your armor has +1 DEF while your hero is in bear form" (`ongoing|armor_def_in_form:bear:1`). The first effect to touch armor DEF, so it is the card that funnelled every DEF read through `StackResolver.get_armor_def` — see the effects-table row. The second real consumer of `form_state` after Thangal's gate, and the first CONTINUOUS one. Pairs with any bear-form source (Bear Form, Bash, Maul) and turns a DEF 0 armor into a shielder. Tests: `_test_natural_defenses_armor_def_aura`.
- **Stoneform** (`dark_portal_132`, 2, Instant Ability, Alliance, **Dwarf Hero Required**, Common) — “Destroy any number of abilities attached to your hero. Ongoing: Each of your armor has +1 DEF” (`requires_hero_race:Dwarf|ongoing|destroy_own_hero_attachments|armor_def_bonus:1`). Two halves that answer the same question from opposite ends — it **removes** what is already stuck on your hero and then **hardens** the armor against what comes next. The destroy is the engine's first “any number” choice over cards **in play** (Poison Water's shape, but on the board rather than in a graveyard), and the aura is Natural Defenses without the form gate. See both effects-table rows. `requires_hero_race:Dwarf` is deck legality only (100.2b). Tests: `_test_stoneform`.
- **Damage prevention & unpreventable damage** — general framework: `GameLogic.prevent` is the single application point (see the Armor damage prevention section), unpreventability is opt-in per packet and derived from the source, and prevention POINTS are suppressed for packets that can't be prevented. First cards: **Lionheart Helm** (`azeroth_297`, 4, Armor — Plate, Head (1), DEF 2): "Damage dealt by your hero can't be prevented" (`equipment:head:2|hero_damage_unpreventable`). **Annihilator** (`azeroth_312`, 2, Weapon — Axe, Melee (1), 3 ATK / strike 2): "Combat damage dealt by your hero with Annihilator can't be prevented" (`equipment:melee_weapon:0|strike_cost:2|combat_damage_unpreventable`). **Brother Rhone** (`dark_portal_157`, 2-cost 0/1 Alliance Dwarf Paladin, Protector): "Prevent all combat damage that would be dealt to Brother Rhone by attacking allies" (`prevent_combat_damage_from_attacking_allies`) — the first prevention shield that isn't armor. Tests: `_test_lionheart_helm_unpreventable`, `_test_annihilator_unpreventable`, `_test_brother_rhone_shield`.
- **Hootie** (`dark_portal_34`, 2-cost 2/2 Owl **Pet**, Hunter, neutral) — "Opposing heroes and allies have -1 ATK" (`opposing_characters_atk_mod:-1`). First ATK aura pointed at the opponent, and the first static aura printed on an ALLY body rather than an ongoing Ability — so it can be attacked off the board, and it costs the controller their one Pet slot (414.3b). See the effects-table row. Test: `_test_hootie_opposing_atk_aura`.
- **Elemental Focus** (`azeroth_108`, 2, Ability — Elemental **Talent**, Shaman) — "Elemental Hero Required. Ongoing: You pay (1) less to play Elemental abilities, to a minimum of (1)" (`ongoing|ability_cost_mod_by_tag:Elemental:-1:1`). First **cost-reduction aura**, and the first aura read out of `GameState.get_play_cost` — see the effects-table row. "Elemental Hero Required" is the Talent-spec restriction the deck authorizer already enforces via rule 100.2c (`Elemental Talent` in the tags column), not a new in-game gate, exactly as with Shadowform's "Shadow Hero Required". Test: `_test_elemental_focus_cost_aura`.
- **Diplomacy** (`dark_portal_128`, 3, Ability, Alliance, **Human Hero Required**, Uncommon) — "Ongoing: You pay (1) less to play allies, to a minimum of (1)" (`ongoing|requires_hero_race:Human|ally_cost_mod:-1:1`). Elemental Focus' cost aura filtered by **card type** instead of by type-line tag — the first cost reduction aimed at bodies rather than at spells, and the card that moved the "is this an ally CARD" predicate onto `CardDef` (`is_ally_card` / `is_totem`), so a Totem is discounted like any other ally in every zone-agnostic reader at once. See the effects-table row. Test: `_test_diplomacy_ally_cost_aura`.
- **Nature's Swiftness** (`azeroth_28`, 3, Ability — Restoration **Talent**, Druid) — "Restoration Hero Required. You pay (5) less to play your next card this turn" (`next_card_cost_mod:-5`). First **cost discount that isn't an aura**: a one-shot, player-scoped, this-turn grant consumed by the next card played, read out of `GameState.get_play_cost` like Elemental Focus' aura but held on `PlayerState` like Rapid Fire's grant. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_natures_swiftness_discount`, `_test_natures_swiftness_ai_gate`.
- **Margaret Fowl** (`dark_portal_179`, 4-cost 5/3 Alliance Human Rogue) — "You pay 1 less to strike with weapons. Opponents pay 1 more to strike with weapons" (`strike_cost_mod:-1:1`). First strike-cost aura, and the first effect that makes an opponent's weapon *unaffordable* rather than merely worse. See the effects-table row. Test: `_test_margaret_fowl_strike_cost_aura`.
- **Ilandre Moonspear** (`dark_portal_168`, 4-cost 2/4 Alliance Night Elf Druid, Elusive, **Night Elf Hero Required**) — "[Activate] → Put your hand on the bottom of your deck, then draw that many cards" (`requires_hero_race:Night Elf|activated_power:0:hand_to_deck_draw:0`). Crown of the Earth's reward effect on a repeatable ally power — see the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_ilandre_moonspear_power`, `_test_ilandre_empty_hand_and_ai_gate`.
- **Lhurg Venomblade** (`azeroth_199`, 4-cost 2/2 Alliance Dwarf Rogue) — "[Activate] → Destroy target exhausted ally" (`activated_power:0:destroy_ally:0::exhausted_ally`). First activated power on the `exhausted_ally` target kind, which lifts Coup de Grâce's restriction out of the instant path and into the shared power-targeting machinery — see the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_lhurg_destroys_exhausted_ally`, `_test_lhurg_fizzle_and_ai`.
- **Power Word: Fortitude** (`azeroth_83`, 3, Ability — Discipline, Priest, Common) — "Attach to target hero or ally. Ongoing: Attached character has +5 health" (`ongoing|attach:hero_or_ally|attached_buff:0:5`). **Pure CSV** — Mark of the Wild's `attached_buff` on Fireball's hero-or-ally pool — but worth knowing for two things the recipe does not say out loud. (1) **The grant reaches a HERO**: `_attachment_stat_mods` is called unconditionally by `get_max_hp`, unlike the ally-scoped health auras beside it (Warchief Thrall, Kailis Truearc) and unlike `hero_health_bonus`, which exists precisely because those skip heroes — so on a hero this is Last Stand's buffer at a third of the cost and with no upkeep, paid for instead by being **destructible** (Dispel Magic, Purge, Stoneform). (2) **Losing it can be lethal with no damage dealt**: max health shrinking below the damage already on the host is a state-based death (118.4/704) swept by `drain_state_based_deaths` at the priority gate — and on a HERO that ends the game, so a hero parked above its printed health on the strength of this card is one Dispel Magic from losing. Either party's characters are legal ("target hero or ally"), copies stack, and 400.5 takes the attachment with a host that leaves play. AI: no new code — `_attach_actions` reads it as friendly (it carries `attached_buff`) and puts it on our own highest-ATK **ally**, so the hero play is deliberately left to humans. Tests: `_test_power_word_fortitude`, `_test_power_word_fortitude_loss`, plus `_test_power_word_fortitude_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`.
- **Hide of the Wild** (`azeroth_294`, 2, Armor — Cloth, **Back (1)**, DEF 0, DrPaPrSh, Uncommon) — "If your hero would heal damage, it heals that amount of damage plus 1 instead" (`equipment:back:0|hero_heal_bonus:1`). The first replacement effect in the pool that touches **healing** rather than damage — see the effects-table row for the "your hero heals" source contract it introduced, and for why it can never overheal. **The +1 is per HEAL, not per card**, which is what makes it scale: Prayer of Healing across a hero and three allies is +4, not +1. It occupies the same **Back** slot as Chromatic Cloak, so a deck runs one or the other — burn or repair. Tests: `_test_hide_of_the_wild`, `_test_hide_of_the_wild_scope`, plus `_test_hide_of_the_wild_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`.
- **Clarity of Thought** (`dark_portal_68`, 4, Ability — Holy, Priest, Uncommon) — "Ongoing: [Activate] ➜ Draw a card. Use only if your hero has no damage on it" (`ongoing|activated_power:0:draw:1|require_hero_undamaged`). Mana Agate's shape — an ongoing Ability living in the hero row with a draw power — but **repeatable instead of self-spending**: a plain `[Activate]` tap symbol and **no resource cost**, so it is a card a turn, forever, off a one-time 4. An ability never carries summoning sickness (701.3 is about allies), so it draws the turn it lands. **The whole price is the condition**, and it is a harsh one in a duel — one point of chip damage on your hero shuts the engine off until you repair it, which is what makes it a Priest card: it is built to sit behind the heal suite (Flash Heal, Heal, Prayer of Healing, Healing Touch), where topping the hero back to full is also what turns the draw back on. Note the deck-legality reading: plain `Holy`, **NOT** `Holy Talent`, so no rule 100.2c spec restriction — but it is not itself an ability being *played* each turn, so it never triggers **Spiritual Healing**. See the `require_hero_undamaged` effects-table row for the announcement-only timing. Tests: `_test_clarity_of_thought`, `_test_clarity_of_thought_condition`, plus `_test_clarity_of_thought_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`.
- **Flash Heal** (`azeroth_78`, 1, **Instant Ability** — Holy, Priest, Common) / **Heal** (`azeroth_79`, 2, Ability — Holy, Priest, Uncommon) — "Your hero heals 4 / 7 damage from target hero or ally" (`heal_target:4` / `heal_target:7`). **Pure CSV** on Healing Touch's key, so everything is inherited: **either party's** characters are legal (no "friendly" clause, so a human may repair the opponent's board), the 706 / 4217 resolution re-check (a target that left play or became Untargetable fizzles the heal, the card still spent), the heal landing **inline**, the cap at damage actually present, and an **undamaged target being a legal no-op**. **The difference between them is SPEED, and it is the whole design**: Flash Heal is an Instant Ability, so at 1 resource it is flashed into a combat window to save an ally mid-fight (its CSV type cell said `Ability`, which would have made that impossible, and was corrected — see `_test_priest_heal_recipes`); Heal is sorcery speed, a bigger main-phase repair. Both are plain `Holy`, **NOT** `Holy Talent`, so no rule 100.2c spec restriction — and being Holy **abilities** they each trigger **Spiritual Healing**, which unlike Holy Light's case is a buildable pairing (both are Priest). AI: no new code — they inherit Healing Touch's branch of `_targeted_instant_actions`, the one targeted hand spell whose target must be **FRIENDLY** (our own most-damaged character, hero winning a tie), and the card is **held entirely** while nothing on our side is damaged. Tests: `_test_priest_single_target_heals`, `_test_priest_heal_speed_and_fizzle`, plus `_test_priest_heal_recipes` in `test_deck_manager.gd`.
- **Prayer of Healing** (`azeroth_84`, 3, Ability — Holy, Priest, Uncommon) — "Your hero heals 3 damage from each hero and ally in your party" (`heal_party:3`). The first **party sweep reached from a hand card** — see the `heal_party:N` (top-level) effects-table row. It is the trade the two single-target heals don't offer: less on any one character, but it can never fizzle, needs no legal target, and repairs a whole board at once — so it scales with how wide the party is, and is dead when it is narrow. Sorcery speed, so it is a rebuild between fights rather than a save inside one. Tests: `_test_prayer_of_healing`.
- **Wyneth Harridan** (`azeroth_224`, 4-cost 2/4 Alliance Human Paladin, Rare) — "Protector. (3), [Activate] → Destroy target Undead ally" (`activated_power:3:destroy_ally:0::undead_ally`). The engine's first target kind filtered by **RACE** rather than by card type, state or cost — see the effects-table row. It is a **hate card**: repeatable, unconditional removal that is dead against half the boards in the pool and answers the Undead ones (Boneshanks, Vexra Darkfall, Masten Everspirit, Plagueborn Meatwall, Ophelia Barrows, Sister Rot) every single turn she readies, on a Protector body that also blocks. Note the two halves compete: protecting exhausts her (602.2), so a turn spent blocking is a turn not spent killing. Tests: `_test_wyneth_destroys_undead_ally`, `_test_wyneth_fizzle_and_ai`.
- **Katsin Bloodoath** (`dark_portal_218`, 4-cost 5/3 Horde Blood Elf Paladin, Protector, Common) — "(3) → Prevent all combat damage that would be dealt to and dealt by target friendly ally this turn" (`activated_power:3:prevent_combat_damage_target:0::friendly_ally:no_activate`). The engine's first **bidirectional** damage shield: `GameLogic.prevent` now checks its buff on a packet's SOURCE as well as its target, which is what makes the card a pure save that can never win a fight — a shielded ally survives any combat and kills nothing. Combat-only, so every ability and power still gets through, and repeatable (`no_activate`). See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_katsin_shields_combat_both_ways`, `_test_katsin_scope_and_expiry`, `_test_ai_katsin_saves_dying_ally`.

Ongoing: Put 1 damage on an ally in your party ➜ Prevent the next 1 damage that would be dealt to your hero this turn" (`ongoing|activated_power:0:prevent_next_hero_damage:1::chosen_friendly_ally:put_damage_ally+no_activate`). The engine's first **counted** prevention shield (spent point by point in `GameLogic.prevent`, and netted out of the armor prevention point) and the first cost paid in damage **put** on a chosen other card — chosen, not targeted, which is what added the `chosen_friendly_ally` kind (706 deliberately not applied) (405.3 — unpreventable, watcher-silent, and permitted to be exactly fatal). Free and repeatable, so the shield is limited only by the party's health: the card is a way to move hero damage onto your allies, one point at a time. Two firsts: the third **slot tag** (`aura`, one line in `SLOT_TAGS` — the 414.3b check was already tag-generic), and the engine's first **blanket party-wide damage reduction**. See both effects-table rows. The reduction is small but total: it trims every packet from every source, forever, and **blanks 1-damage packets entirely** — which silences every damage watcher in the game against chip damage, Thysta's idle-turn clause included. It is also the first prevention effect that protects **allies**, which is what separates it from armor. AI: modelled in `combat_kills`, so both AIs read trades correctly on offence and defence.

The first half is Stalwart Protector's recipe verbatim and needed no code (`_hero_keyword_aura` is keyword-generic, and `get_legal_protectors` already asks `_has_keyword` of every hero-row card, the hero instance included); the second is one new combat trigger — see the `on_hero_protect_heal` effects-table row for why “protects” is strictly narrower than Truesilver Breastplate's “defends”, and why `GameState.combat_protector` is the entire condition. Plain `Ability`, so **sorcery speed**: unlike Stalwart Protector (a 1-cost Instant) it cannot be flashed in mid-combat to conjure a blocker, it has to be down beforehand. Type-line tag is plain `Protection`, **NOT** `Protection Talent`, so no rule 100.2c spec restriction. No AI modelling: it is played as a vanilla 3-cost ongoing, and the protector decision tree reads `_has_keyword` live, so both AIs offer the hero as a blocker for free. Tests: `_test_sacred_duty`.
- **Healing Wave** (`azeroth_112`, 3, Ability — Restoration, Shaman, Common) — “Your hero heals 8 damage from target hero or ally” (`heal_target:8`). **Pure CSV** — Healing Touch's heal at 8 instead of 10, so it needed no engine, UI or AI code: `heal_target` was already registered in `_instant_needs_target` and `_instant_targets_hero_or_ally_only`. Everything is inherited — **either party's** characters are legal (“target hero or ally”, no “friendly” clause, so a human may repair the opponent's board), the 706 / 4217 resolution re-check (a target that left play or became Untargetable fizzles the heal, the card is still spent), the heal landing **inline** (no packet pipeline, nothing to prevent, no `from_ability` tag to boost), a `cannot_be_healed` lock (Mortal Strike) emptying it, and the amount being capped by the damage actually present, so 8 is a ceiling rather than a payout. An **undamaged target is a legal no-op** (`GameLogic.heal` emits nothing), not an illegal announce. Its CSV type cell was already correct as a plain `Ability`, so unlike Life Tap and Hammer of Justice nothing needed fixing: it is genuinely **sorcery speed** and can never be flashed into a combat window. Type-line tag is plain `Restoration`, **NOT** `Restoration Talent`, so no rule 100.2c spec restriction — it mixes freely with Elemental Focus and the other Shaman specs. AI: no new code — it inherits Healing Touch's branch of `_targeted_instant_actions`, the one targeted hand spell whose target must be **FRIENDLY** (our own most-damaged character, hero winning a tie), and the card is **held entirely** while nothing on our side is damaged. Test: `_test_healing_wave_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the segment, the amount and the sorcery speed against the REAL database — the pinning matters precisely BECAUSE there is no engine code: a typo in the recipe would leave a silent 3-cost do-nothing with nothing to fail on.
- **Holy Light** (`azeroth_69`, 3, Ability — Holy, Paladin, Common) — “Your hero heals 5 damage from target hero or ally. Draw a card” (`heal_target:5|draw:1`). **Pure CSV** — Healing Touch's heal with a cantrip stapled on, and it needed no engine code: both segments already share the `_resolve_play_instant` dispatch loop, and `heal_target` was already registered in `_instant_needs_target` and `_instant_targets_hero_or_ally_only`. Everything is inherited — **either party's** characters are legal (“target hero or ally”, no “friendly” clause, so a human may repair the opponent's board), the 706 / 4217 resolution re-check, the heal landing **inline** (no packet pipeline, nothing to prevent, no `from_ability` tag to boost), a `cannot_be_healed` lock (Mortal Strike) emptying it, and the amount being capped by the damage actually present, so 5 is a ceiling rather than a payout. An **undamaged target is a legal no-op**, not an illegal announce — and the draw still happens. **Sorcery speed is real here**: unlike Holy Shield and Hammer of Justice this one's CSV type cell was already correct as a plain `Ability`, so it can never be flashed into a combat window; it is a main-phase repair. Its type-line tag is plain `Holy`, **NOT** `Holy Talent`, so no rule 100.2c spec restriction. (It is a Holy **ability**, but the one card that watches for those — Spiritual Healing — is **Priest**-only while this is **Paladin**-only, so rule 100.2a puts them in different decks: that pairing is unbuildable, not merely absent.) AI: no new code — it inherits Healing Touch's branch of `_targeted_instant_actions`, the one targeted hand spell whose target must be **FRIENDLY** (our own most-damaged character, hero winning a tie), and the card is **held entirely** while nothing on our side is damaged. That last part is a deliberate call rather than an oversight: the cantrip means the card is never truly dead, so a future AI could cast it purely to replace itself (gated on hand room, 503.2a, and a non-empty deck), but spending 3 resources for a card is a tempo judgement the heuristic has no model for — the same call left out for Thangal's second attack and Maul's offensive use. Tests: `_test_holy_light`, plus `_test_holy_light_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins both segments AND the sorcery speed against the REAL database — in the opposite direction from Hammer of Justice, since quietly “fixing” this one to Instant would turn a main-phase repair into a combat trick the card is not.
- **Hammer of Justice** (`azeroth_68`, 2, Instant Ability — Protection, Paladin, Common) — “Exhaust target hero or ally. It can't ready during its controller's next ready step. Draw a card” (`exhaust_target:hero_or_ally|gouge_cant_ready|draw:1`). **Pure CSV** — Gouge's exhaust plus its one-shot ready-lock with a cantrip stapled on, and it needed no engine code at all: all three segments already share the `_resolve_play_instant` dispatch loop, and `gouge_cant_ready` is read generically off the def (`_has_effect_flag`) rather than being keyed to Gouge. So everything is inherited — the `hero_or_ally` pool (either party's, so a human may lock their own), the 706 / 4217 resolution re-check, the `gouge_skip_ready` counter consumed at the target controller's next ready step by `TurnManager._enter_ready` (and the ⛓ badge that probe drives), and the lock applying even to an already-exhausted target. It costs 1 more than Gouge and buys the card back, which is the whole difference. **Instant speed is the point** — flashed in on a combat proposal and aimed at the attacker, the exhaust fizzles it at the 601.3 legality recheck and the attacker never exhausts for the attack; the CSV type cell said `Ability`, which would have made that impossible, and was corrected. Its type-line tag is plain `Protection`, **NOT** `Protection Talent`, so unlike Holy Shield it carries no rule 100.2c spec restriction and mixes with any other Talent. AI: no new code — tagged `combat_instant_exhaust` alongside Gouge, so `exhaust_attacker_action` answers an opposing attacker (hero **or** ally) with Litori's worth math; the ready-lock and the draw are not modelled. Tests: `_test_hammer_of_justice`, plus `_test_hammer_of_justice_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the instant type and all three segments against the REAL database (a typo in any one would silently leave a 2-cost do-nothing with nothing to fail on).
- **Holy Shield** (`azeroth_70`, 2, Instant Ability — Protection **Talent**, Paladin, Rare) — “Protection Hero Required. Prevent the next 5 damage that would be dealt to your hero by target hero or ally this turn. When damage is prevented this way, your hero deals that amount of holy damage to that character” (`prevent_hero_damage_from_target:5:holy`). Two firsts: the engine's first **source-scoped** prevention shield (every earlier one is either source-agnostic or reads a flag off the source's def), and its first effect that **reflects what it prevents** — which is what added `GameState.pending_shield_reflects`, because `GameLogic.prevent` runs inside the pipeline of a packet that has not landed and cannot deal damage from there. See the effects-table row. It shields the HERO only, so it pairs with the protector shell (Draconian Deflector, Treesong, Warrax's flip) rather than saving allies: the hero eats the attack on purpose and hands it back. **AI (`BaseAI.holy_shield_action`, tagged `combat_instant_holy_shield` — held, never blind-played, wired into all three `decide_action`s):** `_hero_damage_sources` is the new read — the per-source split `_forecast_damage_to` deliberately does not keep, since Soul Link only needs a total while this card wards against exactly one character — covering the combat our hero is in (as defender, or as an attacker facing retaliation, where warding against the DEFENDER turns the retaliation into a reflect) plus any opposing damage link on the chain aimed at it, with unpreventable sources dropped and already-banked shield netted out. Two independent bars fire it: it prevents at least `HOLY_SHIELD_MIN_PREVENT` (3, full value on a 5-point shield), **or** the reflect KILLS the character it wards against — the half a plain shield lacks, worth taking even for a small block, and a kill outranks a bigger block when both are available. Tests: `_test_holy_shield_wards_and_reflects`, `_test_holy_shield_scope_and_fizzle`, `_test_ai_holy_shield`.
- **Graccus** (`azeroth_4`, Alliance Human Paladin hero, 29 health, Uncommon) — “(3), Flip Graccus ➜ Prevent the next 3 damage that would be dealt to target hero or ally this turn” (`prevent_next_damage_target:3`). Soul Link's **counted** shield reached from a hero flip and aimed at a **target** rather than at your own hero — see the effects-table row for why that made it a resolution-re-checked, fizzle-able effect while needing no change to `GameLogic.prevent` (its counted branch was already target-agnostic) and no UI code at all. Instant speed, so the flip is held for the opponent's attack window. The professions (Blacksmithing / Mining) and the Protection line on his type line are flavor — no engine meaning. **AI (`BaseAI.graccus_shield_action`, wired into all three `decide_action`s, with a `return result` gate in the on-turn hero-power enumeration so it is never blind-flipped):** the flip is the game's only one, so it fires solely on damage already on its way, at whichever of two things it can actually save. (1) **The hero**, whose loss ends the game, so the bar is low — incoming damage (`_forecast_damage_to`, Soul Link's forecast generalized off the hero) that is lethal, or that would drop us to `FREE_BLOCK_HERO_FLOOR`; a 1-damage poke at a full-health hero is not worth it. (2) **An ally**, and here it is deliberately NOT frugal — the value is the BODY, not the damage, so it is spent to save a body even when only 1 of the 3 points is used. The two real conditions are that the ally would otherwise **die** and that 3 points actually **save** it (a 5-damage swing at a 1-health ally is not saved by 3), plus a printed-cost floor of the power's own cost — `_destroy_is_worth_it`'s convention. Banked shield and unpreventable sources are netted out of both reads, since neither buys anything. Tests: `_test_graccus_shields_target`, `_test_graccus_scope_and_fizzle`, `_test_ai_graccus`, plus `_test_graccus_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the recipe key, the count and the cost against the REAL database.
- **Korthas Greybeard** (`dark_portal_174`, 1-cost 1/2 Alliance Dwarf Paladin, Protector, Common) — “Protector. [Activate] ➜ Prevent the next 1 damage that would be dealt to target hero or ally this turn” (`activated_power:0:prevent_next_damage_target:1::hero_or_ally`). **The “this turn” is ERRATA** — the printed card omits it, and that one word is what let the card reuse Graccus' counted shield verbatim (a `prevent_damage_amount` Buff with duration `turns`:1) instead of needing an open-ended grant; see the effects-table row for the whole inheritance and for the one difference, which is economy. **A 1-cost Protector that also shields**, and the two halves compete: tapping her for the point costs the block (602.2), so the shield is what you reach for when protecting is not enough or not legal. **AI (`BaseAI.korthas_shield_action`, held out of `_get_ally_power_actions` entirely — the generic `hero_or_ally` branch reads a non-heal effect as DAMAGE and would point the shield at the ENEMY — and wired into all three `decide_action`s):** Graccus' hook with the generosity removed, because 1 prevented damage almost never changes anything. Both arms therefore require the point to be **exactly the difference between dying and surviving** (`incoming >= hp` and `incoming - 1 < hp`, read through the shared `_forecast_damage_to` with any banked shield netted out): our own hero first, since its loss ends the game, then the dearest ally of ours that clears the same bar. Unlike Graccus there is no “drops us to the free-block floor” arm and no unused-points allowance for a body, and the Protector tempo cost is deliberately not modelled — the protector decision happens later, at its own point, by which time the shield is already standing. Tests: `_test_korthas_shields_target`, `_test_korthas_scope_and_fizzle`, `_test_ai_korthas`.
