First power that **readies** anything, and the first with **two independent choice-not-target halves** — see the effects-table row. Rule 303.2c allows one weapon per combat, so readying the hero and a spent weapon buys a **second weapon strike in the same turn**: Windfury Weapon's payoff on a 1-cost body, repeatable every turn she readies. The weapon pick is made at resolution and only prompts when there is a real decision (two or more exhausted weapons). Tests: `_test_galway_readies_hero_and_weapon`, `_test_galway_pick_gates_and_ai`.
- **Lady Courtney Noel** (`azeroth_194`, 3-cost 1/4 Alliance Human Priest) — "[Activate] → Lady Courtney Noel heals 1 damage from each hero and ally in your party" (`activated_power:0:heal_party:1`). Healing Stream Totem's party sweep as an on-demand ally power; first non-targeted heal from an activated power — see the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_lady_courtney_heals_party`, `_test_lady_courtney_gates_and_ai`.
- **Stone Guard Rashun** (`dark_portal_234`, 5-cost 5/5 Horde Tauren Shaman, **Tauren Hero Required**) — "When an opposing ally enters play, exhaust it" (`requires_hero_race:Tauren|exhaust_opposing_ally_on_enter`). Watcher Mal'wi's enter-play watcher with the ping swapped for an exhaust, sharing its scan (`fire_opposing_ally_enter_watchers`) and its inline-resolution deviation; the first card that taxes the opponent's *tempo* on every ally they commit. Fires on entering opposing Totems and tokens alike (305.3a — see the effects-table row). Tests: `_test_stone_guard_rashun_exhausts_entering_opposing_ally`, `_test_stone_guard_rashun_ignores_own_allies`, `_test_stone_guard_rashun_totems_and_tokens`.
- **Venomstrike** (`dark_portal_41`, 4-cost 1/5 Scorpid **Pet**, Hunter, neutral, Rare) — "At the end of each turn, Venomstrike deals 4 nature damage to each hero and ally it dealt damage to this turn" (`end_of_turn_damage_own_victims:4:nature`). The fifth effect conditioned on turn history (after Torek's Assault, Thysta, Cold Blood and Operation Recombobulation) and the first to use the log as a **victim list** rather than as a yes/no condition — see the effects-table row. Notable as the worked example of **rule 703.3**: the delayed-looking burn is one triggered power whose trigger event is the end of turn, NOT an effect planted when the damage is dealt, so destroying the scorpid before the end phase stops it entirely. Being a Pet, he also costs his controller their pet slot (414.3b). Tests: `_test_venomstrike_burns_its_victims`, `_test_venomstrike_death_and_scope`.
- **Outrider Zarg** (`dark_portal_227`, 4-cost 4/2 Orc Hunter, Ferocity, Common) — "At the end of your turn, if Outrider Zarg dealt no damage this turn, destroy him" (`end_of_turn_destroy_if_no_damage_dealt`). The sixth effect conditioned on turn history (after Torek's Assault, Thysta, Cold Blood, Operation Recombobulation and Venomstrike), and the first **self-destruction clause** — an aggressively statted body that has to be used every turn or it walks off the board, which is what Ferocity is there to enable (it can swing the turn it lands). See the effects-table row and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Outrider Zarg". The AI is modeled: it never ends a turn holding an idle Zarg. Tests: `_test_outrider_zarg_idle_dies`, `_test_outrider_zarg_scope_and_death`, `_test_ai_outrider_zarg_attacks`.
- **Thysta Spiritlasher** (`dark_portal_236`, 5-cost 3/5 Horde Orc Warlock) — "At the end of each player's turn, if no damage was dealt this turn, Thysta Spiritlasher deals 3 fire damage to that player's hero" (`end_of_turn_damage_hero_if_none_dealt:3:fire`). First **"each player's turn"** end trigger (which is what split `_enter_end` into two sweeps) and — as the second effect conditioned on **turn history** rather than board state, after Torek's Assault — the card that replaced the per-card boolean flags with the **turn event log** (`GameState.turn_events`, see `game_logic/turn_state_flags.md`); Torek's Assault reads the same `damage_dealt` entries. Symmetric: an idle turn burns whoever's turn it was, her own controller included. See the effects-table row and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Thysta Spiritlasher". Tests: `_test_thysta_burns_idle_turn`, `_test_thysta_silent_after_damage`, `_test_thysta_prevented_damage_still_counts_as_none`, `_test_thysta_stacks_and_sees_earlier_damage`.
- **Plagueborn Meatwall** (`dark_portal_228`, 5-cost 0/6 Horde Undead Abomination, Protector, Common) — "When Plagueborn Meatwall defends against an ally, remove all damage from Plagueborn Meatwall, and he deals that much melee damage to each attacking ally" (`on_defend_vs_ally_reflect_damage:melee`). Grunt Baranka's defend-side combat trigger with the self-trade swapped for a reflect — and the clearest demonstration of what putting a 602.3 trigger **on the chain** actually buys: the reflect resolves inside the DEFEND WINDOW, before the conclusion, so killing the attacker with it cancels the combat under 603.1b and the wall takes **no retaliation**. A 0-ATK body whose entire offense is the damage already on it, which makes him better the longer he has been chewed on, and self-cleaning every time he blocks. See the effects-table row and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Plagueborn Meatwall". Tests: `_test_meatwall_reflects_before_conclusion`, `_test_meatwall_scope_and_amount`, `_test_meatwall_trigger_is_respondable`.
- **Grunt Baranka** (`dark_portal_212`, 2-cost 2/2 Horde Orc Warrior, Protector) — "When Grunt Baranka defends against an ally, destroy her. If you do, destroy all attacking allies" (`on_defend_vs_ally_destroy_self_and_attackers`). Three firsts: the engine's first **defend-side** combat trigger, the first effect gated on rule **709.2f**'s "if you do" (the destroy must actually have been PERFORMED, not merely attempted), and — because she is the rulebook's own worked example for 602.3 — the card that moved every combat trigger onto the chain, replacing the inline resolutions that Morik, Donna Calister and Berserking used to be. See the effects-table row, the **Combat-step triggered effects** section, and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Combat triggered effects — drained one at a time". Tests: `_test_baranka_defends_vs_ally_trades`, `_test_baranka_only_vs_ally_and_via_protect`, `_test_baranka_if_you_do_gate`.
- **Elithys Firestorm** (`dark_portal_208`, 2-cost 2/2 Horde Blood Elf Warlock, Common) — "When Elithys Firestorm attacks, she deals 1 fire damage to each other hero and ally" (`on_attack_damage_each_other:1:fire`). Morik's on-attack combat trigger with a **symmetric board sweep** instead of a draw, and the first damage effect that excludes its own source ("each OTHER"). See the effects-table row. A 2-cost body that wipes 1-health boards on every swing — including her own side, which is what prices it. Tests: `_test_elithys_firestorm_attack_burn`.
- **Morik** (`dark_portal_224`, 3-cost 2/2 Horde Orc Shaman, Ferocity) — "When Morik attacks, each player draws a card" (`on_attack_draw_each_player:1`). First **symmetric** triggered power. Originally resolved inline at the 602.1 combat-step start; it is now an ordinary chain link like every other combat trigger — see the effects-table row and the **Combat-step triggered effects** section. In the Grennan deck (2 copies, replacing Guardian Steppestrider). Test: `_test_morik_attack_draws_each_player`.
- **Skorn, Mistress of Shadow** (`azeroth_259`, 5-cost 3/2 Horde Orc Warlock, Rare) — "When an ally is dealt damage, Skorn deals that amount of shadow damage to target hero in that ally's party" (`ally_damaged_reflect_hero:shadow`). First **board-wide damage watcher**, and the card that corrected the engine's notion of "damage dealt": per **405.2** a character *can be dealt* damage in excess of its health (only *put* damage is capped at fatal), so `GameLogic.deal_damage` now reports the full DEALT amount while placing only what fits — which also fixed Steal Essence's drain, Mind Spike/Mind Blast's discard, whelp bounce and the turn event log in the overkill case. See the effects-table row and `data/rules_deviations.md` "Skorn, Mistress of Shadow". Tests: `_test_skorn_reflects_ally_damage`, `_test_skorn_combat_scope_and_gates`.
- **Bestial Wrath** (`azeroth_35`, 1, Instant Ability — Beast Mastery **Talent**, Hunter, Rare) — "Target Pet has +3 ATK this turn. Prevent all damage that would be dealt to it this turn" (`buff_atk_target:pet:3|prevent_all_damage_this_turn`). Two firsts: the **`pet` target kind** (nothing had targeted `card_subtype == "Pet"` before) and an **instance-scoped total damage shield** — Brother Rhone's shield is permanent, combat-only and attacker-restricted off a def; this one is a this-turn Buff on the instance covering every source. See the effects-table row and `BaseAI.bestial_wrath_action` in `game_logic/ai/ai_functions.md`. Tests: `_test_bestial_wrath_buff_and_shield`, `_test_bestial_wrath_targeting_and_ai`.
- **Skewer** (`azeroth_155`, 4, Instant Ability, Horde) — "Choose an ally in your party. It deals damage equal to its ATK to target ally" (`ally_atk_damage:ally`). First card whose damage amount is a **live board read** rather than a printed number, and the first with a *chosen* (non-targeted) friendly source alongside a real target — see the effects-table row. **AI (`BaseAI.combat_instant_action`, tag `combat_instant_ally_atk`):** the card has no printed damage, so the AI values it at the ATK of the best ally it could choose and **always announces that ally** (`_best_ally_atk_source` — the choice is free, so there is never a reason to name a weaker one). Everything else is the `combat_instant_dmg` ambush math verbatim (attack window: the attacker dies to it; defend window: it finishes what the defender alone wouldn't kill; never for a bait cheaper than the card), with one restriction the target kind forces: it can only ever answer an attacking **ally**, never an attacking hero. Tests: `_test_skewer_ally_atk_damage`, `_test_skewer_attacking_bonus_and_ai`.
- **Zy'lah Manslayer** (`azeroth_276`, 7-cost 5/6 Horde Troll Warrior, Protector, Rare) — "When Zy'lah Manslayer deals combat damage to an ally, ready her" (`on_combat_damage_readies_self`). Brigg's conclusion trigger pointed at the **source** instead of the victim, and without his "with damage on it" gate — see the effects-table row. The first self-readying trigger, so she is the first ally that can block more than once a turn: protecting exhausts her (602.2), her retaliation readies her, and the opponent's next attacker meets the same 5/6 wall. Tests: `_test_zylah_readies_on_combat_damage_to_ally`.
- **Vestia Abiectus** (`dark_portal_194`, 3-cost 2/3 Alliance Night Elf Priest, Common) — “When Vestia Abiectus deals combat damage, you may put an ability you control into its owner's hand” (`on_combat_damage_return_own_ability`). The first **optional** combat-conclusion trigger with a real *which one* choice (Green Whelp Armor's is yes/no), and the first effect whose pool is “an ability **you control**” — see the effects-table row. The point of the card is **reuse**: an attachment you control counts even while it sits on an OPPOSING ally, so Entangling Roots can be picked back up and replayed on a fresh target every time she connects. She is deliberately unmodelled by the AI, which always declines — see `data/rules_deviations.md` “Vestia Abiectus”. Tests: `_test_vestia_abiectus`, `_test_vestia_abiectus_scope`.
- **Ja'zaron** (`dark_portal_215`, 3-cost 2/3 Horde Troll Shaman) — Protector; "At the start of your turn, Ja'zaron heals 1 damage from himself" (`heal_at_turn_start:1`). Pure CSV — Ka'tali Stonetusk's (`azeroth_248`) printed text verbatim on a bigger body, so the existing `YOUR_TURN_TRIGGERS` entry covers it: the heal goes on the chain as a `resolve_turn_start_trigger` link during the ready step and is fully respondable (see the **Start-of-turn triggered effects** section). The combination is the point — a Protector that repairs the chip damage it soaks, so it only stays dead if something kills it outright.
- **Scout Omerrta** (`dark_portal_232`, 1-cost 2/1 Horde Orc Warrior) — Protector. Pure CSV, fully vanilla. Aggressive stats for 1, but 1 health means any retaliation kills it: a one-shot blocker, and the cheapest Protector in the pool.
- **Warden Ravella** (`dark_portal_196`, 3-cost 2/3 Alliance Night Elf Warrior) — Protector, Untargetable. Pure CSV (both keywords already live); first card carrying two keywords in one cell.
- **"Lowdown" Luppo Shadefizzle** (`dark_portal_177`, 2-cost 2/1 Alliance Gnome Rogue, **Gnome Hero Required**) — Elusive, Stealth, Untargetable (`requires_hero_race:Gnome`). Pure CSV; first card carrying three keywords in one cell, and the first `requires_hero_race` on an **Ally** (the 100.2b gate reads off `CardDef.effects` irrespective of card type, so no new code). The stack makes him a 2-power evasive body answerable only by a non-targeting effect: he can't be chosen as a defender (elusive) or as a link target (untargetable), and nothing may protect against his attack (stealth) — but AoE kills him outright at 1 health. No AI modeling; the AI plays him as a vanilla 2/1 (it never blind-targets an untargetable ally anyway, since `can_submit` filters its target lists). In the Dizdemona and Timmo decks (1 copy each). Test: `_test_lowdown_luppo_shadefizzle`.
- **Winter's Grasp** (`azeroth_60`, 3, Ability — Frost, Mage, Uncommon) — "Ongoing: Opposing allies can't attack heroes or allies in your party unless their controller pays (1) for each attacker" (`ongoing|opposing_allies_attack_tax:1`). The engine's first **rule 600.3** modifier and the fourth compulsion family — see the effects-table row and the **Compulsion / taunt families** section. It is also the first card to exercise 600.3's interaction with 600.2 (a goaded character under a tax need not attack), and the first additional cost paid at announcement that is **refundable**, which is why the proposal stays retractable. Tests: `_test_winters_grasp_taxes_opposing_allies`, `_test_winters_grasp_affordability_scope_and_stacking`, `_test_winters_grasp_releases_must_attack`, `_test_ai_winters_grasp_tax`.
- **Battle Shout** (`azeroth_135`, 3, Instant Ability — Fury Shout, Warrior, Common) / **Demoralizing Shout** (`azeroth_140`, 3, Instant Ability — Fury Shout, Warrior, Uncommon) — "Ongoing: Allies in your party have +1 ATK" (`ongoing|party_allies_atk_mod:1`) / "Ongoing: Opposing allies have -1 ATK" (`ongoing|opposing_allies_atk_mod:-1`). Mirrored static ATK auras, both **ally-only** — the heroes on both sides are untouched, which is what separates Demoralizing Shout from Hootie's `opposing_characters_atk_mod`. **No uniqueness limit:** "Fury Shout" is a plain type-line subtype with no "(N)" after it (contrast Form (1) / Aspect (1) / Trinket (2)), and rule 414.3b keys on that number, so any number of Shouts — same name or not — may be in play at once. See both effects-table rows. Test: `_test_battle_and_demoralizing_shout`.
- **Lady Jaina Proudmoore** (`azeroth_195`, 7/4 Ally, Unique) — "Opposing allies can't attack." Live static aura (`opposing_allies_cant_attack`) + first card exercising **name-based Unique uniqueness** (rule 414.3a — see Card uniqueness).
- **Warcaller Zin'bawa** (`dark_portal_240`, 3-cost 0/5 Horde Troll Warrior, Protector, **Troll Hero Required**) — "Warcaller Zin'bawa has +1 ATK for each damage on allies in your party" (`requires_hero_race:Troll|atk_per_damage_party:1`). First ATK modifier keyed on **board damage** rather than on a count of cards or a combat condition — a live self-modifier that grows as your own party gets chewed up, and shrinks again on healing. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_warcaller_zinbawa_atk_per_party_damage`.
- **Bala Silentblade** (`azeroth_226`, 3-cost 1/4 Horde Orc Rogue) — "+3 ATK while attacking an exhausted hero or ally." Live conditional self-modifier (`atk_vs_exhausted_defender:3`), natural partner of the Chops/Voss attack-exhaust trigger. See the effects-table row.
- **Chops** (`dark_portal_32`, 3-cost 3/4 Boar, Pet) / **Voss Treebender** (`azeroth_266`, 1-cost 2/1 Horde Tauren Druid) — "When [this] attacks, you may exhaust target hero or ally." First attack-triggered targeted power (`on_attack_exhaust_target`): resolves before the attack window, so it can exhaust a ready Protector and deny the protect point. See the effects-table row.
- **Gartok Skullsplitter** (`azeroth_238`, 1-cost 2/1 Horde Orc Warrior) — "When Gartok Skullsplitter attacks, you may exhaust target armor" (`on_attack_exhaust_armor`). Chops/Voss's attack-exhaust point with the pool swapped for **armor**, which is what turned that point's target list into a kind-aware read (`pending_attack_exhaust_kind`) instead of a fixed hero-or-ally pool — so the trigger reused the whole existing choice point, UI flow and AI hook. Firing before the attack window is the card: the defender's armor is exhausted, so its DEF is spent and the prevention point never opens at the conclusion. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_gartok_exhausts_armor`, `_test_gartok_pool_and_ai`.
- **Polymorph** (`azeroth_58`, 2, Ability — Arcane, Mage, Common) — "Attach to target ally. Ongoing: Attached ally can't attack or protect, loses all powers, and is a Sheep" (`ongoing|attach:ally|attached_cannot_attack|attached_cannot_protect|attached_loses_powers|attached_ally_type:Sheep`). The engine's first **silencing** effect, and the card that introduced **`GameState.effective_def`** — the one funnel answering "what powers does this in-play card actually have?", which every power-reading site now asks instead of reading the printed def. See the three effects-table rows for what a blank text box takes (rule 700.1 — keywords included) and what it deliberately leaves (printed stats, card type, the 202.2 type-line tags, and every grant from elsewhere, since the errata says a blanked card "can later gain powers"). Two divergences from the printed wording are the ERRATA and are recorded in `data/rules_deviations.md` "Polymorph": the Sheep tag is **additive** (202.3) and the card **type is untouched**, so the host is still an ally — still a legal "target ally", still in the party, still exhaustable to pay The Love Potion. It is also the first card to need a **410.6c** sweep (`StackResolver.drain_attachment_host_checks`, drained at the priority gate beside the uniqueness queue and the state-based deaths): the errata calls out that you may Polymorph a **Totem**, but a Totem is an ally only BECAUSE OF A POWER (305.3a), so blanking it stops it matching "attach to target ally" and the game destroys the Polymorph before the next player gets priority — a wasted card for one round of a frozen totem. At 2 resources it is the cheapest hard answer in the pool to a big body: it does not kill the ally (no death trigger, nothing for recursion to find), it just turns it off, and the ally's controller answers by destroying the attachment (Dispel Magic, Purge, Burn Away). AI: no new code — Polymorph carries no `attached_buff` and no `attach_heal`, so `_attach_actions` reads it as a debuff and aims it at the opponent's highest-ATK ally with printed cost ≥ the spell's, never at one of ours. Tests: `_test_polymorph_blanks_the_ally`, `_test_polymorph_scope`, `_test_polymorph_on_a_totem`, `_test_ai_polymorph`, plus `_test_polymorph_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins all four ongoing segments against the REAL database (a typo in any one would silently leave an attachment that does part of its job).
- **Lessons in Lurking** (`dark_portal_146`, 2, Ability, neutral, Common) — "Attach to target ally. Ongoing: Attached ally has stealth" (`ongoing|attach:ally|attached_keyword:stealth`). The engine's first **keyword-granting attachment**, and so the first grant that lives on the HOST's attachment list rather than on the card (a `Buff`), on a card in a row (the auras) or on a form — see the `attached_keyword` effects-table row for why routing it through the one `_has_keyword` funnel meant no combat code. **Stealth is 602.2a**: while the host is attacking, characters can't protect, so it walks an attacker past a board of Protectors without exhausting, killing or targeting any of them — a permanent, repeatable version of what a one-shot removal spell buys, for as long as the host lives. Sorcery speed (a plain `Ability`), so it must be down before the combat. Tests: `_test_lessons_in_lurking`, plus `_test_lessons_in_lurking_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the keyword ARGUMENT against the REAL database — a typo there is completely silent, leaving an attachment that lands and grants nothing.
- **Thorns** (`dark_portal_28`, 4, Instant Ability — Balance, Druid, Rare) — "Attach to target hero or ally. Ongoing: When attached character is dealt combat damage, it deals 1 nature damage to the source of that combat damage" (`ongoing|attach:hero_or_ally|attached_combat_damage_reflect:1:nature`). The engine's first **reflecting attachment**: every attachment before it either modified its host (Mark of the Wild, Polymorph, Cyclone) or damaged it (Fireball, Rend, Shadow Word: Pain), while this one makes the host hit back — see the effects-table row. Two things are worth holding onto. (1) **It is retaliation, not armor**: it prevents nothing, so the host still takes the hit in full, and a shield that DOES prevent the hit (armor, Brother Rhone, Katsin, Bestial Wrath) turns the reflect off with it, since damage prevented in full was never dealt (717.2b). (2) **The amount is flat**, so it is a tax on being attacked *often* rather than on being attacked *hard* — the card is at its best on a hero that protects (Draconian Deflector, Treesong, Bear Form), where it chips every attacker the hero eats, in both directions of the trade. On a hero it is also where a **Chromatic Cloak** finds it, the reflect being ability damage from a hero source. Instant speed, so it can go down inside the opponent's attack window, before the protect point. Tests: `_test_thorns_reflects_combat_damage`, `_test_thorns_scope_and_stacking`, `_test_ai_thorns_targets_own_hero`, plus `_test_thorns_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the key and BOTH its arguments against the REAL database — the amount and the damage type would each fail silently if mistyped, leaving a 4-cost attachment that lands and does nothing.
- **Cyclone** (`dark_portal_21`, 1, Instant Ability — Balance, Druid, Common) — "Attach to target hero or ally, and put three wind counters on Cyclone. Ongoing: Attached character can't attack or protect. At the start of your turn, remove a wind counter from Cyclone. If you removed the last one, destroy Cyclone" (`ongoing|attach:hero_or_ally|attach_counters:wind:3|attached_cannot_attack|attached_cannot_protect|turn_start_remove_counter_destroy:wind`). The engine's first **self-timing** effect — every other duration in the game is either permanent, "this turn" (a `Buff` swept at end of turn) or gated on something else leaving play, while this one counts its own remaining life on the card. See both new effects-table rows. **Not a Polymorph**: it takes Polymorph's two combat restrictions and deliberately NOT its `attached_loses_powers`, so the host keeps every printed keyword, activated power and self-modifier — it just can't fight. It also reaches a **HERO**, which Polymorph cannot, and that is the real use: a hero locked out of attacking and protecting for three of your ready steps, which on a protector shell is a hole in their whole defence. Instant speed, so it goes down in the attack window **before the protect point opens** (602.2) and strips the blocker mid-combat. The answer is to destroy the attachment (Dispel Magic, Purge, Burn Away) or, for an ally host, to let it die — 400.5 takes Cyclone with it. **UI:** the wind counters show as the orange counter badge, Blood Fury's treatment, but moved to the card's TOP-left corner for attachments — an attachment is tucked behind its host with only its top sliver visible, so the badge's usual bottom-left corner would be buried (`CardNode.update_counter` reads `is_attachment`; playtest's `_refresh_atk_badges` gained a pass over the shared `attached` zone, which neither row loop covered). No AI modeling — `_attach_actions` reads it as a debuff (no `attached_buff`, no `attach_heal`) and aims it at the opponent's highest-ATK ally with printed cost ≥ the spell's, never at one of ours. Tests: `_test_cyclone_locks_and_counts_down`, `_test_cyclone_scope_and_fizzle`, plus `_test_cyclone_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins both new keys, the counter count, the instant speed and the ABSENCE of `attached_loses_powers` against the REAL database.
- **Rockbiter Weapon** (`azeroth_115`, 2, Instant Ability — Enhancement, Shaman, Rare) — "Attach to one of your Melee weapons. Ongoing: Attached weapon has +2 ATK. Your hero has protector" (`ongoing|attach:melee_weapon|attached_buff:2:0|hero_keyword:protector`). Four existing recipes on one card, and the first attachment that grants something to a card **other than its host** — which is the only code it needed: `_hero_keyword_aura` scanned the controller's rows alone, and an attachment lives in the shared `attached` zone, so the protector half would have been a completely silent no-op. That scan now covers attachments (see the `hero_keyword` effects-table row), which any future attachment granting the hero a keyword inherits. The ATK half needed nothing at all: `_attachment_stat_mods` is **host-agnostic**, so Mark of the Wild's grant lands on a WEAPON for free and reaches every downstream reader through `get_atk` — the **303.2b struck-weapon sum**, `best_melee_weapon_atk` (Mortal Strike), `BaseAI._forecast_strike_atk` and `choose_strike_weapon`, the `get_legal_attackers` 0-ATK-hero gate, the attack cursor and the ATK badge. Like Eye of Rend's aura it is therefore paid **once per weapon STRUCK**, so under Dual Wield two buffed weapons both count. **The two halves have one shared fragility, and it is the card**: per **400.5** a host that leaves play takes its attachments with it, so destroying the weapon removes the hero's protector grant as well — everything is read live, so both lift the same instant. Copies stack on one weapon (+2 ATK each; the keyword grant is idempotent), and the health field is 0 because a weapon has none. **Instant speed is worth having**: it can go down in the opponent's attack window, before the protect point opens (602.2), conjuring a blocker mid-combat the way Stalwart Protector does. 602.2's other gates still apply — an **exhausted** hero can't protect, which matters here since striking with the buffed weapon does not exhaust the hero but attacking with it does. No AI modeling: `_attach_actions` already puts a `melee_weapon` attachment on our own highest-ATK Melee weapon, and `get_legal_protectors` reads `_has_keyword` live, so the protector decision tree offers the hero for free. Tests: `_test_rockbiter_weapon_buff_and_protector`, `_test_rockbiter_weapon_scope_and_loss`, plus `_test_rockbiter_weapon_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins all four segments AND the instant speed against the REAL database — the card is pure CSV, so every one of them fails silently if mistyped.
- **Attachments** (rule 400) — general framework: `attach:TARGETS[:exhaust_it]` + host-side ongoing segments; shared `"attached"` zone; host leaving play destroys attachments (see the effects-table rows). First cards: **Entangling Roots** (`azeroth_20`, 2, Ability — Balance, Druid): attach to target ally and exhaust it; attached ally can't ready during its controller's ready step (`attach:ally:exhaust_it|attached_cannot_ready`). **Mark of the Wild** (`azeroth_24`, 2, Instant Ability — Restoration, Druid): attach to target ally; +2 ATK / +2 health (`attach:ally|attached_buff:2:2`).
- **Kavai the Wanderer** (`dark_portal_173`, 6-cost 4/6 Alliance Night Elf Warrior) — "1, Destroy Kavai → Destroy target ability or equipment." First `sacrifice_self` extra cost (plain payment power — no tap symbol, usable while exhausted / just summoned) and first `destroy_ability_or_equipment` effect (`TARGETS=ability_or_equipment`, hits ongoing abilities, totems, attachments, and equipment of either player). AI: never fired proactively — `BaseAI.doomed_sacrifice_action` (wired into all three AIs' `decide_action`, after `save_bounce_action`) cashes her in on the opponent's most expensive ability/equipment only when she is DOOMED: target of a lethal opposing removal/damage link on the chain (shared `_chain_threatened_ally` detection with Withdraw), or dying at the open combat window's conclusion (as defender, or as attacker to a lethal retaliation).
- **"Chipper" Ironbane** (`dark_portal_160`, 2-cost 3/1 Alliance Dwarf Warrior) — "(X), Destroy "Chipper" Ironbane → Destroy target ability or equipment with cost X" (`activated_power:X:destroy_ability_or_equipment:0::ability_or_equipment:sacrifice_self`). First **X-cost activated power** — see the `activated_power:X` effects-table row for the cost plumbing and why no X dialog is needed. **AI, the only real work in the card:** the printed text reads "choose X, then find a match", but that is backwards for a heuristic — the AI must *see a target it can destroy and then pay accordingly*. `BaseAI._best_power_destroy_target` (shared with `doomed_sacrifice_action`) therefore enumerates the **opponent's** in-play abilities and equipment, drops the ones whose printed cost exceeds available resources **inside the loop** — so a rich target we can't pay for doesn't hide a cheaper one we can — and takes the highest printed cost that remains, with `_power_x_for` deriving the announced X from that target. Unlike Kavai (doomed-only: a 6-cost 4/6 body outvalues most targets) Chipper is fired **proactively** as well, from a new `destroy_ability_or_equipment` + `sacrifice_self` + `cost_x` branch of `_get_ally_power_actions` — the 3/1 body is cheap enough that trading it for a real threat is fine on its own. The proactive branch carries a value floor of the **source's own printed cost** (2), so he isn't spent on a cost-1 totem or Form; `doomed_sacrifice_action` has no floor and still cashes him in on anything when he's dying anyway. Tests: `_test_chipper_x_cost_destroy`, `_test_chipper_affordability_and_retract`, `_test_ai_chipper_target_first`.
- **Besh'iah** (`azeroth_229`, 6-cost 4/4 Horde Troll Priest) — "Destroy an ally in your party → Destroy target ability" (`activated_power:0:destroy_ability:0::ability:sacrifice_ally+no_activate`). Lafiel's destroy pool paid with Gertha's `sacrifice_ally` cost, and the card that split the EXTRACOST field into `+`-joined tokens — her cost and her lack of an `[Activate]` tap symbol are independent axes, so she reuses both halves unchanged. No resource cost and no tap symbol: she fires the turn she lands, while exhausted, and **repeatedly** — the only limit is having an ally to eat (herself included). AI (`destroy_ability` branch of `_get_ally_power_actions`): spends the least valuable body via the shared `_cheapest_sacrifice_ally` (never Besh'iah herself — she's the repeatable engine) on the most expensive OPPOSING ability, and only when that ability is worth at least what it gives up. Tests: `_test_beshiah_sacrifice_destroys_ability`, `_test_beshiah_repeatable_and_ai`.
- **Lafiel** (`azeroth_196`, 6-cost 4/5 Alliance Night Elf Priest) — "2, [Activate] → Destroy target ability." Kavai's destroy pool narrowed to abilities only (`activated_power:2:destroy_ability:0::ability` — new `TARGETS` kind `ability`, sharing `get_destroy_kind_candidates`, so it hits ongoing abilities, totems and attachments of either player but never equipment), on a normal `[Activate]` tap power: she exhausts at announcement, so it can't be used again until she readies. No Purge-style `except_friendly_attached` exclusion — her printed text has no such clause, so the caster's own abilities stay legal targets. AI (`_get_ally_power_actions` `destroy_ability` branch): destroys the most expensive OPPOSING ability, never its own.
- **Moira Darkheart** (`azeroth_209`, 4-cost 3/4 Alliance Night Elf Rogue) — "1, Destroy Moira Darkheart → Destroy target armor or weapon." Kavai's `sacrifice_self` shape with the pool narrowed the other way from Lafiel (`activated_power:1:destroy_equipment:0::equipment:sacrifice_self` — new `TARGETS` kind `equipment`). "Armor or weapon" IS the equipment pool: every Equipment card is one or the other, which is what Zygore Bladebreaker's `include_weapons = true` already resolves to, so no armor/weapon split is needed. No `[Activate]` tap symbol — usable while exhausted / just summoned — and the sacrifice is paid at **ANNOUNCEMENT** (707.1e), so she is gone before anyone gets priority. AI: `BaseAI.doomed_sacrifice_action` now derives its candidate kinds from the power's effect, so she is cashed in only when DOOMED and only onto opposing equipment.
- **Call the Spirit** (`azeroth_157`, 2, Ability, neutral, Common) — "Put target ally card from your graveyard into your hand" (`graveyard_to_hand:Ally:1:1:own`). Ancestral Spirit's reanimate with the destination changed to the hand — a slower, cheaper recursion that pays the ally's cost again but is uncapped, so it reaches anything in the yard. See the `graveyard_to_hand` effects-table row. In the Grennan deck (2 copies, replacing Chasing A-Me 01). Tests: `_test_call_the_spirit_fetches_ally`, `_test_call_the_spirit_gates_and_ai`.
- **Cold Snap** (`azeroth_50`, 2+X, Instant Ability — Frost Talent, Mage, Rare) — "Frost Hero Required. Remove Cold Snap from the game. Put up to X Frost ability cards with different names from your graveyard into your hand" (`graveyard_to_hand:Ability:0:X:own|gy_tag:Frost|gy_distinct_names|rfg_self`). Three firsts: a graveyard search whose **count is the announced X**, a **tag filter** on the candidate pool, and a spell that **exiles itself** instead of going to the graveyard — see the effects-table row. It is also the first graveyard-search card played at **instant speed**, which is what factored those gates into the shared `_graveyard_play_ok`. In the Litori deck (1 copy, replacing a Liba Wobblebonk). The implemented Frost pool is now Frostbolt, Brain Freeze and Winter's Grasp, so "different names" can fetch a real spread. Tests: `_test_cold_snap_fetches_x_frost_abilities`, `_test_cold_snap_gates_fizzle_and_ai`, plus `_test_cold_snap_pool` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the tag-filtered pool against the REAL database (a Frost ability with a blank or misspelled tags cell would silently drop out of the pool with nothing to fail on).
- **Cannibalize** (`dark_portal_136`, 2, Ability — Horde, **Undead Hero Required**) — "Remove any number of ally cards in graveyards from the game. Your hero heals 2 damage from itself for each card removed" (`requires_hero_race:Undead|graveyard_to_rfg:Ally:0:99:both|rfg_heal_per_card:2`). First multi-card graveyard announce from a hand card, and the card that split the graveyard browser per graveyard — see the effects-table row and the Graveyard browser section.
- **Ophelia Barrows** (`azeroth_253`, 4-cost 1/5 Horde Undead Warrior, Protector) — "1 → Remove target ally card in any graveyard from the game. If you do, Ophelia Barrows heals 1 damage from herself." First graveyard-card-targeted activated power (`rfg_graveyard_ally` + paired `graveyard_to_rfg:Ally:1:1:both` requirement); plain payment power (`no_activate`), repeatable. See the effects-table row.
- **Medoc Spiritwarden** (`azeroth_205`, 5-cost 1/5 Alliance Dwarf Priest, Uncommon) — “[Activate] ➔ Put target ally card from your graveyard into your hand” (`activated_power:0:graveyard_to_hand_ally:0::graveyard_ally|graveyard_to_hand:Ally:1:1:own`). Ophelia Barrows' `graveyard_ally` power with the exile swapped for Call the Spirit's fetch, and the pool narrowed to the caster's **own** graveyard — so it needed no UI code at all, only a resolution arm. A 5-health wall that buys back a body every turn it readies; pairs with Gustaf Trueshot, who fills the graveyard for it. See the effects-table row. Tests: `_test_medoc_spiritwarden`.
- **Wraith Scythe** (`azeroth_339`, 4, Weapon—Axe, Melee (1), 2 ATK / strike 2, Rare) — "When your hero deals combat damage, it heals that amount of damage from itself" (`equipment:melee_weapon:0|strike_cost:2|hero_combat_dmg_heals_hero`). The sixth trigger hosted at the combat conclusion, and the **least restricted** of them: Devilsaur Leggings / Iceblade Hacker narrow to an ally victim, Annihilator narrows to itself, and this one narrows nothing — any combat damage the hero deals, to anything, with any weapon or none, in either combat role. See the effects-table row. It pays off hardest on **retaliation**, so it pairs with a hero that soaks attacks (Bear Form, Draconian Deflector): every blocked swing is lifegain. Test: `_test_wraith_scythe`.
- **The Shatterer** (`azeroth_334`, 4, Weapon — Mace, Melee (1), 3 ATK / strike 2, PaRoShWa, Uncommon) — "When your hero deals combat damage with The Shatterer to a hero, destroy one of that hero's controller's weapons unless he pays (2)" (`equipment:melee_weapon:0|strike_cost:2|weapon_combat_dmg_break_weapon:2`). Thrash Blade's named-weapon conclusion trigger with the victim narrowed to a HERO, and the engine's first pay-or-lose-it point offered to the **OPPONENT** rather than to the card's own controller — see the effects-table row for the CR glossary "Unless" ordering (payment first, decline reaches the destruction) and for why the **STRIKER** picks which weapon. Two things are worth holding onto. (1) **It is a tax, not removal**: a player who keeps 2 spare resources never loses a weapon to it, so what it really does is drain the defender every turn the hero connects — and against a player holding nothing but a cheap weapon, the tax is not worth paying and the weapon goes. (2) **The mirror is real**: no role clause means a defending hero retaliating with a struck Shatterer breaks a weapon too, and a Shatterer is itself a legal pick, so two of them across the table eat each other. Tests: `_test_the_shatterer`, `_test_the_shatterer_scope`, `_test_ai_the_shatterer`, plus `_test_the_shatterer_recipe` in `test_deck_manager.gd`, which pins the trigger key and its cost against the REAL database — the card is pure CSV on the data side, so a typo would leave a silent vanilla 3 ATK mace.
- **Randipan** (`azeroth_213`, 3-cost 2/2 Alliance Gnome Rogue) / **Samuel Grey** (`azeroth_258`, 3-cost 2/2 Horde Undead Rogue) — "When [this] deals combat damage to a defending hero, draw a card" / "…that hero's controller discards a card" (`on_combat_damage_to_hero:draw:1` / `:discard_controller:1`). See the effects-table row.
- **Forms** (rule 414.3b + glossary Bear/Cat Form) — general framework: `form:1` slot uniqueness, **`form_state:NAME` carrying everything the form grants via `GameState.FORM_GRANTS`** (bear: protector + the Feral break; cat: +1 ATK while attacking + the Feral break), `on_destroyed:pay_return_hand:2` optional return (see the effects-table rows). First cards: **Bear Form** (`azeroth_18`, 1, Instant Ability — Feral): hero has protector (from the form) + pay-2 return. **Bash** (`azeroth_17`, 2, Instant Ability — Feral): "Exhaust target hero or ally" on play (`exhaust_target:hero_or_ally` — first hero-capable exhaust) + bear form ongoing, NO return clause. **Cat Form** (`dark_portal_19`, 2, Instant Ability — Feral): hero +1 ATK while attacking (from the form) + pay-2 return. **Claw** (`dark_portal_20`, 4, Instant Ability — Feral Combo): hero deals 3 melee to target hero/ally on play + cat form ongoing, NO return clause. Bash/Claw exercise ongoing-ability **on-play effect segments** (resolved in `_resolve_play_ongoing_ability` before the card settles into the hero_row). AI: Bear Form tagged `combat_instant_bear_form` — `BaseAI.bear_form_action` (wired into all three AIs) flashes it in during the ATTACK window when attacked, hero ready, not already in bear form, and no board protector answers; Bash tagged `combat_instant_exhaust` (Exhaustion role, and unlike Exhaustion it also answers an attacking HERO); Claw tagged `combat_instant_dmg`; Cat Form untagged — played normally in GenericAI's develop step; `BaseAI.choose_form_return` always pays. **AI hero-attack policy:** `get_reasonable_actions` offers a hero attack on an enemy ALLY only when the forecast ATK is lethal on it (attacks on the enemy hero are always offered); GenericAI's kill/trade/chip steps already conform.
- **Maul** (`azeroth_25`, 2, Instant Ability — Feral, **Form (1)**) — "Your hero has +1 ATK this turn. Ongoing: Your hero is in bear form." Bash's shape with the exhaust swapped for a self-pump, so the Form half (slot uniqueness, `form_state:bear`, `form_break:Feral`, the protector grant) is inherited whole and only the on-play segment is new — see the `hero_atk_this_turn` effects-table row. Like Bash and Claw it has **no** `on_destroyed:pay_return_hand` clause. Tests: `_test_maul_hero_pump_and_bear_form`, plus `_test_form_state_flags` in `test_deck_manager.gd`.
- **Fireball** (`azeroth_53`, 4, Ability — Fire, Mage) — "Attach to target hero or ally, and your hero deals 4 fire damage to it. Ongoing: At the start of your turn, your hero deals 1 fire damage to attached character." First hero-or-ally attachment (`attach:hero_or_ally|attach_deal_damage:4:fire|attached_damage_turn_start:1:fire`) — see the effects-table rows. AI targets the opposing hero only.
- **Primal Mending** (`azeroth_30`, 1, Instant Ability - Restoration, Druid, Uncommon) - "Attach to target ally, and your hero heals 2 damage from it. Ongoing: At the end of each turn, your hero heals 1 damage from attached ally" (`ongoing|attach:ally|attach_heal:2|attached_heal_turn_end:1`). Fireball's attachment shape with both damage halves inverted into heals - see both effects-table rows. First **regenerating** attachment, and the first attachment trigger on the each-player end-of-turn sweep rather than the turn-start queue. It also fixed a real AI bug: `_attach_actions` treated any attachment without `attached_buff` as a debuff and aimed it at the OPPONENT, so a heal attachment would have repaired their board - heal attachments now count as friendly and are ranked by the host's DAMAGE (ATK as tiebreak) rather than by ATK alone. Tests: `_test_primal_mending_attach_heal`.
- **Shadow Word: Pain** (`azeroth_87`, 3, Instant Ability — Shadow, Priest) — "Attach to target hero or ally, and its controller discards a card. Ongoing: At the start of your turn, your hero deals 1 shadow damage to attached character" (`ongoing|attach:hero_or_ally|attach_discard_controller:1|attached_damage_turn_start:1:shadow`). Fireball's attachment shape with the burst swapped for a discard — see the `attach_discard_controller` effects-table row. AI aims it at the opposing hero only (a hero host can't be killed to shed the attachment, and the discard half then hurts them rather than us). Tests: `_test_shadow_word_pain_attach`, `_test_ai_shadow_word_pain_targets_hero_only`.
- **Berserker Stance** (`azeroth_136`, 3, Ability — Fury, Warrior, **Stance (1)**, Rare) — "Ongoing: If your hero would deal damage, it deals that amount of damage plus 1 instead. If your hero would be dealt damage, it is dealt that amount of damage plus 1 instead" (`ongoing|stance:1|hero_damage_dealt_bonus:1|hero_damage_received_bonus:1`). Introduces the **Stance (1)** slot tag (Warrior's flavor of Form/Aspect/Aura — pure `SLOT_TAGS` data, no new machinery) and the first **unconditional** dealt-side replacement bonus, which is what forces it to also touch **combat damage** — Chromatic Cloak (ability-only) and Shadowform (typed-only) never do. Its second clause is the engine's first **received-side** replacement effect: rule 717 applies replacement effects to "the amount that would be dealt" BEFORE prevention, so a 2-ATK attack into a Stance hero becomes a 3-damage packet FIRST, and only THEN does armor/a shield act on that increased number — a 2-DEF armor absorbs 2 of the 3 and 1 point still lands, it does not block the attack outright. Both halves are symmetric hazards: the hero also deals +1 to everything it hits, including its own retaliation math, and takes +1 from everything that hits it, including its own ally powers' self-damage costs (`put_damage` is explicitly excluded — that's a COST, not dealt damage). See both effects-table rows (`hero_damage_dealt_bonus:N` / `hero_damage_received_bonus:N`) for the full mechanism. Tests: `_test_berserker_stance_dealt_bonus`, `_test_berserker_stance_received_bonus_vs_armor`, `_test_berserker_stance_scope`.
- **Shadowform** (`azeroth_88`, 3, Ability — Shadow **Talent**, Priest, **Form (1)**) — "Shadow Hero Required. Ongoing: If your hero would deal shadow damage, it deals that amount of damage plus 1 instead. When you play a Holy ability, destroy Shadowform" (`ongoing|form:1|hero_damage_bonus_by_type:shadow:1|form_break_on:Holy`). Third replacement aura (after World in Flames and Chromatic Cloak) and the first **inverted** form break — see both effects-table rows. "Shadow Hero Required" here is the Talent-spec restriction the deck authorizer already enforces via rule 100.2c (`Shadow Talent` in the tags column: a deck may not mix Talents of two specs), not a new gate. The first non-Druid Form, so it exercises Form (1) uniqueness against the Feral forms; it has no pay-2 return clause. Tests: `_test_shadowform_shadow_bonus`, `_test_shadowform_holy_break`.
- **Aspect of the Hawk** (`azeroth_34`, 3, Ability — Beast Mastery, Hunter, **Aspect (1)**) — "Ongoing: If your hero would deal ranged damage, it deals that amount of damage plus 1 instead" (`ongoing|aspect:1|hero_damage_bonus_by_type:ranged:1`). Shadowform's typed replacement aura on `ranged` (so it boosts Aimed Shot / Multi-Shot and any hero-power or attachment ranged packet, but never combat damage — the conclusion has its own path), plus the **Aspect (1)** slot tag, which is what generalized Form's uniqueness check into the per-tag `SLOT_TAGS` scan. See both effects-table rows. Tests: `_test_aspect_of_the_hawk`, `_test_aspect_slot_independent_of_form`.
- **World in Flames** (`azeroth_61`, 8, Ability — Fire, Mage) — "Ongoing: If your hero would deal fire damage, it deals double that amount of damage instead." First damage-replacement aura (`hero_fire_damage_doubled`, doubling applied in `defer_packets` via per-packet `dmg_type`) — see the effects-table row.
- **Chromatic Cloak** (`azeroth_282`, 4, Armor — Cloth, Back (1), DEF 0) — "If your hero would deal damage with an ability, it deals that amount of damage plus 1 instead." Second replacement aura (`hero_ability_damage_bonus:1`, keyed on the `from_ability` packet tag; +1 before World in Flames' doubling) — see the effects-table row.
- **Mana Agate** (`azeroth_57`, 2, Ability — Arcane, Mage) — "Ongoing: 1, Destroy Mana Agate → Draw two cards" (`ongoing|activated_power:1:draw:2:::sacrifice_self`). First hero_row `sacrifice_self` power. AI fires it only when the hand has room for both cards (the draw gate in `_get_ally_power_actions` is amount-aware: hand ≤ max_hand − amount).
- **Arcane Intellect** (`azeroth_47`, 2, Instant Ability — Arcane, Mage) — "Attach to target hero, and its controller draws a card. Ongoing: Attached hero's controller's maximum hand size is increased by three" (`ongoing|attach:hero|attach_draw:1|attached_max_hand:3`). First hero-only attach and first max-hand-size modifier — see the `attach_draw` / `attached_max_hand` effects-table rows.
- **Aimed Shot** (`azeroth_32`, 1+X, Ability — Marksmanship Talent, Hunter) — "Your hero deals X ranged damage to target hero or ally" (`deal_damage_to_target:X:ranged`). First X-cost hand card — see the `deal_damage_to_target:X` effects-table row — and first Talent (deck legality: rule 100.2c in `DeckManager.authorize_deck_def`).
- **Spirit Bond** (`dark_portal_39`, 1, Ability — Beast Mastery Talent, Hunter) — "Ongoing: At the start of your turn, if you have a Pet, your hero heals 2 damage from itself and each of your Pets" (`ongoing|turn_start_heal_hero_and_pets:2`). Second Talent (the other spec, exercising the 100.2c mixing check).
- **Hannah the Unstoppable** (`azeroth_187`, 3/3 Ally) — "Opposing heroes and allies can't protect." Live static aura (`opposing_cant_protect`): while an opponent controls Hannah, that player has no legal protectors (`_protect_locked` short-circuits `get_legal_protectors`). Mirror of Jaina's aura, locking *protecting* instead of *attacking*.
- **Instant Ally** — Tristan Rapidstrike (azeroth_221, 3/3 Protector). The Instant tag (CSV `type` = "Instant Ally" → `CardDef.is_instant`) makes `play_ally` instant-speed in `_can_play_non_instant`: playable any time with priority (combat windows, opponent's turn, non-empty chain — rule 409.1). Resolves as a normal ally (summoning sickness, which does NOT block protecting — 601.2a restricts attackers only). AI: tagged `combat_instant_protector` in `BaseAI.COMBAT_INSTANT_TAGS` (held, never blind-played); `instant_protector_action` flashes it in during the ATTACK window only (never defend — the protect point is past) when being attacked, no board protector answers, and the protector decision tree would pick it (safe kill-and-survive block, or any block to save the hero); the normal `choose_protector` then uses it at the protect point.
- **Ally activated powers with sacrifice-style costs** — Kena Shadowbrand (`azeroth_190`, 1/3): `[Activate]`, put 1 damage on herself → draw (`activate_put_damage_self:1`; keeps the tap/exhaust, may be self-lethal per 405.3; AI won't draw her to death). Bizzik Sparkcog (`azeroth_178`, 2/4): `[Activate]`, destroy an ally in your party → draw (`sacrifice_ally` cost + `friendly_ally` target picker; AI only sacrifices an already-damaged ally). Augustus Corpsemonger (`azeroth_177`, 3/4): `[Activate]`, remove three ally cards in your graveyard from the game → destroy target ally (`destroy_ally` effect + `rfg_allies:3` cost; the 3 exiled cards are auto-chosen — see `data/rules_deviations.md`; AI destroys enemy allies worth killing via `_destroy_is_worth_it`). Gertha, The Old Crone (`dark_portal_164`, 1/3 Gnome Warlock): `1, [Activate]`, destroy an ally in your party → destroy target ally (`destroy_ally` effect + `sacrifice_ally` cost). First **two-pick** ally power — the sacrifice is a separate `sacrifice_id` param (Bizzik's single-target sacrifice keeps riding `target_id`), picked ahead of the destroy target in a two-phase targeting flow (`_is_ally_sacrifice_destroy_power` in `input_router.gd`, mirroring the damage_and_heal two-phase). Like `sacrifice_self`, the cost is paid at **ANNOUNCEMENT** (707.1e) and cannot be answered. AI (`_get_ally_power_actions` destroy_ally + sacrifice_ally branch): sacrifice our lowest-value own ally (never Gertha herself), destroy the enemy's highest-value ally, only when the target outvalues the sacrifice; random tiebreak on equal value.
- **Rod of the Ogre Magi** (`azeroth_332`, 4, Two-Handed Weapon—Staff, Melee (1), 1 ATK) — first "power weapon": `[Activate]`, exhaust your hero → deal 1 damage to target hero or ally (`activated_power:...:exhaust_hero`, same path as Mooncloth Robe + Grimdron). Carries `two_handed` (first 414.3c Two-Handed↔Off-Hand uniqueness card) and the `power_weapon` AI flag (AI uses the ping, never strikes with it) — see both effects-table rows.
- **The Hammer of Grace** (`azeroth_323`, 3, Weapon—Mace, Melee (1), 1 ATK / 3 Strike, DrPaPrSh) — power weapon #3: `1, [Activate]`, exhaust your hero → your hero heals 2 damage from target hero or ally (`activated_power:1:heal_target:2::hero_or_ally:exhaust_hero`, same `exhaust_hero` path as Rod; first `heal_target` activated power). Carries the `power_weapon` AI flag (bad 1-ATK/3-strike; AI uses the heal, never strikes). AI heals its own damaged characters only (existing `heal_target` friendly-only branch in `_get_ally_power_actions`) — no new engine or AI code, purely a CSV recipe.
- **Hypnotic Blade** (`azeroth_327`, 2, Weapon—Dagger, Melee (1), 1 ATK) — power weapon #2: `3, [Activate]`, exhaust your hero → target player discards a card, use only on your turn (`activated_power:3:discard_opponent:1:::exhaust_hero` + standalone `require_turn_player` segment). The activated-power `discard_opponent` effect reuses Mias the Putrid's pending-discard machinery; "target player" is auto-chosen as the opponent — see `data/rules_deviations.md`. AI uses it whenever the opponent holds cards. In hotseat, the off-screen player's forced discard runs in **discard peek mode** (see Hotseat section).

### Playtested heroes (confirmed working)
Alliance : Boris, Dizdemona, Moonshadow
Horde : Ta'zo, Grennan, Boris Brightbeard, Omedus, Radak Doombringer

---

## Equipment (rules 300.1, 303/304, 414.3)

Equipment implemented: **Mooncloth Robe** (`azeroth_298`, Armor—Cloth, Chest, DEF 0), **Pads of the Dread Wolf** (`dark_portal_260`, Armor—Leather, Feet, DEF 1 — first card with functional DEF), **Golem Skull Helm** (`azeroth_290`, Armor—Plate, Head, DEF 3, vanilla blocker), **Draconian Deflector** (`azeroth_285`, Armor—Shield, Off-Hand, DEF 4, "Your hero has protector" — see `hero_keyword:KEYWORD` in the effects table), **Green Whelp Armor** (`azeroth_291`, Armor—Leather, Chest, DEF 1, first *triggered* equipment power — see `whelp_bounce` in the effects table), and **Devilsaur Leggings** (`azeroth_284`, Armor—Leather, Legs, DEF 1, "When your hero deals combat damage to an ally, destroy that ally" — mandatory conclusion trigger, see `hero_combat_dmg_destroys_ally` in the effects table). Weapons with a triggered power: **Iceblade Hacker** (`azeroth_328`, Weapon—Axe, Melee (1), 2 ATK / strike 2, "When your hero deals combat damage to an ally, that ally can't ready during its controller's next ready step" — mandatory conclusion trigger applying Gouge's ready-lock, see `hero_combat_dmg_locks_ally_ready` in the effects table).

- **Play flow:** `play_equipment` action (mirrors `play_ally`'s action-phase timing) → `_resolve_play_equipment` moves the card to the controller's `hero_row` (rule 304.1). Card type is `Equipment`; the recipe carries an `equipment:SLOT:DEF` segment.
- **Activated powers reuse the ally-power path** (`use_ally_power` / `_can_use_ally_power` / `_resolve_use_ally_power`), now generalized to accept a source in `ally_row` OR `hero_row`. Equipment has no summoning sickness. A power's optional 7th recipe field is an `EXTRACOST` token; `exhaust_hero` (Mooncloth Robe, Rod of the Ogre Magi, The Hammer of Grace) requires a ready hero at validation. **EVERY cost of an activated power is paid at ANNOUNCEMENT** (rule **707.1e** — costs are paid inside the announcement sequence, and only then does 707.1f add the link to the chain). Two functions do it, both called from `submit_action`: `_pay_activate_costs` for the exhaust-style costs (rule 412.2: the `[Activate]` tap symbol on the source, the `once_per_turn` mark, and `exhaust_hero`, undone by `_refund_activate_costs` on retraction), and **`_pay_sacrifice_costs`** for the sacrifice-style ones (`sacrifice_self`, `activate_sacrifice_self`, `sacrifice_ally`, `put_damage_self`, `activate_put_damage_self`, `put_damage_ally`, `rfg_allies`). Paying the exhaust costs at resolution left the source and the hero READY while the power sat on the chain, so the same power — or two `exhaust_hero` powers off one hero — could both be announced and both resolve off a single exhaust. Paying the SACRIFICE costs at resolution was a rules deviation with a visible symptom: an opponent could see a source sitting in play with its own "destroy this" power already on the chain, and could waste removal on a body that was already committed to dying. **Three consequences of paying them at announcement, all intended.** (1) The announcement becomes **non-retractable** — a destroyed card can't be un-destroyed, so `_pay_sacrifice_costs` sets the `_cost_paid_irreversibly` params mark that Sever the Cord already used; 707.2's rewind is about failing to FINISH announcing, not about a voluntary take-back. (2) **The source is usually gone by resolution**, which is why `_resolve_use_ally_power` deliberately has no `is_in_play` guard on it — per **707.3** an effect exists independently of its source, so the link resolves in full either way. (3) **Mezzik Darkspark's X** ("the ATK of the destroyed ally") is captured as the ally dies and rides the action as `_sac_atk`, since resolution can no longer see it. Destroying the source at announcement can also fire its own `on_destroyed` trigger during submission; that queues a choice which the priority gate drains like any other destruction. New `draw` effect draws `AMOUNT` cards.
- **Slot uniqueness (414.3b) and hands (406):** **`get_wielding_violation` is the one answer** — it weighs a player's whole hero row against both caps at once. See the **Wielding** section under Card uniqueness for the model. A violation sets `pending_equip_sacrifice_player`/`_ids` and emits `equipment_sacrifice_required`; resolve via `StackResolver.choose_equipment_sacrifice()` (called directly, not through the stack — same pattern as pet sacrifice), which re-asks the whole question rather than shrinking the id list. `can_submit` blocks everything else while pending. Reached from two places: `_check_equipment_uniqueness` for a card that just ARRIVED (the `pending_uniqueness_ids` queue), and the per-player sweep in `drain_uniqueness_checks` for 406.2b, where a card LEAVING is what caused it.
- **UI:** `input_router` `_action_type_for` maps Equipment → `play_equipment`; the context-menu "Activate Power" entry now covers hero_row equipment; equipment-sacrifice mode mirrors pet-sacrifice mode (red highlight, `start_equipment_sacrifice_mode`). `playtest.gd` handles `equipment_sacrifice_required` (AI keeps highest-cost) and the pass button shows "Destroy equipment". Hero_row rendering already spreads non-hero cards, so equipment displays with no renderer changes.
- **AI:** BaseAI now generates `play_equipment` (via `_action_type_for`) and equipment activated powers (`_get_ally_power_actions` scans hero_row too). The `draw` power is skipped when the hand is already full. This is simple-heuristic level (FullRandomAI picks among legal actions; GenericAI still prioritizes safe kills first) — the AI plays the robe and may draw with it, but doesn't reason about hero-exhaust tempo. Covered by `_test_ai_plays_equipment`.
- Tests: `_test_mooncloth_robe_power`, `_test_mooncloth_robe_hero_exhausted`, `_test_equipment_slot_uniqueness` in `test_scenarios.gd`.

### Armor damage prevention (rule 717.2c) — the prevention point

Prevention is a **decision point at the moment a damage packet would hit a hero** — never a chain action ("None of this uses the chain"), and never pre-committed. Direct-call pattern like the strike point:

- **The packet pipeline (`StackResolver.defer_packets`)**: EVERY effect that deals damage — instants, abilities, ally/hero powers, enter-play effects, totem triggers, end-of-turn burns, death-trigger AoE — hands its packets (`{source, target, amount, …hooks}`) to `defer_packets(state, db, packets, after, recursive_destroy)` instead of calling `GameLogic.deal_damage` (see the INVARIANT comment on `deal_damage`). If a packet would hit a hero whose controller has ready DEF>0 equipment (`get_ready_def_armor`), the point opens before the group lands; otherwise the group lands immediately (identical to dealing directly). Groups queue (`pending_prevention_deferred`); per-packet hooks run post-land off the damage actually DEALT: `riders` (Frostbolt restrictions), `discard_per` (Mind Spike/Blast, Ismantal), `drain_heal_per`/`drain_heal_to` (Steal Essence). After-hooks: `"totem_next"` opens the next queued totem trigger. **This makes new damage effects armor-preventable by construction — never call `deal_damage` from a resolution.**
- **Combat** keeps its own open site: when the defend window closes, BEFORE `_do_combat_conclusion` (`_combat_prevention_offers` mirrors the conclusion's damage math incl. Long-Range; both heroes can get sequential offers in a hero-vs-hero fight). Conclusion then runs from `choose_prevention` (resume `"combat"`).
- **Resolution:** `StackResolver.choose_prevention(state, armor_id, db)` — exhausts the armor, adds DEF to `PlayerState.damage_prevention`, and **re-opens the point** while damage remains and more ready armor exists ("you may exhaust another equipment, and so on"). `""` = take the remaining damage. `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while `pending_prevention_player` is set. Event: `prevention_opened` (re-emitted with the reduced amount per exhaust).
- **`GameLogic.prevent(state, db, source, target, amount, opts)` is the ONE place prevention is applied** (rule 717.2), called from `deal_damage` before any damage is placed. It returns `{"amount", "events"}` — what actually reaches the target plus the `damage_prevented` events — and applies, in order: unpreventability (short-circuit, absorbs nothing), character-side shields (Brother Rhone), **instance-scoped granted shields** (Bestial Wrath's `prevent_all_damage` Buff — source-agnostic and this-turn; `_prevention_offer` also suppresses the armor point for an already-shielded target), then the armor pool. `opts` carries `unpreventable` (explicit override; otherwise DERIVED from the source, so new damage effects respect Lionheart Helm by construction) and `combat_attack`. **Unpreventable damage consumes no shield** — an unspent armor pool and a Rhone-style shield both survive it.
- **Consumption:** unchanged — `GameLogic.deal_damage` absorbs from the pool when the target is the controller's **hero** (allies are never protected), emitting `damage_prevented`. Excess DEF is wasted: the pool is cleared right after the packet lands. `discard_per_damage` / `drain_heal_per_damage` / whelp-bounce / `on_combat_damage_to_hero` all key off damage actually DEALT, so full prevention (packet ceases to exist, 717.2b) fires none of them.
- **AI** (`BaseAI.choose_prevention(state, db, player_id) -> String`, called by the scene/sim at the point): exhaust the highest-DEF ready armor for which `remaining >= DEF − 1` (avoids wasting big armor on chip damage); one armor per call, the re-opened point asks again (6 incoming vs DEF 3 + DEF 1 → both get used).
- **UI (playtest.gd):** `prevention_opened` → `_handle_prevention` — AI auto-resolves; a human gets the centered choice popup ("N damage incoming — exhaust armor?" with one button per ready armor + "Take the damage"), armors highlighted and clickable (`_in_prevention_mode`). Board-public, so the off-screen hotseat player is prompted inline too.
- Tests: `_test_pads_block_combat`, `_test_pads_block_decline`, `_test_pads_block_instant`, `_test_pads_block_enter_play_damage`, `_test_pads_overblock_expires`, `_test_ai_armor_block_heuristic`, `_test_prevention_noncombat_sources`, `_test_prevention_reduces_discard_per_damage`.

### Weapons & striking (rules 303, 602.1, 602.3) — hero combat

First cards: **Krol Blade** (`azeroth_331`, Weapon—Sword, Melee 1H, 3 ATK, strike cost 1, vanilla) and **Gorebelly** (`azeroth_9`, Horde Warrior hero, 30 HP, flip: pay 1 → next melee strike this turn costs 3 less).

- **Recipe:** `equipment:melee_weapon:0|strike_cost:STRIKE_COST`. Weapons play via the normal `play_equipment` path into the hero row; slot uniqueness reuses `_check_equipment_uniqueness`.
- **Strike points (don't use the chain):** striking is legal at exactly two moments — as the combat step starts, for the **attacking** player (602.1: in `_resolve_propose_combat`, after the attacker exhausts, BEFORE the attack window opens) and as the defender enters combat, for the **defending** player (602.3: in `_open_defend_window`, after the protect point, BEFORE the defend window). If the wielder is a hero whose controller has a ready, affordable weapon (`get_strikeable_weapons`), `pending_strike_player/_weapon_ids/_side` are set and `strike_point_opened` fires; `can_submit`/`pass_priority` hard-block while pending. The scene resolves via `StackResolver.choose_strike(state, weapon_id, db)` (direct call, like `choose_protector`; `""` = decline). The strike exhausts the weapon + pays strike cost (`weapon_struck` event), then the held window opens.
- **Strike modifier (303.2b):** `GameState.combat_struck_weapons` (wielder → weapon ids) feeds `get_atk` live (+weapon ATK, never cached); cleared in `_do_combat_conclusion` (303.2a). Only heroes are wielders; the hero may be exhausted; one weapon per combat (303.2c). A defending hero that strikes deals combat damage back (Long-Range attackers still zero it).
- **Legal attackers:** the `get_legal_attackers` 0-ATK hero gate now also admits a hero with an affordable strikeable weapon — so a hero is an available attacker only when the strike is actually payable (opponent resources are public, so AI forecasting works both ways).
- **Gorebelly flip** (`melee_strike_discount:3`): sets `PlayerState.melee_strike_discount`; `get_strike_cost` applies it to Melee weapons; consumed by the next melee strike, cleared at every turn start. AI only flips when net save > 0 and the discounted strike is payable (`_strike_discount_worth_it`) — never with Krol Blade (strike 1).
- **AI** (`BaseAI.choose_strike_weapon`, called by the scene/sim on `strike_point_opened`): attacking → always strike with the highest-ATK offered weapon; defending → strike when the counter-damage kills the attacker OR the attacker is the opponent's LAST legal attacker (nothing left to save it for), hold otherwise; never strike defensively vs Long-Range. `BaseAI.forecast_atk` (get_atk + best strikeable weapon) feeds attack proposals, `combat_trade_value`, and `find_safe_lethals` on both sides.
- **UI (playtest.gd):** strike point mirrors the protect point — inline buttons over the pass bar ("WeaponName (+ATK, cost)" / "Don't strike"), weapon highlighted and clickable; `_drain_passes`/`_schedule_next_turn` stall while `pending_strike_player`/`_in_strike_mode`.
- Tests: `_test_weapon_attack_strike`, `_test_weapon_defend_strike`, `_test_strike_gates_and_gorebelly_discount`, `_test_ai_strike_decisions`.

## Hotseat / Duel Table (playtest.gd)

Two humans on one screen: menu Player 2 type "Human (hotseat)" (both human ⇒ `_hotseat`).
`_local_player` is the seat the screen belongs to — hand visibility (`BoardRenderer.set_perspective`
+ `refresh_hand_visibility`) and input (`InputRouter.setup`) follow it; every formerly
p1-hardcoded UI/turbo/wrap-up check in playtest.gd now uses `_local_player` (so a single
human can also sit in the p2 seat vs an AI).

- **Rotating camera (TTS-style):** the board is world-space seen through `_camera`
  (Camera2D, `ignore_rotation = false`); ALL UI lives in the `_hud` CanvasLayer (layer 10)
  and never rotates. `_orient_camera(pid, animate)` turns the view 180° about
  `BOARD_PIVOT` (960,475) for the p2 seat (rotated camera pos = `2*PIVOT − (960,540)`).
  Called from `_begin_handoff` (animated, while the overlay is up), `_set_local_player`,
  and game setup (a single human seated at p2 vs AI gets the flipped view all game).
  `BoardRenderer.view_rotation_degrees` mirrors the camera so transient world overlays
  (targeting cursor, floating damage/heal numbers) counter-rotate and drift up-screen;
  the Alt+hover inspector lives in its own CanvasLayer (15). **Mouse → world must be
  camera-aware:** `CardNode` uses `get_global_mouse_position()`, `BoardRenderer` (extends
  Node, not CanvasItem) uses `_world_mouse()`; never use the raw viewport mouse position
  for world positions. New UI elements go in `_hud` (`_add_label(..., hud=true)`), new
  board elements in the scene root.
- **Handoff timing:** in hotseat the handoff fires at the START of the outgoing player's
  end phase (`phase_changed` → "end"), so the incoming player gets the wrap-up priority
  window (instants with leftover resources) plus their ready/draw on their own screen.
  `_stop_for_end_window` (set on confirm) keeps Turbo from auto-passing that window;
  cleared on their pass or the next phase change. If the outgoing player is over max
  hand size (wrap-up discard needs THEIR hand, rule 503.2a), the end-phase handoff is
  skipped and the `turn_changed` handoff below covers the switch instead. Ctrl+Enter
  is an alias for Ctrl+Space (wrap up / end turn).
- **Handoff:** on `turn_changed` to the other human, `_begin_handoff` hides BOTH hands
  (perspective sentinel `"__handoff__"` matches neither hand zone), sets `_handoff_pending`
  (blocks `_try_pass`, `_schedule_next_turn`, `_drain_passes`, `_maybe_turbo_pass`,
  `_do_ai_turn`, `_do_turbo_pass`, all key input; `CardNode.input_blocked` blocks clicks)
  and shows a fullscreen confirm overlay. Confirming runs `_set_local_player` then resumes.
- **Mulligan:** humans decide sequentially, each behind their own handoff
  (`_mulligan_queue` / `_advance_mulligan_queue`; `_mulligan_current` owns the panel).
- **Ambush (always on — there is no stance selector):** a priority window STOPS
  when the off-screen player has a legal instant
  response (`_offscreen_has_play` probes the router with `local_player` swapped);
  with no legal response it auto-passes through the same paths that drive AI
  (`_do_ai_turn` null-AI branch, `_drain_passes` hotseat branch). The old
  per-player Passive/Ambush stance is gone: Passive existed to skip these windows
  back when instants were rare, and now that they are common, auto-passing a real
  decision point is simply wrong.
  `_enter_ambush_mode` points the InputRouter at them (rules make only instant-speed
  plays legal off-turn) with YELLOW highlights (`AMBUSH_HIGHLIGHT`), while the renderer
  perspective stays with the seat — their hand stays face-down, a playable card's front
  shows only while hovered (`_on_card_hover_scene`). The stop covers combat windows AND
  the CHAIN-response point right after a combat proposal (before the attack window) —
  where Litori's freeze / Exhaustion can still fizzle the proposal via the 601.3 recheck.
  Critical guard: the ambush stop re-points the router at the responder DURING the
  submission's own event emission, so `InputRouter._pass_own_proposal` also checks
  `action.source_player != local_player` — without it the proposer's auto-pass fires
  through the re-pointed router, spends the responder's priority, and resolves the
  proposal unanswered. To skip without revealing anything the ambusher presses
  **their OWN pass button** — the mirrored one above the line, which
  `_update_opponent_pass_btn` arms ("Skip window", yellow) for the duration of the
  stop and leaves inert as a status light the rest of the time (this replaced a
  free-floating top-right Skip button). Mode exits when priority leaves the ambusher
  (`_refresh_ui` guard) or on Skip; `_drain_passes`/`_schedule_next_turn` freeze while
  `_in_ambush_mode`.
- **Mandatory-choice routing (`_route_choice`) — the one funnel:** the engine
  already addresses every pending choice to a specific player
  (`pending_*_player`, and `pending_reveal_pick_chooser` where the decider differs
  from the owner), so the scene's only job is turning "who decides" into "how do I
  collect it here". `_route_choice(decider, visibility)` returns:
  `"ai"` (decider is an AI — the caller auto-resolves via the AI hook),
  `"local"` (the human in this seat — render inline), or
  `"peek"` (a human who is NOT in this seat — hotseat: `_enter_choice_peek_mode`
  re-points the InputRouter at them so their clicks resolve the choice).
  `visibility` is `"private"` (a mandatory discard — their hand stays face-down
  with one-card-at-a-time hover peek, gated on `_choice_peek_hides_hand`) or
  `"public"` (The Princess Trapped's reveal — both players are meant to see the
  cards, so nothing is hidden and no handoff happens). `_try_pass` is hard-blocked
  while peeking (the seated player's Space must not act through the re-pointed
  router); exits via `_exit_choice_peek_mode` (`_on_discard_mode_ended`, and the
  reveal browser's confirm) — router back to `_local_player`, hand visibility
  re-hidden. **A network build replaces the `"peek"` branch alone** (serialize the
  choice, await the remote player's answer); no card-level code changes. New
  mandatory choices should go through this funnel rather than adding another
  `if _hotseat and pid != _local_player` branch.
- **Hand hover magnify:** the local player's own hand cards scale ×1.2 (`HOVER_MAGNIFY`
  on top of `HAND_CARD_SCALE`) + z-bump while hovered; hover/unhover signals are wired
  in `_spawn_card_node`.
  Protect / strike / ready points are board-public and still stop for the choice inline.
  Known v1 limits: a totem trigger owned by the off-screen player is resolved on the
  shared screen (social contract).
- **Wrap-up burst (Ctrl+Space = "end my turn", one press):** ending a turn spans
  FOUR priority windows (502.2 / 503.1) — your action window, the opponent's
  action window, your end-phase window, the opponent's end-phase window — and
  only then the wrap-up step (503.2, discard to max hand, no priority window).
  `_wrap_up_active` is the one-press burst that carries you through the ones that
  aren't real decisions. **Which ones those are, and why, is documented in full at
  `_end_phase_has_consequences` / `_wrap_up_skippable_window` in playtest.gd —
  read that block before widening or narrowing the burst.** In short:
  - **Your end-phase window is always inferred** from the press — you already
    declared you were done (`_maybe_turbo_pass` / `_do_turbo_pass` carry the burst
    through `_is_p1_main_action_window` instead of stopping at it).
  - **The opponent's action window is skipped only when the end phase is inert.**
    Both of their windows offer them the same options (non-turn player ⇒ instants
    only) and the end-phase one is strictly better for them — acting early hands
    you priority back at *sorcery* speed — so normally nothing is lost. **But
    `TurnManager._enter_end` resolves every end-of-turn trigger before opening
    that later window**: Infernal's burn, Venomstrike's victim sweep (rule 703.3 —
    killing it first is the printed answer), Thysta's idle-turn burn. So with any
    in-play card carrying an `end_of_turn_*` segment the stop is preserved —
    that window is the last one in which those can be answered.
  - **The opponent's end-phase window always stops** (via the ambush stop when
    they hold a legal play). That's the one real decision.
  - **The burst dies the moment the opponent adds a chain link** (`_drain_passes`,
    both the "stop" and the "no legal response" branches): your "I'm done" was a
    statement about the board as it stood, and once their link resolves you get a
    genuine sorcery-speed window back and must stop at it.
- `_played_this_action_phase` is a per-player Dictionary (was `_p1_played_this_action_phase`).
