## Graveyard browser (playtest.gd `_open_gy_dialog`)

The one modal used by every graveyard/deck search — quest rewards, hero powers
(Sen'zir), ally powers (Ophelia), hand abilities (Ancestral Spirit,
Call the Spirit, Cannibalize), the reveal-and-pick quests, and the read-only examine/Alt-peek.

- **Sections.** The body is a VBox of sections (`_gy_body`), each an optional
  heading plus its own grid. A search whose requirement is `owner:both` is
  split into **Your graveyard** / **Opponent's graveyard** — a flat grid gave
  the player no way to tell whose card was whose, which is the whole decision
  for Cannibalize and Ophelia. An empty side still renders its heading with
  "— empty —" so "nothing over there" is visible rather than inferred. Every
  other search passes no sections and renders exactly as before.
- **Confirmation step.** `owner:both` searches also set `_gy_ask_confirm`:
  Confirm raises a centered "Remove 3 ally cards from the game and heal 6
  damage?" popup (`_show_gy_confirm_popup`, z 25 — above the browser's
  dimmer/panel at 19/20) before anything is submitted. The heal clause appears
  only when the card carries `rfg_heal_per_card`. **Cancel is free**: nothing
  has reached the engine, so the browser is still open with the selection
  intact, and Esc / right-click-outside dismisses the popup layer first
  (`_on_gy_cancel_pressed`) rather than abandoning the search.
- **Multi-select submission.** `InputRouter.confirm_graveyard_selection`
  announces `target_ids` for exile abilities (`_ability_graveyard_dest` ==
  `"rfg"`) and `target_id` for the single-pick reanimate/power/quest paths.

## Card muting (auto-pass convenience — InputRouter.muted_ids)

Any of the local player's cards can be **muted** via the right-click context menu
("Mute (don't hold priority windows)" / "Unmute"). Muting is pure UI state
(`InputRouter.muted_ids`, instance_id-keyed, cleared on a new game, shared by both
hotseat seats) — the card stays fully playable and keeps its green highlight, but
`has_any_legal_play(true)` (the `exclude_muted` variant used by Turbo layer 3 in
`_drain_passes` and the hotseat ambush stop `_offscreen_has_play`) ignores it, so a
muted card alone never holds a priority window open. Use case: an always-completable
cost-only quest or a power the player never intends to fire at instant speed otherwise
forces a "Skip" click at every window. The player can still play a muted card during
standard windows, or in any priority window another (unmuted) card keeps open.
`toggle_mute` is a context-menu pseudo-action handled in `handle_context_action`
(never submitted to the engine); `card_mute_changed` drives a 🔇 badge on the
CardNode (`set_muted`). The pass-button label (`_update_pass_btn`) still counts muted
cards as legal plays. Muting follows the instance across zones (mute a quest in hand,
it stays muted in the resource row).

### Auto-mute — the `mute_when` CSV column

Muting (see the Card muting section) stops a card holding a priority window
open. `mute_when` is the **data-driven** form: a card can declare conditions
under which playing it is *provably pointless*, and the auto-pass probes then
treat it as muted without the player having to mute it by hand.

**It is PURE UI DATA and changes no rule.** Nothing in `game_logic/` reads it,
the card stays fully playable and green-highlighted, and `has_any_legal_play(false)`
(the pass-button label) still counts it. Being wrong only costs a stray "Skip"
click — never a card the player can't play. That is why the interpreter lives in
`InputRouter._mute_condition_holds` and not in the resolver.

The column is the 20th, which already existed unnamed and empty on every row —
so adding it needed no migration and no row gained a field. Value is a
`+`-joined list of tokens (the EXTRACOST convention); the card is auto-muted
when **any** token holds. `_is_inert_play` evaluates them for any card, ahead of
the two hard-coded hero weapon-bonus cases that predate the column.

| Token | Muted when | Cards |
|---|---|---|
| `end_phase` | `state.phase == "end"`, either player's | Lynda Steele (`dark_portal_178`, as `end_phase+own_turn`) — her "target ally must attack this turn if able" is a **600.2 pass-priority lock** that bites in the target controller's ACTION phase, and no ally can attack in the end phase, so using it there does nothing |
| `opponent_turn_pre_end_unless_discard` | it is an **opponent's** turn AND the phase is not `end` AND no discard is heading for the local player | Zapped Giants (`azeroth_361`), Kibler's Exotic Pets (`azeroth_355`), Big Game Hunter (`azeroth_348`) — the reveal-and-draw quests — plus every other quest whose reward is a **draw** with nothing timing-sensitive attached: Crown of the Earth (`dark_portal_289`), Your Fortune Awaits You (`azeroth_360`), Into the Maw of Madness (`azeroth_353`), In Dreams (`azeroth_352`), A Donation of Wool (`azeroth_351`), Battle of Darrowshire (`azeroth_347`), The Love Potion (`azeroth_356`), Lazy Peons (`dark_portal_303`), Hidden Enemies (`dark_portal_302`), Poison Water (`dark_portal_305`) — plus **Earthbind Totem** (`azeroth_107`), whose "opposing allies can't ready" aura is a static effect with no reveal or draw at all: there is simply no reason to land it before the opponent's end phase, the last moment before it starts locking their NEXT ready step — and It's a Secret to Everybody (`azeroth_354`), whose reward is a deck-stacking LOOK rather than a draw, but whose timing question is identical: the mute defers it to their end phase, which is immediately before your own ready and draw step, i.e. exactly when you want the deck stacked. **Four draw quests are deliberately NOT muted**, and `_test_mute_when_column` pins that too: Torek's Assault (`azeroth_345`) is gated on "your hero was damaged by an ally this turn", and The Perfect Stout's (`dark_portal_293`) "can't attack" mode only bites in the target controller's action phase — for BOTH, the opponent's turn IS the window the mute would hide; A New Plague (`dark_portal_304`) and Thwarting Kolkar (`dark_portal_309`) carry `require_turn_player` and are already illegal off-turn. The Defias Brotherhood (`azeroth_340`) and Counterattack! (`azeroth_343`) are left unmuted as a judgement call — their ally-count gates flicker DURING the opponent's turn, so an auto-pass could carry the player past the moment the gate was true. If you want the cards before the opponent acts you complete on your OWN turn; otherwise you would rather wait for **their end phase**, when they can no longer attack what you commit. Both exceptions are named in the token: their end phase is never muted, and an **incoming discard** un-mutes it at any window, because with one valuable card in hand you may want to draw first and have something else to pitch |
| `own_turn` | it is the controller's OWN turn | Lynda Steele — the same card, on the other axis: her 600.2 lock constrains the TARGET controller's action phase, and the grant lasts only "this turn", so aimed at an opposing ally on your own turn it expires before their action phase arrives. Her full value is `end_phase+own_turn`, which is also the first card to use more than one token |
| `opponent_turn` | it is an OPPONENT's turn — the exact inverse of `own_turn` | For the Horde! (`azeroth_344`), Rayder (`azeroth_45`), Ryn Dreamstrider (`azeroth_214`) — a "+ATK **while attacking** this turn" grant is dead off-turn, since in a duel you can only attack in your own action phase and the grant expires with the turn. **These three used to carry `require_turn_player`**, which made them ILLEGAL off-turn — a deviation, since the printed cards restrict no timing. The mute buys the same quiet windows without it |
| `outside_combat` | **no combat is happening** — no attack/defend window, no protect point, no `combat_attacker`, and no `propose_combat` link on the chain | Ravenous Bite (`azeroth_44`) — every use of the "+3 / -3 ATK this turn" swing is a COMBAT trick: turning a defend window around, pumping the attacker before the protect point to Skewer with it, or shrinking an attacking ally so the hero takes less. Outside combat both buffs just expire at end of turn having changed nothing. Note the CHAIN check is separate and not redundant: `combat_attacker` is not set until the proposal RESOLVES (602.1), so without it the response window to a proposal — a real window, where the pump is legal — would be muted |
| `no_combat_proposal` | there is **no `propose_combat` link on the chain** | Wing Clip (`dark_portal_42`) — the card is an INTERRUPT, and the one window worth holding open for it is a response window to a combat proposal. Once the attack window is open the interrupt moment has passed (602.1), and outside combat the "this turn" lock expires before the opponent's next attack step. Legality is untouched — the 1 melee damage is always a real, if small, play. Note this is **narrower** than `outside_combat` (Ravenous Bite), which stays lit for the whole combat step |
| `hero_not_defending_vs_ally` | the card's controller's hero is **not the settled defender against an attacking ALLY** — no open defend window, the defender is somebody else, or the attacker is a hero | Ghost Wolf (`azeroth_110`) — Point Blank's `hero_not_defending` narrowed by one more clause, because the effect removes **allies only**: a defend window against an attacking HERO is still a window in which the power resolves into nothing. Per 602.3 the attack window is muted too, our hero being merely a *proposed* defender there. **The card stays perfectly legal everywhere** — see the `remove_attacking_allies` effects-table row for why refusing the announcement would repeat the For the Horde! deviation — this only stops it holding priority windows open, which matters because the power is free and otherwise green at every window |
| `empty_hand` | the card's controller has an empty hand | Moonshadow (`azeroth_6`) — "shuffle your hand into your deck, then draw that many cards" shuffles nothing and draws nothing at hand size 0 |
| `hero_not_defending` | the card's controller's **hero is not the settled defender** — no open defend window, or the defender is somebody else | Point Blank (`dark_portal_37`) — its whole effect hangs on "If your hero is defending", and per 602.3 that is false everywhere but an open defend window, the attack window included (there the hero is only a PROPOSED defender). So anywhere else it resolves into nothing. **The card stays perfectly legal there** — see the `require_hero_defending` effects-table row for why refusing the announcement would repeat the For the Horde! deviation — this only stops it holding priority windows open. Note the mute is *narrower* than "no combat": during the attack window the card is muted even though an attacker exists and it is legal to play |

**Adding a token:** add a `match` arm to `InputRouter._mute_condition_holds`, a
row to this table, and the token to the `KNOWN` list in
`_test_mute_when_column` (`test_deck_manager.gd`) — that test also pins every
shipped value against the REAL database, because a typo in this column is
otherwise **completely silent**: the card just keeps holding windows open.

`_discard_incoming` is the incoming-discard heuristic behind the quest token. It
checks a pending discard plus any opposing chain link whose source carries a
discard-causing key (`_DISCARD_KEYS`: `discard_opponent`, `discard_per_damage`,
`attach_discard_controller`, `mark_destroy_discard`), reading the same
positional convention as BaseAI's chain scanners. It is deliberately cheap —
it only decides whether a window is held open. `discard_from_hand` is
deliberately NOT in the set: that is a card discarding its own controller
(A Donation of Wool), not an incoming threat.

**Both auto-pass probes apply it.** `has_any_legal_play` filters
`get_playable_card_ids()` through `_is_inert_play`, AND so does its
activated-power loop — that second loop is broader (it skips the target check),
so without the same filter a card the first loop muted would be re-admitted.


---

## Compulsion / taunt families (rules 600.2, 600.3, 601.2c-d) — four different things

"Taunt" is not one mechanic in this game. Four independent modifier families
look alike on the card and behave completely differently, and mixing them up is
the easy bug. **Check which one a new card is before reaching for an existing
flag.** All four are read live and never cached.

| | What it constrains | Enforced in | Cards |
|---|---|---|---|
| **"must attack if able"** (600.2) | The controller's **PASS**. Not what it attacks, not the order of their actions. | `must_attack_blocks_pass` → the gate in `_pass_priority` | Lynda Steele, Mocking Blow (clause 2 of 3) |
| **"can attack only X if able"** (601.2c-d) | The legal **DEFENDERS** of one attacker. Doesn't force anything. | `get_legal_defenders` (specified-defender set) | Mocking Blow (clause 3), Sarmoth (`sarmoth_taunt`) |
| **"can't attack" / "can't protect"** | Removes legality outright. | `get_legal_attackers` / `get_legal_protectors` | Litori, Frostbolt, Frost Shock, Jin'lak, Lady Jaina, Hannah |
| **"can't attack [character]"** | Removes ONE character from an attacker's legal defenders. Neither forbids attacking nor forces it — it redirects by subtraction. | `_attacker_barred_from` → `get_legal_defenders` **and** the 601.3 re-check | Wing Clip |
| **"can't attack unless its controller pays"** (600.3) | **PRICES** an attack. Neither forbids nor forces — it makes the proposal cost resources, paid at announcement (412.2) and refundable. | `attack_tax` -> `get_legal_defenders` affordability filter + the `propose_combat` arm of `submit_action` | Winter's Grasp |

Four consequences worth holding onto:

- **A "must" and a "can attack only" are orthogonal and often printed together.**
  Lynda forces an attack but leaves the choice of victim entirely to her target's
  controller — she is a tempo tax, not a redirect. Mocking Blow does both, which
  is what makes it an actual redirect: forced to swing, and only at your hero.
  Sarmoth is the mirror image — a redirect with no compulsion at all, so a player
  who simply doesn't attack is unaffected by him.
- **"Can't" beats "must."** A character under both is *unable*, and 600.2's "if
  able" therefore releases the lock. So Frostbolt answers Lynda cleanly.
- **A tax beats "must" too, and this is the subtle one.** 600.3 says a character
  under both "is unable to attack (and consequently need not attack) unless its
  controller **chooses** to pay that cost" — so the lock lifts even when the tax
  is perfectly affordable. A player is never compelled to spend resources, which
  makes Winter's Grasp a clean answer to Lynda Steele and Mocking Blow.
  `get_must_attack_ids` therefore counts an attacker as forced only while some
  legal defender can be attacked for FREE.
- **The specified-defender set is a UNION, not a chain of filters.** Every
  `can_attack_only` source contributes to one set that `get_legal_defenders`
  intersects with the legal defenders once, then falls back to the full list if
  that intersection is empty (601.2c). Narrowing them one after another instead
  would let whichever ran last silently erase the other, and would make an
  unsatisfiable narrowing forbid combat outright — which is exactly what the
  fallback exists to prevent. The rulebook's own worked example (601.2d: Sarmoth
  + Mocking Blow) is the test, `_test_mocking_blow_stacks_with_taunt`.

Not yet implemented, and each needs its own flag rather than a reuse of the
above: 600.2a's "must attack **[a specified character]** if able" (a *combined*
must+only, e.g. Vigilant Guard), 600.2b's interaction between the two groups
(no shipped card pair triggers it yet), and 600.3a's "can't attack **or exhaust**
unless its controller pays a cost" (attacking is free of the extra cost there;
exhausting for any other reason is not — a different gate from Winter's Grasp's).

---

## Combat window rules (enforced in multiple layers)

Rule 601.1 (combat only during non-combat action phase) and rule 502.1/1199 (non-instants same) are enforced in three layers:

1. **`stack_resolver.gd` — submission guard** (`_can_propose_combat`, `_can_play_non_instant`, `_can_play_ability`, `_can_place_resource`): all check `not (combat_attack_window or combat_defend_window or in_protect_point)` and `pending_actions.is_empty()`.
2. **`input_router.gd` — highlight guard** (`get_playable_card_ids`, `can_attack` in context menu): same flags, so allies don't stay green during combat windows.
3. **`input_router.gd` — direct click guard** (`handle_card_click`, in-play ally/hero branch): `can_propose` check before calling `get_legal_attackers`, prevents entering targeting animation when `pending_actions` non-empty or combat window is open.

**Attack animation** (`_animate_attack` in `board_renderer.gd`) fires on `combat_concluded` (not `combat_started`) so the lunge plays toward the actual defender after any protector swap, not the proposed defender before the protect point.

**Cancelled combat (rule 603.1b):** when a combatant leaves play before the conclusion (Withdraw bounce, instant removal, Blink's remove-from-combat), `_do_combat_conclusion` emits `combat_cancelled` (payload `attacker_id`/`defender_id`/`reason` ∈ `attacker_gone`/`defender_gone`/`attacker_removed`) followed by `combat_concluded` with `cancelled = true` and 0/0 damage. The renderer **skips the attack lunge** on a cancelled conclusion, and `playtest.gd` shows a centered transient notice (`_show_combat_cancelled_notice` → `_show_transient_notice`, a non-blocking self-fading HUD banner) plus a game-log line. Any new "combat fizzles" path must go through this branch so the UI stays honest.

**Window-opened events must drain (playtest.gd):** both `attack_window_opened` and `defend_window_opened` handlers call `_drain_passes()`. When the *human's* pass is the one that resolves the pending action, `pass_priority` returns only the resolution events (no `priority_passed`), so nothing else re-drives the AI — priority sits on the AI forever and the game stalls. `_drain_passes()` is re-entrancy-guarded (`_draining`), so calling it from a handler fired mid-drain is safe. Any new event that hands priority to a potentially-AI player needs the same treatment.

---

## Display / layout

- Viewport: **1920×1080**, `stretch/mode=canvas_items`, `stretch/aspect=expand` (fills 16:9 window, no grey bars).
- All card spawn positions and deck back sprites are derived from `_renderer.zone_anchors` — **never hardcode a spawn Vector2**. `_spawn_zone_nodes(zone_id, color)` reads the anchor automatically. The deck back sprite loop does the same via `_renderer.zone_anchors.get("p1_deck"/"p2_deck")`.
- At startup, `_spawn_zone_nodes` is called for every visible zone: hero_row, hand, ally_row, resource_row, graveyard, chain (deck excluded — back sprite only).
- UI bar occupies y=960..1080. Board occupies y=0..950. `CENTER_X = 960`.
- **Symmetric board (all modes, incl. vs AI):** each player's hero/deck/graveyard column
  sits on THEIR right-hand side — P1 at screen-right (deck x=1820, grave x=1640,
  hero_card (1820,620)), P2 at screen-left (deck x=100, grave x=280, hero_card (100,280)).
  The game log is a HUD overlay on the left gutter (covers P2's column), hidden by
  default — L toggles it.
- **Card facing (all modes):** cards in `p2_*` zones render rotated 180° (facing P2,
  TTS-style). `CardNode.facing_degrees` is the base every rotation write composes with:
  exhaust = facing+90, ready/settle = facing. Facing is set from the zone prefix in
  `place_card_in_zone`, `_animate_move` (side change = control change), and
  `reconcile_from_state` (`BoardRenderer._facing_for_zone`). Never write a card's
  `rotation_degrees` with a bare 0/90 — always compose with `facing_degrees`.
  The hero HP bar follows the same facing: P2's bar is rotated 180° about its own
  center (`_ensure_hero_bar`), so its label/fill read upright for P2.
- **Sound** is an autoload (`game_logic/audio/sound_manager.gd`): `SoundManager.play_random("<folder>")` picks a random clip from `assets/card_sounds/<folder>/`. Wired in playtest's event handler — card draw (`SFX_CardGrab`), card play (`SFX_CardMoveFast`) and every in-game shuffle (`deck_shuffled` → `SFX_DeckShuffle`; setup and mulligan shuffle without emitting that event, so they stay silent). New SFX go on an EVENT, not at a call site.
- Pass button shows **"Sacrifice a pet [Space]"** (disabled, no color change) when `pending_pet_sacrifice_player == "p1"`.

---

## Win conditions (rules 102.1a, 410.6b)

A player loses immediately when:
- **`hero_defeated`** — their hero takes fatal damage / is destroyed. Emitted at
  the damage and destroy sites in `stack_resolver.gd`.
- **`decked`** — they are *required to draw* from an empty deck (410.6b).
  **Emptying the deck is not itself a loss** — only the next required draw is.

`GameLogic.draw_one(state, player_id)` is the single "required draw" primitive;
every draw site (`StackResolver._draw_one`, `TurnManager._draw_one`,
`shuffle_hand_into_deck_and_draw`) delegates to it, so a new draw effect gets the
decked rule for free. It emits `deck_empty` + `player_decked` and calls
`GameLogic.mark_decked`, which records the player in `GameState.decked_players`
and ends the game. Effects that merely LOOK at the deck (`reveal_pick`) are not
draws and must not call it.

If every remaining player is decked at that moment the game is a **draw**
(`GameEvent.game_drawn`, payload `draw = true`, `winner = ""`).

**The `game_over` payload carries the win condition**: `winner`, `loser`,
`losers`, `reason`, `draw`. `GameEvent.game_over_explanation(payload, names)` is
the ONE place that turns it into a sentence ("P2's hero received fatal damage.
P1 wins!" / "P2 was decked — required to draw from an empty deck. P1 wins!"),
shared by the game-over dialog, the board status line and the game log. A new
win condition adds a `reason` branch there and nowhere else.

Tests: `_test_decked_loses_the_game`, `_test_empty_deck_alone_is_not_a_loss`,
`_test_simultaneous_decking_is_a_draw`, `_test_game_over_explanations`.
`_drive_turns` stocks both decks with filler so multi-turn scenarios don't deck
out (pass `stock_decks = false` for hand/graveyard-counting scenarios).

## Known open issues / next work

- **Combat window AI:** the non-turn AI now plays tagged **combat instants** (Quick Strike) during attack/defend windows via `BaseAI.combat_instant_action` — see AI conventions above. It still never uses ally activated powers (e.g. Grimdron) to counter-attack during enemy combats. Future: extend the window logic to defensive ally-power plays.
- **Next cards to implement:** Check `data/cards.csv` for candidates.
- **`logic/BasicAI_behavior.txt` is legacy** and may be stale — actual AI is fully in `game_logic/ai/base_ai.gd`.
